# Changelog

All notable changes to the agent-board-toolkit are documented here. The format follows [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/), and the project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html) (pre-1.0: features minor-bump, fixes/refactors/docs patch-bump). See [`../VERSIONING.md`](../VERSIONING.md) for the full policy.

> **This changelog begins at [0.8.2].** Earlier releases (v0.4.x–v0.8.1) predate the changelog and are recorded only as git tags — run `git tag -l 'v*' --sort=-version:refname` and inspect each tag's release commit for their contents. They were not retro-documented here to avoid reconstructing summaries after the fact.

## [Unreleased]
- **card#8556** — **a mutating verb reported success it never read back; `kbcard delete --hard` was the severe member, and a false success there says a DL ref is released.** The class: the verb issued its write, the HTTP status class said `2xx`, and it printed a success built out of what it **sent** rather than out of a read of what the server now holds — so a mutation the server did not apply reached the caller as applied. Twelve such sites were derived across `bin/`; `unlink` (which already re-read **both** ends after its `DELETE`) was the counter-example the fix is modelled on. **Why `--hard` is not one member among twelve:** its false success tells the operator the card's `dl_number` ref is **released**, and that release is an input to the next DL claim (`docs/DL-COUNTER-RECOVERY.md` § *Why it strands*), so a wrong answer there propagates into the board's numbering instead of stopping at one bad print — and the operation is irreversible.
  **THE DISCRIMINATOR NOBODY WOULD HAVE GUESSED, and the reason a naive read-back would have been worse than none.** `GET /tasks/{task}.json` binds `->withTrashed()` **but the controller 404s a trashed card unless the caller passes `?trashed=1`** (read out of the server's own `routes/api.php` and `TasksController::guardTrashedVisibility`, not inferred from a response). So a **bare** re-read answers `404` for a card that is merely in the trash exactly as it does for one that was purged — and a `--hard` confirmed on that read would report *"permanently deleted, DL ref released"* for a card still sitting in the trash **still pinning the allocation floor**, i.e. it would manufacture the very state the runbook exists to recover from. Every card re-read this change adds passes `?trashed=1`, and the selftest **models the server's rule** rather than asserting the query string as a string: drop the parameter and the *still-trashed-after-force-delete* leg stops failing and starts reporting a purge.
  **The contract, and it is a caller-visible change.** One new exit code, spelled as an exit code so the distinction survives `2>/dev/null` and needs no prose parsing: **`0` APPLIED** (read back, and the read agrees with what was asked) · **`1` NOT APPLIED and KNOWN** (refused, or a read-back that MEASURED the mutation missing — reported as *HARD FAILURE*, quoting what the board holds) · **`2` bad invocation, nothing sent** · **`3` UNVERIFIED WRITE** — the request went out and the confirming read could not be made (did not complete, refused `401`/`403`, or a `2xx` nothing could be read out of); nothing claims it landed and nothing claims it did not. **`3` is a THIRD outcome, not a rename of `1`:** every state that was `1` and is still knowable is still `1` — asserted in the suite, because an `rc 3` arm that swallowed a definite refusal would turn every known failure into *"we cannot say"*, the opposite of the fix. A caller testing `non-zero` is unaffected; one that must not act on an unconfirmed write tests for `3`.
  **What now reads back, and on which surface** — one hoisted owner each, not twelve call-site patches: `_kbc_card_witness` (the card, `?trashed=1`, an exact sibling of the existing `_kbc_link_witness`) plus `_kbc_confirm_card` (read + rule, saying nothing — the verb's own words stay at the call site) and `_kbc_unverified` (the single owner of the unverified report and its rc, replacing seven hand-written near-copies of that sentence). **`delete`** — the soft step is confirmed by `deleted_at` reading back stamped, and `--hard`'s force-delete by the card reading back **absent**; under `--hard` the soft read-back is also the force-delete's **precondition** (it `422`s unless the card is trashed), so a soft step this run could not confirm now sends **no force-delete at all** — an irreversible request does not go out behind a precondition nobody read. **`archive`** — `archived_at` stamped. **`comment`** — the echoed id must be in the card's own `comments` array (the only read surface a comment has; `GET` on the comments path is `405`). **`link`** — the link must be on the from-card's `linked_tasks`, through the same witness `unlink` uses (its diagnostic verb is now a parameter, so one owner serves both ends of the pair). **`field create` / `set-options` / `retype`** — off the board's custom-field index, the only read surface a field has, matching what `field delete` already did; `retype`'s check runs **before** any `--restamp-dl` pass, since canonicalizing card values against a type this run never confirmed would be rewriting them on a guess. **`field delete`** stops collapsing its primitive's three distinct boards to one rc: its *2xx-then-unreadable-re-read* arm is now `3`, while *refused* and *still-defined* stay `1`.
  **What deliberately did NOT change, stated because a half-claim is worse than none.** `create-card`, `move` and `patch --column` gained a **comparison**, not a second request: the echo is the server's own post-write resource (`update` returns the persisted model, the `_action` path returns `$task->fresh()`), so comparing it is a real check — but only `workflow_stage_id` is compared, because `name` / `tags` / `external_id` / `payload` ride the server's own normalization (a decorated `--pr` goes as a string, `--dl` is canonicalized, the tag list is replaced wholesale) and a byte-wise compare there would manufacture a *HARD FAILURE* on a write that landed exactly as asked — a false claim in the other direction, and a worse one. Widening it needs those round-trips measured first. `bin/dl-a0-backfill-triaged`'s batch `PATCH` and `bin/board-card-start`'s hook `PATCH` are **not** fixed here: the first needs the same unmeasured tag round-trip, and the second would add a `GET` to every `git checkout` — a cost decision, not a defect ruling. Both stay on the card.
  **`bin/adopt-to-dl`** stops saying *"card not adopted"* on a `kbcard` rc that means nobody read the board: on `3` it names the stamp UNVERIFIED, says the DL is already claimed, and points at the idempotent re-run — the old sentence would have sent an operator to re-adopt a card that may already carry the stamp.
  **Coverage.** `tests/kbcard-selftest.sh` gains a block driven against a faked kanban whose route table **models the trashed-visibility rule**; every new guard was watched RED under a mutation of the thing it guards — the re-read dropped (`--hard` then reports a purge over a card still in the trash, printing *"HARD-deleted"* on the still-trashed leg), `?trashed=1` dropped, the soft precondition removed (the force-delete then goes out unread), `_kbc_unverified` returning `1`, and `archive`'s confirm removed. New `tests/readback-before-success-census.sh` re-derives the population across `bin/` on demand and carries a self-control that refuses to print a population its classifier can no longer discriminate; it is a **census, not a gate** — deliberately not named `*-selftest.sh`, so the matrix-parity orphan check does not claim it and no workflow runs it, the same shape as `hand-enumerated-population-census.sh` beside it.
- **card#8548** — **three shipped surfaces described a guard's reach in their own words, and all three were wider than its predicate.** `docs/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/CONSOLIDATION-PLAN.md` and PR #323's body each said `tests/prelude-shadow-selftest.sh` *"reds on the seventh copy"* of the adopted `_adopt_fn` extractor. It compared **names**: it red only on a copy *called* `_adopt_fn`, and a seventh hand-spelling under any other name left it at **rc 0** — which is the exact shape of the five residual sites the same PR documented, so the doc promised the guard covered the drift the PR itself said remained. ⛔ **Not hypothetical: further copies had already been minted.** Measured on `dev` at `52125a6`, `promote-source-qualify-selftest.sh` carried two `awk` copies of the same extraction — landed one release after the claim, with the suite green — and PR #323's own out-of-scope note had already named a third spelling, a `grep -E '^uint_ok\(\)'`, that no name comparison could see either.
  1. **`prelude-shadow-selftest.sh` grows a second leg, over the IDIOM rather than the name.** Its population is every **pattern literal anchoring a shell function's definition at column zero**, re-derived from `bin/`, `hooks/` and `tests/` on every run — the same population `.github/workflows/ci.yml` shellchecks, taken from `tests/_shipped-shell-lib.sh`, this repo's one owner of that derivation — one record per file with an occurrence COUNT, dispositioned in an `EXTRACTORS` list carrying each site's reason. Three ways to red: an underived file, a listed file the scanner no longer finds, and a listed file whose count MOVED (the N+1th-copy case). ⛔ **The ANCHOR is the predicate, not the tool — and the DELIMITER is not enumerated either.** A list of known spellings is an inclusion list and fails open on the next one, which is precisely how two `awk` copies of `sed`'s job walked past; a list of known DELIMITERS is the same defect one level down, and the first two cuts of this leg were exactly that. **Not a hypothetical improvement:** the shipped three-delimiter predicate was blind to two copies live in this tree — `locale-range-guard-selftest.sh` and `next-dl-selftest.sh` — both now derived and dispositioned. ⛔ **The guard's own header is the ONE authority for what it covers, what it deliberately cannot see, and where its remaining bound is, and this entry does not paraphrase any of it** — three independent paraphrases of one predicate is how card#8548 happened. Positive fixtures drive a tool the guard has never heard of in the spelling that tool's authors actually write; negative fixtures pin each bound to a spelling live in this repo.
  2. **`_fn_src` is the text-returning half the residual was waiting on, and `_adopt_fn` is now that plus an `eval`** — one definition of *where does this function's text start and stop* for callers that RUN it and callers that READ it. The sites that needed only a function's TEXT are migrated onto it, **including the three that eval THROUGH A RENAME** (`kb-host-guard-selftest.sh` × 2, `kb-positional-guard-selftest.sh`): the rename is a `${var/…}` those call sites apply themselves, so the **alias parameter** an earlier draft of this entry filed as a design call was never needed — the claim is corrected here rather than quietly dropped, because a reader would otherwise have left three copies in place waiting on a primitive that was not missing. Byte-identical output verified before each migration. ⛔ **And `_fn_src` no longer answers a ONE-LINE function silently.** `max_int() { …; }` has no `^}` line for the range to stop at, so it ran on to the NEXT function's closing brace and handed back that function's whole body too — 129 lines, at rc 0, to a caller its docblock had promised "text, or exit 1". It now REFUSES, naming what would have been swallowed. So the residual is one shape, not two: sites that locate a one-line function and need a one-line MODE the primitive still does not have. **Which files those are, and how many, is in the guard's `EXTRACTORS` list and in no prose anywhere** — the guard derives and prints it, because *"five hand-spellings"* was two short within one release.
  3. **The runtime-check call-site pin no longer reds on an unrelated population.** `tests/token-duplication-selftest.sh` compared `_rc_expand_home` call sites in the bin against `_kb_expand_home` call sites **anywhere in `bin/_kb-board-lib.sh`**. The lib is a far larger surface than the four functions runtime-check mirrors, so a legitimate new expansion in an unmirrored lib function reds it with **no mirror drift at all** (measured: planted in `kb_resolve_env` ⇒ old pin **1 FAIL**, `expected '2' got '1'`) — and because the failure named an expected-vs-got COUNT, the remedy it invited was to add a matching call to the mirror, the opposite of correct. **The population is now the mirrored region, in two legs**: (A) every `_rc_expand_home` call in the bin is INSIDE the store-pointer mirror — the bin has no business expanding `~` anywhere else, and both dropped sites were outside it; (B) inside that mirror it expands as often as `kb_coord_store_token_file` does, the only leg that reads the lib and it reads one function of it. Each failure **names the drift, the direction and the remedy** instead of a count, and says outright which side must NOT be changed to match. The negative control is **shipped in the file**, not merely measured once.
  4. **`tests/_selftest-prelude.sh` stops enumerating its own contents.** Its docblock listed them and went stale on every addition — `has_line`, `expect_value_flags`, `_value_flags`, `_adopt_fn`, `_since_stamp` and `_stamp_taken` all landed without it, and it named `fails` (a counter VARIABLE) as a helper. Three rounds each appended the one missing name and left the next addition to re-mint the drift. The list is **derived** now: `prelude-shadow-selftest.sh` extracts it from the file and prints it as a denominator every run, so `bash tests/prelude-shadow-selftest.sh` IS the current contents and cannot disagree with them. The admission rule it also stated — *"only the harness the selftests all shared verbatim"* — is dropped as false too: `_adopt_fn` arrived with one caller.
  **Seen to fail, each on a copy of the tree, each reverted and re-verified.** Leg 2: a seventh hand-spelling under a DIFFERENT name planted in `kbcard-selftest.sh` ⇒ **1 FAIL**, undispositioned file named (this is the card's own scenario, and the pre-change guard was rc 0 on it); an N+1th copy inside an already-dispositioned file ⇒ **1 FAIL**, count drift named; a dispositioned site migrated away ⇒ **1 FAIL**, stale disposition named. ⛔ **And the widened predicate was seen to fail on code nobody planted**: replacing the three-delimiter list with the derivation put `locale-range-guard-selftest.sh` and `next-dl-selftest.sh` on the undispositioned list ⇒ **1 FAIL** naming both — two hand-spelled copies that had been live and invisible, which is the card's own failure reproduced against the fix rather than against a fixture. Its predicate also carries six negative controls, each pinned to a spelling live in this repo that a sloppier one sweeps up: the idiom in a COMMENT, a `${src/host_ok() \{/…}` rename, a plain function DEFINITION, a `jq` `def` extraction and a runtime-composed anchor (both outside the stated bound), and — the price of an open delimiter set — an ordinary function CALL sitting after the literal has closed. `_fn_src`'s new refusal: driven over every live caller in the suite ⇒ **0** refusals, and over the two one-liners the disposition list names ⇒ refuses both, naming `_ata_adopt_decision` and `max_dl`/`board_claim` as what the range would have swallowed. The `ci.yml` drift row: run against a root with no workflow ⇒ **1 FAIL**. Leg 3: `_rc_add_source`'s expansion restored ⇒ leg A reds naming the line; the mirror's call removed ⇒ leg B reds *"MISSING an expansion its original performs"*; a second expansion inside the mirror ⇒ leg B reds *"expands MORE often … DROP the extra call"*; and the unmirrored-lib plant ⇒ **GREEN** where the old pin red. ⚑ **Not changed:** no bin, no `.github/`, no `VERSION`; nothing about what any tool accepts or rejects moved.
- **card#8538** — **⚠ THREE ACCEPT/REJECT CHANGES: the release correlation keys are guarded, the declared source is checked against the repo it is declared in, and an empty card manifest is no longer a clean line.** `.release-pr.json`'s `promote.source`, `ref_token_regex` and `card_token_regex` are what a release correlates on, and until now nothing anywhere read them for correctness — `bin/release-artifacts-check`'s own header named them as keys that *"stay head-read and unguarded"*. Every one of them fails **silently**: with `card_token_regex` absent, `release-pr-body` emits a **0-id** `shipped-cards` manifest at **rc 0** under the line *"All shipped refs have a tracking card"* — a clean assertion about coverage nothing measured. That is how the gap reached the consumer repos card#8423 enumerates before anyone noticed — that card owns the list, and no count of them is restated here — and a silent zero is the one failure shape a release gate must not have.
  1. **`bin/release-artifacts-check` refuses a `promote`-declaring config that omits a correlation key.** PREVIOUSLY: rc 0, unread. NOW: rc 2 naming the key **and the tool that reads it**, unless the key is declared absent **by name** in a new top-level `"unset_correlation_keys": ["card_token_regex"]` array — logged as an `UNSET:` line, exactly like `retired_artifacts`. A key both declared and listed is a config error, and so is a name that is not one of the three (a misspelling acknowledges nothing and would otherwise read as if it had). Also refused: a key that is present but empty/whitespace-only, and a token regex `grep -E` cannot compile — the readers pipe these into `grep -oiE` behind a `|| true`, so an uncompilable regex does not fail, it yields the same empty manifest. **The trigger is the `promote` block, never the `artifacts` array:** that block is the repo's claim that its releases move cards, so a repo with no `promote` block owes none of the three keys and is unaffected. Read at **head only** (there is no narrowing to catch — deleting a key is what this reds on), run **before** the empty-`artifacts` opt-out, and **skipped under `--classify-only`**, whose post-merge caller cannot act on a config finding. ⚠ **Head-only has a cost the refusal message now names:** a branch cut BEFORE the keys landed on its base branch reds here having touched no config, and "declare the key" is the wrong instruction for its author — the key is already on their base, so the message tells them to **merge the base branch** instead of landing a duplicate declaration (⚠ round 3 below replaces how that instruction is CHOSEN — the message now derives it from the key's state at the fork point and the base tip rather than asking the author questions).
  2. **`bin/promote-released-cards` refuses a resolved `.promote.source` that is not `$GITHUB_REPOSITORY`.** PREVIOUSLY: a typo'd or copy-pasted slug passed every shape check, correlated against no card, and the job promoted **0** at **rc 0** with `⊘` lines nobody reads. NOW: rc 2 **before any board read**, case-folded on both sides, naming the declared value, the channel it came from and the repo the run is actually in. It does **not** fire on `"*"` (a declaration, not a repo name) or when `$GITHUB_REPOSITORY` is unset (off a runner there is no identity to check against), and it is **not** escapable via `--source` — that flag overrides the value, not the check. A **fork** running a release workflow is refused by the same leg. Presence and shape are local facts; *"is this the right repo"* is not, which is why this leg lives here and not in the config check.
  3. **`bin/release-pr-body` reports an empty manifest over a non-empty range** in a new `## Correlation gaps` body section (and on stderr), instead of omitting a footer indistinguishable from one that legitimately had nothing to say. One rule: a **declared** key that matched nothing is named; an **undeclared** key is named only when the range correlated nothing through the other one either, so a repo that uses one spelling is not warned every release about the other. Exit status is unchanged — this tool generates the body a reviewer reads, and a non-zero would block the PR the finding is written for. The section needs no board, no token and no promote tool, unlike `## Card coverage`.
  **Blast radius, measured rather than asserted** — the population is every `.release-pr.json` at every branch head of the org, re-derived by enumerating repos with `gh repo list` and probing each branch's config (no hand-kept list). **Zero currently-shipping consumer config is newly refused by any of the three legs**: the three `dev`-line configs declare all three keys, `promote.source` equals each repo's own slug (or is `"*"`), and the fourth repo runs none of these tools. What *is* newly refused is a PR whose **head is cut from `main`** in a repo whose `main` has not yet received the keys through a release — the config there predates card#8421, and the fix is the same one-line edit on that branch. ⚠ **The SHA pin does NOT contain this, and an earlier draft of this entry said it did.** On a `pull_request` event GitHub takes the WORKFLOW — and therefore the action pin — from the base-merged ref, while `release-artifacts-gate.yml` hands the action `pull_request.head.sha`: the pin travels with the base branch and the config does not. So the day a consumer bumps its pin on its integration branch, every branch it has open that was cut before the keys landed reds on a config it never touched. That is the population the third remedy in leg 1's message exists for; it is a one-merge fix, not a config edit, and no branch that carries the keys is affected.
  **Seen to fail** — each leg watched red before it was fixed, and each leg's new selftest arms run against the pre-fix binary (`git show origin/dev:bin/<tool>` in place) and watched red: new cases in `tests/release-artifacts-selftest.sh` and `tests/release-pr-body-selftest.sh`, and a new § 7 plus an `identity-off` mutant in `tests/promote-source-qualify-selftest.sh`. No count is restated here: re-derive it by putting the pre-fix binary in place and counting the file's own `FAIL` lines. **`tests/_selftest-prelude.sh` now `unset`s `$GITHUB_REPOSITORY` for the whole suite** — a floor, not a per-site scrub: Actions sets that variable, leg 2 reads it, and several files drive `promote-released-cards` with a fixture source while asking about something else, so on a runner they would refuse for the wrong reason while passing on a laptop. A per-site scrub is a hand-kept list whose missed site is a CI-only red; three hand scrubs had already missed a fourth file when the full suite surfaced it. `promote-source-qualify-selftest.sh` § 7 sets the variable explicitly per arm — it is the subject there — and asserts the floor holds. **The floor covers `tests/`; `ci.yml`'s `promote-action-selftest` was the one CI surface outside it** — it executes the script on a runner, where `$GITHUB_REPOSITORY` IS set, and its fail-closed step asserted only `outcome == 'failure'` while its stated subject is the `KANBAN_WRITEBACK_TOKEN` guard. Leg 2 runs BEFORE that guard, so an outcome-only assertion now had a second way to pass. It asserts the refusal MESSAGE instead (this repo's `.promote.source` is `"*"`, so leg 2 is exempt here — which is precisely why an outcome-only assertion would have gone on passing after it stopped being exempt).
  **⚠ Review round 2 — a leg that minted a copy of the rule its own comment enumerates, and a refusal that could not tell two authors apart.** Leg 2 canonicalized `$GITHUB_REPOSITORY` with two inline lines byte-identical to the config-side copy six lines of comment above them: a FOURTH mirror of the server's `canonicalizeSource`, minted directly under the comment claiming to enumerate every copy of it, which made BOTH hand-kept censuses false in the same commit — this bin's, and `bin/adopt-to-dl`'s *"plus TWO in `bin/promote-released-cards`"* — with no drift guard behind either. The shell rule is now ONE function, **`src_canon`**, called by both sides of the comparison (canon #5 at the second real caller), so the censuses describe an owner instead of enumerating copies. **And the census is no longer only a comment: `tests/promote-source-qualify-selftest.sh` § 3d re-derives the mirror set from `bin/` on every run** — attributed per file with a COUNT, comment lines excluded, and redding when a copy appears, moves, or stops being named in either census. ⚠ **The PREDICATE that census was derived with is replaced in round 3 below** — it matched two spellings of case fold while claiming to cover every one, so read that paragraph for what this leg actually enforces. ⚠ **That is a new red condition in the suite CI runs:** a PR minting a fifth spelling of this rule, or renaming one without updating both censuses, now fails. It carries controls in both directions (a re-minted fourth copy counts 3; a fold merely NAMED in a comment counts 2), because the cost is measured rather than argued — card#8421 already forced one correction to this rule across this same pair, and a maintainer patching the three named copies would have left the identity comparand behind, at which point the leg either reds every consumer's release or accepts a false declaration. **Leg 1's third remedy discriminated three authors instead of one** (⚠ **superseded in round 3 below: there is a FOURTH, and the partition is now derived from the config's history rather than enumerated**). *"If the key is already on your base branch, merge the base branch"* is true of the deliberate DELETER too — the author this leg's head-only read exists to catch — and their remedy is the opposite one. Measured on fixture repos through the real binary: the stale author's `git merge <base>` lands the key and the rerun is **rc 0**, while the deleter's says `Already up to date`, the config does not move, and the rerun is **rc 2 on a byte-identical message** — a loop. The refusal now asks two questions its reader can actually run (is the key on your base at all; has THIS branch edited the config), names the no-op merge for the deleter, and points them at `unset_correlation_keys`; the never-declared repo — card#8423's population, this leg's primary catch — is the one Q1 sends to "declare it", since merging is equally a no-op there. Driven end to end on three fixtures: stale ⇒ merge ⇒ rc 0; deleter ⇒ acknowledge ⇒ rc 0 with an `UNSET:` line; never-declared ⇒ declare ⇒ rc 0. No accept/reject rule of any of the three legs changed in this round.
  **⛔ Review round 3 — a guard whose STATED SCOPE was wider than its predicate, and a fourth author class in four rounds.** Both are closed by DERIVING the population rather than adding a case to it, because both had gained exactly one cell per round. **(1) § 3d's predicate now IS its stated population.** (⚠ **ROUND 4 BELOW REPLACES THIS ENTIRE MECHANISM** — the two-stage derivation described in this item was still an ALLOW-LIST and was defeated five more times; read that paragraph for what actually ships.) It matched two SPELLINGS of case fold — `tr '[:upper:]' '[:lower:]'` and `ascii_downcase` — while its stated scope was *"every case-fold under `bin/`"*, and those two covered **5 of the 12 folds actually present**. Measured, not argued: a fifth mirror written `${SOURCE_ALT,,}`, an idiom this repo already uses three times under `bin/` (one of them in `bin/release-artifacts-check`), was minted inside `bin/promote-released-cards` as a new site with nothing removed, and the leg answered **rc 0, 0 FAIL, 14/14 ok** — the exact defect it exists to stop, invisible to it. A predicate narrower than its stated scope is worse than silence: the maintainer auditing against the census gets confidence instead of a question. The predicate is now derived in **two stages**. STAGE 1 re-derives the set of LANGUAGES under `bin/` every run — every file's shebang (plus the `# shellcheck shell=` directive a sourced lib declares itself with) union every case-folding-capable interpreter invoked at a COMMAND POSITION on a non-comment line, so a mention inside a string is not mistaken for a language — and a new language reds there, before any of its fold spellings can hide in a stage-2 blind spot. STAGE 2 enumerates, for those languages only, every construct the LANGUAGE provides for folding case: bash's `${v,,}`, `${v@L}` and `declare`/`typeset`/`local -l`, `tr` in three spellings, `sed`'s `\L`, `\l` and `y///`, awk's `tolower`, jq's `ascii_downcase`, python's `.lower()` and `.casefold()`, and `dd conv=lcase` — 16 rows. **Every row is checked twice on every run, so neither half can be a decoration:** its sample is EXECUTED and must really fold `AbC` (with its declared expectation itself checked to be a case-only transform that folded at least one character), and it is minted as a fifth mirror inside `bin/promote-released-cards` and must move the census by **exactly one** — coverage demonstrated row by row rather than asserted for the table. The census over `bin/` is consequently **12 folds across 7 files**, up from 5 across 4, and the nine that are NOT mirrors (`_kb_looks_like_pasted_secret` and its awk env parser, in the lib and again in `agent-board-toolkit-runtime-check`; `kbcard`'s stage-name fold; `_shellcheck-pinned`'s `uname -s`; `release-artifacts-check`'s CHANGELOG selector) are declared by name and reason. **Seen to fail: five legal fold spellings minted into the REAL bin** — the `${SOURCE_ALT,,}` block that defeated the predecessor, plus `${v@L}`, `awk tolower`, `tr A-Z a-z` and `sed \L`, four of which the old predicate could not see at all — **each reds 21 arms; each restored byte-exact.** **(2) The correlation-key refusal DERIVES its remedy instead of asking for it.** The old message asked *"has THIS BRANCH edited the config?"*, a proxy for *"did this branch ever carry the key"* that is neither necessary nor sufficient: a branch cut before the keys landed that edited `.release-pr.json` for an unrelated reason answers YES, and was told **THIS BRANCH DROPPED IT** (false), that merging the base is a NO-OP returning the same message (false — driven on a fixture: the merge is rc 0, the key lands, the rerun is rc 0), and offered `unset_correlation_keys` as the alternative — which **tombstones a key that is LIVE on the base**, the exact action this same message's other branch, [`INSTALL.md`](INSTALL.md) and `release-artifacts/action.yml` all warn against. The remedy is now a function of the two facts the tool already holds: the key's state at `merge-base(base, head)` and at the base TIP. **Four cells, three remedies, no residual** — deleted-here / retired-on-the-base-too / stale-so-merge / never-declared — printed together with the two facts it was derived from, and asking the author nothing. ⚠ **The verdict is unchanged and still HEAD-ONLY:** every cell is rc 2 exactly as before, the fork-point read selects the WORDING only, and nothing read from it can turn a refusal into a pass. A ref whose config cannot be read there is treated as not declaring the key, and the evidence line says `unreadable` rather than `absent`, so an author looking at a config they can plainly see is never sent to the wrong fix. **All four cells are driven END TO END on their own fixture repos, merges included** — the stale cell's head carrying its own committed config edit, which is the input the old proxy got wrong — plus the unreadable-tip fold and one closing arm pinning the whole partition at once. Seen to fail: the battery reds **25 arms** against the pre-round binary, the closing arm reading `none none none none`. **(3) Two of § 3d's six census arms could not fail.** They used the prelude's `has`, a literal substring test, and `_ata_canon_source` CONTAINS `canon_source` — so redacting every standalone mention of the jq def from both censuses still answered `true`. They are word-bounded now, and each of the six carries its own redaction control. **(4) A justification on the review record was false, and is corrected rather than carried:** `_ata_canon_source` was said to sit in a vendored standalone that must not source the shared lib — but `bin/adopt-to-dl` sources it. The conclusion stands and the REAL reason is now stated at the function: it is a deliberately PARTIAL canonicalizer (no trim, no 255-cap) whose omissions are safe only because `kb_is_repo_slug` has already refused every value they would change, so publishing it in the lib under a general name would offer a partial rule to callers with no such gate — and the hoist would not shrink the mirror set anyway, since `promote-released-cards` still cannot reach the lib. **No accept/reject rule changed in this round: `bin/release-artifacts-check` refuses exactly what it refused before, with a different sentence.**
  **⛔ Review round 4 — the guard was widened twice and defeated twice, so its POLARITY is flipped instead.** Round 3 replaced two fold spellings with a 16-row construct table derived per language, and five more legal spellings walked straight through it, each minted into the REAL `bin/promote-released-cards` as a new site with nothing removed, each answering **rc 0, 0 FAIL, 344 ok** — the exact clean baseline: `${1,,}` and `${1@L}` (the table's own bash rows, on a POSITIONAL parameter, which its EREs required to be identifier-shaped — and `${1,,}` is the most idiomatic spelling there is for a canonicalizer helper taking `$1`), `tr "A-Z" "a-z"` and `tr -- 'A-Z' 'a-z'` (its own `tr` row, which hard-coded an optional SINGLE quote), and a `perl -pe '$_=lc'` after a `then`, which the language leg's command-position anchor missed along with six other ordinary invocation shapes. **The structural fault, not the missing cells:** an allow-list of spellings can always be defeated by a spelling nobody listed, and the per-row control that was supposed to prove otherwise minted THE ROW'S OWN SAMPLE — which demonstrates that a sample matches its own regex, never that the regex covers the construct. That is a control that cannot fail for the property it appears to guarantee, i.e. the very defect § 3d exists to catch, sitting inside the catcher. **So the predicate now ADMITS TOO MUCH ON PURPOSE and every admission is declared.** Two disjuncts over one LOGICAL line (backslash continuations joined first — a wrapped `tr '[:upper:]' \` / `'[:lower:]'` pipeline was invisible to every line-scoped predecessor): the line NAMES a lowercase operation (matched against the line lowercased, so `tolower`, `toLowerCase` and `mb_strtolower` are one case of one rule; `lower`/`downcase`/`casefold` are bare substrings on purpose), or it WRITES one whose operation has no name (bash's case-modification operators and `-l` attribute, bounded by bash's own grammar rather than by observation; `\L`/`\l`/`y///`; `conv=lcase`; and an UPPER alphabet and a LOWER alphabet appearing as two separate operands, which replaces three `tr` rows that each pinned a quoting style). (⚠ **ROUND 5 BELOW DEMOTES THIS CLAIM** — the polarity flip landed on the NAMED half only, and the sentence that stood here, *"it can no longer be defeated by an unlisted spelling"*, was measured FALSE; read that paragraph for what § 3d actually promises.) The 16-row table survives only as a REGRESSION BATTERY — 23 rows now, every construct the languages under `bin/` provide plus every spelling that has ever defeated a predecessor, each EXECUTED and required to really fold `AbC`, each minted and required to move the count by exactly one — and because the predicate is written once, independently of them, those arms can now fail. **Six negative controls are what stop the battery being vacuous**, since every `minted ⇒ 3` arm is also satisfied by a predicate matching everything; each pins a real line of this tree that must NOT be admitted (an `LC_ALL=C` pin, an `[A-Za-z0-9]` character class, a `${!prefix@}` expansion, a `-l` flag on another command, a plain assignment, a fold merely named in a comment). **The census over `bin/` is consequently 22 admitted lines across 10 files** — the 3 mirrors, the 9 real folds that are not mirrors, and 10 lines that are not lowercase folds at all, every one of the 22 declared by name and reason in the leg itself. **The LANGUAGE census is DELETED, not fixed:** it existed solely to bound the construct table, the table is gone, and keeping a broken check whose purpose has been removed would be churn that reads as coverage. **And the census walk is now recursive** — every predecessor iterated `bin/*` behind an `[ -f ]`, which skips a directory silently, so a mirror written to `bin/helpers/` was outside the population with nothing saying so; `bin/` is flat today, so the expected block is unchanged, and a control mints exactly that file. **Seen to fail: all five of the spellings that defeated round 3, plus the continuation-split pipeline, each minted into the real bin ⇒ rc 1 with 37 FAIL, each restored byte-exact**; and four mutations of the leg itself, each watched red **for its own named reason** — the walk put back to non-recursive reds the subdirectory control and nothing else; the predicate made to match everything reds the negative controls; the SPELLED disjunct gutted reds the battery rows that disjunct is what catches; the continuation join removed reds the continuation control. ⚠ **The FAIL counts these mutations produce are deliberately not quoted**: the text does not pin the exact mutation, so a count taken from one spelling of "gut the disjunct" is not re-derivable from the tree by anyone else — the property above is what each mutation demonstrates, and it is re-derivable by making the mutation. **A second defect in round 3's own remedy, from the same root cause: two definitions of one predicate.** `_key_state_at` scored a correlation key `present` at a ref on `type != null`, which is COARSER than the same leg's head-side notion of a usable key — so a base tip carrying `"source": "   "` or an uncompilable `card_token_regex` put the author in the STALE cell, whose remedy PROMISES that merging the base turns the check green. Driven on fixtures: the merge is rc 0, the unusable value lands, and the rerun is rc 2 on a different message — the loop that partition exists to end. There is now ONE `_key_usability`, and both readers route through it. Separately, "no config at that ref" and "a config this run cannot PARSE" were one answer and are now two: the first is genuinely `absent` (it is the state of the very PR that adopts the file), while the second establishes nothing — it used to be folded into the retired/never-declared remedy, which printed a retirement the run had not established and routed the author to `unset_correlation_keys`, a TOMBSTONE, two clauses after its own evidence line said the ref was unreadable. Both now carry their own remedy and their own verdict word, and the closing arm pins **seven** cells onto six remedies with no residual. **Seen to fail: 9 arms against the pre-round binary.** **No accept/reject rule changed in this round** — every cell is rc 2 exactly as before, every head-side refusal fires on exactly the same condition with the same words, and the fork-point and base-tip reads still select WORDING only.
  **⛔ Review round 5 — the fifth escape, and the fix is a DEMOTION rather than a sixth widening.** Round 4 flipped the predicate's polarity, and review measured that the flip landed on the **NAMED** half only. That half is genuinely over-broad and was not defeated. **`_FOLD_SPELLED` is still an INCLUSION LIST at its alphabet arms** — the three tokens `A-Z`, `ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZ` and `[:upper:]` — so a `tr` whose alphabets are written some other way is a real, legal, literal case fold the predicate scores **0**. Three were minted into the REAL `bin/promote-released-cards` and each answered **rc 0, 0 FAIL, 376 ok**, the exact clean baseline: `tr '\101-\132' '\141-\172'` (octal — and the LOCALE-SAFE `tr` idiom, so it is what a careful author writes), `tr 'A-MN-Z' 'a-mn-z'` (both operands literal, no `A-Z` substring) and `tr 'A-M' 'a-m' | tr 'N-Z' 'n-z'`. Three more escape at predicate level: `sed 's/A/a/g;…'`, `awk gsub` and a `case`-per-letter mapping. **⛔ NO WIDENING WAS APPLIED, DELIBERATELY.** Three consecutive rounds were each closed by adding the spellings the round before had missed, and each next round found more; a `tr`-invocation disjunct was costed at 9 net declared lines and still refused, because the escape after that one is a spelling nobody has listed either. **An inclusion list cannot be finished, so this one is DECLARED instead of extended.** **§ 3d is therefore demoted, on every surface that carried the claim, from a completeness guarantee to a BEST-EFFORT LOCATOR with a stated residual** — the section heading, the polarity paragraph, the residual-control framing, § 3e's preamble, both bins' census comments and this entry. The six known escapes are named in the leg as the declared residual, and a reader who wants the guarantee is now sent to **§ 3e**, which is where it always actually lived: § 3e extracts the **three named** `canonicalizeSource` copies from the shipped files and **EXECUTES** them over one corpus, so a DIVERGENCE between them reds whatever spelling minted it. ⛔ **That guarantee is bounded too, and the entry says so rather than trading one over-claim for another:** § 3e's population is those three, so an unnamed fifth mirror is outside § 3e exactly as it is outside § 3d's predicate — the two sections do not close each other's gap. **No mechanism moved: the predicate, the census, the battery, the controls and every accept/reject rule are byte-for-byte what round 4 shipped.** This round changes what the leg CLAIMS, not what it does. **Also withdrawn: the four leg-mutation FAIL counts round 4 quoted** — the text does not pin the exact mutation, so the integers are not re-derivable from the tree (two of the four were independently measured to differ), and the property each mutation demonstrates replaces them. **Seen to fail:** the three literal-alphabet `tr` folds re-measured against the shipped predicate score **0** while `tr 'A-Z' 'a-z'` and `tr '[:upper:]' '[:lower:]'` score **1** — the instrument discriminates, and the escape is real.
- **card#8376** — **nothing on a seat could see that it was carrying TWO kanban tokens.** The coord-store rung shipped in v0.31.0 (card#7316) so a seat whose credential store already holds the token stops minting a second plaintext copy for this toolkit — but shipping the rung does not DELETE the copy an operator already made. Both keep working, `agent-board-toolkit-runtime-check` reported healthy, and the shadowed one is a live credential nothing rotates. **`agent-board-toolkit-runtime-check` now judges the seat's credential topology** beside its runtime topology: it resolves every source a token can come from — the ambient `KBCARD_TOKEN_FILE`, `~/.kanban-host.env`, every `~/.kanban-*-board.env`, and the store's `[kanban] api_token_file` pointer — gives every board its EFFECTIVE token file under the documented precedence, and compares the files **by digest — over the content their READERS see, not their raw bytes**: every consumer takes a token file through `$(cat …)`, which strips trailing newlines, so [`INSTALL.md`](INSTALL.md) §3b step (b)'s own `printf '%s'` recipe and the same secret an editor terminated are ONE credential, and a raw-byte digest answers `each a different credential` at rc 0 over exactly the seat this leg exists to detect (measured). **The population is those declared sources PLUS the literal glob `~/.kanban-*-token`, and the second half is what makes the check true of the state [`INSTALL.md`](INSTALL.md) §3b promises it catches:** migrating onto the store's pointer means DROPPING the `KBCARD_TOKEN_FILE` line that named the old copy, so the file left behind is declared by nothing, and a declared-only population answers `ok — one kanban token file resolves` over exactly that seat (measured). An undeclared copy is reported in the same two arms as a declared one, with `rm` as its whole remedy — there is no config line to drop or repoint. The glob is stated **verbatim** on every surface that describes the leg, because a check's edge is not guessable from its output: a `~/.kanban-dev-token.bak` is outside it and is not reported, and a control pins that. **The same credential in two files ⇒ `✗` and rc 1**, naming the file to delete, the file to keep (the store's pointer wins, since the store already manages it) and the declaration to drop, pointing at [`INSTALL.md`](INSTALL.md) §3b. **A readable token file no board resolves, holding a different token ⇒ `⚠` and rc 0** — a second live credential, reported and not gated, because its innocent twin is a supported install: a store holding the framework's own kanban token while every board declares its own. **Several boards each with their own token, all in use, is reported as `ok` and never as a finding** — per-board isolation is the topology card#7245 exists to produce, and a check that red on it would tell every correct install to break itself. **Three identities and one bound, each of which was a wrong answer before it was fixed:** a trailing newline is not part of the credential, because no reader can see one — the reference seat's own `~/.kanban-dev-token` is newline-terminated while §3b step (b) writes none, so one seat carrying one credential twice read as two different tokens (measured; the digest now runs over the file with trailing newlines stripped, and a control pins that an INTERIOR newline still separates two credentials); two names for ONE FILE are one credential — `readlink -f` settles a symlink but gives each name of a HARD LINK its own canonical path, so a hard-linked pair drew a keep-one-DELETE-the-other verdict over a single inode (measured; grouping now compares device+inode with `-ef`); the mirror that reads a board env's declaration now sources the HOST env first, as `kb_resolve_env` does, so a board env spelling `KBCARD_TOKEN_FILE="$KB_TOKENS/dev"` against a host-set variable resolves to the file the tools resolve rather than to `/dev` — it failed SILENTLY, dropping the source as unreadable and reporting one token file where two held the same credential (measured); and every message that speaks about "any board on this seat" now carries the bound that IS its predicate — `~/.kanban-*-board.env`, with a board env reached only through `$KBCARD_BOARD_ENV` named as outside what the check consults, **the GATING `✗` included**: it is the arm that tells an operator to DELETE a file, so it says "NO source THIS CHECK WALKS declares it", names that bound in the same words the `⚠` uses, and makes its "`rm` is the whole remedy" sentence CONDITIONAL on it — a `$KBCARD_BOARD_ENV`-only board declaring the copy would otherwise be told to delete a file it resolves, and would then refuse at `kb_resolve_env` rc 5 on this check's own advice (measured). **The `⚠` also offers a third reading:** if the second file belongs to the framework's own tooling while this seat's boards deliberately declare their own, that is the supported shadowed-pointer install §3b sanctions and nothing needs doing — the two remedies it offered before BOTH changed a topology that document blesses, and the first deleted a declaration and a token file this toolkit does not own. ⛔ **It reports SOURCES, never VALUES** — token bytes travel one route, the file into a POSIX-awk normaliser's stdin through a redirect and down a pipe into `sha256sum`'s stdin: not argv (`ps` is world-readable), not a temp file, not a variable a message can interpolate, and the digests are compared rather than printed. `tests/token-duplication-selftest.sh` drives both verdicts and both controls, plants a known token value and searches the tool's whole stdout+stderr for it AND for its sha256 — each search preceded by a positive control proving it finds that string when present. ⛔ **The sha256 needle is ADOPTED from the tool, not re-spelled:** identity here is the content the readers see, so a `printf … | sha256sum` needle in the test was a second definition of that rule, it stayed on RAW BYTES when the bin's was corrected, and both absence rows then searched for a string the tool cannot emit on any input — measured, a mutant interpolating the compared digest into the `✗` message leaked it with the suite at rc 0 and 0 FAIL. The test now `sed`-extracts `_rc_digest` out of the bin and derives the needle through it, the same extract-or-exit-1 the mirror block uses; the mutant reds with 2 FAILs. That extract-or-exit-1 (`_adopt_fn`) now lives in `tests/_selftest-prelude.sh` rather than in this one file, where it was one more hand-spelling of a `sed` range the suite already carried several of. **What `prelude-shadow-selftest.sh` does and does not catch is stated in that file's own header and is deliberately not restated here** — an earlier cut of this sentence said it *"reds on the seventh copy"*, which was false of the guard as shipped (card#8548, entry above). **A DECLARED `~/…` is LITERAL, here as everywhere else:** `_kb_expand_home` has one call site in the lib — on a value out of the credential STORE — while `kb_declared_token_file` returns a declared candidate verbatim, so a board env spelling `KBCARD_TOKEN_FILE="~/tok"` is a literal `~` directory to every tool (`kb_resolve_env` rc 5). This check expanded it at two further sites and could therefore credit a board with declaring a copy, and elect a path the tools cannot read as a duplicate group's survivor; the two expansions are gone, the shape is driven through the RESOLVER, and the pin is the CALL GRAPH — where the check expands, derived behind a positive control — because a parity block over mirrored FUNCTIONS cannot see a divergence in which sites CALL one, and neither can a scenario row: driving the resolver reds for ONE of the two dropped sites and left the other, on the arm that says DELETE, at rc 0 / 0 FAIL (measured; the call-graph pin reds for either, at either end). ⚠ **card#8548 narrowed that pin's population** — it compared the check against the WHOLE lib and so red on an unrelated lib call with no mirror drift; the entry above carries the correction. runtime-check must not source `_kb-board-lib.sh` (it judges it), so the store rung and its helpers are **mirrored** into it; that selftest extracts the mirrors and drives them row-by-row against the lib's originals over a shared store/expansion/pasted-secret case table, and **exits 1 if it cannot extract one** — guarded duplication on the terms `docs/CONSOLIDATION-PLAN.md` § Stage D settled for `host_ok`. Measured, this file against three earlier bins — re-measured after the needle was adopted from the bin, because that moved the first extraction guard ahead of every check: against the **pre-change bin** (the leg absent) it now **exits 1 having run no check at all**, naming `_rc_digest` as the function it could not extract, which is the guard reporting an absent subject rather than a count; **29** of its checks fail against a bin carrying the leg WITHOUT the undeclared population, that run aborting at the population-glob extraction guard before the mirror block; and **15** fail against a bin carrying all of that but digesting RAW BYTES and leaving the `✗` arm's "nothing declares it" unbounded. `⚠` arms ride stderr and are NOT silenced by `--quiet` (which is how SessionStart runs it); that was true and untested — a `warn()` mutated to honour `QUIET` left the whole suite green — and each of the four `⚠` arms is now driven under `--quiet` and was watched red under that mutant.

- **card#8421** — **⚠ BREAKING CONFIG CHANGE: `.promote.source` is now REQUIRED in `.release-pr.json`, with no default. Every existing install fails `promote-released-cards` at rc 2 until it declares one; single-repo boards want `"source": "*"`.** `dl_number` and `pr_number` are **repo-scoped** counters — repo A's PR #15 and repo B's PR #15 are different pull requests wearing the same number — and `promote-released-cards` correlated on the **bare** number while writing cards into a **terminal** stage. On a board tracking one repo that is harmless; on a board tracking several it promotes **another repo's cards**. Measured by a reporting peer on a three-repo board: one release's **15 shipped PRs matched 24 cards across all three lanes**, including cards already sitting at a released stage from a different repo's own version line. Not reachable on this repo's own boards — every one of them is a single repo — so this was a defect shipped to consumers rather than suffered here.
  **⚠ THE THREE ACCEPT/REJECT NARROWINGS THIS PR CARRIES — the complete list.** Each one refuses, STRICTLY EARLIER, an input that could not have succeeded later, and none of them is reachable by a GitHub-legal `<owner>/<name>`. The accept set this PR settles on is ASCII letters, digits, `.`, `_` and `-` either side of the one `/`: the published read-side contract (`DL-SOURCE-CORRELATION.md` § 2) states that a GitHub `<owner>/<repo>` is **ASCII by construction**, and the characters these narrowings refuse (`+ : ~ @ % , = ! ' " $`, a backtick, `*`, whitespace, a `/` beyond the first) appear in no owner login or repository name GitHub issues.
  1. **`.promote.source` becomes REQUIRED in `bin/promote-released-cards`.** PREVIOUSLY: an absent key was a permissive default — the run correlated on a bare `dl_number`/`pr_number` and promoted every ref-matched card, whatever repo it belonged to. NOW: absent ⇒ rc 2 before any board read, with a refusal naming the key, the file, the `promote` block and the `"*"` single-repo answer. WHY NOTHING GITHUB-LEGAL REGRESSES: this narrows a CONFIG surface, not a repo name — `"*"` is an explicit single-repo DECLARATION that restores the previous behaviour byte for byte, summary line included, and it is what every single-repo board declares. PINNED BY: `tests/promote-source-qualify-selftest.sh` §§ 1, 1b and 2 — the refusal is asserted against the GET log, so "before any read" is measured rather than inferred — plus § 6's mutant that gives the key a permissive default.
  2. **`run-coverage-check --repo owner/name.git` is refused at rc 2.** PREVIOUSLY: accepted by a bare shape regex, interpolated into a `repos/<slug>/…` request path, and answered **404** by GitHub. NOW: refused at the flag, before any request is made. WHY NOTHING GITHUB-LEGAL REGRESSES: `.git` is a git-remote suffix and is never part of a repository name in a REST path, so every value this refuses already failed — one layer later, and less legibly. PINNED BY: `tests/run-coverage-check-selftest.sh` (`--repo with a .git suffix (narrowed)`), over the predicate's own arms in `tests/kb-board-lib-selftest.sh`.
  3. **`adopt-to-dl --repo` is judged by `kb_is_repo_slug` — charset AND shape — instead of by a shape-only regex.** PREVIOUSLY: `^[^[:space:]/]+/[^[:space:]/]+$` accepted anything carrying one `/` and no whitespace; measured, that is **16 further spellings** — `git@github.com:acme/widget`, `acme/*`, `acme/widget.git`, a non-ASCII name, and any name carrying `+ : ~ @ % , = ! ' " $` or a backtick. NOW: every one of them is refused at rc 2 before anything is stamped. WHY NOTHING GITHUB-LEGAL REGRESSES: not one of the 16 is a legal `<owner>/<name>` — and accepting them was WORSE than refusing, because this value becomes BOTH the stamped placeholder `pr_url` AND the `source=` of the by-ref verify, so the scp-style and glob forms had the verify compare the same garbage to itself and pass vacuously, while `.git` failed loud only AFTER the DL counter was consumed and the card stamped. PINNED BY: `tests/kb-board-lib-selftest.sh` (the predicate's own arms, including the now case-insensitive `.git` suffix), `tests/adopt-to-dl-selftest.sh` (an end-to-end wiring arm, watched red by replacing the call with `true`) and § 3c of `tests/promote-source-qualify-selftest.sh` (the accept set is EQUAL, row for row, to the one `bin/promote-released-cards` applies to `.promote.source`).
  **`.promote.source`, and why it has no default.** `"<owner>/<repo>"` qualifies every **ref**-matched card against the card's own by-ref source; `"*"` is an explicit **declaration** that the board tracks exactly ONE repo, and is byte-identical to the previous behaviour, summary line included. There is no safe default for the same reason `KANBAN_EXPECTED_HOST` has none — a vendored tool cannot assume the operator's topology, and the unsafe assumption here is a **write into a terminal stage** — so the tool refuses **before any board read**, and the refusal names the key, the file, the `promote` block, and the fact that `"*"` is the single-repo answer. A hard failure whose message does not say how to satisfy it is a worse defect than the one it closes.
  **What the qualified run does with the cards it will not move.** A ref-matched card belonging to a different repo, and a ref-matched card with **no derivable source**, are each **named individually on stderr and counted in the summary** — never silently promoted and never silently dropped. Both are WARN-only and neither touches the exit code: they are a pre-existing board condition, not a failure of this run, and a red clearable only by editing board data is a red that gets ignored. The unsourced list also goes to `$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY`, because a stderr line in a green job is invisible in a collapsed log. Refusing the unsourced card **inverts** the reconciler's "a null source matches every repo" rule on purpose: that rule governs a DEFER, whose failure mode is to do nothing, whereas here "matches every repo" would mean every repo's release promotes that one unstamped card.
  **`--cards` is exempt, and says so.** A card *id* is not a repo-scoped counter — it names exactly one card on exactly one board — so an explicitly named id has no collision for the guard to break, and refusing it would refuse an unambiguous instruction (and make `release-pr-body`'s coverage line for any other-repo card read "no card"). An id-matched card whose source is not this repo IS promoted, and the exemption is printed on stderr rather than applied silently.
  **Also:** a `--source <owner/repo>|*` override (and a matching optional `source` input on `promote/action.yml`), which beats the config for one run; validation refusing a URL, an scp-style `git@host:owner/repo`, a `.git` suffix, a glob like `owner/*`, a bare name, extra path segments and embedded whitespace — each a spelling that would look configured while correlating to nothing; value canonicalisation (trim, cap 255, ASCII-lowercase) matching the server's, so `ACME/Widget` and `acme/widget` are one board arity; and a **repo-qualification ON/OFF line on stderr every run**, the sibling of the stage-guard state line, so a `"*"` declaration made once in a config file is auditable in the log of the run that acted on it.
  **New `tests/promote-source-qualify-selftest.sh`**, including a **mutation battery**: the qualification switched off wholesale, the foreign arm dropped, the unsourced arm dropped, and the key made optional-with-a-permissive-default are each applied to a copy of the bin and asserted to flip the observable — because a guard nothing can red is a decoration. Measured against the pre-change binary the file reds **84 arms**. The `curl` stub that drives this tool end-to-end moved to `tests/_promote-curl-stub.sh` at its second real caller; `promote-ref-canon-selftest.sh` keeps its own url-only copy for now (its `moved()` asserts whole-line equality against the logged url), recorded in `docs/CONSOLIDATION-PLAN.md` rather than folded in here.
  **⛔ Review round — the qualified run told release PR authors to duplicate a card that already existed.** `MATCHED_DLS` was computed from the PLAN (matches only), so under qualification the DL of an **unsourced** card — a card that EXISTS and carries the ref, and needs only a stamp — fell into `$MISSING` and was printed by the one `matched NO card` WARNING line. `release-pr-body`'s `## Card coverage` section greps exactly that line, so a body rendered "⚠ Shipped refs with no tracking card: DL-77 … Create (or correct) a board card" for a card sitting on the board. Measured end to end on the two real bins over a stubbed board, then held still by new arms in both selftests. **The no-card report is now exactly TWO lines with opposite remedies**, both read by `card_coverage_section`: `matched NO card` (create or correct a card — **`foreign` refs stay here on purpose**, since "no card OF THIS REPO carries it" is exactly true of them and the `⊘` line names the other repo's card that did) and a new `matched ONLY an unsourced card` (**do not create a card — stamp the one that is there**, `kbcard patch --pr-url …`). The contract, including the rule that a THIRD kind may not be added without teaching the reader, is stated in the tool at the site that computes it; the release body renders each as its own bullet. Under `"*"` nothing can classify as unsourced, so the second line is absent and the first is byte-identical to the report this tool has always printed. **Also from that round:** `foreign` now reaches `$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY` alongside `unsourced` (the stated reason — a stderr line in a GREEN job is invisible in a collapsed log — never distinguished the two, and the `foreign` lines are the only evidence the guard fired at all); every `--source`/`.promote.source` refusal now echoes the **operator's own spelling**, with the canonical form only when canonicalization changed it, because a message naming only `replace_me` cannot be grepped for by whoever wrote `REPLACE_ME`; and the jq `repo_from_gh_url` mirror now **type-guards like the server's `is_string`** instead of `tostring`-ing first — an object or array payload value whose JSON *text* contained a GitHub URL derived a source here and `null` on the server, and that disagreement ran toward **promoting**. Two comments that were **false** are corrected rather than re-synced: the "both kinds go into ONE list" invariant (now two lines, stated as such), and `canon_source`'s claim that "BOTH sides of the ONE comparison run through THIS def" — the config side is canonicalized in **shell**, and what actually binds the two sides is that `src_charset_ok` plus the shape `case` admit only an ASCII set over which `src_canon` and the jq def are identical; the remaining PHP divergences are now listed by direction, with `\f` named as the promoting one.
  **`bin/adopt-to-dl`: the same class, one bin over — a validator that could not fail.** `_ata_validate_repo` was shape-only (`^[^[:space:]/]+/[^[:space:]/]+$`) and **ACCEPTS**, measured, `git@github.com:acme/widget`, `acme/widget.git` and `acme/*`. The value it gates is spent TWICE, on both sides of one comparison — it becomes the `<owner>/<name>` of the placeholder `pr_url` the card is **stamped** with, and the `source=` of the step-5 by-ref **verify** — so for the first and third the verify derives the same garbage on both sides and passes **vacuously**: "adopted ⇒ correlatable" was certified by a check that could not fail (canon #9), while the bridge writeback and the reconcile correlate that card to nothing. The predicate is now the **charset+shape pair** `promote-released-cards` applies to `.promote.source` — the same value at the other end of the same correlation — under `LC_ALL=C`, and the refusal names the scp-style spelling as the usual cause. ⚠ **This narrows what `--repo` accepts:** a spelling that is not a bare `<owner>/<name>` is refused at rc 2 **before** anything is stamped, where two of the three previously produced a green adoption and the third (`.git`) failed loud only *after* the write. `promote-released-cards` remains a vendored standalone that must not source `_kb-board-lib.sh`, so one shared implementation is not available and the copy stands — every mirror of `canonicalizeSource` (this one, the shell copy, the jq copy) now cross-references the others by name.
  **⛔ Review round 2 — two guards cited a test that did not reach them, and four arms could not tell "declined" from "crashed".** **(1) The repo-slug charset guards were UNWATCHED.** `src_charset_ok` in `promote-released-cards` and the `--repo` charset arm in `adopt-to-dl` each carried a `local LC_ALL=C` whose only justification was a citation of "the class `tests/locale-range-guard-selftest.sh` exists for" — and that file reached neither: its cases stopped at `uint_ok`/`uint_csv_ok`/`dl-a1 --sentinel`/`adopt-to-dl <card-id>`/`--issue`, and its static backstop matched only the `=~` spelling while its own header claimed the glob `case "$x" in *[!0-9]*` form as well. Measured: with `local LC_ALL=C` removed, `--source acme/widg<U+0663>t` is **ACCEPTED** under `en_US.utf8` at both sites while every other arm in the file stays green — a citation true of the class and false of the site, which is worse than no citation because the next author reads it and believes the line is watched. **Both sites now have behaviour arms** (both locales, each watched red against the unpinned bin), **and the static backstop matches the GLOB form too**: lines are quote-stripped first (a bracket range inside a quoted `grep`/`sed`/`awk`/`jq` argument belongs to an engine that does not widen — without the strip the scan yields 40+ false positives), and case-arm context is tracked through `case … in` / `esac` so an arm written on its own line is reached. Its positive control gains a re-minted glob one-liner and a re-minted bare case ARM, plus benign controls for a window pin, a FILE pin, a quoted range and a range after `esac`. ⚠ **One fixture rule is now stated in the file, because it is not guessable:** `en_US.utf8` widens the GLOB class `[!a-zA-Z0-9._/-]` to admit U+0663 and U+2163 but **NOT** U+00E9, while the REGEX form admits all three — an arm written with `é` here stays green with the pin removed and reads exactly like coverage. **The widened backstop immediately found a sibling** (canon #7): `bin/next-dl`'s `case "$http" in 2[0-9][0-9])` carried no pin at all. It is not reachable — `$http` is curl's own `%{http_code}` — and it is pinned anyway, because what makes a range legitimate is that it is C-collated, not that today's writer can vouch for today's input. `bin/release-tag-check`'s `require_uint` was reported by the first draft of the widening and is **NOT** a defect: that file carries a top-level `export LC_ALL=C`, which the scanner now honours as a file-level exemption with its own control. **(2) Four arms in `promote-source-qualify-selftest.sh` asserted only `moved 7 == false`.** "Did not move" is also what happens when the bin **dies**: mutating the `is_string` type guard to `if false then null` makes jq throw mid-correlation, promote exits **5** with an empty patch log, and all four non-string-derivation arms went on **passing** while the tool they certify had crashed. `src_case`/`ext_case`/`ext_json_case` now report `<rc>/<moved>`, so every row in that section carries a **presence witness**; all four were watched red at `5/false` against the mutant and green at `0/false` restored.
  **Also from that round — one predicate, three spellings, now two callers and one documented standalone.** `adopt-to-dl`'s `_ata_validate_repo` (three hand-rolled `case` steps) and `run-coverage-check`'s bare `kb_ere_match "$REPO" '^[A-Za-z0-9._-]+/[A-Za-z0-9._-]+$'` were **two copies of one accept-set that did not agree** — `run-coverage-check --repo` accepted `owner/name.git`, which the other two refuse for a stated reason. Both now call **`kb_is_repo_slug`** in `bin/_kb-board-lib.sh`; `promote-released-cards` keeps its copy because it is vendored standalone and must not source the lib, and the two are bound by cross-reference plus behaviour arms driving **both ends** of the same correlation. ⚠ **This NARROWS `run-coverage-check --repo`:** `owner/name.git` is refused at rc 2 instead of being interpolated into a `repos/<slug>/…` path GitHub answers 404 for, and the narrowing carries its own arm. The predicate's arms moved with it to `tests/kb-board-lib-selftest.sh`; `adopt-to-dl-selftest.sh` keeps a new **end-to-end WIRING arm**, because a lib unit test cannot tell whether its caller still calls it (watched red by replacing the call with `true`). Full entry in `docs/CONSOLIDATION-PLAN.md` § Post-program dispositions. **Three false or stale comments corrected rather than re-synced:** the jq mirror's "OWNER OF THE RULE" pointed at `DL-SOURCE-CORRELATION.md` **in the kanban repo**, where it does not exist — it is published by the coord plugin, and the entry now names that path (the only DECLARE surface a vendored consumer can reach) **and** `app/Services/ExternalReferenceNormalizer.php` as the code SoT; the `NO APOSTROPHE ANYWHERE IN THIS jq PROGRAM` warning stated its bounds as "the `PLAN=` line", but the variable is now `CORREL=` and `PLAN=` is a different one-line program further down, so the warning sent the next author to the wrong bounds; and the `matched ONLY an unsourced card` line said **ONLY** of a ref that had also matched a foreign card. That line **names the precedence** instead of being reworded, deliberately: `release-pr-body` greps the phrase, resolves this tool through `command -v`, and the two are separately vendorable (one ships as a SHA-pinned composite action), so a reworded anchor is a silently half-read report across any version skew.
  **⛔ Review round 3 — one accept set, two verdicts, and the equality is now a test rather than a comment.** `kb_is_repo_slug`'s `.git` reject arm was written `case … in *.git)` — case-SENSITIVE — so `acme/widget.GIT` and `acme/widget.Git` were **ACCEPTED**, while the declared duplicate in `promote-released-cards` lowercases before its own `case` and refused them: one accept set, two verdicts, at the two ends of one correlation. Measured end-to-end, `adopt-to-dl <card> --repo acme/widget.GIT` passes the gate and reaches the mint/stamp path; the server's `repoFromGitHubUrl` is `/i` and trims `.GIT` while `canonicalizeSource` does not, so the step-5 verify queries `acme%2Fwidget.git`, misses, and reports VERIFY FAILED **after** the counter is consumed and the card stamped — the exact half-applied write that arm's own comment says it prevents. The arm is now `*.[Gg][Ii][Tt])`, a bracket LIST of explicit ASCII members rather than a bracket RANGE, so it consults no collation order and needs no `LC_ALL=C` window of its own; both spellings were watched red first, at `expected '1' got '0'`. **The drift was found by a shared corpus, and that corpus is now the check:** § 3c of `tests/promote-source-qualify-selftest.sh` drives 26 rows through BOTH copies — the predicate directly, which is also its DEPLOYED verdict because `adopt-to-dl` and `run-coverage-check` hand it the raw flag value with no canonicalization of their own, and `promote-released-cards` end-to-end — pinning each side's verdict row by row, with the only two spellings on which they may legitimately differ (`*`, and a value promote trims before judging) declared by name and asserted in both directions. Both legs were watched red: the pre-fix arm reds the two lib rows, and deleting the bin's own `.git` arm reds three promote rows. Sibling audit (canon #7): the only other literal-suffix `case` arms under `bin/` are `promote-released-cards`' own (which runs after the `tr` lowercase) and `run-coverage-check`'s `*.yml|*.yaml` over a GitHub workflow LISTING rather than operator input — one instance, no consolidation item. ⚠ **This reverses a DROP recorded in round 2** ("no test asserts the two accept sets are EQUAL — a miss would surface as a promote/adopt disagreement, not silent corruption"): the miss existed, and it was the half-applied write above.
  **Considered and rejected, recorded rather than assumed:** letting the server answer the qualification via `GET /boards/{b}/tasks/by-ref.json?system=dl&ref=N&source=<repo>` instead of a fourth runtime. Read live, its filter is `->where('source', $source)` — a strict equality — so a foreign card and an unsourced card are both simply **absent** from the answer, indistinguishable from "no card carries this ref": those two rows ARE this tool's report, so the endpoint that answers the qualification cannot produce the report the qualification exists to make. Plus `external_references` is `whenLoaded`-gated and `tasks/search.json` eager-loads only `lastStageMoveChangelog`, so the paged read this tool already makes carries no `source` (adopting by-ref means one request per `(system, ref)` rather than one scan — and that scan is where `workflow_stage_id` comes from), and the `--cards` leg correlates on a card **id**, which is not a by-ref system at all. Full entry, with the conditions that would reopen it, in `docs/CONSOLIDATION-PLAN.md`.

- **card#8545** — **`kbcard` could CREATE a card link and never remove one, so a mislinked relation was PERMANENT with the shipped toolkit — and the new verb reports a removal only when it has READ the link's absence back, because the API's `204` is the server's claim and not the removal.** The case is not cosmetic: a `blocks` link later ruled to owe no work is a blocker the blocked work cannot discharge, so every gate count derived from links stays wrong by one, forever, and the only remedy the toolkit offered was prose alongside the link contradicting it — the restatement-that-drifts shape this repo files defects against elsewhere. **New: `kbcard unlink --link-id N --on ID-OR-EXT`**, with `--from`/`--to`/`--relation` as a convenience form that resolves the id off the from-card first. **The contract is a READ contract, not a status one.** Nothing is deleted that was not first read: an id absent from the card you named is **rc 2 with no DELETE at all**, and after the DELETE **both ends are re-read** — a cross-board link projects onto both boards, so one end is half a measurement — with success reported **only** when the link is absent from each. A success status with the link still present is a hard failure that prints the card's own `linked_tasks` entry as evidence; an end whose list comes back `linked_tasks_truncated` makes the absence **UNMEASURED**, reported as a failure and never as a success; and each remaining way it can fail is **named rather than collapsed** — 401/403 as the board policy refusal, 404 as no such link, and a request that never completed as exactly that, claiming no outcome in either direction. **Why `--link-id` takes a card:** the API deletes by link id only and offers no per-link GET, so a card is the only surface a link can be read back on. Either end will do — the entry carries `direction`, so the reported from/to comes from the card's own declaration rather than from which flag the caller used. ⛔ **One shape fact the fixture is built on, because getting it wrong would 204 and read back clean:** a `linked_tasks` entry's `id` is the **other task**; the link id is `task_link_id`, so reading `id` as the link id DELETEs a link belonging to a different pair of cards. `linked_tasks_count` is deliberately **not** used as a second truncation oracle — a second source that can disagree with the server's own flag would manufacture UNMEASURED verdicts of its own. **Selftest.** `tests/kbcard-selftest.sh` gains the verb's own section, every leg paired with the stub's request log so a refusal that says the right words while still issuing the DELETE cannot pass. **Seen to fail:** a mutant `kbcard` with the post-witness re-read loop deleted (success straight off the `2xx`) reds a block of checks sitting ENTIRELY inside the `unlink` section, every one of them a post-witness leg. No figure is quoted here on purpose — it moves with the file; apply the mutation and read the count off the run. ⚠ A TRANSCRIPT DIFF IS NOT THAT COUNT: it moves two lines per flipped check (`ok` out, `FAIL` in) plus the two the summary line itself becomes, so a moved-line total is `2N + 2` and reading it as `N` or as `2N` both misreport the confinement. ⛔ **The condition this verb ships under, because a changelog is where a consumer looks for it:** the READ side of the `linked_tasks` shape above — the per-entry keys, `id` being the other task while `task_link_id` is the link, `direction`'s two values, the truncation flag's type, and the cross-board case — was confirmed against live boards read-only, with no link created; **the DELETE side was not**. **No live `unlink` has ever run**, so that `DELETE /task_links/<id>.json` answers 204 and that the row actually goes are still taken on the originating seat's word, and the tests measure the tool against a fixture on that point. It ships rather than waits because a wrong shape there is still fail-closed (nothing matches ⇒ rc 2 with no DELETE) or loud (a swapped `id`/`task_link_id` finds the link still present on the re-read and reports HARD FAILURE at rc 1, so it costs one wrong DELETE on first use and says so). **This bullet is a pointer, not the record:** `bin/kbcard`'s `unlink` header is the one surface that states what is measured and what is still owed, and card#8545 owns the measurement, its denominator and the run — re-derive from there, never from this bullet. **Also:** the success object projects `relation_type` off the card's own entry, so an entry carrying no such key reports JSON `null` instead of the string `"null"` a consumer could round-trip back into `link --relation`. **Adopted** from a patch handed over by another fleet seat (roundtable #400); the mutant control and the two self-found-defect legs were re-run here rather than inherited. No other verb changed.

### Fixed
- **card#8442** — **`tests/changelog-card-entry-selftest.sh` told a pre-fold branch its entry was missing when the merge had moved it, and an author who followed that advice shipped a duplicate.** `_region` reads `## [Unreleased]` down to the newest tag's header and that scope is correct — but a branch cut BEFORE a release fold has its `[Unreleased]` entry carried UNDER the folded `## [X.Y.Z]` heading by the 3-way merge, **cleanly, at rc 0**: step 4 of `VERSIONING.md` is textually a pure insertion at the TOP of the old section's body, the branch's bullet is an insertion further down that body, and git applies both. (Measured both ways when the fixture was written: an entry filed at the very TOP of the section CONFLICTS instead, which is a louder and different failure.) The entry then stands outside the region, `_missing` names the card, and the only thing the gate said was "missing" — so the fix was to file a second entry, which is the `_stale_dupes` shape this same file exists to catch, minted by its own remedy. **The verdict does not move.** `_missing_remedy` chooses which SENTENCE prints beside a red that is already decided; there is no arm in which a diagnosis makes the gate pass, and an unestablished one prints exactly the text this gate printed before. **The fold is ASSERTED, not inferred from the label** — `_added_section_label` locating a branch line inside a released section is ALSO true of a stale-fix branch correcting a shipped line and of a release PR filing under the section it is cutting, so `_prefold_heading` adds two history legs: the heading must EXIST on `$BASE` and be ABSENT at `git merge-base $BASE $HEAD`. Both reads go through `_has_version_header`, the section parser the live preconditions already use — a second `grep '## \['` would be two carvings of one boundary free to disagree. **The remedy is PER CARD.** `_missing` routinely names several cards and a fold is a property of ONE card's bullet, so the needles are narrowed to the line-initial `- **<card>**` bullets the branch added for the card being asked about, and each missing card gets the arm that is true of it — a fold sentence naming its heading, or the ordinary "add a bullet".
