A verdict-wide sentence would tell the author of a genuinely undocumented card "do NOT file a second entry", which is the exact wrong instruction and leaves that card undocumented; the shared paragraph now prints only when some card really was folded and points at those lines by name. ⛔ **Two disclosed residuals, both erring toward saying nothing:** a pre-fold branch that has ALREADY merged the folded base makes that base its own merge-base, so leg 3 goes false and the generic text prints; and the refs come from the pull_request MERGE COMMIT, so a local run and the `push` runs on main/dev establish no diagnosis at all. Six fixture shapes, four of them real repos that fork, fold, file and let GIT do the merge — one positive and three negatives, because the label alone is true in all four and the fold sentence in exactly one. Same class as bridge card#8339 / DL-329, whose corrected predicate this carries; that repo spells leg 3 at the branch's fork point instead, and the difference is recorded at `_prefold_heading`. ⚑ **The `_pr_merge_refs` fixture pair drives each arm against the OPPOSITE exported ambient**, because asserting "with `GITHUB_BASE_REF` unset" by simply not setting it is an assertion about the developer's SHELL: a runner exports that variable to every step of a `pull_request` job, so the cell's stated condition never held there and it passed locally while reddening on the real event. `VERSIONING.md` § The `[Unreleased]` entry rule gains a POINTER at that predicate — three sentences of author-facing advice and no restatement of the legs, because a narrower second copy of a predicate is the thing that drifts.
- **card#8485** — **`release-tag-check-selftest.sh` sequenced two of its arms on a wall clock that could not see the thing it was racing, so the suite reported the BOX as a defect in the tool.** Measured over 30 runs under a scratch `HOME` on a loaded host: **21 red (70%)**, never the same set of cells, all of them in two arms — `…and it reports it WAITED` (19/30) and the pair `…and states the absence is MEASURED` / `…while still counting the polls that failed` (16/30 each). **The pair's counts are IDENTICAL on every run, and that correlation is the diagnosis**: both clauses hang off `UNREADABLE`, so both go quiet together exactly when it is zero. Both arms forked a `( sleep N; <event> ) &` immediately before the run and needed the event to land *after* the tool's first poll. But the timer starts at the FORK, while the tool does not poll until it has finished an **unbounded startup** — `git rev-parse`, then the `release-artifacts-check` and `release-pr-body` delegations, each spawning git of its own. This file's own elapsed clocks, read off the 21 red runs, put that startup at **2–9s against timers of 2s and 3s**: the tag was already pushed and the remote already renamed into place before poll 1 ever looked, so the arms' preconditions — a tag that arrives *while* polling, a read that fails *before* the remote answers — were never established. ⚑ **`bin/release-tag-check` is NOT at fault and is unchanged.** Its measured/unmeasured classification is a pure function of the polls' exit statuses and was CORRECT on every red run: an all-answered absence carries no failed-poll caveat, which is what this file's own control asserts, and a tag present on the first look is not a tag that was waited for. The defect is entirely the fixture's, and a fixture whose precondition is "the tool starts in under 3 seconds" measures the host. **Fixed by sequencing both events on the tool's OWN READS**, via one `ext::` remote helper, `_counting_remote`, that tallies each read it is asked for — an idiom this file already used for the hung-poll arm, and which the unreadable-follow-up arm had hand-rolled a third copy of; that copy is migrated onto the same helper, so the file's three read-sequenced arms and their controls now share one owner. The late-tag arm creates the ref immediately before serving its **second** read; the mixed-poll arm refuses its **first** read and serves every one after it. Nothing is stubbed and no timeout moved: the arrival is a real ref found by a real `ls-remote`, and the failure is a real non-zero read with git's own stderr reaching the tool. "The event beat the tool to the first poll" is now **unrepresentable** rather than merely unlikely. Three assertions gained: the late-tag arm now checks **its own precondition** (the tool really did read more than once) instead of assuming it; the mixed arm pins **how many** polls failed (`1 of N` — every other cell in that arm is satisfied by any non-zero count, so a fixture that drifted to refusing three of five reads would leave them all green while no longer testing the one-lost-read case); and every control now drives the **same helper** as the case it controls, differing in the one setting under test — the mixed arm's control additionally witnesses that its reads *answered*, since `rc 1` alone is shared with the unreadable verdict and would pass vacuously. Re-measured after the fix: **36 runs, 0 red** — 30 serial, plus 6 alternating with the pre-fix file under a fixed synthetic load, where the pre-fix file still went red and the fixed one did not. ⚑ **The rate tracks host load, and that is the mechanism restating itself**: the pre-fix baseline's 70% was taken at load ~26, and interleaved pre-fix controls on a quiet host (load ~6) pass — a fixture that only reds when the box is busy is measuring the box.
- **card#8533** — **card#8485's class in its second and third spellings: three assertions started their clock in the TEST, so the tool's own startup sat inside the interval the bound was judging.** `tests/release-tag-check-selftest.sh` bounded a test-side `SECONDS` at **30** over a `--timeout 60 --interval 5` run ("does not wait out the timeout") and at **10** over the hung-read arm; `tests/board-session-close-selftest.sh` bounded one at **15** over a leg whose budget is 1s. None of those intervals is the thing under test: `release-tag-check` spends the startup the **card#8485** entry in this same section measures — `git rev-parse` plus its `release-artifacts-check`/`release-pr-body` delegations — *before* its first poll, and `board-session-close` runs its whole snapshot/branch/PR/drift report before the leg being timed — so a slow box reds the cell for a defect the tool does not have. **Two of the three are re-expressed by READ COUNT rather than by seconds.** "Refuses without waiting" is exactly "it looked ONCE", which `_counting_remote` — the fixture primitive card#8485 introduced, now the one owner of every `ext::` remote in the file — states rather than approximates; waiting out that arm's own timeout would have been roughly `--timeout ÷ --interval` polls rather than one — the exact count is host-decided, since the tool truncates its own `WAITED` to whole seconds, which is why no figure is asserted on any surface — so the wall clock was also the weaker predicate. **The third really is elapsed time** — one read's own duration — so it keeps a clock but starts it where its subject starts: `_counting_remote` gained a `<hang-seconds>` mode and now stamps the instant read 1 began, and the bound is measured from that stamp with the tool's startup outside the window. The advisory-leg arm is the same shape, with the hanging delegate stamping its own launch. **The stamp READER is one behaviour and shipped in two copies, so it was extracted at that second caller:** `_since_stamp` / `_stamp_taken` now live in `tests/_selftest-prelude.sh`, which `prelude-shadow-selftest.sh` reds on the third copy of. **Both surviving numeric bounds were re-derived for the windows they now cover, 10 and 15 → 5.** The inherited numbers were sized for windows containing a startup; each window now measures ~1s (three consecutive runs at load ~8), and the companion cell cannot cover for a loose one — `KILLED after ${READ_BOUND}s` and `KILLED after ${budget}s` are both printed from the FLAG value, never from the measured duration, so a read or a leg that announced its bound and then failed to apply it left every cell green. Watched red at 5 and green at 10/15 against exactly that: a 6s read still announcing `1s`, and an 8s advisory leg still announcing `1s`. Both timed arms gained a **precondition cell** asserting the fixture was actually reached, because a window whose start never happened otherwise surfaces only as a wrong number. **Seen to fail** with a startup pad injected into each tool (35s before `release-tag-check`'s `rev-parse`, 20s before `board-session-close`'s `main`): the three pre-fix cells red at **37s**, **39s** and the 15s bound, and the post-fix cells are green under the *identical* pad and the same bins. A five-mutant battery reds each new cell in turn — the wrong-commit refusal made to keep polling, the per-read bound removed, the advisory bound removed, and each launch stamp dropped — so none of them is a decoration. **Denominator, re-derived at fix time** (`SECONDS|EPOCHSECONDS|EPOCHREALTIME|date +%s|%N|TIMEFORMAT|_t0|START=|_start=|elapsed|duration` over all 72 `tests/*.sh`, comment lines excluded, re-run on the merged head): exactly the two surviving windows, their two fixture writers and the prelude's one shared reader — no fourth member; the `--timeout N` values elsewhere in the file are the tool's own budget, measured by the tool after startup, not test-side clocks. **⚠ The shared reader shipped total over only TWO of the three "no stamp" states, found at review and fixed before merge:** `date +%s > stamp` opens its redirect *before* it execs `date`, so a fixture killed in that window leaves a zero-byte file that EXISTS — `cat` succeeds and prints nothing, the `|| echo 0` never fires, and the arithmetic becomes `$(( now -  ))`, a syntax error in the READER rather than a verdict about the subject. Driven, not read, with the hung-read fixture holding its stamp redirect open past the tool's 1s read bound: `release-tag-check-selftest` is `set -e` and evaluates `_since_stamp` one cell AHEAD of the `_stamp_taken` cell written to diagnose exactly this, so the file aborted there and **44 of its 142 assertions went unrun, with no FAIL line and no summary**. `${s:-0}` now covers that third state BESIDE the existing `|| echo 0` rather than replacing it, so absent/unreadable stay independent of `inherit_errexit`; `_stamp_taken` already answered `false` for it (`-s`, not `-e`), and both docblocks now state the states the code actually handles instead of two of three.

### Docs
- **card#8536** — **`.github/pull_request_template.md` omitted the `Built:` and `Coordinated in:` fields the coord plugin declares REQUIRED on every impl-repo PR body, so every PR minted from the template shipped without them** (#321/#324/#325 among them) — a reviewer auditing the build path or the coordinating card had nothing to read. The template now carries both, as a new `## Scope` block, with a **pointer** to the owning section (`coord-thread` skill § "The `Built:` line") rather than a copy of the four-value semantics. Class fix across three repos (the bridge and kanban-board templates carried the same omission); no behaviour, tool or CI change here — the template is not read by any gate.

## [0.31.1] - 2026-08-31
- **card#8261** — **`main` and `dev` carried the whole branch-protection model with `required_status_checks: null`, so the repo read as protected on every surface an operator inspects while NO CI result was required to merge.** Read live: `branches/{main,dev}/protection` answer `required_status_checks: null` on both. The clause was left off deliberately, because a protection `contexts` list is a hand-maintained registry of check names standing in front of a `selftest` matrix that **ordinary pull requests edit** — `run-coverage-check-selftest` was added three PRs before this one — and such a list rots in both directions, only one of them loud: a name dropped from the matrix never reports again and deadlocks every PR, while a name **added** to it is required by nothing at all. **New job `ci-gate` in `ci.yml`** — `needs:` every other job in that file, `if: always()`, and a step that asserts on the collected `needs.<job>.result`. It is the one context that does not move when the matrix does. ⛔ **`if: always()` is the load-bearing half:** without it the job is *skipped* the moment a needed job fails, and a skipped required check does **not** block a merge — the gate would be satisfied precisely when the suite was red, which is strictly worse than the honest `null` it replaces. **The ruling on the three non-success results, stated in the job's own comment:** `failure` and `cancelled` fail (a cancelled run measured nothing; there is no verdict to inherit, and `always()` rather than `!cancelled()` is what keeps the job alive to say so), and **`skipped` fails too** — no job in `ci.yml` carries a job-level `if:` or its own `needs:`, so nothing there can be skipped today and the arm is a ruling about the next edit: reading it as a pass would let a future `if:` drop a leg out of the merge gate with no signal anywhere. The results are read from `toJSON(needs)`, **derived from the `needs:` list rather than re-enumerated**, so a job added there is gated with no second edit — a hand-written `needs.a.result != 'success' || …` chain would be card#5910's subset-reading guard again. **`tests/ci-gate-selftest.sh`** holds all of it: the `needs:` list is two-way-compared against every top-level job in `ci.yml`, `if: always()` and the absence of a `name:` (which would silently rename the required context) are asserted from the parse, every job in the whole workflow directory must be disposed of by one of three buckets, and the **shipped verdict step is extracted by step name and driven over planted `needs` payloads** — success, `failure`, `cancelled`, `skipped`, all-skipped, mixed, and an empty `needs:` — because a copy of the command here would be a second thing that can disagree with the one CI runs. **All 25 assertions were each observed RED** under 16 targeted mutations (the `if:` deleted, a `needs:` entry dropped, a `name:` added, the job renamed, the verdict narrowed to `failure` only, the verdict made unconditional in both directions, the empty-`needs:` control removed, the `toJSON(needs)` wiring key typo'd, the step renamed, a phantom `needs:` entry, `jobs:` emptied, a workflow deleted, and an ungated new job planted), with the union of red labels diffed against the full assertion list to prove none was left uncovered — two gaps came out of that pass and were fixed rather than reported: the `NEEDS: ${{ toJSON(needs) }}` wiring had **no** assertion at all (a typo'd key left every probe passing while the shipped gate read an empty environment), and two "positive control" legs could not fail because `_need -r` already refuses the state they described. ⛔ **This changes no repository setting.** Setting `required_status_checks` is an admin operation on a protected branch and is the operator's, after this lands and `ci-gate` has reported on `dev` at least once — a required context that has never reported blocks everything. **Docs:** three comments asserting that a job or matrix-entry name "is a required status check on `main`" were **read live and are false** — true when written, and dropped by the 2026-08-30 fleet-model application, which replaced the whole protection object without carrying the contexts over; each now records what it used to say, why it is wrong, and points at the one name that is load-bearing (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `tests/drift-check-fixture-selftest.sh`, `tests/promote-stage-guard-selftest.sh`).


## [0.31.0] - 2026-08-25
- **card#7694** — **`kbcard`'s two read-side gaps: `search` had no terse projection, and no verb read the stage id → column name map — so on both, the affordable instrument was the wrong one and both wrong answers were a confident number rather than an error.** Filed from roundtable #361 with an independent second-seat confirmation of the second gap the same day. **Both additions are opt-in; no existing caller's output moves.** **(1) `search` gains `--brief` and `--count`.** `search` prints one block per hit — header plus the card's FULL description — which is correct when you want the body and unusable to ask *which* cards match: on a 1,405-card board a handful of hits is tens of KB, so a caller who cannot afford it falls back to `list` + a title match, which is **structurally incapable** of answering a body question and returns a candidate set dressed as a derived population (already instanced on a live board: a 72-card estimate carried with the explicit caveat that it is a candidate set rather than a derived population, and a floor rather than a count — derived that way for exactly this reason). `--brief` prints the **header lines only, byte-identical** to the header the block form already emits — one jq expression now owns that line and both forms interpolate it, so the identity is structural rather than two renderings kept in step; asserted as an equality over a fixture carrying a multi-line body, an absent body, an `ESC` in the description, a **newline in the name** and a non-string name, at the unit level and again through the shipped bin. `--count` prints **one line and no bodies** — the existing `<M> of <N>` denominator, from the same string the stderr line uses, on **stdout** because with that flag the count IS the requested output; it replaces the zero-result prose, so `0 of 0` and `0 of N` are what separate *nothing matched* from *the filter removed everything*. Neither flag changes which cards match, so the filter flags and the stderr denominator are untouched. **The full-body form stays the DEFAULT** — a forgotten flag must cost too much output, never a silent truncation — and the two together are **rc 2 before any request** rather than one silently winning: they answer different questions, and returning a number where header lines were asked for is the shape both exist to prevent. **(2) A `stages` verb — an EXPOSE, not a build.** `list` projects `stage` as a native numeric id, every `--column` flag takes a name, and no verb read the map: one seat inferred `23 → shipped_to_dev` from the ordering of the `--column` enum while reporting an awaiting-release count, another filtered a board sweep on `workflow_stage_id` when `list` projects `stage` and got **`OPEN 0` over a real backlog, twice** — a vacuous zero that reads exactly like a clean board. `kbcard stages` emits `{id, name}` per mapped stage, JSON, symmetric with `field list`, **built on the existing `stage_name`** rather than a second mapping table, so the output is by construction the set of pairs `(id, stage_name(id))` and cannot disagree with the `column` that `show` prints. ⚠ **It reads the CALLER's own config, not the server** — the pairs are `~/.kanban-<board>-board.env`'s `KB_STAGE_*` vars and **no request is issued** — which strengthens the read rather than weakening it: the ids are per-install, so a hardcoded one is guaranteed wrong on another box. **No ordinal field, deliberately:** nothing this read can see carries the board's column ORDER, so an ordinal would be a plausible-looking sequence with no source; rows are sorted by name for determinism and the help says that is not a board order. An env mapping no stage is **rc 1 with empty stdout**, never `[]` (an empty array reads as "this board has no columns", which is not a state this tool can address at all), and a value that is not a stage id — including the `000` placeholder a copied board-env template still carries — is **named on stderr** rather than dropped. **Coverage:** `tests/kbcard-selftest.sh` gains **78** assertions across both parts (`bash tests/kbcard-selftest.sh | grep -c '^  ok'`: 793 → 871, re-derivable rather than trusted here), each watched RED under a mutation of the code it guards — a `--brief` header expression drifting from the block form's, `--brief` falling through to the block form, the mutual exclusion resolved instead of refused, `--count` re-deriving its own numbers, the DEFAULT silently narrowing to headers, `stages` inventing an ordinal, emitting a string id, answering `[]` at rc 0 on an empty map, dropping a non-stage-id value silently, reaching the wire, and losing its name order. **One leg is documented as NOT discriminating what it looks like it does:** replacing the `stage_name` call with a local lowercase of the variable suffix reds nothing, because the two agree on every input a board env can hold, duplicate ids included — the leg guards the DRIFT between verb and primitive (shown red by moving `stage_name` under a private copy), and the no-duplication argument is a canon-#5 judgement stated as one rather than a measurement. **Consumer impact:** additive. No existing verb's stdout, stderr or rc changes — measured, not asserted: the **pre-change** selftest file runs green against the **post-change** bin, so every byte it pins is unmoved. `kbcard` is the only bin touched.
- **card#7615** — **A CI check that NEVER RAN is indistinguishable from one that PASSED, and nothing in the fleet could tell the two apart.** GitHub creates *no workflow runs at all* for a pull request whose merge ref it cannot compute (the base moved, the PR is conflicting); `mergeStateStatus` does not separate never-ran from passed; and for a `paths:`-filtered workflow **zero runs is the normal, correct state** — so *never ran*, *correctly skipped* and *passed* are one reading, and no count-the-runs check separates them without also computing the path match. **New tool: `bin/run-coverage-check`.** Given `--repo <owner/name> --pr <N>` it derives, per workflow file at the PR's head, whether THIS pull request matches the `on:` triggers and the `branches:`/`paths:`/`paths-ignore:` filters — the path legs evaluated against the PR's REAL changed-file list, with `!` negation applied in order as GitHub applies it — and compares that derived expectation against the runs that exist for the **full 40-hex head SHA**. **RAN / SKIPPED / MISSING are three separate report lines, and the verdict rides the EXIT CODE** (`0` covered · `1` at least one MISSING · `2` this run stands behind nothing), because prose in the body of a passing report is not a gate. ⛔ **REPORT-ONLY:** it is wired into no workflow, is not a required status check, comments on nothing, issues only GETs (asserted from the recorded argv) and writes nothing — gating on it would change what gates a merge, which is ask-first and was not approved. **Both arms measured on the live API:** `agent-webhook-bridge` PR #574's push-created head `8623d5fa…` reports **1 MISSING** — `pr-title-lint.yml`, whose `types:` omitted `synchronize` — beside 6 RAN and 1 correctly SKIPPED, at rc 1, which is the historical instance re-found automatically; `kanban-board` PR #651, bridge #588 and toolkit #312 report rc 0 with their path-filtered gates named SKIPPED *with the reason the silence is correct*. ⭐ **A read that returned nothing is never reported as coverage:** a changed-file list short of the PR object's own `changed_files` (GitHub's files endpoint caps at 3000) makes every path-filtered workflow **UNMEASURED** rather than SKIPPED while its unfiltered siblings keep sound verdicts; and a run list short of its `total_count`, an unreadable PR / workflow directory / workflow-state listing, an empty or entirely out-of-scope population, an `on:` shape or glob feature the parser does not implement, and a head SHA that is not 40 hex are each an rc 2 refusal naming what failed. A short SHA is refused BEFORE the runs query because `actions/runs?head_sha=` answers **HTTP 200 with `total_count: 0`** to one (measured on this same fixture) — this card's own defect arriving through the query string. **Fix shape 1 — a REQUIRED STATUS CHECK — remains the preferred fix and is already done where it was available** (`lint-title` on the bridge, `changelog-card-entry` on the toolkit); it is **permanently unavailable on `kanban-board`**, whose `branches/dev/protection` answers `403 "Upgrade to GitHub Pro or make this repository public"` — a **plan** limit no credential can fix — which is why this shape exists and why the card stays open. ⚠ Still **UNMEASURED**, unchanged by this: whether any merged PR in any of the three repos ever passed through the hole. The absence leaves no trace. Not claimed clean.
- **card#7616** — **Two shipped example env files still told operators the token ladder has three tiers, and that a board no tier names a token file for is REFUSED — false since card#7316 put the coord credential store's `[kanban] api_token_file` POINTER below them.** `examples/kanban-host.env.example` asserted *“There is no tier below this: a board that neither this file nor its own board env declares a token for is REFUSED rather than falling back to a shared default”*, and `examples/kanban-board.env.example` restated the same three-tier/REFUSED model — so both were wrong on the one input they exist to describe, and an operator whose store already holds the token reads them as saying their working install cannot work. **Measured, with a control:** with all three declared candidates empty (0 declarations in the host env, 0 in the board env, ambient unset) `kb_declared_token_file` in `bin/_kb-board-lib.sh` returns **rc 0** and a readable path; pointing `COORD_CREDENTIALS` at a non-existent store makes the same call return **rc 1** with the refusal, so the rc 0 is the store rung firing rather than a probe that cannot say no. **The resolver was already right, and so is every other surface that names the rung** — `docs/INSTALL.md` §3b owns the four-tier precedence and has since card#7316; only these two examples drifted, and they drifted BECAUSE they restated the list instead of pointing at it. **Fixed by DELETING both restatements rather than re-syncing them:** each example now points at §3b and says why the list is not repeated at the copy site, so there is no third copy left to go stale. Docs-only — no executable line moves, both examples still parse under `bash -n`.
- **Framework orientation block re-synced to coord v0.43.0** (`/coord:update` area 4). Docs-only: the managed `coord:solo-orientation` block gains the note that only `## Who you are` and `## Read at session start` are auto-surfaced to a session, so a standing rule written elsewhere in the doc silently never loads. No executable line changes; the project addendum below the block is untouched.
- **card#7599** — **`release-pr-body` rendered a malformed version for any repo whose version lives INSIDE a code file, and the cause was that the repo's version had two extractors.** The version was resolved as the **whole first `version_regex` match** in `version_file`. That is correct where the match *is* the version (a bare `VERSION` file), and wrong where the version is declared inside a config or source file: there the regex must carry SURROUNDING CONTEXT to be unambiguous, so the match is the context too. Measured on a consumer repo declaring `"version_file": "config/app.php"` + `"version_regex": "'version' => '[0-9.]+'"`, the release PR's scope line rendered `release PR — v'version' => '0.43.0'.`, and the same value fed the artifact checklist's `{{version}}` expansion and `--tag`. **NOT a tag defect** — that repo's tagging workflow never used `version_regex`; it called the repo's own `bin/extract-version.sh` and minted the right tag. That is the real finding: **two extractors, one question, different answers**, and the repo was safe only because the AUTHORITATIVE one was right and the cosmetic one was wrong.
- **NEW OPTIONAL KEY `version_extract_cmd`** — a repo-relative path to an executable whose **stdout IS the version**. Resolution order is now `--version` (unchanged, and still a complete bypass that runs nothing) → `version_extract_cmd` → `version_file`+`version_regex`. **Chosen over honouring a regex capture group, deliberately:** a capture group would have corrected the display and LEFT both implementations standing, which is the defect; naming the repo's own extractor collapses the two answers into one, by construction. The value is taken **verbatim** — no pattern is applied to it, because a check in that position could only narrow what the repo itself just said (the same reasoning as card#7208's deleted second pattern, one indirection out). Empty output, output spanning more than one line, and a non-zero exit are each **rc 2**, never a silent fall-back to `version_regex` — falling back would restore the very divergence the key removes.
- **A CONFIG THAT DOES NOT SET THE KEY IS UNCHANGED, byte for byte** — the `version_regex` path is untouched and still gated on the same conditions; the only other executable-line change is the `could not resolve version` message, which now also names the new key. Measured, not asserted: full bodies generated by the pre-change and post-change tools over the **same two real repos** — one with a bare `VERSION` file, one with the `config/app.php` shape above — are **byte-identical on stdout and stderr** (1910 and 3677 bytes), and across a fixture matrix of **8 config shapes × 2 modes = 16 runs**, 12 are byte-identical and the 4 that differ are the same rc-2 refusal differing only in that one remediation string. The existing selftest battery passes unmodified.
- ⛔ **It runs a command named by a config file — the trust boundary is stated at the site, and constrained anyway.** What is trusted is the REPO: `.release-pr.json` and the script it names are both committed, and this tool is run by a releaser in a checkout of the code they are about to ship, where anyone able to edit either can already edit `bin/`. Constraints regardless, so a typo refuses instead of surprising: **no shell** (the value is an argv[0] — arguments, `>`, `|`, `;`, `$(…)` are never interpreted and a value carrying them is refused by name), **inside the repo only** (absolute paths and `..` components refused), **no `$PATH` search** (a value with no `/` runs as `./<value>`, so a same-named script on `$PATH` can never answer for the repo), and it must exist and be executable.
- **`release-artifacts-check` does NOT read the new key, and its header now says why it must not.** Two independent reasons: its classification compares the version value **at two refs** (`git show <ref>:<file>`, never the working tree), which a command running in one checkout structurally cannot answer — it would return one value for both ends and classify every release PR as "version unchanged", silently asserting no member; and it runs on `pull_request` over an **untrusted head**, where executing a script the PR names would hand that PR arbitrary code execution inside the job judging it, a strictly larger hole than the head-retarget it already refuses to honour. The two keys answer two different questions and `version_regex` is right for that one: a context-matching regex classifies correctly, since equal-vs-changed is all the gate asks of it. **Consumers keep `version_file`/`version_regex` set** even after adopting the new key.
- **Coverage.** `release-pr-body-selftest.sh` gains 34 assertions over the incident's own config shape — the fix, the value reaching `--tag` and the artifact checklist as well as the header, a disagreeing-extractor case that proves the COMMAND is the source rather than a quietly narrowed regex, every refusal, a `$PATH`-decoy case with a control proving the decoy is what a lookup would have found, and the redirection target asserted absent. **29 of the 34 were watched RED against the pre-change tool**; the 5 that pass on both are the deliberate controls (the no-key path's unchanged output — malformation and all —, `--version` still bypassing with the command provably never run, and the decoy's own reachability). `release-artifacts-selftest.sh` gains a fixture whose version file genuinely moves while a declared extractor prints a constant, pinning that the gate classifies from the refs; **watched RED (3 of its 5 legs) on a bin wired to honour the key** — `not-release 9.9.9`, no member asserted — with the two `rc 0` legs deliberately noted as non-discriminating, since the failure being pinned is a silent non-run.
- **Consumers who vendor:** re-vendor `bin/release-pr-body` for the new key; `bin/release-artifacts-check` changes in COMMENTS ONLY (no executable line moves) and needs no re-vendor for correctness. Adopting the key is opt-in per repo: `docs/INSTALL.md` §4 documents when to set it and what it costs.


## [0.30.1] - 2026-08-24
- **card#7592** — **⛔ A branch checkout could move somebody else's card.** `board-card-start` — the `post-checkout` git hook that promotes a branch's card to In Progress — selected which card to act on by the **FIRST DIGIT RUN** of each card's stored `payload.dl_number` (`capture("(?<n>[0-9]+)")`). A card stamped `2026-08-23` therefore answered to **DL-2026** and a card stamped `1.5` to **DL-1**, so checking out a branch named for either DL **moved that unrelated card**, and with `.[0] // empty` a mis-read card could shadow the genuine one. It is the **over-matching** member of the class card#7587 opened: that one CONCATENATED the runs and needed a release to fire; this one TRUNCATES to the first run and fires on an ordinary checkout, with the move landing in the board's changelog as an event that looks legitimate afterwards. The stamp needs no bad tool to get there — the board UI, another tool, an API caller and a human can all write one — so the READER is where it has to be answered. **All three tools now apply the rule the board itself applies** (kanban DL-251, `^\D*(\d+)\D*$`): a stored value that is not ONE integer, optionally decorated, correlates to **nothing**. **⚠ THIS IS A NARROWING, in the recoverable direction:** a DL that now matches no card falls through to a card-id token in the branch name, or lands on the existing loud unresolved-DL refusal — an un-promoted card is moved by hand, a wrongly-moved one has no clean revert. **Nothing well-formed changes**, measured as a differential against each pre-fix expression rather than against the new code's expectations: every value the fixture correlates (`178`, `#178`, `PR-178`, `PR-085`, `DL-0253`, `093`, `(#12)`, `7587`, `0`, `000`, a whitespace-padded value and `-5`) selects byte-identically to before, with the pre-fix expression separately asserted to still produce its old wrong keys so a fix that was never applied cannot satisfy that block. **Two same-shape siblings from card#7587's audit are closed with it:** `next-dl`'s board DL-floor scan **SPLIT** a multi-run stamp, so one card holding `2026-08-23` raised the floor to `2026` and burned every number under it — the direction was safe (a floor only over-estimates, so it can never re-mint a used DL) but the floor is shared state and a mint only ever moves up, and under the board's own rule such a stamp names no DL and so occupies none; and `board-card-start`'s own stamp-conflict comparison read the first run too, so a multi-run stamp whose first run matched the branch's DL compared EQUAL and the conflict WARNING was suppressed — both arms there are write-free, so the whole cost was an operator never being told the card holds a value no correlating tool can read. **One sibling is deliberately UNCHANGED:** `kb_dl_int_lenient` still concatenates, its docblock already rules on that, and its one unknown-provenance caller (`adopt-to-dl`'s already-adopted guard) is **fail-closed** — it refuses to adopt and writes nothing, so narrowing it is a behaviour change to that guard rather than to this class. **130 assertions, 14 of which red against the pre-fix tools** (the wrong card moved on two spellings, the miss going unreported on both channels, the conflict warning suppressed on two more, the DL floor lifted to `DL-2027` on two legs, and the census below reporting an unguarded read), beside 116 that pass against **both** builds — four stored spellings that genuinely name their DL, including the JSON-number, legacy 3-pad and canonical 4-pad forms, an unrelated DL that moves nothing, an agreeing stamp that warns about nothing, a well-formed conflicting stamp that still warns, and a board whose real floor still wins — so a tool that had simply stopped selecting anything could not satisfy this block.
  - **⚠ THE RULE NOW HAS ONE OWNER, and one copy that cannot be removed.** The jq text is `KB_JQ_REF_CANON` in `bin/_kb-board-lib.sh`, prepended by each reader to its own filter, so the call sites share one expression instead of one apiece. `promote-released-cards` is a vendored standalone that must not source it and therefore keeps its inline copy — new `tests/reader-ref-canon-selftest.sh` asserts the two jq texts are **BYTE-IDENTICAL**, both taken from the shipped files rather than restated, so a copy that cannot be deleted is pinned instead of tolerated. Both selftests run the shared `tests/_ref-canon-cases.sh` table, which now covers three implementations.
  - **The tests close the CLASS, not only its instances.** A **derived census** — over the bins that source the shared lib, resolved by the same anchored pattern that lib's own header publishes, never a list — requires every read of a stored correlation stamp to be canon-guarded or **declared with a reason**, and asserts no such bin defines a `norm` of its own, so the N+1th reader cannot join the tree unguarded and unnoticed.
  - **⚠ VENDORED SURFACE AFFECTED — re-vendor the shared lib together with every bin you copied.** Which bins those are is DERIVED, not listed here: the recipe is `docs/UPGRADE.md` §3, and the lib's own header publishes the one-line derivation.
  - **What a HALF-vendored install does, measured rather than guessed.** A bin copied beside an older copy of that file hits `KB_JQ_REF_CANON: unbound variable` under `set -u`, before jq is ever reached: `board-card-start` loses its DL leg — the failure is inside a command substitution, so it degrades to the card-id fallback or to its own loud unresolved-DL refusal and still never blocks a checkout — and `next-dl` refuses to mint. Same class as v0.29.0's `kb_parse_resp: command not found`, and the same remedy.

- **card#7587** — **⛔ `promote-released-cards` could move a card onto somebody else's pull request.** The correlation reader normalised a card's stored `payload.dl_number` / `pr_number` / `id` with `gsub("[^0-9]";"")`, which **CONCATENATES the digit runs** of a value carrying more than one: `1.5` → `15`, `2026-08-23` → `20260823`, `PR 12 of 34` → `1234`. A card holding `1.5` was therefore correlated against genuinely-shipped **PR 15** — a real but unrelated pull request — and the sweep **PATCHed that card** to the released stage, with nothing on any channel saying so. A stage move is an event in the board's changelog, so the wrong answer is durable and looks legitimate afterwards, and it needed no bad tool to arrive: the stamp's provenance is the board UI, another tool, an API caller or a human, and this reader sits downstream of all of them. **The reader now applies the rule the board itself applies** (kanban DL-251, `^\D*(\d+)\D*$`): a value that is not ONE integer, optionally decorated, correlates to **nothing**, the card is left where it is, and — for a DL or an explicit card id — the shipped ref lands on the existing `matched NO card` WARNING, which is what makes the recovery possible at all (an un-promoted card is moved by hand; a wrongly-promoted one has no clean revert). **⚠ THIS IS A NARROWING, and the direction is the point:** a card whose stamp is not one integer was previously moved on whatever ref its digits happened to spell, and is now not moved. **Nothing well-formed changes.** The guard is applied to the SINGLE `norm` def, i.e. to all three ref legs at once — re-splitting it per field is exactly the divergence that def exists to prevent — and a board `id` is one digit run by construction, so it is a no-op there (asserted, not assumed). Behaviour-neutrality is **measured as a differential against the pre-fix expression itself**, not against the new code's expectations: over all twelve fixture rows the reader still correlates (`178`, `#178`, `PR-178`, `PR-085`, `DL-0253`, `093`, `(#12)`, `7587`, `0`, `000`, a whitespace-padded value and `-5`) the two expressions answer **byte-identically**, and separate legs pin a JSON-NUMBER stamp, a `null` (an absent key) and the card-`id` leg end to end, and the pre-fix side is separately asserted to still produce its three wrong answers so a fix that was never applied cannot satisfy that block. **⚠ THE RULE NOW LIVES IN TWO RUNTIMES AND CANNOT LIVE IN ONE.** `_kbc_require_ref_int` (bash, card#7536) refuses to MINT such a value; this def (jq) refuses to CORRELATE one — and jq cannot call the bash predicate, while this tool is a vendored standalone that must not even source `_kb-board-lib.sh`. What is shared is **the table**: new `tests/_ref-canon-cases.sh` carries one value per row with the answer each side must give, and `tests/promote-ref-canon-selftest.sh` asserts BOTH implementations against it — **both EXTRACTED from the shipped files rather than restated**, because a copy in a test is a third expression of the rule that stays green while the tool rots. ⚠ **What it holds is CONTAINMENT, not equality, and the fixture's very first run is why:** card#7536 has since narrowed the MINT site *below* the board's own rule (it refuses a NEGATIVE value and `0`) while this reader is pinned *to* that rule, so the two accept sets are legitimately different and an equality assertion would have forced one side to move on a ruling nobody made. The invariant asserted is the one that matters — **every value the mint site accepts is one the reader correlates**, so a stamp `kbcard` wrote can never be silently skipped by the sweep — plus a **declared divergence set**: each divergent row carries a reason tag, the selftest measures the real divergence off the two shipped implementations and asserts it equals exactly the tagged set, so a new divergence nobody named reds and a tag left behind after one closes reds too. The canonical FORM is asserted on the jq side only, because `kbcard` stores the operator spelling verbatim and has no canonical form to compare. **122 assertions, 19 of which red against the pre-fix tool** (eight reader answers, the divergence-set leg, the pre-vs-post differential leg, the JSON-number leg, and seven end-to-end arms driving the REAL script over a canned board with its PATCH set observable — three spellings that all concatenate to 15 left alone, the multi-run DL left alone, and the operator WARNING that now names it), beside 103 that pass against **both** binaries — four spellings that genuinely name PR 15 including the JSON-number and zero-padded forms, an unrelated PR left untouched, the `--cards` id leg, and every mint-site row — so a guard that simply refused everything could not satisfy this block. **⚠ ONE IN-FILE CLAIM WAS FALSE AND IS CORRECTED, NOT RE-SYNCED.** The comment above `numlist` said the shipped side and the card side were "the SAME rule … keep the two in sync"; that was true of the leading-zero strip and of nothing else — `numlist` is `grep -oE '[0-9]+'`, which emits **each run separately**, so the two sides of one comparison had already disagreed on every multi-run value. It now states exactly what is shared (the leading-zero strip, mirrored in two runtimes) and where they diverge BY DESIGN (this side splits, the card side refuses), with the asymmetry's reason — the shipped side names what shipped and every feed into it is already single-run, the card side decides a PATCH on a card of arbitrary provenance — and the fixture's third column pins both answers so the divergence stays a decision rather than becoming an accident. **⚠ Sibling audit — the card's own Bound said the population had NOT been re-derived, so it was re-derived here, on two axes, and it is LARGER than the one instance filed.** Axis 1, every site that derives digits by stripping or extracting a character class, over `bin/`, `hooks/`, the three composite actions, the workflow directory and the python shims: **10 hits, 1 of them prose in a comment, leaving 9 code sites** (positive control: the predicate finds this reader and its `numlist` sibling). Axis 2, every read of a stored card correlation stamp (`payload.dl_number` / `pr_number` / `issue_number`), which is the population the card actually names: **5 sites** — and axis 2 is what makes the audit honest, because the highest-severity sibling below **derives its integer with a jq `capture`, not a strip, so axis 1 structurally cannot see it**. Each is dispositioned, and **four are same-shape findings reported rather than closed** — every one of them changes what a tool mints, writes, moves or warns, which is a separate ruling from this one. **Three of the four have since been ruled on and CLOSED by card#7592 (entry above); the fourth stands as dispositioned:**
  - ⛔ **`board-card-start`'s DL→card resolution** (`bin/board-card-start:422-426`) — it selects the card to act on with `capture("(?<n>[0-9]+)")` over each card's stored `dl_number`, i.e. by that value's FIRST digit run. A card stamped `1.5` therefore answers to `DL-1`, and this tool then **moves that card** on branch checkout (and can stamp it) — the same wrong-card write as the reader fixed here, on the checkout path rather than the release path, and with `.[0] // empty` a mis-read card can shadow the genuine one. This is the one sibling whose direction is OVER-match, and it is the reason the audit was worth running. **Reported, not fixed here — it changes which card an automated checkout hook acts on. ✅ CLOSED by card#7592, above.**
  - **`next-dl`'s `max_int`, over every card's stored `dl_number`** (`bin/next-dl:171`, called at `:387`) — it SPLITS a multi-run stamp and every run competes for the maximum, so one card holding `2026-08-23` raises the board's DL floor to `2026` and the next offline mint becomes `DL-2027`. Real and reachable; the direction is the safe one (the scan is documented as a FLOOR, it over-estimates and so can never re-mint a used DL, and the primary path is the server's own counter), but it burns DL numbers and reports a floor nobody can account for. **Reported, not fixed here. ✅ CLOSED by card#7592, above.**
  - **`board-card-start`'s stamp-conflict comparison** (`bin/board-card-start:505`) — it reads the card's stored `dl_number` and takes the FIRST digit run, so a multi-run stamp whose first run matches the branch's DL reads as "already correctly stamped" and the conflict WARNING is suppressed. Both arms of that branch are **write-free** (it never overwrites a differing stamp), so the entire cost is a missing diagnostic line. **Reported, not fixed here. ✅ CLOSED by card#7592, above.**
  - **`kb_dl_int_lenient`** (`bin/_kb-board-lib.sh:1455`) still concatenates, and its docblock already rules on that: its unknown-provenance caller is `adopt-to-dl`'s already-adopted GUARD, whose divergence is **fail-closed** (it refuses to adopt, writing nothing) and whose only cost is a diagnostic naming a DL that does not exist. **Unchanged, as its own docblock already dispositions it.**
  **Closed as not-this-class:** `release-pr-body`'s `emit_card_ids` reads commit SUBJECTS, not a stored stamp, and already takes each token's TRAILING run for this exact reason; `board-card-start`'s three other sites read a BRANCH NAME or a local git config through a token pattern that already bounds the match to one run; `hooks/agent-dispatch-card-start` requires a whole-match `#([0-9]+)` and strips nothing; `_dependabot-reconcile.py`'s `isdigit` parses a SemVer part; `board-stats`' two `tr -cd '0-9'` sites are test-side digit extraction from a rendered report. **⛔ TWO SHAPES FOUND AND DELIBERATELY NOT CLOSED, both recorded in the bin beside the code they concern, because narrowing the SHIPPED side's accept set changes which cards the sweep moves and is its own ruling:** a `ref_token_regex` whose own match carries two digit runs (`PROJ2-DL-[0-9]+`) makes `numlist` split out a spurious extra ref that can match an unrelated card — the default `DL-[0-9]+` cannot, and `emit_card_ids` shows the fix if it is ever ruled on; and `numlist` matches with the RANGE `[0-9]`, which a UTF-8 collation widens (measured: `DL-4<U+0663>` yields the ref `4<U+0663>` under `en_US.UTF-8` and `4` under `C`), which the ASCII-only reader cannot equal, so that one is UNDER-promotion. The fixture is ASCII-only for that reason and says so; both implementations it aligns use a NEGATED ASCII set and are asserted locale-invariant. ⚑ **The sign is still accepted BY THE READER, and that is now an asymmetry rather than a shared position** — `-5` is one decorated integer under the rule the board itself applies, so this reader correlates the card to PR `5` (a real but different ref: this card's own defect, one property over). card#7536 closed that shape at the MINT site only; closing it HERE means narrowing the reader **below** the board's own rule, which is a separate ruling nobody has made, and a `-5` written before that mint guard — or by the board UI, or by any other writer — still reaches this reader. It is left at the rule it implements and the divergence is **declared** in the fixture with its reason rather than hidden, so the ruling cannot land, or fail to land, silently.

- **card#7536** — **`kbcard --pr -5` was accepted and stored, and the three readers of that one stamp then gave three different answers, none of them an error.** `-5` is ONE decorated integer under the board's own by-ref rule, so the shape check landed for this card (entry below) passes it by construction — and the value it minted was then read three incompatible ways: the tool stored `-5`; the board drops the sign with the rest of the decoration and indexes the card under `github_pr` ref `5`, a **real but different** pull request (**measured**, on the vendored 1:1 mirror of that rule: `canonicalize('github_pr', '-5') === '5'`); and the reconciler's own admission test, `is_numeric && (float) > 0`, declines the value outright, so the card is invisible to it. **Stored, mis-correlated and invisible at once.** `--pr` / `--issue` now require **one POSITIVE integer**, optionally decorated — a negative value is **rc 2 before any request**, on `create-card` and `patch` alike, with a diagnostic naming the flag, the value, and the sign as the cause (a refusal reading "must name ONE integer" would be wrong about `-5`, which is one). Pull requests and issues are numbered **from 1**, so refusing costs no legitimate caller, and it collapses three answers into one at the **mint site** — the cheapest place to fix it and the only point at which the value is still unambiguous. **ZERO GOES WITH IT, as an explicit ruling rather than a side effect of the sign.** `0`, `00`, `#0` and `PR-000` were all accepted and stamped — this change's own predecessor pinned `0` as a positive control, reasoning that the pre-PR placeholder is `.../pull/0`. That placeholder is a **URL**: it rides on `--pr-url` / `--issue-url`, which this predicate does not cover and which are **untouched**, and nothing in the toolkit passes `--pr 0`. There is no pull request or issue `0` to correlate to, the board indexes ref `0` for it and the same positive-only admission test declines it, and `--dl` — the third correlation key — has refused a token resolving to `0` in its own words since it was written. The three keys now agree. **This is a narrowing: an invocation that was accepted and stamped a correlation naming nothing, or naming somebody else's work, is now refused.** Nothing naming a positive integer changes — the accepted set, the coercion, the emitted payload and every rc are byte-identical for it, decorated spellings included, and no bare-integer requirement was added. **Sibling audit on the sign, over the toolkit's other integer correlation keys, both dispositioned:** `--external-id` (`kb_is_uint`, anchored canonical decimal) and `--dl` (`kb_dl_num`, anchored DL token) **never had the hole** — neither predicate ever admitted a sign, and `kb_dl_num` already refused `0` — so neither is touched. The hole was specific to the **decoration-tolerant** predicate, whose `[^0-9]*` prefix reads a leading `-` as decoration; it is one instance, not a class, and no consolidation is owed. Both siblings now carry a selftest leg pinning that they refuse a sign, so widening either predicate to a decoration-tolerant one cannot re-open it unnoticed. **The selftest leg that pinned the accepting behaviour is FLIPPED, deliberately, with the ruling recorded at the leg** — that is what the pin was for. The positive controls that keep the refusals falsifiable stay and are still decorated (`178`, `#178`, `PR-085`), plus a new one asserting an **interior** hyphen is decoration and not a sign, and two scope legs asserting the URL flags still take the `.../pull/0` placeholder. The locale-range guard's `--pr` probe now keys on the refusal's flag-and-value prefix rather than one arm's wording, which had silently answered ACCEPT for a value a second arm refused.

- **card#7536** — **`kbcard --pr` / `--issue` refused nothing, so the tool that MINTS a card's correlation stamp would write a value no reader can agree about.** `--pr 1.5`, `--issue 2026-08-23`, `--pr "PR 12 of 34"` and `--issue TBD` were all accepted and stamped. A stored value like that is then read two incompatible ways and **neither reader reports an error**: a board applying kanban's one-decorated-integer rule derives **no** reference, so the card correlates to nothing while its stamp looks set, while a reader that strips every non-digit derives the **concatenation** of the runs — `1.5` → `15`, `2026-08-23` → `20260823` — which is a **real but different** pull request or issue. Both flags now require the value to name **one integer, optionally decorated** (`178`, `#178`, `PR-178`); anything else is **rc 2 before any request**, on `create-card` and `patch` alike, with a diagnostic naming the flag and the value. The rule is the board's own, applied at the one place the value is unambiguous — the point it is typed — which is upstream of every consumer that would otherwise have to guess. **This is a narrowing: an invocation that was accepted and stamped a garbage correlation is now refused.** Nothing that named a single integer changes — the accepted set, the coercion, the emitted payload and every rc are byte-identical for it — and `--pr-url` / `--issue-url` are URLs and are untouched. **Entailed, and the reason the refusal is worth its rc:** `patch` now assembles the payload **above** the task-ref lookup, with the rest of the offline preflight, so a bad correlation value costs no traffic at all; before this, `patch --task <external-ref> --dl not-a-dl` had already issued a `GET` by the time it refused, and `--dl` inherits the fix unchanged. **Sibling audit, over the tool's derived value-flag population — 27 flags, re-derived by the suite's own `_value_flags` from `kbcard`'s guard call sites rather than listed, so the census moves the day the tool grows a flag.** Every member is dispositioned. **Already strict, unchanged:** `--dl` (`kb_dl_canon`) and `--external-id` (`kb_is_uint`) refuse these shapes and more. **Fixed here:** `--pr`, `--issue`. **Resolve against a closed set or a board lookup, and refuse rather than transform:** the task refs (`--task` / `--from` / `--to`), `--column`, `--swimlane`, `--type`, `--field`, `--board`. **The server owns the closed set:** `--relation`. **A field DEFINITION, not a correlation ref:** `--key`, `--options` (already refused empty and duplicate). **URLs:** `--pr-url` / `--issue-url` — the `owner/repo` a reader derives off them comes from a URL pattern both authorities share, so a URL that does not match derives nothing on either side and there is no second answer to disagree with. **Derive no identifier at all:** the nine prose / label flags (`--name`, `--description`, `--content` and their `-file` twins, `--tags`, `--label`, `--origin`). ⭐ **`--version` is the informative NON-member, and the reason this rule is scoped to correlation refs rather than to "value flags":** a correct version target (`v0.9.2`) carries THREE digit runs by design and no consumer derives one integer from it — the release sweep compares the string against a tag — so applying this predicate there would refuse every well-formed value. **⛔ One shape found and deliberately NOT closed HERE — the SIGN.** `--pr -5` is one decorated integer under the board's rule, so it still passed after this change — pinned by a selftest leg (proven to red when the predicate is narrowed) so that the narrowing could not land silently, and reported on the card rather than taken here, because closing it means REFUSING a value the rule this change implements accepts. **That ruling has since been made and the shape is closed — see the entry above, which flips that pin.** **⚠ Sibling audit, one layer out — the toolkit still contains a CONCATENATING READER of this same stamp, reported and NOT fixed.** `bin/promote-released-cards`'s correlation normaliser is `gsub("[^0-9]";"")`, applied to a card's stored `payload.dl_number` / `pr_number`: a card holding `1.5` normalises to `15` and is correlated against shipped PR **15**, so the release sweep would MOVE that card on somebody else's pull request. It is the reader half of exactly the divergence this change closes at the mint site, on an outward path (it PATCHes cards), and it is unreachable from `kbcard` only for values `kbcard` itself wrote — the board UI, another tool and a human can all still write one. Closing it changes which cards the sweep acts on, which is a separate ruling, and that file is under concurrent change; reported on the card rather than taken here — **and closed in this same release by card#7587 above**. `kb_dl_int_lenient` is the third reader and is the benign one — its docblock now says which of its two callers takes a value of unknown provenance (the already-adopted guard), and why the divergence there is fail-closed (it refuses to adopt) rather than a mis-stamp, correcting a claim in this branch's own first draft that both callers were repo-written. The new guard also gets a case in the locale-range guard, because the ASCII-digit claim in its docblock is exactly what a collation-widened range would falsify (measured: widening it accepts U+0663 as a PR number, which the board derives nothing from). One more doc-currency correction rides along: `adopt-to-dl`'s `--issue` comment restates why its stricter bare-integer rule is still worth having on top of the new one — `kbcard` now refuses a value that is not one decorated integer, which still leaves a DECORATED spelling accepted there and refused on the wire by the number-typed field, and a padded `007` silently renumbered to `7` by the payload merge.

- **card#7535** — **`promote-released-cards` accepted an explicit `--base`/`--head` naming a ref this repo does not have, and reported the resulting empty range as SUCCESS: `no shipped refs in range — nothing to do` at rc 0.** A promotion run that promoted nothing and called it clean — the release's cards stay un-promoted, and the line it prints is the one a genuinely empty range prints, so nothing distinguishes the two after the fact. The mechanism is one line: `SUBJECTS="$(git log "$RANGE" --no-merges --format='%s' 2>/dev/null || true)"` — `2>/dev/null` swallowed git's *bad object* and `|| true` converted its rc 128 into success, leaving an empty subject list. This is the same class card#7525 closed one release bin over, found by re-deriving that fix's siblings; the population was re-derived again here from the shipped shell (24 files; 15 invoke git; 15 hits for a neutralised git call site consuming a ref-shaped variable, one of which is prose inside a message string, leaving 14 real sites; cross-checked against an independent axis — every flag anywhere in `bin/`/`hooks/` whose value is a git ref) and it converges on the same four release tools: `release-artifacts-check` fails closed at every one of its six sites (`|| die`, or a handled branch), `release-tag-check` refuses its own `--after` and delegates `--before` to the classifier, `release-pr-body` was closed by card#7525, and **this was the one instance**. **Now both explicit legs are checked for RESOLVABILITY before the range is built and refuse at rc 2 naming the flag and the value.** The base leg is checked first, matching `release-pr-body`'s ordering so the two tools answer the same way about the same pair of flags; the head leg is nonetheless checked before the `git describe "${HEAD_REF}^"` above it, which swallows its own failure — an unresolvable explicit `--head` with a defaulted base used to surface as `no base tag found before <head>`, a refusal that blamed the baseline for a bad head after printing a NOTE about a baseline of `''`. **The predicate peels to a commit (`<ref>^{commit}`), and that is load-bearing:** `git rev-parse --verify -q <40-hex>` exits **0** for a full-length hex string naming no object — it verifies that the spelling can become a raw object name, not that the object is present (measured, git 2.43.0) — while `git log` on it dies `bad object`, so the bare spelling would have passed the deleted-ref case straight through, and a sha copied off a rebased-away branch is the natural hand spelling of `--head`. **Deliberately the EXPLICIT legs only:** a defaulted `HEAD_REF` (`HEAD`) and a defaulted `BASE` keep their existing refusals, whose failure direction is the opposite one and which name no flag because the caller passed none; the explicit-refs path (`--dls`/`--cards`) builds no range at all and is untouched, so an unresolvable `--base` beside `--dls` is still simply unused. ⚠ **ONE BEHAVIOUR CHANGE FOR CALLERS:** a hand-run that exits 0 with `nothing to do` on an unresolvable explicit ref now exits **2** with a message; the CI path is unaffected because `promote/action.yml` passes neither flag. A ref that resolves is unaffected — measured across **17 invocations** under the pre- and post-change binaries (both legs defaulted, each leg explicit, a branch name / `HEAD` / raw sha / annotated tag, the `--dls` and `--cards` paths, `--dry-run`, both stage-guard arms, a no-match set and the four usage refusals): **rc, stdout, stderr and the PATCH set byte-identical on every one**, the sole difference in the whole comparison being `--help`, which prints this tool's own header. `tests/promote-stage-guard-selftest.sh` gains a block of **38 assertions, 17 of which red against the pre-fix tool** (the same 17 with `$GITHUB_ACTIONS` set and unset — the block pins that variable, because the derived-baseline NOTE is emitted only when it is unset, which made one arm fail in CI while passing locally and another pass in CI vacuously) — both legs at rc 0, the misattributed baseline refusal, and four hex-sha arms that red if the peel is dropped (measured against a peel-dropped mutant: exactly those four, and nothing else) — beside 21 that pass against **both** binaries, including four resolvable `--head` spellings, an annotated-tag `--base`, the defaulted-leg run with its LOCAL-tags NOTE and the opposite side of that environment branch — an arm that reds, alone, against a mutant with the tool's suppression branch deleted, so the runner's side is held by something rather than merely avoided — and a `--dls`-with-a-dead-`--base` arm that reds if the guard is hoisted out of the derive branch (measured against that mutant too), so a check that refused everything could not satisfy this block.

- **card#7539** — **`kbcard search`'s usage text and the README both claimed "comment text is not searched", and that has been FALSE since the server added its comment arm** — a word appearing only in a card's comment does reach that card, verified live. Both surfaces also quoted a hit count measured against a live board as the worked example of the short-token fallback; that figure had already drifted once and is now removed rather than refreshed, because a live-board number in committed prose is a re-mintable staleness source. Both surfaces now describe the SHAPE and name the kanban API's own free-text contract as the authority, and state that which arm runs is **version-dependent** — newer servers narrow the fallback to an AND of words and disclose the arm in the response meta, older ones do neither, and this tool cannot tell which instance it is pointed at. **Docs only — no behaviour, exit code or request changes.**

- **card#7525** — **`release-pr-body` accepted an explicit `--base`/`--head` naming a ref this repo does not have, and answered with an empty range instead of a refusal.** card#7517 hardened the two DEFAULTED legs — resolve from `origin`, fetch, die loudly — and the explicit legs are deliberately exempt from that fetch, because the flag is the caller's override and `--base <tag> --head <ref>` is the offline route that same change introduced, so **nothing ever looked the handed-in ref up at all**. `git log "$RANGE"` runs under `2>/dev/null` at four sites and every observable outcome was silent: `--manifest` and `--card-manifest` printed an **empty list at rc 0** — the machine-read surfaces, the second of which is the input deciding which cards `promote-released-cards` promotes — while the full body path aborted inside the `COUNT=` pipeline (`pipefail` promotes git's 128 out of the substitution and `errexit` takes the script) at **rc 128 with not one byte on either stream**, naming neither the flag nor the ref. In all three a typo'd or deleted ref was indistinguishable from a genuinely empty release, and the explicit-`--head` path additionally lost its own advisory note, since `merge-base --is-ancestor` on an unresolvable ref fails under its own `2>/dev/null`. **Now both explicit legs are checked for RESOLVABILITY before the range is built and refuse at rc 2 naming the flag and the value.** It is not a freshness check and adds no fetch — the override is still honoured verbatim, and the only question asked is whether the named ref exists here, precisely because skipping the fetch is what leaves nobody else to ask it. The base leg is checked FIRST, before the head leg's fetch, so a bad baseline refuses without touching the network. **The predicate peels to a commit (`<ref>^{commit}`), and that is load-bearing:** `git rev-parse --verify -q <40-hex>` exits **0** for a full-length hex string naming no object — it verifies that the spelling can become a raw object name, not that the object is present (measured, git 2.43.0) — while `git log` on it dies `bad object`, so the bare spelling would have passed the deleted-ref case straight through, and a raw sha copied off a rebased-away branch is the natural offline spelling of `--head`. An annotated tag peels through unchanged; a DEFAULTED base still resolves to empty on a tagless first-ever-release repo, and `--tag` is untouched (it returns before the range block, builds no range, and still answers with a nonsense `--head`, which is what `release-tag-check` needs inside a CI job with no promise the range's refs are present). ⚠ **ONE BEHAVIOUR CHANGE FOR CALLERS:** an invocation that exits 0 with an empty manifest today — or 128 with an empty body — now exits **2** with a message; a ref that resolves is unaffected. Measured across **16 invocations** under the pre- and post-change binaries (both legs defaulted, each leg explicit, a tag / branch name / raw sha / `origin/`-prefixed head, both query modes, an empty range, a tagless repo whose baseline resolves to nothing, and the usage refusals): **rc, stdout and stderr byte-identical on every one**, the sole difference in the whole comparison being `--help`, which prints this tool's own header. `tests/release-pr-body-selftest.sh` gains a block of 33 assertions, **13 of which red against the pre-fix tool** — the two manifest modes at rc 0, the body at rc 128, the hex-sha arm that reds if the peel is dropped, and an ordering arm that pre-fix reported the unreachable origin instead of the bad baseline — beside 20 that pass against **both** binaries, including four resolvable `--head` spellings and a byte-identity arm against the unguarded output, so a check that refused everything could not satisfy it.
- **card#7517** — **`release-pr-body` resolved the HEAD of the release range from the LOCAL integration ref while its own baseline leg refused to do exactly that three lines below it, so a local `dev` that lagged the remote silently dropped PRs from the bundled table AND from the `shipped-cards` manifest that decides which cards get promoted.** The two ends fed one `RANGE`, and only one of them was resolved against `origin` — the baseline leg fetches, `die`s when it cannot, and its own message says a local ref *"is NOT a usable fallback"*; the head leg was a bare `HEAD_REF="${HEAD_REF:-$DEV_BRANCH}"`. The precondition is the normal case, not an exotic one: a release worktree is cut once and the integration branch keeps moving under it, so every merge after the cut is a commit the local ref does not have. **The failure is silent because a short range is WELL-FORMED** — the body renders, the row count looks plausible, the manifest footer is valid and simply missing its newest entries, and nothing downstream cross-checks the manifest against the commits the PR actually contains. Measured at one release cut: a one-commit lag produced 38 rows instead of 39, omitted the newest PR, and shipped a manifest missing that PR's card — a card `promote-released-cards` would therefore never have moved to Released. **Now: a DEFAULTED leg is fetched from `origin` and resolved against the remote tip, a fetch failure is fatal, and that leg's explicit flag is its override.** The head default becomes `origin/<dev_branch>` (with a `FETCH_HEAD` fallback for a clone whose refspec does not cover the branch), and a local integration ref that diverges from it draws the same advisory note the baseline leg has always printed for `main`. **An explicit `--head` still wins, verbatim and without a fetch** — a caller may legitimately want a local or a release-branch ref, and that was the hand workaround for the release this was found on — but a ref that is **strictly behind** a remote tip already in the repo now draws a note naming the commits it costs the body and the manifest. Strictly-behind rather than "differs", deliberately: a release branch legitimately diverges from the integration branch, and noting that on every release is noise nobody reads. **⚠ ONE BEHAVIOUR CHANGE FOR CALLERS:** each explicit flag skips only ITS OWN leg's fetch, so an offline run now passes **both** — `--base <tag> --head <ref>`. `--base` alone was a complete offline override only because the head leg silently took the local ref, which is the defect; with the remote unreachable it now refuses at rc 2 and the refusal names `--head`. Output is otherwise unchanged: with the local and remote tips in sync a non-empty range renders a **byte-identical** body (measured, 8877 B, same sha256), and the only difference anywhere is the empty-range placeholder, which now names the ref actually used — `v0.1.0..origin/dev` where it used to say `v0.1.0..dev`. `tests/release-pr-body-selftest.sh` reproduces the incident against a fixture whose origin carries a commit the workstation's local **and** remote-tracking refs both lack, so only the tool's own fetch can surface it, and asserts the PR and its card are PRESENT in the body, in the footer and in `--card-manifest`. Ten assertions across the two new blocks red against the pre-fix tool and pass against this one; the in-sync arm passes against both, which is what makes it a constraint witness rather than a defect witness.

## [0.30.0] - 2026-08-23

**Minor — the credential a board tool sends must be one somebody DECLARED and the API host it resolves must be one somebody NAMED, the toolkit's own DL-board setup tool stops standing boards up broken and becomes runnable on a board with no swimlanes, five files land under `bin/`, every operator-facing number that can be built from a partial read now carries its floor on the number itself, a SECURITY fix stops ten error paths echoing a credential the api base legitimately carries, and `promote-released-cards` becomes runnable against a real host at all.** 39 PRs since v0.29.0 (#259–#298, less this release's own #295): thirty-six card-carrying, plus two docs-only solo-orientation syncs (coord v0.37.0 #259, coord v0.41.0 #290) and one card-less `.gitignore` widening (#264) whose generalisation is card#7036.

**⚠ UPGRADING — five host actions, a SECURITY rotation the upgrade does NOT perform for you, a broad re-vendor and a re-pin of all three composite actions, every one of them in [`docs/UPGRADE.md`](UPGRADE.md) §6 `v0.30.0`. Take the rotation first (card#7500): an api base carrying `user:password@` is an accepted input, and ten error paths printed it verbatim — four into durable on-disk logs — so treat any such value already emitted as leaked and rotate it; upgrading stops the next one, it cannot unprint the last.** **[host] re-run the `PATH` symlink loop** — this release ADDS five files under `bin/` (`gh-code-search`, `gitignore-secret-family-check`, `release-tag-check`, `_shellcheck-pinned`, `_kbc-stale-blocker.py`), and a symlink tracks its target's *content*, not the *set* of tools. **[host] every board must now NAME a token file** (card#7245): the baked `~/.kanban-dev-token` default is removed at all four resolution sites and a board for which neither its own env, nor `~/.kanban-host.env`, nor the invoking environment declares `KBCARD_TOKEN_FILE` is REFUSED — unless the coord credential store's `[kanban] api_token_file` pointer names one (card#7316, the new last-resort tier). **[host] `KANBAN_EXPECTED_HOST` must be set in `~/.kanban-host.env` on every seat** (card#7245): a new preflight refuses an api host nobody declared, and it refuses when the variable is *unset* — which is the arm that catches the incident this closes, where one bad write deleted the declaration along with everything else. **[host] a DL board stood up by v0.29.0 or earlier is already broken, and this release only stops NEW ones being broken** (card#6517): `dl-a1-register-field` declared `dl_number` as a `number` field, which rejects the canonical `DL-NNNN` **string** every `--dl` writer in this toolkit sends (`422 Must be a number.`) — the in-place remedy is `kbcard [--board <name>] field retype --field dl_number --to string --restamp-dl`, and the sibling constraint is that `pr_number` / `issue_number` must stay declared `number`. **[host] `promote-released-cards` can be pointed at a real host for the first time — set `KANBAN_API_BASE`** (card#7494): `.promote.api_base` is correctly a scrubbed placeholder in a vendored repo and had no env channel, while the `KANBAN_EXPECTED_HOST` guard constraining it did, so the target and the constraint could never agree and every run died at `refusing to send token`, taking the release flow's `## Card coverage` step with it. `$KANBAN_API_BASE` now overrides the committed value — unset or empty is the committed value, byte-identical to before — and is host-validated through the same single `host_ok` call, so an env-supplied base off the expected host still refuses with nothing sent. **⚠ One narrowing beyond those:** `kb_is_uint` now refuses a LEADING ZERO, so every numeric argument these tools take (`010`, `08`, `007`) is refused where it was previously read as base 8 or died mid-guard (card#6912). **[vendor] 22 of the 26 files under `bin/` changed or are new** — `bin/_kb-board-lib.sh` is among them and carries the token/host resolution both narrowings live in, so re-vendor the lib TOGETHER with any bin you copied. **`bin/_kb-board-lib.sh`, `bin/board-card-start` and `bin/promote-released-cards` move as a SET this release** (card#7500): the new `kb_redact_url_userinfo` primitive lives in the lib and both bins call it, so a new `board-card-start` beside an old lib dies at `kb_redact_url_userinfo: command not found`. `bin/board-snapshot` changed too (card#7305 — a runtime guard that did not RUN was rendering byte-identically to one that found nothing). Only `adopt-to-dl`, `dl-a0-backfill-triaged`, `dependabot-deploy-reconcile` and `agent-board-toolkit-drift-check` are untouched. **[release-CI] ALL THREE composite actions move this release — re-pin every one.** `promote/action.yml` **CHANGED** (card#7494): it keeps `api-base` as an interface but stops rewriting `.promote.api_base` into the checked-out `.release-pr.json` with `jq`, passing the value as `$KANBAN_API_BASE` instead — a blank input is still the committed value, so an absent override is byte-identical, and the action no longer mutates a security-sensitive file in your workspace mid-job. `release-artifacts/action.yml` changes in DESCRIPTION only, but re-pin it anyway because the script it runs gained the card#6488 classification fix (a version-shaped line above the version line no longer reads as "version unchanged", which silently skipped every member's assertion at rc 0) and the card#7208 removal of a second, wider version shape. `release-tag-check/action.yml` is **new** and available to consume (card#7203, recipe in `docs/INSTALL.md` §6d).

- **card#7500** — **SECURITY: an api base carrying `user:password@` is a SUPPORTED, explicitly-accepted input, and ten error paths echoed it verbatim — four of them into a durable on-disk log.** `https://user:password@host/api/v3` passes the host guards *by design*: they judge the HOST and nothing else, and `tests/kb-host-guard-selftest.sh` has pinned `https://u:pw@<expected-host>/api/v3` as an **accept** row since the guard was written. That acceptance is correct and is **unchanged** — no verdict moves in this release, and the hostile matrix (`https://good.host@evil.example/`, the `#@` and `?@` authority splits) refuses exactly as before. The defect was at the **render**: every message that named the resolved base printed the credential with it. Six sites reach stderr, and therefore the CI log of the run that just failed; **four reach a file** — `$KB_LOG_FILE` for the paginator's three failure lines, `$KB_BCS_LOG` (`~/.cache/agent-board-toolkit/board-card-start.log`) for `board-card-start`'s two — where a password persists until something deletes it. **Every one of the ten is an ERROR path**, which is to say they fire exactly when an operator is most likely to paste the output into a ticket. Canon #20: a resolved secret must never reach an output stream, and *already emitted* means leaked, not fixable in place. **The fix is one primitive, `kb_redact_url_userinfo`, applied where the base is RESOLVED** — once per scope, into a `*_shown` / `*_SHOWN` variable that every message then prints — not a regex over finished message text, and not ten inline parses. It masks the **whole userinfo** to `***` and keeps the host, port, path, query and fragment byte-identical, because these messages exist to tell an operator *which host* was refused and one that redacted the host would be reverted by the next person to read it; a base with **no** userinfo comes back unchanged, so nothing about an ordinary refusal moves.
