It reads its authority boundary from `kb_url_host`, and that is load-bearing rather than tidy: `https://evil.example#@good.host` has **no** userinfo — the `@` is in a fragment curl discards — so a redactor cutting at the last `@` anywhere would rewrite the one hostile input the guard exists to refuse into a message naming the protected host as the destination. `promote-released-cards` carries an inline mirror on the same terms as its `host_ok` mirror (it is vendored standalone and must not source the lib), and the two copies are asserted to agree row-by-row, including on that hostile matrix. **The population was RE-DERIVED, not inherited.** The card was filed with four sites, found by grepping `$API`/`$api` — and a grep for a variable name answers a question about the variable name. Re-deriving by *use* (which identifiers reach a host guard, the RFC 3986 parser, or the paginator, plus the assignment closure over them) reproduced all four and found **six more**, including all three of the paginator's durable log lines — where the base is not the rendered value but a *prefix* of one, which is the shape a name-grep structurally cannot see — and `board-card-start`'s second durable site. **The class is now a gate, not ten fixed call sites.** `tests/url-userinfo-render-selftest.sh` re-derives that population from the shipped tree on every run and reds on any render of an unmasked base, so the eleventh message — the one added next year, in the same scope, reaching for the same `$api` — inherits the redaction instead of having to remember it. It carries a negative control (an untouched tree is clean) and four mutants that each red it, and its own header states the three things it cannot see. **Watched red in both directions**, which for a redaction is the only way to be sure: with the render sites reverted, 21 assertions across four selftests fail on the **credential value** (never on the absence of a `***`, which a tool printing both the mask and the password would satisfy); with the primitive mutated to redact the *whole* base, 45 fail on the **host** legs and the verbatim controls. **Consumer impact:** `bin/_kb-board-lib.sh`, `bin/board-card-start` and `bin/promote-released-cards` are vendored surfaces — **re-vendor them together** if you copied them; the lib and its callers move as a set here, and a new `board-card-start` beside an old lib fails at runtime with `kb_redact_url_userinfo: command not found`. No exit code, request, verdict or accepted input changes; the only observable difference is that a base carrying userinfo now reads `https://***@host/api/v3` wherever it is printed. `promote/action.yml` and `release-artifacts/action.yml` are untouched.

- **card#7494** — **`promote-released-cards` refused UNCONDITIONALLY on this repo's own committed config, and on every consumer's: the security fix that took the real host out of the repo gave the host GUARD an env channel and left the guard's TARGET config-only, so the two could never agree.** `.promote.api_base` is where `KANBAN_WRITEBACK_TOKEN` is sent, and the file naming it is PR-editable, so the value is host-validated against `$KANBAN_EXPECTED_HOST` before the token leaves the process. `KANBAN_EXPECTED_HOST` lives in the environment precisely so a PR cannot move it. But the committed `api_base` it is checked against is (correctly) a scrubbed `kanban.example.com` placeholder — the real host must not live in a vendored repo — and had **no** env channel, so an operator could supply the constraint and not the target, and every run died at `refusing to send token — api_base 'https://kanban.example.com/api/v3' is not https:// on '<host>'`. The only two ways to run the tool were to commit a real host into a vendored repo or to carry a permanent uncommitted edit to a security-sensitive file. That took the release flow's card-coverage step with it: `release-pr-body`'s `## Card coverage` section runs this tool and renders **nothing at all** when it exits non-zero, and a body with no coverage section is not a claim that coverage passed. **`$KANBAN_API_BASE` now overrides `.promote.api_base`** (unset or empty = the committed value, so an absent override is byte-identical to before). **The guard is not weakened and its posture improves:** both sources feed **one** resolution and **one** `host_ok` call — the env value is validated identically, and an env-supplied base off the expected host still refuses with nothing sent — while an api_base supplied out-of-band means a PR editing `.release-pr.json` can now move **neither** the token's destination nor the constraint on it, where before it could always move the destination. Every run names, on stderr, **which channel** the base came from — not the base itself, since `host_ok` legitimately accepts one carrying userinfo and echoing it on the happy path would print a credential into every CI run log — and a refusal names the channel beside the value it is refusing, so the fix for a refusal is unambiguous. The composite action's `api-base` input is unchanged as an interface but stops **rewriting `.promote.api_base` into the checked-out `.release-pr.json` with `jq`** — that rewrite was this defect's workaround, available only to Actions consumers (a host or non-Actions run had no way in at all), and it is removed rather than left standing beside its own fix. Three arms are asserted end-to-end over the real script with the PATCH destination observed — override absent, override valid, override **invalid and still refused** — each watched to red under a mutant, including one that skips the guard for an env-supplied base and PATCHes a card to `evil.example`. `.release-pr.json` keeps its placeholder.

- **card#7305** — **`board-snapshot`'s runtime-staleness leg rendered a guard that DID NOT RUN byte-identically to a guard that found nothing.** The leg ended `agent-board-toolkit-runtime-check --quiet 2>&1 || true`, under an in-file note reading *"the MESSAGE is the signal here, not the status"* — and that note contradicts the guard's own header, which says the opposite for a measured reason: the guard **buffers its entire report and writes it once at the end**, deliberately, because progressive output is what let a truncating reader destroy its verdict (card#6911), and its header's conclusion is *"Gate on the exit code; it is now what a truncating consumer actually receives."* Under `--quiet` **silence is the clean signal** on this leg, so a run that dies before that single write produces exactly what a current runtime produces: nothing. **Measured through the real bin, not argued** — a healthy run and a SIGKILLed one both rendered **0 bytes at an identical caller status of 0**, while a genuine `✗ STALE COPIES` finding was loud. **SIGKILL is not the only way in, and this is the part that makes it more than a corner case:** an `EXIT` trap makes the flush unconditional on every *ordinary* death, so an unbound variable or an early refusal still reports — but a signal cannot be trapped, and the guard's header already accepts on the record that *"with stdout on a full filesystem this tool exits with the right rc having printed nothing."* Both routes end in the same place: a measurement that never happened, reported as a clean one, on the surface an operator reads to decide whether their on-PATH tools are current. **The fix reads the status instead of discarding it**, and adds a line **only where the guard said nothing** — a run that already carried its own `✗`/`⚠` is neither softened nor given a redundant second line, and a current runtime stays silent, so the silent-and-dead case is the only one that gains a word. It **still never blocks**: the status is reported, never propagated, and `board-snapshot` exits 0 exactly as before, which is what SessionStart requires of it. **Watched red before it was trusted, with the discriminator asserted directly.** Against the pre-fix bin the three finding assertions red and — the one that names the defect rather than the symptom — *"no longer renders identically to the CURRENT run"* reds at `expected 'false' got 'true'`, i.e. the two renders were byte-equal. The two control arms (a current runtime adds no line; a real `STALE` finding still reaches stdout and is **not** softened into "did not complete") pass on the **pre-fix** bin as well, so neither is certifying the change that introduced it. The killed arm does not simulate the event — its stub `kill -9 $$`s itself, so the untrappable signal is real and the caller genuinely sees 0 bytes at 137. **Scope, stated so a green tick is not over-read:** this closes ONE site. It was reached by a derivation rather than a recollection — re-derive it rather than trusting a figure here: take the repo's own program names from the tree, find every site in `bin/`, `hooks/`, the workflows and the composite actions that invokes one of them, and ask of each how it classifies a non-zero status carrying **no output**. Every other reader keeps that third outcome, most of them explicitly: `board-session-close`'s advisory runner names a 124 kill separately from any other non-zero exit; `install-board-hooks` maps 129/130/137/143 onto an INDETERMINATE refusal at rc 4, with dedicated wording for the case where the probe was killed before it could report; `release-tag-check` refuses rather than assume a range is not a release; `kbcard`'s archive gate resolves an empty decision to a fail-closed token; the suite's containment sweep files a subject that did not complete as UNMEASURED rather than contained. This leg was the one site that discarded **both** channels at once — the status by `|| true`, and the report by being the same silence a healthy run produces. **Consumer impact:** `bin/board-snapshot` is a vendored surface — **re-vendor it** if you copied it. A host whose runtime is current sees **byte-identical** output; nothing else about the snapshot, its request sequence or its exit code changes. `promote/action.yml` and `release-artifacts/action.yml` are untouched.

- **card#7464** — **`dl-a1-register-field` put a fabricated `swimlane_id: 1` on its throwaway card, so the toolkit's own documented DL-board setup step could not complete on a board that has no swimlanes.** The create was refused `422 {"message":"The selected swimlane is not on this board."}`, and **no value existed that the caller could pass instead** — a board with no lanes defines no id — so neither `--swimlane` nor `KB_A1_SWIMLANE` was a way out and the tool was simply unrunnable on that board shape — which is the shape every board this toolkit is operated against takes: a live read of all three at the time of this change returns **733** cards whose `swimlane_id` is `null` and no other value (242 / 283 / 208). `docs/UPGRADE.md` §6 v0.5.0 tells an operator to run this tool **once per board that mints DLs**, so a new install following the docs met a `422` with no remedy printed and no remedy available. **The key is now OMITTED from the create body when the caller names no lane**; `--swimlane N` and `KB_A1_SWIMLANE=N` are honoured verbatim, unchanged, when either is set. **Omitted, not `null`** — key-absent is the shape `kbcard create-card` has always sent when no `--swimlane` is given, so it is the form already known to work against this API on these boards, and it is the strictly weaker request of the two. **There was nothing for a default to be derived from, which is why the fix removes the value rather than computing a better one:** the card is a throwaway the cleanup trap deletes seconds later, and its lane placement is irrelevant to the one thing step 2 measures — that the server derives the `system=dl` by-ref index from `payload.dl_number`. The `1` was not a default the tool had a basis for; it was a value it had no basis for. Two alternatives were considered and declined: **deriving a lane from the board** (mechanism, and a read, in service of a value nothing needs) and **making `--swimlane` required** (that is the same unrunnable board, with the refusal moved earlier). **Coverage —** the absence is asserted with `jq 'has("swimlane_id")'`, which is the one test that separates an **omitted** key from a **null-valued** one: `jq -r '.swimlane_id'` prints `null` for both and would have passed over either. Both arms were **watched red**. Against the pre-fix bin the new absence check reds (`expected 'false' got 'true'`) while every presence assertion beside it stays green — that green is the witness that keeps "no key" from passing because the tool stopped emitting one at all — and mutating the fixed body to never emit the key reds **6** checks across the `--swimlane`, `KB_A1_SWIMLANE` and flag-beats-env arms. **Consumer impact:** `bin/dl-a1-register-field` is a vendored surface — **re-vendor it** if you copied it. No exit code, request sequence or other body field changes, and on a board that *does* define lane 1 the only observable difference is that the throwaway now lives laneless for the few seconds it exists. `promote/action.yml` and `release-artifacts/action.yml` are untouched.

- **card#7316** — **the kanban token resolver gains a FOURTH tier: the coord credential store's `[kanban] api_token_file` POINTER, read pointer-only and discovered only when every declared tier is silent.** A host that runs both this toolkit and the coordination framework resolved the same kanban secret through two conventions that were unaware of each other, so a seat whose credential store already held the token had to keep a **second plaintext copy** of it for these tools to find one — and after card#7245 removed the baked default, such a seat is *refused* unless it hand-declares `KBCARD_TOKEN_FILE`. The resolver now reads `${COORD_CREDENTIALS:-~/.config/coord/credentials.ini}` for `[kanban] api_token_file` and uses the file it names.

  **Precedence: last, under all three declared tiers** — a board env's `KBCARD_TOKEN_FILE` > the host env's > an ambient one > **the store pointer**. An operator's own declaration must beat one a tool went looking for, or a board pinned to its own credential would silently be handed another; and the rung lives in the single `kb_declared_token_file` primitive all four resolvers already funnel through, not at four call sites that could drift apart on it. ⚠ **It is board-INDEPENDENT** — the store holds one pointer, so every board that declares nothing resolves the same file. That is the shared-credential *shape* the old default had, minus the two properties that caused the incident: nothing here invents the path, and the file it names is one the operator wrote and can rotate.

  ⛔ **POINTER-ONLY — an inline `[kanban] api_token` is REFUSED, not read**, with a message naming the pointer form (that message is the migration prompt). That store file is also the framework's first-stop diagnostic surface, so reading a value out of it would put a secret's value through toolkit code and give the inline form a second consumer at the moment the framework is removing its first. **No store value reaches any output stream**: a pointer that has the shape of a credential — the realistic mis-paste, and the case where the "path" IS the token — is refused *here*, before it can be handed back as a `KB_TOKEN_FILE` that callers' own failure messages print verbatim; refusals name the store coordinate and a non-reversible `sha256:` fingerprint instead. Two shapes where guessing would hand out a token are refused rather than resolved: a duplicated key, and a pointer containing `%%`/`%(`, whose meaning differs either side of the framework's move off `%`-interpolation.

  **No dependency on the framework's Python** — the parse is awk over the ini, so the toolkit stays standalone on a box that has none. It follows configparser's reading of the shape it cares about (`#`/`;` full-line comments, first `=`/`:` delimiter, case-sensitive section, exact-then-case-folded key) and expands a leading `~`, `$HOME` or `${HOME}` without `eval`. **A host with no store is unaffected in every observable way** — same rc, same refusal wording, no mention of a store it does not have; point `COORD_CREDENTIALS` at a non-existent path to disable the tier deliberately.

  **Seen to fail before it was trusted, and the pass found two defects in its own checks.** 12 mutants, one per new predicate, each with a pre-committed expected red set: 11 were caught, and the no-store regression assertions stayed GREEN under the mutant that deletes the rung entirely — which is what makes them a measurement of the rung's absence rather than of its presence. Mutant 1 also exposed that the new length witness read `${#KB_TOKEN}` unguarded, so under `set -u` a failing read **aborted the whole suite** and left every later case, the regression check included, unrun and reported as nothing. The twelfth mutant (deleting the `#`/`;` comment skip) **survived**, and the fixture written for it was passing for a reason unrelated to comments; the parser's comment handling turns out to be unreachable-by-input under its own key match, so the two fixtures now pin the *property* — a comment neither opens, closes nor fills a slot — with that limit stated in the file rather than implied by a green tick.

  **Docs updated in the same change**, because a resolver whose stated precedence differs from its actual predicate is a defect and not a wording nit. Prose surfaces: `README.md`, `docs/INSTALL.md` §3, `docs/HOOKS.md` and the superseded-ladder note in `docs/UPGRADE.md`. In-tree surfaces: the resolver's own header, and the ladder comment carried by every tool that names the variable or the primitive — derived from the tree rather than listed here, `grep -rln 'KBCARD_TOKEN_FILE\|kb_declared_token_file' bin/`, which answers five files against the five this change edits. Two of them also stated the tiers they had searched in their "no token declared" text, and now say the store was searched too.

  **Consumers who vendor:** re-vendor `bin/_kb-board-lib.sh` — the rung lives there, and every tool that resolves a token inherits it from that one file. Any tool you also carry changed in comment and failure-message text only; `agent-board-toolkit-drift-check` names the ones your tree actually holds. `promote/action.yml` and `release-artifacts/action.yml` are unchanged.

- **card#6517** — **`dl-a1-register-field`, the toolkit's own DL-board setup tool, declared `dl_number` as a `number` field — so the boards it stood up rejected every `--dl` write this toolkit makes, and its own verification step passed anyway.** kanban validates each payload value against its custom field's DECLARED type, per board (`CustomFieldValidator::validateValue` dispatches on `$def->type` and nothing else). Every writer of `dl_number` in this repo — `kbcard --dl`, `next-dl`, `adopt-to-dl` — stamps the canonical `DL-NNNN` **string** through one renderer (`kb_dl_canon`), and a `number`-typed field answers that with `422 {"message":"Must be a number.","errors":{"payload.dl_number":["Must be a number."]}}`. **A board stood up by any version up to v0.29.0 was therefore broken at its first real use, with nothing in the setup log saying so.** Three things kept it invisible, and all three are closed here. **One:** step 2, the only acceptance test the setup path has, wrote the sentinel with `--argjson` as a bare JSON **number** — the one value shape no toolkit writer ever produces — so it verified a board on which every production write fails. It now stamps the sentinel's canonical `DL-NNNN` form through `kb_dl_canon`, the same renderer production stamps through, which is what makes it a test of anything. **Two:** a `409`/`422` on the registration POST was reported as `idempotent OK` on the strength of the **status alone**, and that status says only that the key is taken — never the type it was taken **with**. The type is now READ BACK off the board's field index and disposed on three outcomes: `string` is the idempotent success it always claimed to be; **any other declared type is a refusal at rc 1 before a single card is created**, naming the type found and printing the in-place remedy (`kbcard [--board <name>] field retype --field dl_number --to string --restamp-dl`); and an index that carries **no** `dl_number` at all means the refusal was never "already registered", which used to be reported as success. An index that cannot be **read** is likewise rc 1 — the existing declaration's type is then unknown, and this tool does not certify a board it cannot read. **Three:** the derived `system=dl` by-ref index canonicalizes a ref by **stripping non-digits**, so it resolves from *either* type — the index looking healthy is exactly why the wrong declaration survives setup, and is not evidence that it is right. Also changed, and entailed rather than incidental: the `--sentinel` default moves `999000001` → **`999001`** and is validated by `kb_dl_num`, the same strict parser `kbcard --dl` uses, because what step 2 now puts on the wire is `kb_dl_canon`'s output and that renderer is bounded to 1..6 digits — the old 9-digit default is a value this toolkit cannot render. A sentinel outside that bound is now rc 2 **before any request** rather than a mid-run failure after the registration write; the flag correspondingly accepts a `DL-`prefixed value, as every other DL input in this toolkit does. **Docs, the same class one layer up:** `examples/kanban-board.env.example` never mentioned declared types at all and told the reader to project `{id,key}` off the `custom_fields` endpoint — the `type` that decides whether the board works was the one column it dropped; it now projects `{id,key,type}` and carries the constraint, `docs/INSTALL.md` §3b carries it where a board is actually being set up, `README.md`'s tool row states it, and the historical `docs/UPGRADE.md` v0.5.0 entry — which told operators to run this tool — carries a correction pointing here. The **sibling** constraint is stated with it, because it is why "declare everything `string`" is not the safe default: **`pr_number` / `issue_number` must be declared `number`**, since `kbcard` coerces a numeric value to a JSON number before the write and a `string`-typed field rejects that with `Must be a string.` **Measured, not argued:** the `422` was reproduced against the live API by writing the canonical `DL-0305` string into a number-typed custom field, with the control that pins it — the byte-identical request carrying a real number returns `201` — and the fixed write shape was then verified end to end on a real board, where a `string`-typed `dl_number` stored `"DL-999001"` verbatim and `by-ref?system=dl&ref=999001` resolved the card. The new refusal legs were watched to fail: with the type read-back removed, 25 checks in `tests/dl-a1-register-field-selftest.sh` go red; reverting the declared type alone reds 1; reverting the sentinel to a JSON number reds 4. **Upgrading:** a board that already declares `dl_number` as `string` reaches the same outcome it always did — the visible differences there are the wording, the sentinel value, and **one extra request**: the 409/422 path now GETs the board's custom-field index before it certifies anything. On a board that declares it otherwise the tool now **refuses where it used to report success**, which is the defect surfacing rather than a new failure; the fix is the printed `field retype`, which per that verb's contract converts the definition and every stored value in one server-side transaction, issuing no delete. **Not certified here:** that remedy command was not executed against a wrongly-typed board — provisioning a disposable board for one is `403` for a non-admin token — so it is derived from `field retype`'s documented contract and its `--to string` requirement, not from a run.
- **card#7036** — **a `.gitignore` rule for a secret-bearing file covers the NAME, not the backup FAMILY, and the per-rule fix for that omitted `*.swp` in its own first pass.** `.env` being ignored says nothing about `.env~`, `.env.bak`, `.env.save`, `.env.dpkg-old` or `#.env#`; each holds a byte-identical copy of the same secret, nothing reds, and each sits in `git status` as an ordinary untracked file among others (found live on a peer install as a 4,339-byte `.env~`, mode 600, nine days in the tree). The remaining enumeration is of AFFIXES and gitignore has no "any backup of this" concept, so that floor cannot be removed — only held level with what the real world produces, which is what shipped here. **Added `bin/gitignore-secret-family-check`**, which for every secret-bearing rule instantiates one concrete sample from the glob, checks the sample is ignored **by that rule** (the positive control — a broken instantiation would otherwise answer about a path the rule does not match), and asserts every affixed sibling is ignored too. Both halves are DERIVED: which rules are secret-bearing comes from the `.gitignore` itself — a secret-ish keyword in the rule's own text **or** in the contiguous comment block above it, so a rule whose name says nothing (`.kanban-*`) is still classified and a secret file added tomorrow is classified with no edit to the tool — and the affix table is a declared enumeration (there is nothing to derive one from) in which **every entry names the producer it was read off**: `vim`'s `backupext`, `patch(1)`'s `.orig`/`.rej`, `nano`'s `.save`/`.save.N`, `dpkg`'s `.dpkg-old`/`-dist`/`-new`, `rpm`'s `.rpmsave`, `git mergetool`'s `.BACKUP.N`, coreutils' `~`/`.~N~`, and emacs's `#<name>#` — each taken from that producer's own documentation or measured directly, never recalled. It creates NO file in the repository (`check-ignore` answers about pathnames, so the guard never plants the very file it hunts) and fails **closed**: a run deriving ZERO secret-bearing rules is a REFUSAL at rc 2, not a pass, because an empty population satisfies every assertion made over it (`--allow-empty` says so on the record instead), and an un-instantiable rule surfaces as `UNRESOLVED` rather than shrinking the population silently. `--no-index` is load-bearing and measured: `check-ignore` suppresses INDEXED paths and answers "not ignored" for them, so without it a **tracked** `.env.example` would be reported as an uncovered member. **Fixed the repo's own rules at the primitive**, not per-rule: ONE universal affix block over every file, so a secret file added above it inherits the coverage instead of needing its own siblings enumerated. Running the new check against `dev` as it stood found the block genuinely open on 17 spellings each for `*-token` and `*.env` (`.old`, `.rej`, `.dpkg-old`, `.BACKUP.N`, dated `.bak-<date>` …) and — the shape a suffix rule can never reach — the emacs WRAPPER spelling `#<name>#` open even for the already-family-complete `.env*`. ⚠ **`#*#` in a `.gitignore` is a COMMENT**: the leading hash must be backslash-escaped (`\#*#`) or the rule silently does nothing, which is how this fix needed a second pass, and the guard is what caught it. ⛔ **Read a green run for exactly what it measures — the rules in THIS repo AT THIS REF.** It says nothing about which ref an *install* runs (a tag-pinned install runs the old rules and must be asked separately, by that install), nothing about files already TRACKED (`.gitignore` has no effect on them), and the affix table is a **LOWER BOUND** on an unbounded family — `nano` writes `.save.1`, `.save.2`, … without limit and `cp .env .env.for-alice` invents a suffix on the spot. A trailing-`*` rule is the only construction covering a whole suffix family unconditionally; the tool's remediation says so rather than recommending more affixes. Over-collection by the keyword predicate errs RED (a harmless extra demand); under-collection errs GREEN and is the one blind spot with no red, stated in the tool's header. Exposure NIL: nothing secret-bearing is tracked in this repo and nothing ever was — `git log --all --diff-filter=A` over the derived patterns returns only the two placeholder `examples/*.env.example` templates.

- **card#7207** — **CI's own syntax gate was re-minting `bin/__pycache__` on every local gate run, defeating the fix card#6871 shipped — and the one-line fix first proposed for it would have LOOSENED what the gate proves.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s "python shims compile clean" step ran `python3 -m py_compile bin/*.py`. `py_compile` **writes** the bytecode it produces, beside each source, i.e. into `bin/__pycache__` — the exact non-tool directory card#6871 removed, in the directory `docs/INSTALL.md` §2 symlinks onto PATH entry by entry. Measured on the reference host before the fix: the directory was **already present**, and re-minted at rc 0. **Every instrument the repo had read green over it** — a GitHub runner's checkout is disposable so the artifact costs nothing there, and `.gitignore` keeps it out of `git status`. The damage is **local-only**, on the seat that runs these gates by hand before opening a PR, which is the documented workflow here, and it surfaces later as an unrelated-looking install refusal (card#7234's `RESULT=NOT_INSTALLED SHAPE=none rc=1`).

  **⛔ `ast.parse` — the fix the card prescribed — is NOT what shipped, because it is a weaker question.** It writes nothing, which is why it was proposed, but measured on python 3.12.3 across the ten error classes `py_compile` rejects, `ast.parse` **ACCEPTS eight of them**: `return`, `break`, `continue`, `yield` and `await` outside their construct, `def f(a, a)`, an unbound `nonlocal`, and `*a = [1]`. Only the two genuine parse errors (`def f(:` and `1 = 2`) survive it. Adopting it would have traded a real gate for a quieter artifact — a loosening to make a failure go away. What shipped is **`compile(src, f, 'exec')`**: `py_compile`'s own check MINUS the write. It rejects all ten, takes **bytes** so a PEP 263 coding cookie is handled exactly as `py_compile` handled it (a decoded `str` would false-RED a valid latin-1 file), reports **every** bad file rather than dying at the first, and caches nothing anywhere. `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1` and `python3 -B` were also rejected: both suppress the symptom at one call site and leave the next `py_compile` caller to re-mint it.

  **The population was derived empirically, not grepped.** `py_compile`/`compileall` appears seven times in this tree and **six are prose**; a token sweep alone would have answered a question about the token. So every candidate invocation across `.github/`, `bin/` and the composite actions was instead RUN with `bin/__pycache__` cleared before each and a repo-wide `find … -name __pycache__` census after, with `python3 -m py_compile` as the control proving the census discriminates. **Exactly one site writes into a repo-owned directory** — the ci.yml step. The two `python3 - <<PY` wiring heredocs write nothing, and all five `bin/*.py` entry points still write nothing, so card#6871's guard was re-measured rather than inherited. **N=1, so there is no class item owed** — but the reason this ONE escaped is worth recording: card#6871's audit derived "every `python3` invocation site in `bin/` + `hooks/`", and `.github/workflows/` was outside that denominator. The site was not missed; it was never in the population.

  **New `tests/python-syntax-gate-selftest.sh`, and it runs the shipped block VERBATIM.** The gate command is extracted from `ci.yml` by step name and executed against planted fixtures in a scratch `bin/` — a copy of the command in the test would be a second thing that can disagree with the one CI runs. Leg 1 holds a no-`py_compile`/`compileall` rule over the whole executable surface (every `*.yml` AND `*.yaml` in `.github/workflows/`, plus every `action.yml`/`action.yaml` at any depth, re-derived each run, every `run:` value collected wherever it sits), so the artifact cannot come back by another hand — fixing the site without that rule is what let card#6871 be defeated in the first place. Legs 2–6 assert the gate accepts a valid tree, **rejects a bare syntax error**, **rejects a compile-stage error** (the leg that reds if anyone ever swaps in `ast.parse`), **reports a file it cannot even tokenize without ending the run** (leg 4, below), and mints **no** `__pycache__` across any of it. Every absence assertion carries a control: leg 1's planted dirty/clean fixtures, and leg 6's `py_compile` run over the same fixture tree that must plant the directory. `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1` — which `_selftest-prelude.sh` exports suite-wide — is cleared per probe, or the control would be suppressed too and the file would certify a property of the environment.

  **Seen to fail before it was trusted.** Reverting the step to `py_compile` reds **two** legs (the population rule and the empirical no-mint census); swapping `compile()` for `ast.parse` reds **exactly one** — the compile-stage leg, and nothing else; renaming the step exits loud at the extraction rather than as four mysterious rc failures. The headline measurement, directory removed between runs: old gate `ABSENT → PRESENT (5 .pyc)`, new gate `ABSENT → ABSENT`, both at rc 0. The extractor itself was caught by its own control — it joined records with `\0` instead of terminating them, and `read -r -d ''` drops an unterminated final record, which for a single-`run:`-block file is the whole file.

  **Six stale claims fixed in the same change**, found by sweeping the artifact and the concept as well as the token (`py_compile|compileall`, `__pycache__|\.pyc`, `bytecode`): `.gitignore`, `tests/_bin-set-lib.sh`, `tests/_shipped-shell-lib.sh` and `tests/lib-set-derivation-selftest.sh` each asserted that CI's `py_compile` step still mints the directory, and `tests/path-link-recipe-selftest.sh` plus **card#7170's own `[Unreleased]` entry below** each carried a **stale forward reference** to this card as future work — the second one found only by the ARTIFACT predicate, since it names neither token and sits two entries under card#7207's own, telling a reader the work is still pending. Each now says CI no longer mints it and that a HAND-RUN still can — which is why every defensive measure those files describe (`grep -d skip`, the `-type f` exclusion, the by-NAME rule) is kept rather than relaxed. `docs/INSTALL.md` §2's `__pycache__` sentence and `tests/readme-bin-coverage-selftest.sh`'s claim were both checked and **left** — the first says such a directory is what *has really happened* in `bin/` and is why the recipe's `[ -f ]` guard exists, which a closed CI producer does not make untrue while a hand-run still mints one; the second: it says the directory is present only where something ran `py_compile`, which this change makes more true, not less. card#6871's own `[Unreleased]` entry was audited and **not edited** — it is scoped to entry points and load sites and asserts nothing about CI.

  **Review round — the new gate's STATED population was not its ACTUAL predicate, and the fix is ONE owner rather than a third correction.** Leg 1 globbed `.github/workflows/*.yml` only. GitHub Actions loads `.yaml` on identical terms, so a planted `.github/workflows/sneak.yaml` running `python3 -m py_compile bin/*.py` passed the gate at `all checks passed` while the byte-identical file renamed `.yml` reds it — an absence verdict over a population smaller than the file's own header claimed, and a nested `action.yml` was outside it too. The two sibling gates that ask the same question (`ci-matrix-parity-selftest.sh`, `shellcheck-pin-selftest.sh`) already read **both** spellings, so correcting the third copy would have left three implementations of one rule with two predicates. **New `tests/_gha-surface-lib.sh` owns the derivation** — both workflow extensions, and `action.yml`/`action.yaml` at any depth with `.git` pruned — and all three gates call it; the directory stays a **parameter**, which is what lets each gate point the same derivation at its own fixture tree. Adoption is behaviour-preserving **by measurement, not by argument**: all nine of `ci-matrix-parity`'s projections plus both siblings' full assertion streams are byte-identical before and after. Watched red: the planted `.yaml` and a nested `.github/actions/probe/action.yml` each red leg 1's live assertion, and narrowing the shared owner back to `*.yml` reds leg 1's derivation control (and, measured, **leaves both siblings green** — one owner, one guard, recorded in the lib's header).

  **Review round — the failing path had two defects that a green `rc 1` hid.** A source containing a NUL byte is rejected by a class that MOVED under the gate — CPython gh-96670, *"the parser now raises SyntaxError when parsing source code containing null bytes"*, quoted from the NEWS file shipped with the 3.12.3 interpreter this was measured on — so a one-class `except` is a bet on the runner's interpreter version. **Measured** on 3.12.3: `SyntaxError` with `lineno` **None**, so `except SyntaxError` printing `line={e.lineno}` annotated a location that does not exist. On an interpreter raising the other class (documented as `ValueError`; **not verifiable on this box**, which has only 3.12) it escapes as an uncaught traceback, which **aborts the loop and leaves every later file unchecked** at an exit status that still reads 1. The step now catches both classes and omits the `line=` field when there is none. New leg 4 asserts the three OBSERVABLE properties — reported, later files still checked, no phantom location — rather than the interpreter's classification, which is the part that moves; its `ValueError` arm is unreachable by any input on 3.12 and is therefore **driven by a `sitecustomize` stub on PYTHONPATH**, with the stub's own message as the positive control that a real `ValueError` really entered the arm, and a following probe proving the stub is not global. Five mutations of the shipped block were watched red across the eleven new assertions (one-class `except`, `except ValueError` only, a `break` in the loop, a dropped `rc = 1`, and a fixture planted without its NUL byte).

  ⛔ **Bounds.** Leg 1 keys on the two stdlib tokens; it cannot see a bytecode write reached some other way (a `run:` block exec'ing a script that imports a repo module, or a third-party `uses:`). YAML comments are invisible to it by design — pyyaml drops them, and `ci.yml`'s own step comment discusses `py_compile` at length. And this closes the PRODUCER only: **card#7170**, the consumer-side `[ -f "$t" ] || continue` on the install recipe, is the guard that holds when some other non-file entry appears in `bin/`, and neither subsumes the other.

- **card#7303** — **the changelog gate's duplicate-entry leg said "in the region" and meant it on ONE half: a bullet removed from a FROZEN released section could cancel out a real duplicate standing in `[Unreleased]`, and the check reported nothing.** `_stale_dupes` in `tests/changelog-card-entry-selftest.sh` reds a card only when BOTH conditions hold — the PR's NET bullet contribution for that card is ≥ 2, and two of its bullets are still standing in the region. The presence half came from `_bullets`, which is region-scoped. The net half summed `_added_bullets`, which is scoped to the **path** `docs/CHANGELOG.md` and carried no region filter at all, so the two halves of one stated scope were two different sets.

  **It errs GREEN, and the direction was reported backwards before it was measured.** The card#7293 review recorded this as over-reporting (erring red, harmless); it is the opposite, and the construction is what settles it rather than the argument. Built against the shipped functions and a real git history: ONE commit that adds two `card#4000` bullets to `[Unreleased]` **and** deletes two `card#4000` bullets from the frozen `## [0.23.1]` section leaves two contradictory wordings standing for one card from one PR — and `_stale_dupes` answered **nothing**, because the path-scoped net was `2 − 2 = 0`. The same commit with the released-section deletions removed reds `card#4000`, so the out-of-region removals are the whole difference. ⛔ **A suppressed red is not a weaker guard; on the axis where it errs it is a decoration** — which is why this shipped as its own card rather than folded into the region work that surfaced it. The reachability is live, not hypothetical: **`6f7b4af` (card#7293) is itself an instance of the arithmetic** — it removed a `card#7227` bullet standing at **line 1** of the parent revision, eight lines above that revision's `## [Unreleased]`, i.e. outside the region, and that removal was subtracting from the same card's net. Harmless there because only one bullet survived; the live leg's own counter moved from `2 removed` to `1 removed` when the filter landed.

  **The STATED scope was right and the PREDICATE was wrong, so the predicate is what moved** — the alternative (restate the scope as "the path") would have kept a check that cannot see the duplicate it exists to name. **The two signs are filtered in different places, and that is the design rather than an inconsistency: each half is scoped where the evidence for it lives.** An ADD can be judged against the file as it stands — the bullet is either standing in the region or it is not — which is the presence test `_stale_dupes` already ran, so an add now counts toward net only when that same test found it, deleting the second copy of the test instead of adding one, and making `net ≥ present` **structurally**. A REMOVAL cannot be judged that way at all: the line is GONE from the file, so the only witness to where it stood is the revision it was removed from. `_added_bullets` therefore takes the release version, reads the parent revision through a new `_bullets_at` — **the same `_region`/`_bullets` the live leg uses, deliberately not a third carve of "the unreleased region" (`card#7304` tracks the two that already exist; a third would BE that card's defect)** — and emits a removal only where that revision proves the line stood in the region. **THREE arms drop a removal, and the third is a GUARD rather than an absence** (review round): the path is absent at that revision; the revision carries no `## [Unreleased]`, so the region is empty; or the revision carries `## [Unreleased]` but **not the stop header `## [<version>]`** — and that third one is the arm the first draft neither enumerated nor guarded. `_region`'s ceiling is an `index(…) == 1 { exit }` that simply never fires on such a revision, so the carve runs `[Unreleased]` → **EOF**, swallows every frozen released section, and a removal from one of them reads as in-region and subtracts — **card#7303's own defect, re-minted one layer down**, reproduced end to end on a branch whose parent predates the release. The live leg refuses that state outright (it is the load-bearing hard exit in the preconditions block); `_bullets_at` is handed arbitrary historical revisions and cannot refuse, so it gates on the same `_has_version_header` and drops the revision. **With that gate in place** a dropped removal can only leave net HIGHER, so this side can only over-report — the claim is what the guard establishes, not more.

  **The same defect also erred RED on a shape the filed report ruled out**, found by constructing the other direction rather than reasoning about it: *"out-of-region adds cannot change the verdict"* holds only when the PR removes nothing. A PR that rewords a standing entry (`+1/−1`), files its own bullet (`+1`) and backfills one into a released section (`+1`) reached `net = 2` with two survivors and reported a **false** `card#7038` — the legitimate two-PRs-on-one-card shape this leg was built to let through. Gating adds on presence closes that arm too; measured green after, against a control that was already green.

  **Watched failing on the case it passes today — one mutant per HALF, because a mutant that flips both predicates cannot show which of them a fixture is witnessing.** Three, each restored byte-identically: both halves reverted to the pre-card#7303 predicates reds **5**; the **stop-header gate alone** removed from `_bullets_at` reds **4**, including `expected '' got '- **card#4000** — a released wording, frozen.'` and the end-to-end `expected 'card#4000' got ''`; and the **add-gating alone** reverted to its `else` reds **1**, the false-RED fixture. That last one is the round's finding as much as its fix: before that fixture existed the single-half revert left the suite **46/46 green**, so the whole "exactly 2 red" result was attributable to the other half and the add-gating had no witness at all — a check that cannot fail is a decoration, which is this file's own standard. The MIRROR CONTROL beside the defect fixtures stays green under all three. That control is the point of the pair: two adds, two removes, one PR, differing from the defect fixture only in WHERE the removed bullets stood, so *"stop counting removals"* would satisfy the fixture and red the control. `_bullets_at`'s own arms are unit-fixtured — a revision carrying the region reads back its bullets (positive control), a path absent at the revision answers nothing, and a revision with no `## [Unreleased]` answers nothing **while still carrying the bullets** (the witness that separates "the region says nothing" from "the file is empty"). **The live leg's own counter line is relabelled in the same change**, because its predicate narrowed with everything else: `%s removed` now counts removals **from the region**, and the third figure counts **PRs with a surviving record** rather than "contributors" — a PR whose only contribution to this file was an out-of-region removal now has no record and is not among them (measured on a two-PR fixture: `299 300` → `299`). A counter whose label is wider than its predicate is this card's own defect shape, and this counter is cited as evidence. ⛔ **Residual, deliberate and unchanged in kind:** a PR that removes two IN-region bullets for one card and files two of its own still nets zero and stays green — the replace-an-existing-entry case at multiplicity two, the same trade the net rule already makes at multiplicity one.

- **card#7203** — **`release-tag-check` gets a distribution surface AND stops hardcoding a `v` prefix: a third composite action, whose tag name is derived from `.release-pr.json`'s `tag_format` by the one tool that reads that key.** The two ride together because the action is what would have shipped the hardcode outward. **The surface.** The tool has shipped since card#6579 and this repo runs it from its own tree (`.github/workflows/release-promote-cards.yml`), but the toolkit shipped `promote/action.yml` and `release-artifacts/action.yml` and **nothing for this one** — so a consumer that vendors no toolkit bin and consumes everything as SHA-pinned actions could adopt the untagged-release gate only by vendoring `release-tag-check` **and** the `release-artifacts-check` it delegates its release classification to, into each repo. `release-tag-check/action.yml` is built on the two existing wrappers' terms: thin, all logic in the script, inputs passed through the step `env:` and never interpolated into the `run:` body. **The knobs whose defaults are ARGUED in the tool (`timeout`, `interval`, `read-timeout`, `remote`) are optional and blank-by-default, translated to an ABSENT flag** — restating `300`/`10`/`30` in the wrapper would mint a second copy of a cost decision that a change to the script could not move, and the script rejects `--flag ""` anyway; `config` is the deliberate exception, on `release-artifacts/action.yml`'s terms (it has a meaningful default, so a blank one is a wiring error rather than "use the default"). **Exercised, not asserted:** the `run:` body was extracted from the YAML and executed against a real bare remote through five arms — blank optionals (non-release, rc 0), an untagged release refused at a `timeout` the input carried through (rc 1), the same push passing once the tag exists, a non-blank `remote` reaching the script (proved by the refusal naming that path), and a blank required input failing closed at rc 2 — and CI invokes the composite on the runner in **both** directions, the second one asserting the wrapper does not swallow the script's refusal. **The third action did NOT get a third copy of the per-action wiring assertions.** `ci.yml` carried that block inline in each of the two existing `*-action-selftest` jobs and they had already drifted (only one asserted the no-`${{ }}`-in-`run:` rule); the shared properties now live in `tests/composite-action-wiring-selftest.sh`, whose population is **every `action.yml` OR `action.yaml` in the tree** (both spellings, because GitHub resolves an action directory by either — a walk keyed on one answers about that filename, not about the actions this repo ships) — so a fourth action is covered by code nobody has to edit. **Each of its eight finding codes has a mutant, and each mutant reds an assertion no other mutant reds** — including `NO-STEPS`, which has TWO emission sites and whose two fixtures are therefore discriminated on the finding's DETAIL column rather than its code. Three further fixtures cover what a code alone cannot: that second site, an input whose NAME PREFIXES a used one, and the `action.yaml` spelling. The first cut claimed "one mutant per code" over six fixtures for eight codes, with `NO-STEPS` and `UNPARSEABLE` unproven and the latter missing from the `CODES:` list as well: two brand-new predicates that nothing had watched fail, which is the decoration shape the file's own header exists to forbid. The two existing actions were measured clean against it rather than assumed to be. Residual, stated rather than left to be found: those two jobs still carry their own copies of the shared assertions, which this card does not strip. **The defect the surface would have shipped.** `TAG="v${VERSION}"` was correct for the default scheme and a **hard refusal of every release** for any other one: the tool polled for a tag that cannot exist, waited out its full 300 s bound and exited 1 — the refuse-every-release outcome the wait was designed to avoid, delivered to exactly the consumers `docs/INSTALL.md` §4 tells to set the key (.NET `Major.Minor.Build.Revision`, date-string tags, any `release-{{version}}` scheme). Measured on the pre-change binary, not argued: with `tag_format: release-{{version}}` and `release-0.28.0` present at the pushed commit it refused, and with only `v0.28.0` present it **passed** — a false "shipped" on a release the config says is untagged. **The mapping was NOT copied a second time.** `bin/release-pr-body` has owned `tag_format` since card#4761, so it gains a `--tag` query mode that prints the tag name and returns **before** the baseline block — no fetch, so it answers with the remote unreachable, which is the state its caller exists to survive — and `release-tag-check` asks that sibling, exactly as it already asks `release-artifacts-check --classify-only` for the release classification. It passes the version the CLASSIFIER chose (not a re-read of the version file, which could name a different one under the card#6488 arm), and **fails closed** if the sibling is missing rather than falling back to a `v` prefix — the fallback would be the hardcode re-minted at the one moment the real answer was unavailable. **The three-way query mode check is counted, not pairwise:** `--manifest`/`--card-manifest` were compared as a pair, and a third mode makes three pairs, so the combination nobody wrote down is the one that silently drops an answer. **Fire-first:** 11 new assertions in `tests/release-tag-check-selftest.sh` and 11 in `tests/release-pr-body-selftest.sh` were watched RED on the pre-change bins, against controls that stayed GREEN on both — the absent key and the key set to its own explicit default `v{{version}}`, since a fixture setting a key to its default cannot tell "the key was read" from "the default fired". A missing-sibling refusal is driven for real (a `bin/` holding the tool and its classifier but not the tag reader), and the delegation is asserted on the tool's CODE with comments stripped — the first cut of that assertion read `true` off the word "jq" inside a comment. **Docs:** `docs/INSTALL.md` gains **§6d** (the recipe, the ordering rule, the `timeout-minutes` sizing, and what each of the three refusals means), §4 says `tag_format` names the tag in both tools that act on one, and §6b's *"`promote-released-cards`, `release-pr-body` and `release-artifacts-check` are standalone and need no lib"* — an enumeration that never listed `release-tag-check` — is replaced by the sibling-EXEC rule it was missing, with a recipe that derives on the PROPERTY (see the review round below); `README.md` gains a row; and two stale enumerations in test headers are deleted rather than re-counted (`readme-bin-coverage-selftest.sh` said "today exactly one, `promote/action.yml`" while the table already carried two, and `ci-matrix-parity-selftest.sh` claimed "no step in these workflows carries an `if:` today", which card#6579 falsified when it landed the tag gate's own). **⚠ Consumer-visible:** a repo that sets `tag_format` and consumes §6d gets the tag its scheme produces; a repo that does not set it is byte-identical. **A pre-PR review round then closed six findings on this branch.** (1) **The bound-reached refusal named a cause that the delegation had just made sometimes FALSE** — *"The release merged and was NOT tagged — check the auto-tag-version workflow run"* — because with `tag_format` and the tagger disagreeing the release IS tagged, under a name this gate was not waiting for, and the operator was sent to a run that succeeded. This is the sibling of a defect this same file fixed once on its UNREADABLE branch (card#6579: **a refusal names its own cause, or claims nothing**), left on the other branch of one shape. The measured-absent path now takes a **second look without the tag-name filter** and reports which of three worlds it measured — no tag of any name at the commit (the untagged release, and it says the alternative was excluded by measurement), some OTHER tag at it (named, with `tag_format`-vs-your-tagger as the candidate cause), or a second look that could not read the remote either, in which case the **cause** is reported as unmeasured rather than guessed. Reproduced before and after against a real bare remote, and all three worlds are driven in the selftest — the third by an `ext::` helper that serves the poll and refuses the next read, so the failure is deterministic rather than a timing race. (2) **`tag_format` became a gate predicate carrying an unstated obligation:** a config key produces no tags, and nothing in the toolkit constrains the workflow that does — so §4, §6d and `release-tag-check/action.yml`'s precondition (2) now each say that the key tells the gate what to EXPECT and must be set together with the tagger, since a mismatch costs every release refused. (3) §6b's *"which siblings a bin needs is derived"* recipe **matched two variable names rather than the property** — re-run against this tree it returns only the two bins spelling their own directory `$SELF_DIR`/`$d`, a documentation filename as a spurious third, and a `grep: bin/__pycache__: Is a directory`, while `adopt-to-dl`, `dependabot-deploy-reconcile` and `board-session-close` (which exec `next-dl`/`kbcard`, `_dependabot-reconcile.py`, and `install-board-hooks` plus its advisory-leg helpers out of their own directory) appear nowhere; it now tests the property (`dirname` of `$0`/`BASH_SOURCE`/`__file__`) and intersects the file's non-comment text with the actual contents of `bin/`, which collects every member of the review's five-bin control table (`release-tag-check`, `release-pr-body`, `adopt-to-dl`, `dependabot-deploy-reconcile`, `board-session-close`), emits no `Is a directory` noise, and can only ever name a file you can copy — with its weakest properties stated. (4) The `DEAD-INPUT` rule was a **substring** test, so an input whose name prefixes a used one was a silent false negative (no collision today; the fixture that proves it is new). (5) The tag reader's `-x` guard is **hoisted next to the classifier's**, so a mis-vendored `bin/` refuses on the first ordinary push instead of running green until the first real release — the highest-cost moment to discover it. (6) Two `docs/CONSOLIDATION-PLAN.md` weakest-property notes and one in this file said *"the two composite actions"* while a third was landing: **de-enumerated** to point at the selftest's derived population rather than re-counted, and the claim (not just the file) was swept across the tree with a positive control — the remaining hits are released entries, accurate for the release they describe. **A second review round then closed two more, both in the first round's own verification rather than in the code it changed.** (7) The `NO-STEPS` mutant **witnessed nothing**: the code has two emission sites and the assertion read only the finding's CODE, so deleting the empty-steps predicate left the other site catching the fixture and the block still passed — measured on both mutations of that predicate. The assertions now read the DETAIL column (the pattern the file already used for `prefix-input`), and each mutation reds only its own. The harness blindness underneath it is closed too: the file asserts its own **leg count**, because `_summary` reports failures and a battery that silently lost half its assertions has none — deleting one `eq` line dropped a check and it still printed `all checks passed`. (8) The tag-elsewhere refusal **asserted the config carries `tag_format`** when the unset key is the normal case (the default is applied by the sibling that reads the file, not written in the file), so the operator was told to check a key they would not find; both that branch and its unreadable-second-look sibling now name the tag, the config, and the fact that the name is *either* the key's answer or the `v{{version}}` default. The combination that let it through — **key absent × another tag present** — is now a fixture. Also here: **a `tag_format` carrying whitespace or a control character is refused at rc 2** rather than polled for — a newline in it put an attacker-chosen `::error::` line into the promote job's log, and no such git ref can exist anyway (the remote side needs no guard: `git check-ref-format` already forbids those characters); the worst-case runtime the caller sizes its `timeout-minutes` backstop from was **wrong in all three places it was written** once a second bounded read landed (`timeout` + **2×**`read-timeout` + classification), and now lives in the tool's header alone, with the promote workflow, `release-tag-check/action.yml` and §6d pointing at it; and the tool's file header no longer claims all three refusal branches name a measured cause — the tag-elsewhere branch offers two candidates it has not told apart, and says so.
- **card#7245** — **⚠ TWO NARROWINGS, both fail-closed, both requiring an operator action: the api base a tool resolves must name a host somebody DECLARED, and the token file must be DECLARED too — there is no `~/.kanban-dev-token` default any more.** An E2E harness (`tests/_kb-api-stub.sh`) overwrote the reference host's live credential config at 00:42 on 2026-08-22 — `~/.kanban-host.env` replaced wholesale with a single `export KBCARD_API="https://kanban.test/api/v3"`, `~/.kanban-dev-token` (which held a prod-capable bearer) overwritten with the literal `stub-token`, and a stray `~/.kanban-e2e-board.env` left behind — and every board tool on the box failed until the operator restored it by hand, first with `Could not resolve host: kanban.test` and then, for twenty minutes, `HTTP 401 Unauthenticated`. **The file was the symptom; three structural facts were the defect,** and each has its own fix here.

  **1. `~/.kanban-dev-token` was the DEFAULT of a fallback chain and nothing pinned away from it.** Measured on the reference host: **not one** of the four board envs set `KBCARD_TOKEN_FILE`, so `toolkit`, `bridge`, `dev` and the harness's own board all resolved to the same file — and the only other place a pointer could live was the host env, which the same write replaced. So one write to one unpinned default took every board out at once. The trailing `:-$HOME/.kanban-dev-token` is **removed**, at all four sites that carried it, through one new `kb_declared_token_file` primitive rather than four edits: a board for which neither its own env, nor `~/.kanban-host.env`, nor the invoking environment declares a token file is now **refused**, naming the file to edit and the exact line to add. `kb_resolve_env` returns a new **rc 7**; `board-snapshot` reports it per board and keeps rendering the others; `board-stats` puts it on that board's failure list; `board-card-start` skips the move and stays fail-soft. The ambient tier is deliberately **kept** — `KBCARD_TOKEN_FILE=… kbcard …` is an explicit declaration by the invoker, where the baked default was a declaration by nobody. ⚠ **Action: every board env (or the host env) must now name a token file.** The name was also a trap worth recording: `dev` in `~/.kanban-dev-token` names the **agent**, not an environment — it held a prod-capable token.

  **2. An unrecognised api host was reachable at all.** `KANBAN_EXPECTED_HOST` already existed and was already fail-closed, but only two token-sending guards read it, both judging the *committed* `.release-pr.json` `api_base`. Nothing judged the `KBCARD_API` the tools actually resolve from `~/.kanban-host.env`. A new `kb_require_known_api_host` — same variable, same fail-closed-when-unset regime, same RFC 3986 authority parser — runs as a **preflight, before the token file is even located**: `kb_resolve_env` returns a new **rc 6** (so `kbcard`, `dl-a0-backfill-triaged`, `dl-a1-register-field` and `adopt-to-dl` exit 2 and `next-dl` skips its board check), `board-stats` exits 1, and `board-snapshot` prints a `SKIPPED` line and still exits 0. **Its predicate is the HOST alone** — not the scheme, unlike its sibling — because it judges the operator's own host env, where an `http://127.0.0.1` board is a legitimate install; a scheme rule there would refuse those with a message about a host. **It refuses when nothing is declared, and that is the arm that catches this incident:** the 00:42 write deleted the `KANBAN_EXPECTED_HOST` line along with everything else, so a guard keyed on *declared-and-mismatched* would have been silent through the one write it exists to catch. ⚠ **Action: `KANBAN_EXPECTED_HOST` must be set in `~/.kanban-host.env` on every seat** — it was already documented as required there, but only for the checkout hook; it is now required for every tool. The two guards' shared authority parser is extracted as `kb_url_host` at this second caller, so the hostile-URL history lives in one function; `promote-released-cards`' vendored standalone mirror is unchanged and `tests/kb-host-guard-selftest.sh` still asserts every case against both copies.

  **3. The harness wrote shared paths when a per-invocation override was already available.** `$KANBAN_HOST_ENV` has always been honored by the lib; `kb_stub_board_config` did not use it, and its only guard was `: "${HOME:?…}"` — which fires when HOME is *unset*, i.e. it was satisfied by precisely the value that did the damage. It now writes its host env to its own scratch path and exports `KANBAN_HOST_ENV` at it, declares `KBCARD_TOKEN_FILE` and `KANBAN_EXPECTED_HOST` inside that file (so the harness satisfies both new refusals by declaration rather than by exemption), and refuses outright — `exit 1`, not `return`, because most selftests run without `set -e` — unless `HOME` is the scratch dir `_mktmp_scratch --home` created. New `tests/e2e-harness-containment-selftest.sh` drives it with a planted decoy home and asserts the decoys are **byte-identical** after the refusal, each absence paired with a presence witness. The harness's api host is now a **declaration** (`KB_STUB_HOST`) that `KB_STUB_API` is built from, so no second copy of the URL parser lives in the test tree.

  **4. FOLLOW-UP ROUND — the two new guards were correct about the wrong things, and the checks around them were narrower than the properties they were trusted for.** Six fixes, each measured before and after. **(a) The authority parser could not read an IPv6 board.** `kb_url_host` stripped the port from the FIRST `:`, and an IP-literal is nothing but colons, so `https://[::1]:8080/api` parsed as `[` — and the refusal then told the operator to `set KANBAN_EXPECTED_HOST="["`, which matches EVERY bracketed authority: the guard's own remediation text minted the bypass. The authority now ends at the first `]` (where curl ends it), a single trailing dot is stripped on both the parsed and the DECLARED side (`h.` is the same DNS name; `h..` is not and stays refused), and the **vendored `host_ok` mirror in `promote-released-cards` carries all of it** — `tests/kb-host-guard-selftest.sh` asserts both copies agree on every row, and caught them diverging twice, once on an `EXPECT_HOST` of `"."` normalising to empty and turning the subdomain arm into a `*"."` wildcard. **(b) `board-snapshot`'s SKIPPED line pointed at a stream its consumers discard.** It said `(see stderr)` while `board-session-close` runs `board-snapshot 2>/dev/null` and the SessionStart hook surfaces stdout alone — an operator told a board was skipped and given nothing to act on. The variable, the file and the refused host are now ON the line; the `exit 0` is unchanged, because no consumer gates on the rc. **(c) The containment check's DENOMINATOR was a sub-population.** `tests/e2e-harness-containment-selftest.sh` declares "every path `kb_stub_board_config` opens"; the property it is trusted for is "the suite writes nothing outside its scratch". Two live members were escaping while it was green — `kbcard-field-selftest` and `locale-range-guard-selftest` both source `bin/kbcard`, which resolves `KB_LOG_FILE` from `$HOME` at **source time**, appending fabricated HTTP-422/413/500 records to the operator's real `~/.kbcard-failures.log` (measured: 20 records, 8746 bytes, per run of the first alone). ⛔ **The obvious derivation, `grep '$HOME/' bin/ tests/`, scores ZERO on both** — neither file mentions `$HOME`; the path is built inside the bin they source. So the new `tests/suite-home-containment-selftest.sh` measures the property EMPIRICALLY: every `tests/*selftest*.sh`, re-globbed each run, executed under a sacrificial `HOME`, with the leftovers in that HOME as the observable. A nonzero rc is reported as **UNMEASURED** rather than counted clean, and four controls — a deliberate leaker, a scratch-only writer, an aborting script, and one that leaks *and* aborts (filed as a leak, never softened) — prove the instrument can say all three words; the controls run through the **same** verdict function the population does, so they certify the check that actually runs rather than a parallel copy. **It is its own CI job, not a `selftest` matrix entry, for the same two reasons `ci-matrix-parity` is:** it would otherwise be handed itself and recurse, and it needs **`fetch-depth: 0`**, which the matrix's single shared depth-1 checkout cannot give it. That second reason is not hypothetical — it is what the instrument caught on its first CI run: `changelog-card-entry-selftest` derives its obligations from `git log <last release tag>..HEAD` and deliberately **refuses** (rc 1) when no tag resolves rather than passing blindly on a truncated history, so under depth 1 it never ran and its containment was never measured. Fixed in the **population** (give the subject the history it needs) rather than in the verdict; measured both ways, one variable: shallow ⇒ rc 1 with the subject's own *"Check out with fetch-depth: 0"*, full history ⇒ rc 0. **There is no exclusion list** — the only name skipped is the file itself, and that it is the *only* skip is asserted, so no future subject can fall into an exemption unnoticed. **(d) The new rc contract was published stale in three surfaces.** `kb_resolve_env` grew rcs 6 and 7; `bin/next-dl`'s header, its runtime message and `README.md` all still named 3/4/5 — measured, a host-mismatch run printed a **five-cause enumeration containing none of the actual cause** and then `rc=6`, and under `--require-counter` that list was the ONLY thing printed before a refusal ending "Fix the cause above". The two prose copies are **deleted** in favour of the contract's one owner, and the runtime line now **relays** `resolve_board_cfg`'s own reason instead of restating a contract. **(e) `board-snapshot`'s baked `https://YOUR-KANBAN-HOST/api/v3` fallback is gone.** That placeholder is the literal `KBCARD_API` in `examples/kanban-host.env.example`, whose `KANBAN_EXPECTED_HOST` is `YOUR-KANBAN-HOST` — so the preflight PASSED on a box nobody had configured, a guard satisfied by a placeholder matching itself. Unset is now a fail-soft SKIPPED line naming the file to create. **(f) Three selftest assertions could not fail** — each asserted a variable was empty right after something had unset it, so they passed against a function that never ran; one was additionally routed through a `$( )` subshell that made a leak unobservable in principle. All three now seed a sentinel only the code under test can remove, and each was confirmed to red under a targeted mutation.

  **Docs updated in the same change:** `README.md`, `docs/INSTALL.md` §3/§3b and its worked example (which never set up the host env at all), `docs/HOOKS.md`, `ADOPTION.md`, both `examples/*.env.example`, and stale one-line claims in `bin/kbcard`'s and `bin/board-snapshot`'s own headers. One stale pointer fixed in passing: `kb_require_https_host`'s header named `tests/kb-board-lib-selftest.sh` as the home of the hostile-URL matrix, which lives in `tests/kb-host-guard-selftest.sh`.
- **card#7293** — **the changelog gate's scanned region gains a FLOOR: a `- **card#NNNN**` bullet filed in NO section no longer discharges its card.** `_region()` in `tests/changelog-card-entry-selftest.sh` had a ceiling and nothing else — it printed from **line 1** down to the `## [<last released version>]` stop header, so its stated purpose (*"`[Unreleased]` is where this repo records shipped work"*) and its actual predicate (*"anywhere above the last released section"*) were different sets: the `# Changelog` H1, the format blurb and the `begins at [0.8.2]` note were all inside the region. **The instance was live, not hypothetical** — the card#7227 entry landed at **line 1 of `docs/CHANGELOG.md`**, above the H1 and inside no section, and this gate reported `21 obligation(s) · 21 discharged` over it and passed. The region now starts at the literal **`## [Unreleased]`** header, floor line included, and that bullet is moved into `[Unreleased]` where it belongs (it had no other bullet, so this is a move, not a dedupe — `_stale_dupes` reports nothing for it. **Corrected under card#7303:** the net was zero *because* the removal was counted from outside the region; region-scoping that half makes it **one**, measured. The verdict is unchanged either way — one surviving bullet cannot reach the threshold — but the arithmetic in the original sentence no longer holds).

  **The floor is the LITERAL `## [Unreleased]`, and a release PR is the reason for it rather than the objection to it.** Step 4 of `VERSIONING.md` retitles the accumulated `## [Unreleased]` block as `## [X.Y.Z]` **and opens a fresh, empty `## [Unreleased]` above it**, so on a correct release PR the region runs `[Unreleased]` → `[X.Y.Z]` → the previous version's header and the retitled section is still inside it, discharging exactly as before. Measured over the tag history as it stood at v0.29.0, rather than assumed: of the **42** `v*` tags, 31 carry a `docs/CHANGELOG.md` and **29 of those have `## [Unreleased]` as their first section header** — only the pre-rule v0.23.0/v0.23.1 do not (re-derive rather than quoting those figures; they were a reason, not a constant) — and the live file, rewritten by hand into the step-4 shape, still reported `22 obligation(s) · 22 discharged` at rc 0 under the literal floor. **The generous spelling — floor at whatever the first `## [` header happens to be — fails at the worst possible moment:** a release PR that retitles and forgets the fresh header PASSES it, merges and is tagged, and from that tag on the retitled section *is* the stop header, so the region is empty and the new precondition reds **every subsequent PR on `dev`**, blocking someone who did nothing wrong. The literal floor reds on the release PR itself, where the omission is and while the person who made it is looking at the check. Both ends of the region are matched with `index($0, …) == 1` rather than a regex — the ceiling because an interpolated `0.23.1` also matches `0x23y1`, the floor because `[` is a metacharacter.

  **Absent floor is a HARD EXIT, not an empty region**: it cannot err green (an empty region discharges nothing, so every obligation reds), but the uniqueness leg asserts an ABSENCE over that same region and would answer `""` for a reason unrelated to the file being clean — so it joins the live preconditions, and its message names the missing header, and step 4's second half, instead of listing every shipped card as undocumented. **That block now holds five hard exits** — not-a-work-tree, shallow clone, no reachable `v*` tag, no stop header, empty region — **derived rather than incremented**: an `awk` pass over the block between the `# Live preconditions.` and `# The live assertion.` banners counting line-`exit 1`, with the control of re-running the same pass over a copy with one `exit 1` removed (5 → 4). The inherited comment said "three" and was **already off by one before this card touched it**, so the number is now **deleted** from the prose rather than corrected — the count lives in the code the reader is three hundred lines from reading, and a restated one only rots again.

  **This supersedes two claims in the frozen `[0.24.0]` card#5767 entry below**, which is deliberately left unedited (it is a released section — this file does not retro-document, see the `begins at [0.8.2]` note). That entry says a missing stop header *"widens the scan to the whole file"* and that there are *"three checkout preconditions"*. Neither holds now: the scan runs from `## [Unreleased]` to EOF, which is exactly what the guard's own remediation string was changed to say (`silently covers the WHOLE file` → `runs to EOF`), and the preconditions are five. A reader walking forward from `[0.24.0]` should take this entry as the current statement of both.

  **Inherited from `dev`, and fixed here because encountering a false claim makes it yours (canon #16):** five places in the same test file (`:65`, `:71`, `:244`, `:250`, `:481` as they stood on `dev`) still asserted that `docs/CHANGELOG.md` carries `merge=union`, that `.gitattributes` holds the rule, and that merging `dev` into a branch is the conflict-free way to take siblings' entries. `6cbf5e5` dropped that attribute in card#7227 — *"its benefit was measured and does not exist"* — and this repo has **no `.gitattributes` at all** (`git check-attr merge -- docs/CHANGELOG.md` → `unspecified`). The operational cost was real: a maintainer reading those lines was told a catch-up `git merge origin/dev` needs no hand-edit, and it conflicts. The union fixture stays — it creates the attribute in a **throwaway repo of its own** to exercise the mechanism, and now says so — because the shape the leg catches (one PR leaving a superseded bullet beside its replacement) is reachable through a hand-resolved conflict with no attribute anywhere.

  **Seen to fail before it was trusted, and re-measured at source rather than relayed.** Against `docs/CHANGELOG.md` as it stood on `dev` (`8a40466`, the stray bullet at line 1) with the obligations derived from `v0.29.0..8a40466`: **21 obligations, 21 discharged, nothing missing** under the old floorless region — the gate passes over the defect — and **20 discharged with `card#7227` reported missing** under the literal floor, on the same file, before the instance was touched. The floor fixtures were then watched red under three mutants: floor **deleted** (5 assertions red, including the preamble-bullet leg and its no-flood control), floor set to the generous **`## [`** (exactly the 2 assertions that separate the two designs red — the release-PR-that-forgot-the-fresh-header pair), and floor pinned to a **never-occurring header** (11 red, the control proving the region is not vacuously empty). The live empty-region precondition was exercised on the real `docs/CHANGELOG.md` rewritten into the half-done step-4 shape — rc 1, printing its own message — and the file restored byte-identical.

  ⛔ **Bound: this moves the region's floor only.** It says nothing about a bullet filed under the WRONG section header *inside* the region — `### Added` versus `### Fixed`, or a just-retitled version section — which remains outside every assertion this file makes. And **neither end is fence-aware**: a line beginning `## [Unreleased]`, or the stop header, at column 1 inside a fenced code block in the preamble would be taken as the real header. That is the ceiling's own pre-existing exposure, unreachable in today's 6-line preamble, and deliberately not closed with a fence-aware parser.

- **card#7227** — **a superseded `[Unreleased]` entry standing beside its replacement is now a FAILING check, and the `merge=union` half of this card is NOT shipped because its benefit was measured and does not exist.** `tests/changelog-card-entry-selftest.sh` was presence-only: it discharged an obligation when a line-initial `- **card#NNNN**` bullet existed, so a PR that left a stale bullet beside its correction passed. It now also asserts that no PR leaves two surviving bullets for one card — grouped by the `(#NNN)` token squash-merge leaves in the subject, on **net** contribution (adds minus removes), because `[Unreleased]` legitimately carries two duplicate-id pairs (card#6645 from #261/#262, card#7038 from #265/#266) and a raw count reds on correct history. That false red is itself a fixture, as is a union-corrupted rebase with its no-attribute control. ⛔ **Bound: the population is BULLETS, not entry bodies** — a duplicated line inside a multi-line entry is invisible to it. **`merge=union` on `docs/CHANGELOG.md` was built, measured and DROPPED:** the card's justification was that one merge stops dirtying every open sibling and that a dirty PR gets no CI, but **GitHub's server-side merge does not apply a `.gitattributes` merge driver** — measured with the attribute on both heads *and* the merge base, `POST /repos/{o}/{r}/merges` returned **409** where local `git merge-tree` returned rc 0 clean, against a positive control (different files) that returned **201**. What survived was only that a catch-up `git merge origin/dev` needs no hand-edit, which does not pay for three residuals, two of them silent corruption (a `git revert` of an entry becomes a no-op; reword-vs-reword ships both contradicting spellings; merge direction can file an entry under an already-released version). The measurement is recorded on card#7227 so the next person does not re-derive it.
- **card#7235** — **`board-stats`' FLOW COUNTS carry the floor marker themselves on a changelog window this tool could only partly read — this changes what an operator-facing report renders.** The window is read by paging backwards until the rows precede the cutoff, and several break sites in `_bs_window_rows` end it short of that, enumerated once in `bin/board-stats` itself — in the comment above the `flow_complete` guard in `_bs_one_board`, which this entry points at rather than copies (see the fail-open paragraph below for why). `_bs_one_board` accepts every one of them when at least one page was read — the rows are in hand, so a section is rendered rather than dropped — and appended `changelog window INCOMPLETE: … — the flow counts below are a floor, not a total`.
