It then printed every quantity in that section BARE under that one line — **fifteen of them** for the fixture board, a figure the selftest re-derives from the emitted document on every run rather than asserting as a literal. A number survives being quoted out of its line and the ⚠ does not: this is the THIRD member of the class card#6365 closed for `board-snapshot` and card#7228 closed for this tool's STOCK section, and it is the same fix one section lower rather than a second mechanism — the `fl($f)` carrier card#7228 built takes a cell of the existing right-padded column, so alignment holds, proven by a strip control that compares the floored render to the complete one as a **byte equality** rather than by eye.

  **The denominator is wider than the report of it, and the two additions are why it was re-derived rather than copied.** Beyond `created`, `moved`, `same-stage moves` and every per-transition / per-resolution / per-wash count, two more classes of quantity print in that section and both are floors for the same reason: **the human/service/unattributed numbers inside every split note** (they count the same rows the line they hang off counts, so `split_note` now takes the marker as an argument), and **`transitions: none in window` / `resolutions: none in window`** — a ZERO is a quantity, and `none in window` asserts a TOTAL of zero that survives being quoted out of the ⚠ line exactly as a digit does. That one is the only claim in the section the marker cannot reach, because there is no number to prefix, so a floored render names the population instead — `none in the events read` — the route `idq` already takes for the oldest-card id, not a new one; a complete window still prints `none in window`, with its own control. Two things are deliberately NOT marked and neither is a quantity: the stage LABELS, and the `⤷ the changelog reaches back only to …` line, whose instant is the oldest row that WAS read and is exact.

  **The two completeness states are SEPARATE, and that is the ruling, not an implementation detail.** `flow_complete` is derived at the changelog guard and `stock_complete` at the card guard, from two reads with two failure modes: a whole card read under a partly-read window floors the flow half ALONE, and the reverse floors the stock half ALONE. One shared flag would floor a section that was read whole, and a qualifier on every report is a worse defect than the one this closes — so both directions are asserted against the same bin in the same run. **The two flags are NOT fail-closed the same way, and this entry says so because the review of this change caught it asserting they were.** `stock_complete` dispatches on an **enumerated rc**, so an rc nobody listed falls to `*)` and floors by default. `flow_complete` dispatches on the **absence of a string**: it is initialised false and the one arm that claims a whole window is an empty `error`, which the break sites in `_bs_window_rows` that set `err` are what leave non-empty. A `break` added to that loop which does not set `err` reaches that same arm and claims the window is WHOLE — **that direction is FAIL-OPEN**, and adding a break means adding its `err=` in the same edit. **Which break sites those are is enumerated ONCE, in `bin/board-stats`, in the comment above that guard** — this entry deliberately carries no copy, because the copy it carried named four and there are five (the omitted one is the failed changelog read: the server answering a non-2xx, or the request never completing, which are two messages on one break). The shape, the nearest existing instance of it (`oldest == none` on the short-page arm), the live measurement that shows the trigger absent on the host measured on 2026-08-22, and the ask-gated affirmative rewrite that would close the direction (**card#7298**) are owned once, by [`docs/CONSOLIDATION-PLAN.md`](CONSOLIDATION-PLAN.md), which this entry points at rather than restates.

  **`--format json` is deliberately UNCHANGED**, for the reason card#7228 recorded rather than a fresh one: the emitted per-board object still carries exactly `board` / `board_id` / `failures` / `flow` / `label` / `stock`, and the internal `flow_complete` the text renderer reads is stripped by the `json` arm beside `stock_complete`. Publishing either is a machine-readable CONTRACT change whose consumer set has not been enumerated, and that is one decision for both halves — it stays OPEN on `docs/CONSOLIDATION-PLAN.md` rather than made here.

  **Coverage:** `tests/board-stats-selftest.sh` gains **33** assertions across a partly-read window, a no-move window that reaches the word arm, both cross-section controls, and a WASH row. **16 of them were watched failing against the pre-change bin**, with every control that asserts UNCHANGED behaviour green — including the byte-identity strip control, the one that catches a fix that floors every report. (Measured, in the same run: of the 49 `control`-labelled checks, **47 were green against the pre-change bin and 2 are inside the 16** — the two PRESENCE witnesses that pair with an absence assertion, `…and its flow half is marked COMPLETE` (the field does not exist on that bin, so it reads `null`) and `each render DOES carry markers, in its own section` (`true false` — the stock half marks, the flow half does not). Both assert behaviour this change ADDS; reding there is what they are for.) **Both figures are re-derived rather than carried, and the derivation ships with them** so the next pass re-runs instead of quoting: the assertion count is `grep -c '^  ok\|^  FAIL'` over this file's run minus the same over `git show origin/dev:tests/board-stats-selftest.sh` (230 − 197 = 33), and the fail-first figure is that same run with `git show origin/dev:bin/board-stats` swapped in, which reports `16 check(s) FAILED`. **The WASH row was added in review, and it is the reason to distrust an unwatched marker:** `.flow.washes` was empty in every window fixture, so the washes line never rendered at all and the floor marker on that render arm was a check that COULD NOT FAIL — dropping `fl($fflow)` from it left all 228 checks green; with a terminal→non-terminal row in both window fixtures the same mutation reds 3, and its opposite (an always-on marker on that arm) reds the control that says the wash count is bare on a complete window. The row also exposed that the helper reading a row's count took the FIRST line carrying the label, so the same move's `[WASH]` transition row would have answered for the washes block; row counts are now read inside their own subsection. **Six mutations of the shipped bin were each watched reding their own targets:** the flow flag driven off the STOCK state, an always-on flag, an unmarked actor split, an always-qualifying `none` arm, `flow_complete` claimed by the partial arm too, and the washes arm's marker dropped.

  **Found by the sibling audit, filed not fixed** (`docs/CONSOLIDATION-PLAN.md`, filed 2026-08-22 as **card#7291** and **card#7292**, both ask-gated operator-facing report changes): `bin/board-session-close`'s `_bsc_advisory_leg` prints a KILLED leg's partial output on **stdout** and says it is partial on **stderr, below it** — both halves of card#6365 at once, and a route no read-driven predicate selects, because this tool reads no collection; and `bin/_kbc-stale-blocker.py`'s summary prints bare counts under a corpus one board short, where a blanket `≥` would be WRONG on `unresolved`, which moves the other way.

  **Consumers who vendor:** re-vendor `bin/board-stats` only. The shared lib (`bin/_kb-board-lib.sh`), `promote/action.yml` and `release-artifacts/action.yml` are untouched.

- **card#7170** — **⚠ CONSUMER-FACING PROCEDURE CHANGE — the documented `PATH` install loop planted a symlink CYCLE inside the consumer's own checkout, and it stood in FOUR places.** `docs/INSTALL.md` §2's loop globs `bin/*` and links each entry with `ln -sf`, and **`ln -sf` does not replace a destination that is a symlink-to-directory — it follows it and creates the new link INSIDE the target, at rc 0**. So a directory in `bin/` (a `__pycache__/` left by a python helper is the one that has really happened — card#6871 closed that source, card#7207 closed CI re-minting it) was linked onto `PATH` on run 1 and, on run 2, linked back through that `PATH` entry into the source: `bin/__pycache__/__pycache__ -> bin/__pycache__`. Measured before the fix, run 1 rc 0 / run 2 rc 0, and **invisible to `git status`** because the directory is gitignored. **The recipe now refuses any `bin/` entry that is not a regular file** — `[ -f "$t" ] || { echo "skipped (not a regular file): $t" >&2; continue; }` — and links with **`ln -sfn`**, which closes the same defect at the *destination* end (a `~/.local/bin/<tool>` that is itself a symlink-to-directory used to swallow the link and leave the tool off `PATH`). **The newly-refused set is every non-regular-file entry of `bin/`: directories, dangling symlinks, FIFOs and sockets stop being linked, and each refusal is NAMED on stderr rather than silent** — the CHANNEL is executed, not just documented: `tests/path-link-recipe-selftest.sh` captures the two streams separately and asserts each refusal present on fd 2 and stdout empty, so a refusal that moved to stdout (and corrupted a caller piping the loop) reds. The merged-stream shape that hid this is filed as a class, card#7236. On a clean checkout nothing changes — every `bin/` entry is a regular file today, so the same tools are installed and a symlink to a regular file (`-f` follows) is still linked; the guard is stated over the shape, not over `__pycache__`. **It does NOT merely restate the framework's own `install-linked-bin.sh` install arm — measured, that arm refuses the DESTINATION shape only.** Its source glob (`items=("$srcabs"/*)`) carries no `[ -f ]` filter, so with a `__pycache__/` in the source `bin/` it reported `RESULT=INSTALLED SHAPE=link` at rc 0 on run 1 with `__pycache__` linked onto PATH, and on run 2 its destination guard fired on the symlink run 1 had just planted and refused the WHOLE install (`RESULT=NOT_INSTALLED … #install-arm-failed rc=1`). That source-end gap is the canon #7 sibling of this fix and is filed against the coord plugin as card#7234, not patched from here.

  **The consolidation is the fix, not the one-liner.** The loop stood VERBATIM at `docs/INSTALL.md` §2, in `docs/INSTALL.md`'s worked example and in `docs/UPGRADE.md` §2, plus a fourth near-copy in `VERSIONING.md` step 11 against the release-pinned checkout — and **all four carried the defect**, which is how four copies of one recipe end: a correction reaches one of them. **`docs/INSTALL.md` §2 is now the one owner** (the block plus the prose explaining why both guards are load-bearing), which is also what every pointer in the tree already named. The worked example and `docs/UPGRADE.md` §2 carry **no copy** and point at it; `VERSIONING.md` step 11 is the **one sanctioned second copy**, because it links a *different* source checkout and a pointer there would be a by-hand substitution in a deploy step. `tests/path-link-recipe-selftest.sh` holds all of it: it derives the population from `git ls-files` (any `ln -s` line inside a fenced block of a tracked `*.md`), reds if a member appears outside those two files, refuses to silently skip a member it cannot execute, and **runs each surviving copy twice against a fixture `bin/`** carrying a regular file, a non-executable regular file, a symlink-to-regular-file, `__pycache__/`, an ordinarily-named directory and a dangling symlink — asserting both the absence (nothing planted in the checkout, no `PATH` entry resolving to a directory) and the **presence witness** (exactly the three regular-file tools linked, each refusal named), since an absence-only leg passes for free against a driver that never ran. Its control is the defect itself: the pre-fix recipe text goes through the identical driver and **must** be caught planting `./bin/__pycache__/__pycache__`.

- **card#7214** — **`next-dl` stops degrading SILENTLY from the atomic DL claim to the very non-atomic `max+1` scan that allocator exists to retire, and gains `--require-counter` to refuse the degrade outright.** When the board's DL-sequence counter could not answer, `next-dl` printed a number from the offline scan at **rc 0 with an empty stderr** — byte-identical, on every channel a caller can read, to a server-side RESERVED claim. A caller could not tell an atomic claim from a guess, so two concurrent allocators could be handed the same DL by the tool whose whole purpose is to stop that, now carrying the tool's authority. **The fallback is now announced, in the CAUSE's own words.** `dl_sequence_call` returns rc 1 for **four** distinct situations and the rc is identical for all four, so stderr is the only place they are separable — each now prints its own line: the board's **config did not resolve** (no request was issued at all) / the endpoint **could not be REACHED** (curl transport failure — no HTTP status came back, so whether the route exists here is *unknown*) / the endpoint is **NOT DEPLOYED** (`404`) / it **answered `2xx` but carried no usable number**. One generic "falling back" line was deliberately rejected: *not there* and *could not reach it* prescribe different operator actions, and collapsing them re-mints the conflation this closes (a sibling of card#7210's class — a failed read scored as a usable negative). A **disposition** line follows the cause, naming what the scan is (not atomic; a floor that cannot observe a claimed-but-unstamped DL) and the opt-out. **`--require-counter` (opt-in) turns the degrade into a refusal: rc 4, nothing minted**, so a consumer whose atomicity claim depends on the counter gets a refusal it can surface rather than a number it cannot distinguish. **Strict is deliberately NOT the default and the default is unchanged in this release** — a single-stream caller on an install whose kanban predates the DL-sequence route is safe on the offline scan, and flipping the default would start failing it; that flip is a separate, gated decision. **A present-but-errored endpoint still aborts at rc 1**, with or without the flag — strict does not renumber an outcome that already refused. **For an existing caller nothing changes except that stderr gains a line on the degraded path** (asserted, not merely stated: a run whose claim endpoint answers leaves stderr byte-empty). Measured through the shipped bin against the four causes, each in both modes: permissive mints `DL-0301` from the local floor with its own cause line, strict exits 4 having minted nothing and — on the `404` leg — never reads the board at all. The strict legs are paired with a **positive witness** (endpoint answering + `--require-counter` → `DL-0093` from a real claim, no refusal, no notice), which is what a `next-dl` that simply refused unconditionally would fail; each cause additionally asserts the other three causes' phrases **absent**, so one shared string reds 10 assertions. `tests/_kb-api-stub-curl.sh` gains a `!curl <rc>` route answer — a real **transport** failure rather than an HTTP status — because the `curl … || fallback` arm every bin in `bin/` carries was previously unreachable from the suite.

- **card#7208** — **the version SHAPE was declared twice, incompatibly, and the comment that named the resulting truncation named it as PREVENTED.** `.release-pr.json`'s `version_regex` is the one declaration of what a version looks like — `auto-tag-version.yml` anchors that same key at merge time — and `bin/` carried a second, wider one: a hardcoded `[0-9]+(\.[0-9]+){1,3}` at `release-pr-body:107` and at both of `release-artifacts-check`'s extraction sites. It ran as a SECOND stage, over the FIRST stage's output, so it only ever saw what the config regex had already matched: **a stage in that position can narrow a match but can never widen one.** Measured on the pre-fix bin, through a real fixture repo — a 3-segment `version_regex` over a `1.22.1.0` version file rendered `release PR — v1.22.1`, the exact `1.22.1.0→1.22.1` failure the comment four lines above it claimed the 2-4-segment pattern prevented. **Both false comments are corrected**, and `.github/workflows/auto-tag-version.yml`'s claim that `version_regex` is "the one declaration … so the two no longer diverge" — false while `bin/` hardcoded a wider shape — is left standing because this change **makes it true**: after it, no file under `bin/` states a version shape at all. **What this does NOT do:** it does not widen `version_regex`. That key is what the merge-time tag gate accepts, and changing it is a separate, gated decision. So 4-segment support under a 3-segment `version_regex` is now **genuinely absent instead of falsely claimed** — and the consumer said to be relying on the literal to rescue a 3-segment config is an **empty set, by construction rather than by survey**: the literal ran second and could not rescue anything. A repo that wants four segments declares four in its own `version_regex`, and one that already does (the case the existing selftest covered) is byte-identical. **FOUR behaviours DO change.** **The first three are the literal's overrides of a legal config** — a single-segment `version_regex` resolved to nothing and the tools REFUSED it (`release-pr-body` rc 2 `could not resolve version`; `release-artifacts-check` rc 2 `no version matched version_regex`, a message that was itself false — it had matched), a legal five-segment one was cut to four, and any non-numeric text a `version_regex` was deliberately written to match was silently dropped from the value. The first two are now the red-when-reverted coverage; the third is a real narrowing of an undeclared capability, and the tool's own remedy text already tells consumers to write a `version_regex` that "matches only the version declaration". **The FOURTH is stated separately because it is different in kind: it moves an ACCEPT/REJECT surface inside a gate**, and it is a consequence of the same deletion rather than an override. `release-artifacts-check`'s candidate-set dedupe keyed on the literal's numeric run and now keys on the whole `version_regex` match, and that set's CARDINALITY *is* the classifier's verdict — 0 = version unchanged, 1 = release (with the `::warning::` naming which value), ≥2 = rc 2 `cannot determine the version being shipped`. ⚠ **A NEW REFUSE CASE, measured both ends on a fixture repo:** under `version_regex: "v?[0-9]+\.[0-9]+\.[0-9]+"`, a head version file gaining `next: v2.0.0 == 2.0.0` was **rc 0 · `release 2.0.0`** before and is **rc 2 · `… 2 values … (v2.0.0 2.0.0)`** after — so **a consumer whose regex admits an optional `v`, or any second spelling of one version, gets a hard-red release gate where it previously classified and ran every artifact leg.** Keeping the old cardinality is not available without re-declaring the version shape this change exists to remove, and the same fold ran in the OTHER direction: under a legal five-segment config, `2.0.0.0.5` and `2.0.0.0.9` both keyed to the truncated `2.0.0.0`, so the gate silently discarded one genuinely different version and asserted every declared member against `2.0.0.0` — **a string the file never carried** (measured, rc 0 on the pre-change bin). Narrowing the key back to buy the first case re-mints the second, and additionally faults `set -u` (`seen: bad array subscript`) on the single-segment config this same change just made legal. Both directions are red-when-reverted coverage in `release-artifacts-selftest.sh`, each with a one-candidate-written-twice control that keeps the refusals attributable to the candidate COUNT. ⚠ **UNMEASURED, and stated as such:** whether any consumer repo runs a 4-segment version, a single- or five-segment `version_regex`, or one carrying non-numeric context — and, for the fourth behaviour above, whether any consumer's `version_regex` admits two spellings of one version. No survey was run; this is not a claim that any of those sets is empty. **Coverage:** `release-pr-body-selftest.sh`'s one-case 4-segment block is replaced by five, including the **paired witness** an absence assertion needs (an ordinary 3-segment release still extracts whole — without it "stopped truncating" is indistinguishable from "stopped extracting") and the honest pin that a 3-segment config over a 4-segment file yields three segments *because the config says three*; `release-artifacts-selftest.sh` gains a single-segment `version_regex` classification case with an unchanged-version control, plus the two dedupe-key cases above (two spellings under a `v?` regex; two five-segment values sharing four segments), each with its own one-candidate-written-twice control. Every new leg was **watched red** — the first two on the restored pre-change bins, the dedupe-key ones against a bin whose key alone was narrowed back, which reds 5 of them and faults the single-segment case on `set -u` — and green after. `tests/read-outcome-collapse-selftest.sh` loses exactly one line — the disposition for `release-artifacts-check:v`, a variable this change deletes; the gate reds on a disposition that outlives its site, and the drop cannot land separately (before the bin change the same line is required, after it it is stale), which is why it rides here. **Consumers who vendor:** re-vendor `bin/release-pr-body` and `bin/release-artifacts-check` **TOGETHER** — executable lines move in both, and the second carries the accept/reject move above; a consumer that pins the `release-artifacts/` composite action instead vendors nothing but picks the same change up when it re-pins, because the action runs `$GITHUB_ACTION_PATH/../bin/release-artifacts-check` at the pinned SHA. Nothing else moves — `promote/action.yml`, `release-artifacts/action.yml`, `bin/_kb-board-lib.sh` and every other bin are untouched. **Ordering against #277:** `docs/INSTALL.md:109`'s stale "accepts 2-4 numeric segments" claim is corrected in **#277**, which owns that file; the two are independent to MERGE but not to READ, and if #277 lands first `dev` carries a doc describing this extraction for the window between the two merges. **#277 lands the POST-change claim** ("keeps exactly what YOUR `version_regex` matches"), so the order decides which way `dev` is briefly wrong: **#277 first** and `dev` documents an extraction the bins do not yet perform until this entry merges; **this first** and `dev` keeps the old, already-false "accepts 2-4 numeric segments" line until #277 merges. The second is the pre-existing state and the one this PR does not make worse, so **merge this before #277** where the choice is available; neither PR blocks the other and nothing fails in either window.

- **card#6911** — **a tool's verdict no longer dies with the reader that stopped listening, and the class gets the gate that forbids the N+1th instance.** A bin writes its report to stdout and only afterwards reaches a verdict-carrying `exit`; a consumer that stops consuming — `| head -1`, a pager the operator quit — kills the writer at the write, and the caller gets a signal death in place of the verdict AND its explanation. **Worse than no guard, because the invocation looks like it worked.** **Fixed:** `bin/agent-board-toolkit-runtime-check`, the one confirmed-and-unfixed instance, adopts the shape ratified for `gh-code-search` under card#6884 — `trap '' PIPE` as the first statement after `set`, plus one tolerated write per stream of an already-built string. Driven to a real STALE-COPIES verdict it now answers **rc 1 with 259 B of stderr** through `head -n 0`, where it answered **rc 141 with 0 B** before; on a passing tree the clean **rc 0** survives too, where truncation previously reported 141 — a runtime that is fine, reported as broken. **⚠ ONE OBSERVABLE CHANGE: its report is now BUFFERED** — nothing appears until the run ends, and it can spend up to 10 s per runtime in `git fetch`. Progressive output is what made the verdict losable; `--quiet`, the exit codes and the text are unchanged. **Added:** `tests/verdict-through-truncating-reader-selftest.sh`, which **measures** every shipped bin rather than grepping for the shape — the first sibling-audit instrument tried on this class failed its own control (it called the already-known-defective `gh-code-search` clean) and was discarded, and the card records that membership cannot be decided by reading. The population is re-derived every run — **23** files in `bin/` + `hooks/` today, **15 DRIVEN** through a truncating reader and classified on rc PRESERVATION, **8 UNDRIVEN and named as UNMEASURED-not-clean**, 0 unaccounted — with a stdout-byte **presence witness** that reds a driver which never reached a write (a fixture that cannot reach the condition it tests is how this class was misdiagnosed twice). **The remaining 12 known-defective bins are dispositioned by name, and the list is a RATCHET** — a member that starts surviving reds just as loudly as one that starts losing, so a fix cannot land without deleting its line and a new bin cannot join the class in silence. **Three prior wrong diagnoses of the mechanism are corrected on the record and planted as fixtures**: it is not builtin-vs-external (bash's `printf` builtin dies — measured); it is not `set -e` either (a bare builtin write with **no** errexit at all still dies, so `board-stats` is clean because it is *both* errexit-free *and* renders through children — naming only the errexit half excuses the commonest case); and **`rc 141` is not the mechanism and is never asserted on** — a GitHub Actions runner starts bash from Node with `SIGPIPE` set to `SIG_IGN`, a disposition that survives `execve`, so in CI the write returns EPIPE at rc 1 and any test expecting 141 passes locally and reds in CI. **That last one goes one level deeper than a hardcoded number, and the gate measures BOTH dispositions because of it:** the *outcome* differs too, not just the rc — a bin with no errexit is killed outright by the signal but only gets a non-zero `printf` it never inspects when the signal is ignored, so 12 of 15 driven members lose their verdict at `SIG_DFL` and only 9 do at `SIG_IGN`. Recording either alone would have produced a gate that reds in the other environment. Both legs are pinned with `env --default-signal` / `--ignore-signal` rather than `trap`, because **a signal ignored on entry to a non-interactive shell cannot be reset from inside it** — `trap - PIPE` is a silent no-op on a CI runner, so the first cut measured `SIG_IGN` twice while reporting two dispositions; the gate's own disagreement control caught it, and the gate now refuses (rc 1, naming the requirement) rather than run without the capability. `tests/runtime-check-selftest.sh` gains the verdict-path legs the general gate structurally cannot reach; `promote/action.yml` and `release-artifacts/action.yml` vendor surfaces unchanged. **⚠ EVERY COUNT ABOVE IS A READING, NOT THE POPULATION** — the gate prints its own re-derived denominator on every run, and that is the figure to quote. The first cut of this entry said 21 / 11 / 14 / 8 and was already false when written: **two bins landed on `dev` in parallel and arrived by merge-up** — `bin/release-tag-check` (card#6579) and `bin/_shellcheck-pinned` (card#6619) — and **the gate caught each one and CI went red, which is the gate working.** `release-tag-check` is now driven on `--help` (rc 0, 6 783 B) and dispositioned **LOSES** under both dispositions: `awk` renders its help as a child under errexit. Its `::error::` refusals are on stderr, but its **stdout** carries the not-a-release rc-0 line and the `<tag> must exist at <sha>` banner it prints *before the first poll* — so a truncating reader kills it at that banner, before it ever looks for the tag, and `release-promote-cards` gets a signal death in place of either answer. `_shellcheck-pinned` is **UNDRIVEN with its reason**, decided on measurement not convenience: it writes **nothing to its own stdout on any path** (all five write sites are stderr, a variable capture, or a pipe into `sha256sum`), every stdout byte a caller sees belongs to the analyser it `exec`s, and reaching that `exec` at all needs the pinned analyser already present — otherwise it downloads, or refuses at rc 9 with 0 B of stdout, which this gate scores UNREACHED and reds. ⚠ **Neither PR's CI could see the pair**, and that is now stated in the gate's header as a structural limit rather than softened: an entry may only be added once the file is in the tree, because the symmetric leg — a listed member the population no longer carries — is what stops the roll accumulating fiction. **Also fixed, the same class one level IN:** `agent-board-toolkit-runtime-check` was itself piping a producer into an early-exiting reader — `git tag --list 'v*' … | head -1` under `set -e` + `pipefail`, which dies at rc 141 *before* the verdict. Measured with a planted tag set: **0/40 runs died at 350 `v*` tags, 21/40 at 400, 40/40 at 12 000** — a RACE at git's ~4 KiB stdio buffer, not a cliff at the 64 KiB pipe buffer — and **`trap '' PIPE` does not cover it**, because git restores `SIGPIPE` to `SIG_DFL` for itself and the parent's ignore is not inherited. Reachable in a **toolkit vendored inside a consumer repo**, where the `git -C` root is the consumer's repository and its tag count is not ours to bound. Taken with a parameter expansion instead: identical output, no second process. The wider `| head` / `| tail -N` class stays advisory and un-gated — that cost decision is unchanged and lives in `docs/CONSOLIDATION-PLAN.md`, where this member is now recorded. **The buffer's own obligation is now a shipped assertion, not prose:** the report is buffered, so `trap _flush EXIT` is what stops an unexpected mid-run death printing nothing at all — a pair of planted fixtures identical but for that one line now proves it (`DIAGNOSIS-SO-FAR` vs empty), where deleting the trap previously left the whole suite green. **Refactor (canon #5):** CI's shell-file population had been hand-copied into three class gates; the derivation now lives once in `tests/_shipped-shell-lib.sh`, which exports CI's **two halves separately** — the three populations genuinely differ and flattening them would be a regression — so each caller composes its own union. `ci.yml` stays the authority and a `run:` string cannot source a bash lib, so the restatement is **guarded** rather than deleted: `_ci_shellcheck_drift` reds when the workflow stops running either half. The other two gates are untouched and can adopt it in their own PRs; adoption is byte-identical. **And the file's own writer-derivation recipe is corrected:** its three buckets called `ver="$(git … || echo '?')"` a new direct writer, which it is not — a write into a command substitution touches no stream, and routing it through `_put` would emit the fallback marker instead of assigning it. A fourth bucket now says so. **A SECOND defect CI found that a developer box structurally cannot see:** `bin/kbcard`'s driver read the invoking user's `$HOME`. `kb_load_config` resolves `$HOME/.kanban-dev-board.env` *before* the no-argument help arm, so the same commit measured **rc 0 / 25 394 B → LOSES** on a configured box and **rc 2 / 0 B → UNREACHED**, a hard red, on a GitHub Actions runner — the gate's own "a driver that measured nothing is never dispositionable" rule firing correctly. Closed with a **planted config** (three files, no network, a literal non-secret token) and the ambient overrides cleared, so the measurement is now byte-identical on both; a control drives both sides. ⚠ The control is a **CI-side guard only**, measured: deleting the plant reds three checks on a bare box and reds *nothing* on a configured one, which is exactly how this survived a review cycle. The gate's "returns the same answer whatever started it" claim is therefore **corrected to the signal axis alone** — it was verified against `SIG_IGN`, never against host config — with one residual named rather than swept: `board-session-close`'s direct rc and byte count still differ by box (rc 0 / ~20 KB configured, rc 3 / ~3 KB bare, rc 1 / ~1.4 KB on the runner), though its classification agrees in all three. The whole 42-selftest matrix was re-run under a bare `$HOME` to look for siblings: none.
- **card#6619** — **the shellcheck gate's analyser is pinned and self-reporting instead of inherited from the runner image.** `ci.yml` ran the bare `shellcheck` that `ubuntu-latest` happened to ship and printed no version, so a CI verdict named no program, a contributor running "CI's own command" locally could not certify the gate, and the analyser could change under the repo with no change to it. The two ends of that drift genuinely disagree at ERROR severity — measured on the same file with the same flags: `helper() { echo one; }; helper; unset -f helper; helper() { echo two; }; helper` is **SC2218 (error)** to 0.9.0 and **clean** to 0.11.0. **The pin is 0.9.0 — today's observed behaviour written down, not a change to it**: ubuntu-24.04 image `20260816.277` (the image the last `dev` CI run used) ships `shellcheck 0.9.0-1`, and over the gate's own file population at `-S error`, 0.9.0 and 0.11.0 both report zero findings — on the tree as it stood (69 files) and again on the tree this change ships (71) — so no verdict moves. **`.shellcheck-version`** is the one place the version is written (a workflow spelling it out again is gated against); **`bin/_shellcheck-pinned`** resolves it — an explicit `$SHELLCHECK_PIN_BIN`, then a cache, then PATH, then the sha256-verified upstream release — accepting only a binary whose own `--version` says the pinned version and **refusing, by name, anything else** rather than falling back to it, and printing on stderr which binary ran, on **every** invocation rather than in one dedicated step, so a job added later cannot forget to say. All three of `ci.yml`'s shellcheck call sites go through it: the two that analyse a **generated** file drifted exactly as silently as the one that analyses the tree. **CI downloads nothing today** (the image's shellcheck matches the pin, so the PATH arm wins) — which is why `tests/shellcheck-pin-selftest.sh` drives the download, sha-mismatch and refusal arms network-free through a `curl` stub over a real `.tar.xz`, and holds "no workflow invokes shellcheck outside the wrapper" over the whole workflow directory so the N+1th job cannot re-mint the bare call. **No behaviour of any shipped tool changes** — `bin/_shellcheck-pinned` is `_`-prefixed on purpose: it is the gate's analyser, outside README's tool inventory and outside `agent-board-toolkit-runtime-check`'s PATH contract.

- **card#7175** — **`<producer> | grep -q <needle>` under `pipefail` reported a MATCH as a NON-MATCH, and the rule that was supposed to bound the damage was measured wrong.** `grep -q` exits the instant it matches. Its upstream is usually still writing, so the writer fails on the closed pipe, `pipefail` promotes **its** non-zero status to the pipeline's, and the `&& echo true || echo false` tail every call site wore then reported a match as `false` — a wrong answer at rc 0, not an error, with nothing for the reader to notice. **That status is 141 where SIGPIPE is at its default and 1 — an EPIPE write error — where an ancestor has ignored SIGPIPE, which the GitHub Actions runner does** (the step's bash is started by the runner's Node process, and `SIG_IGN` survives `execve` into every child); both are non-zero, both are promoted, and the second is what the original `lib-set-derivation-selftest.sh` red actually recorded — its evidence was a printed `grep: write error: Broken pipe`, which is the EPIPE path, not a kill. It had already cost a CI red this cycle: `lib-set-derivation-selftest.sh` leg 3 was green on five consecutive local runs and red in CI on the same commit, once a second multi-KB `[Unreleased]` entry pushed the payload over the pipe buffer. **The population was 47 pipeline sites across 11 files; 46 are migrated here and the 47th is named below.** **⚑ The load-bearing correction: a bash `printf`/`echo` BUILTIN upstream is NOT exempt, and the in-tree claim that it was is now pinned false.** `fetch-board-cards-caller-claims-selftest.sh` recorded *"bash's printf BUILTIN does not die on SIGPIPE (rc 0 over a 5MB body)"*, and that sentence was the stated reason **36** sibling `printf | grep -q` sites were judged safe and left standing — 34 in `tests/` and two in the shipped `bin/board-card-start`, counting OCCURRENCES (a line can carry two) on non-comment lines where the `printf` is the grep's DIRECT upstream, re-derived at the fork point rather than recalled. **An earlier cut of this entry said "~28", and the figure was wrong the way a hand count is wrong** — 19 + 9 is exactly the two largest files, so the six-occurrence tail across five more files and the two in `bin/` were never added in. The counts that GATE the work (47 / 11 / 46 / 1) were derived and re-derive exactly, including a multi-line pipeline a single-line match misses; this one was typed. Bash forks a subshell for a builtin in a pipeline and that subshell takes SIGPIPE like any other process. The real conditions are two, and neither is the writer's class: **(a)** `grep -q` leaves EARLY, i.e. the match is near the start of the stream so the reader is gone while the writer still has bytes to push, and **(b)** the writer has more than the **64 KiB pipe buffer** left to write at that moment. **The original measurement is reproducible, which is exactly why it fooled everyone** — it holds only with the match at the END of the body, where grep reads the whole stream and exits after the writer does, so condition (a) never fires and no signal is ever sent. Re-measured on the reference host (bash 5.2.21), `printf '%s\n' "$big" | grep -qF MATCH-ME`: match at END / 5 MB → rc 0 `PIPESTATUS 0 0`; match at START / 5 MB → **rc 141** `PIPESTATUS 141 0` (that host's default-disposition rendering — the same cell reads `1 0` under an inherited `SIG_IGN`); match at START / 1 KB → rc 0. A control whose fixture cannot trigger the condition it tests reports clean and teaches the wrong rule, and this one propagated from a test comment into the class card before anyone re-measured it. **Added:** `has_line <line> <text>` in `tests/_selftest-prelude.sh` — the line-anchored twin of `has`, and the one owner every `| grep -qx` in the suite now asks whole-line membership through. It runs **no pipeline and no subprocess** (a `case` glob over the text with the newline sentinels made explicit), so the window cannot exist rather than being narrowed; a herestring would also close it but still forks a `grep`, and the point of one owner is that the next author cannot re-open the window by reaching for whichever idiom reads most naturally. The needle is quoted inside the pattern, so glob metacharacters match literally. One divergence from `grep -qx` is stated at the definition and is command substitution's, not the helper's: `$(…)` strips trailing newlines, so an EMPTY needle cannot tell "emitted nothing" from "emitted one empty line". `tests/pipeline-free-match-selftest.sh` is its control battery — four fixtures spanning (match position) × (payload size), each pair moving **one** axis so a verdict is attributable to that axis, asserting **PIPESTATUS** rather than the pipeline's rc alone so the reader can see which stage died, plus the negative side (absent line, substring-not-line, prefix, extension, empty text) that forbids the strictly-worse fix of matching everything. Driven red by six single-defect mutations of the helper (always-true, always-false, substring-instead-of-line, unquoted needle, swapped arguments, dropped sentinel), each restored and `cmp`'d. **Changed — the vendorable surface moves:** `bin/board-card-start`'s placeholder-host test and `branch_just_created` reflog test are now herestrings; behaviour is identical at today's payloads and the sites were latent, not safe. `bin/board-session-close` gains `_bsc_names_start`, one owner for the two files its hook-reach test asks about — the chained-hook site was a live `sed … | grep -q`, so a hook that DOES reach `board-card-start` would have read as one that does not once the hook grew past the buffer. **The one deferral is now closed, not carried:** `tests/lib-set-derivation-selftest.sh:407` was left to open PR #270 (card#6680) to avoid colliding with work in flight; #270 has since merged and replaced that `grep -F | grep -qF` pipeline with a `case` glob, so the population at `dev` is **zero live sites outside the deliberate fixtures**. **Added — the GATE, because deleting the copies is not closing the class:** `tests/piped-match-gate-selftest.sh` derives the live population from the tree on every run (`bin` + `hooks` per `ci.yml`'s own shellcheck expression, **plus `tests/*.sh`** — 44 of the 47 copies were in the harness, so a `bin`-only gate would have said nothing) and reds on any occurrence its disposition list does not carry. Members are keyed on the **file plus an occurrence count**, not a line number, so an N+1th copy inside an already-dispositioned file still reds; the one disposition is the `_piped*`/`_stat*` fixtures that ARE the construct held still. Its own positive fixtures are written through a placeholder expanded at write time, so the scanner needs no exception for its own source. Six controls (one positive spelling, four negatives — herestring, `||`, a grep with no `-q`, the construct in a comment — and a real-tree reach check) plus all three red paths watched to fire. It replaces a sentence in `pipeline-free-match-selftest.sh` that declined the gate in writing while two copies survived the audit, which this repo's `docs/CONSOLIDATION-PLAN.md` had already ruled against twice. **Swept — the corrected claim, not just the corrected code:** the rule that *the dead writer's status is 141* survived in **12 places** after the code fix, two of them forward-looking predictions in the shipped `bin/release-artifacts-check` sitting directly above a live `head -1` pipeline, where a maintainer hardening it would have written an `rc 141` assertion that greens locally and reds in CI — the third repetition of this card's own propagation path. All 12 now carry the two-rendering statement or a disposition caveat (`bin/release-artifacts-check` ×2, `bin/gh-code-search` ×5, `tests/gh-code-search-selftest.sh` ×5). Two assertion labels there read `…, not 141`; measured on both shapes at both dispositions, the pre-fix lib-less refusal ALSO answers rc 1 under `SIG_IGN`, so that leg discriminates only on a default-disposition host — recorded at the leg rather than left implied. `bin/_kb-board-lib.sh` was checked and carries **no** live instance and no stale claim. **⚑ For vendoring consumers: `bin/gh-code-search` and `bin/release-artifacts-check` change COMMENTS ONLY here** — not one executable line moves in either, `--help` gains the caveat paragraph in `gh-code-search`'s header, and a re-vendor is not owed by this entry. `promote/action.yml` and `release-artifacts/action.yml` vendor surfaces unchanged.

- **card#6365** — **`board-snapshot` stops rendering a PARTIAL board read as a complete one: the incompleteness now lands on STDOUT, the only channel its consumer surfaces.** `board_report` accepts `fetch_board_cards`' **rc 3** (page cap hit) and **rc 4** (short read — the server's own `meta.total` exceeds the rows the pages delivered) on purpose: both emit their partial array so a fail-soft SessionStart display shows what it has rather than going dark. What it then did was render `• <board>: in-flight N` off that partial row set, while the paginator's `⚠ … list INCOMPLETE` line went to **stderr** — which this script's own header records its consumer as DISCARDING. So the number an operator acts on was derived from an incomplete read and the only signal saying so was on a stream nobody reads; the untriaged list is built from the **same** partial array, and the obligation stated in this tool's own section header ("none may be silently missed") is evaluated against it. The asymmetry sat inside one file: twelve lines below the render, `agent-board-toolkit-runtime-check` is folded `2>&1` for exactly this reason, and the fetch warnings were not. **The fix is completeness state handed to the render path, not a check remembered at each render:** one value derived from the rc at the guard, feeding (a) a `≥` prefix on **every** count this pass prints — all **three** of them: `in-flight ≥200`, `≥200 UNTRIAGED`, and the untriaged list's overflow tail `… +≥194 more` — so the number cannot be read as a total even quoted out of its line, and (b) one note per section, because the two sections are written on two channels (stdout and fd 3) and printed under **different headers**, so a bare `≥` arriving under the second would have no explanation beside it. The fd-3 note also covers the silent shape: a truncated read whose surviving cards are all triaged prints **no untriaged block at all**, and *nothing to triage* is exactly the claim a partial read may not make. The predicate is `rc -ne 0`, derived from the accept-guard above it rather than re-listing `3|4`, so widening that guard cannot leave a new rc rendering as complete. **Both notes name the rc and no cause**, per the wording rule at `fetch_board_cards`' header, and are registered as arms in `tests/fetch-board-cards-caller-claims-selftest.sh` (which red on them until they were ruled on — its derivation saw them the moment they were written). **Nothing about the fail-soft contract moves:** a hard read failure still exits 0 and still prints its board line, and a **complete** read is **byte-identical** to before — asserted by exact-equality, not by substring, because a fix that marked every snapshot incomplete would be worse than the defect. **Coverage** (`tests/board-snapshot-selftest.sh`): the bin is driven as a **PROCESS** against a stubbed `curl`, so rc 3 and rc 4 are produced by the **real** paginator (a page cap actually hit; a `meta.total` the rows fall short of) rather than by a stubbed shell function, and every assertion reads the process's **STDOUT with STDERR discarded** — what the hook sees. Both positive legs were watched failing on the pre-fix bin (9 checks red, the unqualified `in-flight 200` among them) while both controls stayed green. The review round's additions were watched the same way against the same pre-fix bin: **15 checks red** — every unread-arm leg on fd 3, the derived early-exit denominator (`expected '4' got '0'`), the untriaged render fallback, the bare `… +194 more`, and the source leg that re-derives the three count interpolations — with every control and every pre-existing check still green. **Sibling audit** (canon #7): the paginator's other rendering consumer, `bin/board-stats`, already carried the equivalent stdout NOTE (`card snapshot INCOMPLETE (fetch rc=$rc) — every stock count below is a floor, not a total`) at its `3|4` arm; every other consumer refuses on 3/4. The note is not the marker, though — every stock number `board-stats` prints is bare (measured at nine per board on all three boards here — eight columns plus the total — under one `⚠`), and its rc-3/rc-4 arm leaves the internal `stock_ok` **true**, so `--format json` emits a fully populated `.stock` object with no field distinguishing it from a whole read; the only machine-visible signal is a prose string in `failures[]`. That is the same defect this entry fixes, in the other renderer, and it is recorded **OPEN** on `docs/CONSOLIDATION-PLAN.md`'s entry rather than closed here: changing what `board-stats` prints and what its JSON asserts is an operator-facing report change, outside this card's scope.

  **Every arm that ends a board's pass without a card list marks the untriaged channel, not just the two that render.** The first cut covered rc 3 and rc 4. The other five arms that reach the untriaged section — env file missing, env sets no `KB_BOARD_ID`, token file unreadable, and the read guard's rc 1 / rc 2 — reported once on stdout and wrote **nothing** to fd 3, so the untriaged section rendered **empty** under a header that says *"none may be silently missed"*, for a board where **zero rows were read**. Measured against a 500-ing stub with stderr discarded exactly as the hook discards it, that is a stronger version of the very claim the fd-3 note forbids one line above it. All five now report through one `board_unread` helper that writes the same reason to **both** channels, and each of the two jq renders gained a fallback on its **own** channel, so a render that dies contributes a line to the section it failed to fill instead of leaving it silently empty. The prior behaviour was documented as a preserved *"fail-soft asymmetry"*; it was inherited from the era when the two sections were two independent passes, and it is now ruled on rather than left implicit. `tests/fetch-board-cards-caller-claims-selftest.sh` gains `board_unread` in its **emitter vocabulary** — a named helper is the only spelling its derivation can follow, and the arm went invisible to it the moment the `echo` became a helper call.

  **What is actually REACHABLE against a live board — a claim, so it is stated as a derivation plus the measurement that fed it.** `bin/board-snapshot`'s header used to assert *"board 5 is already ~98 cards"*; board 5 answered **242** when re-measured on 2026-08-21, and boards 5 and 8 both report `meta.last_page: 2` (242 and 271 cards; board 12 is one page at 189). So **two of the three configured boards are already MULTI-PAGE reads**, and that is exactly the precondition **rc 4** needs: `meta.total` is captured on page 1 only, the census compares it against the summed rows, and any row leaving the result set between the page-1 and page-2 requests — one archive (`board-session-close` does this), one card moved off the board — makes `sum_n < total`. rc 4 is therefore **live-reachable today, benignly**, on an ordinary concurrent write. **rc 3** is not: the page cap (25) times the paginator's fixed `limit=200` needs more than **5,000** cards on one board. The header now carries the derivation and the query that re-runs it, not a board size that rots.

  **Consumers who vendor:** re-vendor `bin/board-snapshot` only. The shared lib (`bin/_kb-board-lib.sh`), `promote/action.yml` and `release-artifacts/action.yml` are untouched — this consumes the paginator's existing rc contract and does not change it.

- **card#7228** — **`board-stats`' STOCK NUMBERS carry the floor marker themselves on a partial card read — this changes what an operator-facing report renders.** The tool accepts `fetch_board_cards`' **rc 3** (page cap) and **rc 4** (short read) and renders the partial array on purpose: both emit what they read so a report shows what it has rather than going dark, and it already appended `card snapshot INCOMPLETE (fetch rc=N) — every stock count below is a floor, not a total` to that board's ⚠ lines. Then it printed **nine bare numbers per board** under that one line — eight columns plus the total, measured on all three boards on the reference host — and a number survives being quoted out of its line where a ⚠ several lines above it does not. That is the argument card#6365 closed `board-snapshot` on (*"so the number cannot be read as a total even quoted out of its line"*), and `board-stats` is the second member of that class. **The state is derived ONCE at the rc guard and handed to the render path**, and it is fail-closed: it is initialised false, and exactly one arm — rc 0, the whole read — claims otherwise, so an rc added to the accepted-partial `3|4` arm floors its numbers whether or not anyone remembers to say so, and only an explicit edit to the rc-0 arm can claim a read is whole. **The population has a denominator:** one count per column, the total, and the oldest-card AGE in each pullable column that holds one — the age joins the counts because a card the read never delivered can only be OLDER than the oldest one it did, so the printed age is a floor for the same reason. The card ID beside that age is not a number and cannot take `≥`, so it takes a **qualifier in place**: a floored render prints `oldest ≥232.3d (#11 among the cards read)` where a complete one prints `(#11)` — a bare id asserts *the oldest Backlog card is #11* off a read this tool has just declared incomplete, and by the same premise that floors the age the actually-oldest card can be one the read never delivered, under a different id. The ruling, and the two routes that were rejected, is owned once — by the card#7228 disposition in [`docs/CONSOLIDATION-PLAN.md`](CONSOLIDATION-PLAN.md), which this entry points at rather than restates. **Measured against the pre-change bin, side by side through the shared curl stub: byte-identical on FOUR of the six format × rc combinations** — the text render at rc 0, and `--format json` at rc 0, rc 3 and rc 4. The two that differ are the text render at rc 3 and rc 4, which is exactly this change and nothing else. (An earlier draft of this entry said "byte-identical … on text AND `--format json`, at rc 0, rc 3 and rc 4", which reads as a claim the partial TEXT renders are unchanged; they are not, and the measurement above is what was actually run.)

  **`--format json` is deliberately UNCHANGED, and the gap it leaves is recorded rather than implied.** The emitted per-board object still carries exactly `board` / `board_id` / `failures` / `flow` / `label` / `stock`, with `.stock` fully populated on a partial read and **no completeness field at all** — so a JSON consumer still has to string-match a prose entry inside `failures[]` to learn the counts are floors. Adding that field is a machine-readable **contract** change whose consumer set has not been enumerated, which is a separate decision and not this one; the document behind the renderer does now carry a `stock_complete` (the text renderer computes nothing of its own and needed a field to read) and the `json` arm strips it, asserted on both a whole and a partial read. **Which members of the class are still OPEN is owned by [`docs/CONSOLIDATION-PLAN.md`](CONSOLIDATION-PLAN.md) and is deliberately not counted here** — this entry named a count that a later change in the same `[Unreleased]` block (the FLOW half, card#7235) falsified, which is what a second copy of a moving number does. Read the plan for the live set; what belongs to THIS entry is that the JSON field is one of them, and that the sibling audit run while shipping this found the **FLOW** counts printing bare in the same renderer one section lower, under their own `changelog window INCOMPLETE … a floor, not a total` line.

  **Consumers who vendor:** re-vendor `bin/board-stats` only. The shared lib (`bin/_kb-board-lib.sh`), `promote/action.yml` and `release-artifacts/action.yml` are untouched — this reads the paginator's existing rc contract and changes nothing in it.

- **card#6680** — **`kb_api` stops answering "the request did not complete" and "the server answered 500" with the same return code.** It returned **1** for a transport failure (curl exited non-zero — the request may never have arrived, and a write it DID deliver may already be committed) and **1** for any completed non-2xx (403/404/422/500 — the server answered, so the outcome is KNOWN and the status says why). One rc, two opposite epistemic states, and every caller in the tree buckets on a bare `|| …`. Measured through `kbcard`'s DL restamp pass: a 500 landed in the failed bucket and the run printed `kbcard: HTTP 500 on PATCH /tasks/9.json` and then, two lines later, `the request did not complete, so this run CANNOT say whether it landed` — the status the server answered with, followed by a denial that it answered. **`KB_HTTP` could not close this**: the caller shape this tree is built out of is `resp="$(kb_api …)" || …`, a command substitution whose subshell takes the global's assignment with it, which is why the signal has to ride the rc (asserted, so a future edit cannot move it back onto the global). **The rc now discriminates:** `0` a 2xx, **`1` THE SERVER ANSWERED non-2xx** (unchanged — this is what rc 1 meant on the path it was reached by most, so every `|| …`, `if !` and `if` bucket in every bin behaves byte for byte as before), and **`$KB_API_RC_TRANSPORT` THE REQUEST DID NOT COMPLETE**, with `KB_HTTP` at the `000` sentinel. Callers branch on the **name**, never the number; the number is pinned in the lib and is deliberately not `2` (every CLI here exits 2 for a usage error, so an rc propagated onward by a future `|| return $?` would read as one), not `3`–`5` (`fetch_board_cards`' separate rc vocabulary) and not curl's exit status, which is logged and never returned. **`kb_api_status` — the sibling — keeps `rc 0` and keeps its discriminator on the status line**, because `dl-a1-register-field` captures it with a bare assignment under `set -e` where any non-zero rc would kill the script before the status is read; `000` is now stated once as the sentinel BOTH functions use, and the transport arm sets `KB_HTTP` to it rather than leaving the previous request's status standing beside a status line saying `000`. **ONE call site changes behaviour: `board-stats`' changelog window** — the instance where the merged rc was already rendering a FABRICATED status. Its read was `resp="$(KB_API_QUIET=1 kb_api …)" || err="changelog read failed (HTTP ${KB_HTTP:-000}) …"`, and a command substitution is a subshell, so the `KB_HTTP` kb_api set inside it never reached that line: the status came from the lib's own empty initialiser and **every** failure rendered `HTTP 000` — the sentinel for a request that never completed — including the 403/500 the server ANSWERED, while `KB_API_QUIET=1` suppressed kb_api's own `HTTP 500 on GET …` line, so nothing anywhere in the run contradicted it. Measured before the fix, an answered 500 and an unopened connection produced the byte-identical `changelog read failed (HTTP 000) after 0 page(s)`, the 500 run with **empty stderr**. The page body now lands in a FILE rather than a `$(…)`, which is what lets `KB_HTTP` cross at all, and the arm branches on the rc: an answered non-2xx is `changelog read failed — the server ANSWERED HTTP 500`, a transport failure is `changelog read DID NOT COMPLETE … no answer was read, so this run cannot say why` — naming no status, because `000` is this state's own sentinel and not something the server sent. Every call here is a GET, so neither arm is a write-ambiguity. Asserted in `tests/board-stats-selftest.sh` as a DISTINCTION between the two messages in one run ("an error is reported" was already true of both, so it could not have failed on this defect), and watched RED under two mutants: the two messages merged back into the pre-fix line, and the body read put back through a `$(…)`. **`kbcard`'s restamp bucket is NOT split here** — that one changes what the pass reports to an operator about a WRITE, and is tracked on the card. **[vendor]** re-vendor `bin/_kb-board-lib.sh` **TOGETHER with `bin/board-stats`**: that bin now branches on `$KB_API_RC_TRANSPORT`, and under `set -u` a new `board-stats` beside an old lib dies on the unbound name at the first changelog read that fails. No other bin references the constant. **Two corrections in the tests this change touches, both to claims rather than to code.** `tests/fetch-board-cards-caller-claims-selftest.sh` cited `yes | head -c 50M | grep -q x` as the measurement behind *rc 141* for the SIGPIPE-eats-a-match shape it prescribes a herestring against — that command is **rc 1**: `yes` emits no `x`, so the grep never matches, never exits early, drains all 50MB and leaves its own `1` as the rightmost non-zero status, demonstrating nothing. The shape needs the reader to MATCH while the writer is still writing; `grep -q y` is the command that measures **141**, and the line now says so (the prescription itself was correct and is unchanged). `tests/kb-board-lib-selftest.sh` drops a companion assertion that `KB_HTTP` does not survive the caller's `$( … )`: a command substitution is a subshell by the language's definition, so no edit to this repo's code could red it — it passed against kb_api, against a kb_api that never touches the global, and against no kb_api at all. The half this repo DOES own, that kb_api sets the global to the `000` sentinel, is already asserted in the same block off a call that is not wrapped in `$( … )`, so restating it there would have been a second copy of one assertion rather than a second assertion.

- **card#6488** — **`release-artifacts-check` stops reading a real release PR as "version unchanged" when the version file carries a version-shaped line above its version line.** Classification is by version VALUE, and that value is the **first** `version_regex` match over a file **head owns** — so one prepended `# was 0.1.0` made the fork point and head extract the same value, the PR classified as `not a release PR`, and every declared member's leg silently did not run, at **rc 0**. Reproduced on the shipped script: an honest 0.1.0 → 0.2.0 release PR that forgets `NOTES.md` reds rc 1, and the same PR with one comment line above the version greens rc 0 — no config edit needed, which made it both a live defect on any repo whose version file grows a version-shaped comment and the cheapest evasion of the whole gate. The unsteerable half of the range is the **fork point's SET of matched values**, and a release must introduce a value there that head carries and the fork point does not, so on an equal classifying value the two sets are now compared — needing neither a content diff nor match positions: **exactly one value new at head NAMES the version being shipped** (a `::warning::` says so and every member is then asserted against it, so the gate *runs* rather than refusing), **more than one is rc 2** because which version is being shipped is then genuinely unknown, and **none is the ordinary unchanged verdict**. A value the fork point carries and head *drops* is a deletion, not a release, and deliberately does not enter the test — that is what keeps the predicate on "a value head introduced" rather than on "the version file changed at all", which would red every comment edit against a version nobody is shipping. **⚠ ONE CHANGE TO WHAT THE TOOL ACCEPTS OR REJECTS, fail-closed:** a PR whose version file gains a version-shaped value while its first match is unchanged was rc 0 and is now judged — rc 0/1 as a release of that value, or rc 2 where there are several. Nothing that classified as a release before classifies differently now. **The cost surface is the regex,** and it is priced in `docs/INSTALL.md` §6c: a `version_regex` matching more than the version declaration (a bare `[0-9.]+` rather than kanban's `'version' => '[0-9.]+'`) puts an ordinary PR in reach of the first rule, and the remedy — tighten it — must land in **its own PR**, since `version_regex` is read from the fork point. **Coverage:** the selftest's case 22 flips from pinning the defect (`rc 0`, `version unchanged (0.1.0)`) to pinning the fix, with the poisoned-but-otherwise-CORRECT release as the control that separates "classified as a release of 0.2.0" from "reds for some reason", three negatives that pin the direction (an echoed value, a dropped value, and one new value written twice) and the ambiguous rc-2 refusal. Five mutations of the new mechanism were each watched red and red exactly the checks they should: the test removed (pre-fix behaviour, 11), the direction flipped so dropped values count as new (12), the ambiguity classified instead of refused (2), the value list truncated to the first match (11), and the dedup removed (2). `release-artifacts/action.yml`'s `description:` CHANGED (its lockstep copy of the classification and fail-closed rules); `promote/action.yml` and every board-writing CLI are untouched.

- **card#6579** — **`release-promote-cards.yml` stops reporting a release as shipped before the release is tagged.** `auto-tag-version.yml` and `release-promote-cards.yml` both fire on `push: main` as **independent runs with no `needs:` between them**, so the promote side wins the race on **every** release — the board reached "Released to main" before the tag existed as normal operation, and nothing anywhere asserted the tag. On the **v0.28.0** merge that latent gap became a false claim: auto-tag's `git push origin v0.28.0` was refused (`remote: Permission to … denied to github-actions[bot]` / `403`, exit 128) at 01:59:46Z, and `release-promote-cards` reported `2 moved … 0 failed` and concluded **success** at 01:59:49Z. Board 12 asserted a shipped release for **17m27s**, until a human re-ran the job at 02:17:06Z; had nobody been watching it would have asserted it permanently, with the back-merge, the tag-pinned deploy (`git checkout v<version>`) and every consumer pinning the tag all reading a stale world. **The 403's mechanism is still unnamed and this does NOT fix it** — the failing job's own log shows `Contents: write` already granted, the same token had just fetched successfully, `rulesets?includes_parents=true` is `[]` with no legacy tag protection, the owner is a User account so no org policy layer exists, the workflow file was unchanged since the v0.24 era, and the identical push succeeded at the identical SHA on re-run; every repo-side cause is excluded by measurement and the refusal came from GitHub's authorization service. What ships here closes the **silence**, which is the part that actually bit. **New `bin/release-tag-check`** polls the remote for the expected tag and gates the promote job on it, wired as a step *before* promotion so a missing tag stops the board write rather than following it. It is **wait-then-verify, refuse on timeout**: an immediate hard-refuse would red every release (the race is the normal path), and a bare warning would be a line in a run log nobody reads — the same silence one level up. The bound is **300s, a stated cost decision, not a convergence claim**: auto-tag's entire job measured ~8s on the run that failed, so 300s is ~35x observed latency and absorbs runner-queue variance, and it is deliberately **not** sized to the incident's 17m27s, which was human reaction time — at the bound it refuses and fails loudly, because stamping "Released" late is recoverable and stamping it wrongly is not. A tag found at a **different** commit refuses immediately rather than waiting, since a tag does not move on its own. **The release classification is delegated, not re-derived:** `release-artifacts-check` gains `--classify-only`, exposing the repo's one definition of "this range is a release" (the fork-point version against head's, by VALUE, **including card#6488's arm that re-decides an equal classifying value** — the mode answers from the same variable the artifact legs are judged by, decided before either is answered, so the two consumers cannot disagree about what a release is) as a machine-readable `release <v>` / `not-release <v>` line at rc 0 for both, with every rc-2 refusal path unchanged and still fail-closed — a range that cannot be classified is never softened into "not a release", because that misclassification is a silent non-run of the whole gate. A second rule here would have been free to drift; the naive alternative ("a tag must point at every merge on the release branch") refuses any push whose tip is untagged, which over `main` at v0.29.0 (**denominator: 46 first-parent commits, 42 `v*` tags**) would have refused **4** of them — the root commit and three direct pushes predating the release flow. All **42** first-parent merges are tagged today, so the naive rule survives merges and fails only on direct pushes: one docs commit pushed to `main` reds a run that ships nothing. The bin's header carries the two-line command that RE-DERIVES those figures, because they move with the history. (An earlier draft of this entry and of the bin header said "false-alarmed on **all six** non-release merges"; that figure reproduces under **no** denominator on this repo — 4 of 46 first-parent commits, 0 of 42 first-parent merges, 55 of 97 merges across full ancestry — and is removed rather than restated. The design it was offered in support of is unchanged.) The mode **skips** the artifact legs rather than running and ignoring them, since those carry their own verdicts on every PR class. **`permissions:` are untouched — `contents: read` is unchanged and sufficient** (it reads refs and writes nothing); nothing here widens a token, and adding `contents: write` would have been a no-op, as the block has been present since v0.5.0. `tests/release-tag-check-selftest.sh` drives a real fixture repo with a real bare remote — no stubs — covering tag-present, tag-absent-past-the-bound (asserted on the **emitted message**, not the exit code alone), the v0.28.0 state reproduced literally, a **tag that arrives mid-poll** (the only case that distinguishes wait-then-verify from look-once), an annotated tag dereferenced to its commit, a wrong-commit tag refused without waiting, and both unclassifiable-range refusals; each was **watched red under a targeted mutation** (never polls · no classification · always passes · ignores where the tag points), and the non-release arm is driven with **no tag present at all** so it cannot pass by accident. **A READ HAS THREE OUTCOMES, AND THE FIRST CUT OF THIS TOOL HAD TWO** — the class `tests/read-outcome-collapse-selftest.sh` (card#7210) guards, re-minted here one commit after its own parent closed it at `install-board-hooks`. `git ls-remote`'s status was captured and then discarded (`FOUND="$(_remote_tag_sha || true)"`) and the survivor tested for emptiness, so *"the server answered and carries no such tag"* and *"I could not look"* became the same empty string, with git's own reason dropped by a `2>/dev/null`. Reproduced with the tag **genuinely present** and the remote made unreadable: `v0.28.0 does not exist on <remote> after 4s … The release merged and was NOT tagged — check the auto-tag-version workflow run`, rc 1 — the right refusal carrying a false cause, routing the operator to a workflow run that is green. **The rc is now the gate and git's stderr is kept.** Present / absent / **unreadable** are three states; an unreadable poll is retried and says so at the time (`this is NOT evidence that v0.28.0 is absent`), and at the bound it gets its OWN refusal — *the tag is UNMEASURED, not absent* — naming the read failure and its last error, with the tagging workflow not mentioned at all. Both refusals stay **rc 1** deliberately: the operator action is identical (do not report this shipped) and the defect was the cause NAMED, not the code returned. A run whose earlier polls failed but whose FINAL poll answered still reports the measured absence, with the failed-poll count and last error attached rather than dropped. **Each poll is now bounded** (`timeout 30 git ls-remote`, tunable with `--read-timeout`) and the wait is measured in **real elapsed time** instead of by counting sleeps — which is what makes `release-promote-cards.yml`'s stated ordering (*"the tool's own bound fires first … a timeout-minutes kill reports as `cancelled`, not `failure`"*) TRUE rather than merely asserted: no individual poll was bounded before, so one hung read could consume the job's whole `timeout-minutes: 10` and produce exactly the `cancelled` outcome that comment claims to avoid. `timeout` is probed, not assumed; where it is absent the poll is unbounded and the run says so on a `::warning::`. **`--interval 0` is now refused**: it passed every non-negative test and then polled with no pause at all — measured on the pre-fix binary as an `ls-remote` flood that never advanced its own clock and had to be killed from outside (rc 124 under an external 8s timeout). `--timeout 0` stays legal and means "look once". **New coverage, each watched red under a targeted mutation:** an unreadable remote carrying a **presence witness** that the tag really is on the real remote (so a "not tagged" verdict there is a false claim, not an empty fixture), with a readable-remote control and a measured-absence control beside it — 8 checks red when the collapse is restored; a **real hang** (an `ext::sleep 20` remote helper) killed by the bound in 2s, with elapsed time as the discriminating control — 21s, and red, when the bound is removed; and a **mixed** run whose remote appears mid-poll by an atomic rename, asserting both that the resulting absence is measured AND that the failed polls are still counted, with an all-answered control beside it so the caveat cannot be printed unconditionally. **`--classify-only` also stops telling a repo that declares NO artifacts that it "declares artifacts but no version_file".** The mode skips the empty-set opt-out (the artifact legs are not its question), so a config carrying `"artifacts": []` reached the classification-key check and died rc 2 on a false premise — for a vendored adopter, the entire diagnosis they get when the promote job reds on a key they never set. The refusal stays rc 2 (a range that cannot be classified must never read as "not a release"), and the message now says those keys are what the MODE requires, whether or not any artifact is declared; it claims nothing about the artifact set either way, because under this mode the set is never read. And `release-tag-check` **relays the classifier's own explanation** instead of swallowing it: where card#6488's arm re-decides an equal classifying value it emits a `::warning::` naming the value it chose, and that value is the tag being asserted — the note now lands in the push's log, not only on the PR gate. **Fleet note: the same two-workflow shape exists on `agent-webhook-bridge` and `kanban-board` (3 instances of one class).** Only the toolkit is changed here; the other two are deliberately deferred to **card#7203** rather than fixed in triplicate. `kanban-board` is private and its `rulesets` read returned **403, not empty**, so its configuration is **unmeasured, not clean**. **⚠ This makes a previously-silent condition a RED release run** — a release whose tag genuinely fails to appear now fails the promote job instead of stamping the board; that is the intent, and the remedy is to fix the tag, not to re-run past the check. **Consumer impact — two, and the second one touches every seat.** (1) `bin/` is a vendored surface: re-vendor `release-artifacts-check` alongside `release-tag-check` if you adopt it, since the tool delegates to its sibling in the same `bin/` rather than to whatever a PATH lookup finds. (2) **`agent-board-toolkit-runtime-check` now names `release-tag-check` in its `TOOLS` array**, and a tool the runtime ships that PATH lacks is an incomplete install, not an unused one — so every existing host install emits `runtime $root ships release-tag-check but PATH lacks them — re-run the install symlink loop (INSTALL.md §2)` until §2's loop is re-run. It is a **⚠ warning, not a failure** (`runtime-check` exits 0 on warnings), but `board-snapshot` runs it at SessionStart, so it is a line every seat sees on every session until the symlink is created: `for t in ~/agent-board-toolkit/bin/*; do ln -sf "$t" ~/.local/bin/"$(basename "$t")"; done; hash -r`. A **copies** install (Windows/MSYS, or any `cp` install) needs the same loop re-run for the same reason — see §2's own warning.

- **card#6871** — **the `bin/*.py` entry points stop minting `__pycache__` inside the directory a consumer symlinks onto PATH.** `docs/INSTALL.md` §2 installs the toolkit by globbing `bin/*` and linking every entry onto PATH one by one, so **every entry of `bin/` is a PATH entry** and one that is not a tool is a defect the moment it exists. Three helpers were creating one on every run: `_kbc-may-archive.py`, `_kbc-archive-eligible.py` and `_kbc-stale-blocker.py` path-load `_kbc-archive-lib.py` with `importlib.util.spec_from_file_location`, and a by-path load **caches the compiled bytecode beside its target** — measured, one read-only `--help`-shaped invocation at rc 0 wrote `bin/__pycache__/_kbc-archive-lib.cpython-312.pyc` onto a clean tree. **A second directory was taking the same writes and is not even ours — by TWO routes needing two different fixes:** the shared lib path-loads the framework plugin's `kanban_common.py`, so a `__pycache__` appeared beside it under `~/.claude/plugins/…` (closed in-process by the same guard line); and `board-session-close` hands the plugin's **own** `kanban-reconcile.py` to `python3`, and that file `sys.path.insert`s its own directory and imports a sibling by name, caching `_stdio.cpython-312.pyc` into the plugin's `hooks/bin/` (reproduced directly). That file is foreign and cannot carry the guard line, but this repo owns the **invocation**, so that call now passes `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`. Downstream, the framework's install arm symlinked `bin/__pycache__` onto PATH and then refused the whole directory on the next run (that half is the framework's, roundtable #292 / its card#6865); on this side, `tests/lib-set-derivation-selftest.sh` had already had to grow a `grep -d skip` so its verdict would not depend on whether the maintainer had run a python helper. **`__pycache__/` was already gitignored, which is correct and is also what let this sit** — an ignored artifact is invisible to `git status` while still being globbed by every consumer of `bin/*`; nothing was ever tracked, so nothing is un-tracked here. **The fix is `sys.dont_write_bytecode = True` in each entry point's own module body**, and the rule is stated over ENTRY POINTS rather than over load sites because a module cannot suppress its own `.pyc` (the loader writes the cache while it *compiles*, ahead of running the module body) — so `_dependabot-reconcile.py` carries the line too though it path-loads nothing today, and `_kbc-archive-lib.py` does not, because there it could not work. These are one-shot CLI invocations, so the cache bought nothing: rc and output are asserted byte-identical either side of the change — for every entry point on its refusal path, and for one helper on a path that reaches the primitive and returns a decision, with the board-reading paths named as out of reach offline rather than implied. **New `tests/bin-artifact-hygiene-selftest.sh`** derives the whole `bin/*.py` population by glob on every run and — the part that decides whether it can actually go red — derives the **exemption** too, rather than pattern-matching it: a file is excused only if some other `bin/*.py` names it AND it carries no `__main__` marker at all, and that exemption's premise is then tested dynamically (a by-path load caches the `.pyc` regardless, so a guard line inside a library could not help). Anything else must carry the line, and a file the classifier recognises as neither is reported UNCLASSIFIED and **reds**. A first cut keyed on the single exact spelling `if __name__ == "__main__":`, which err-GREEN in the worst direction — a helper using single quotes, or with top-level code and no `__main__` at all, was filed as a library, excused, and dropped out of every dynamic leg as well.
