The forward-looking note this paragraph used to carry — that refusing on all three causes needed them separated first, or `next-dl` would break on every offline or board-less checkout — is retired there on two grounds: the offline breakage is now the ruled-for behaviour, and the board-less half was never true, because a board-less checkout fails at `resolve_board_cfg` and never reaches a paginator rc. Closed outright at `board-card-start`, which previously fell through to a co-present `card#NNNN` token and issued **two PATCHes** — stamping `payload.dl_number` and moving the card — off a board read it had not been able to perform; it now skips.

  **`kbcard archive`'s twin check swallows the rc by design** (`|| true`, then `board="[]"`), so its behaviour is unchanged — and the direction, previously recorded as safe by *contract* in two places, is now measured: the same card with `surviving_cards: []` returns `blocked — source unresolvable … and no surviving twin — archive withheld (fail-closed)` where a populated census returns `ok`. A failed board read there can only withhold more archives, never permit one.

  **Two residuals, named rather than implied — one of them closed later in this same release.** A *later* page's unreadable body read as `[]` and ended the scan; on any server that DECLARES `meta.total` the pre-existing short-read census caught it at **rc 4** with a loud `INCOMPLETE`, so the silent case required a server that omits it. This API declared `meta.total` on every probe taken for this card — an observation, not a guarantee, and the same unproven *"always"* card #4623 recorded for `last_page`. That half existed in both copies of the loop and is **closed by card#6630 below** (which also corrects what *caught at rc 4* was worth: rc 4 emits its partial array, so the rendering consumers rendered a truncated board). And this API answers a board the token cannot see with the *same* well-formed empty envelope as a board with no cards, so that case still reads as an empty board here. **The API behaviour is shared with the mirror; the consequence is not.** `bin/promote-released-cards`'s `fetch_whole_board` does refuse it — it dies on zero *cards*, and its message names board membership as the likely cause — which it can afford because for a mover an empty board is never a working state. `fetch_board_cards` must not adopt that predicate: `kbcard list` on a genuinely empty board has to succeed, so a zero-row refusal would refuse a legitimate read. Closing it needs a signal the envelope does not carry (membership), not a stricter row count.

  **Every caller-side claim about WHY the paginator failed is now governed by one written rule, instead of the per-message tally this entry used to carry.** Giving rc 1 a third cause turned the caller messages that enumerated the old two into a wrong diagnosis — one that sends an operator to check a network and a token which both answered. Those messages were then corrected **one site per round, for three rounds, each round's own report finding another**: the sixth was `board-card-start`'s arm, which an earlier round had itself rewritten into the *cause-naming* shape while giving `board-snapshot`'s identical situation the *rc-naming* shape — the same situation, two answers, in one commit — and the seventh was `next-dl`'s second arm, which enumerated causes for three rcs at once and named none of them. Each individual fix was correct, reviewed and green, which is what made the miss comfortable. So the rule is written down rather than re-applied:

  - an arm reached by **exactly one** paginator rc may name that rc's causes — the rc contract closes them;
  - an arm reached by **more than one** names the **rc** (`fetch rc=N`) and **no cause**, because no cause set is true across the rcs it catches. A shared *outcome* word — "did not return a complete card list", `INCOMPLETE` — is not a cause and is allowed, because it holds for every non-zero rc.

  `bin/_kb-board-lib.sh`'s `fetch_board_cards` header **owns** the rule, beside the rc contract it is derived from; that contract carried a compact SECOND copy two lines above the full one, and the two had already drifted (only the compact one named **rc 4** — the rc every bare `||` arm silently catches), so it is now stated once. `tests/fetch-board-cards-caller-claims-selftest.sh` owns the per-consumer **dispositions** — which rcs reach which arm, measured by driving each real bin as a process over all seven paginator outcomes — and **re-derives both the invocation list AND the arms from the tree on every run**, so a new consumer in any call spelling, a new call inside an existing consumer, a new arm at an already-registered call, or a reworded arm reds until it is ruled on — within the bounds that file states for itself. That leg is what was missing: nothing in the suite could see the class, so it was rediscovered by reading, three times.

  **⚠ Operator-visible wording changes at SIX sites, measured against v0.28.0 — the baseline this entry documents.** Five on stderr plus one on `board-snapshot`'s stdout snapshot line. Wording only at every one of the six: no rc, no control flow, no new exit status, no change to what anything accepts or rejects. That is deliberately not the broader claim — the acceptance change above is real and is this entry's headline: against v0.28.0 a page-1 `2xx` carrying no card array moves from **rc 0 + `[]`** to **rc 1 + no output**, and every caller's behaviour on that one outcome moves with it. What was measured **byte-identical** across six bins × seven paginator outcomes — every rc, every request count, every emitted line — is the final class round alone (`0b4f17d` → head), which is a mid-change baseline and not this entry's. Both are named rather than blended, because quoting a figure taken against one of them as though it held against the other is exactly how the first version of this paragraph got its arithmetic wrong — the same trap the `card#6426` entry above records falling into. Old → new:

  - **`bin/board-card-start`** — `board <id> fetch failed (unreachable or auth rejected)` → `board <id> did not return a complete card list (fetch rc=N)`. Reaches the operator on stderr **and** in `board-card-start.log`, which is the record that survives the installed hook wrapper's `>/dev/null 2>&1`.
  - **`bin/kbcard`** (`list`) — `kbcard: failed to read board <id>` → `kbcard: board <id> did not return a complete card list (fetch rc=N)`. This entry previously recorded it as deliberately left alone for being cause-free, and that ruling judged only the cause half of a rule nobody had yet written: it names no cause still, and now names the rc its four reachable outcomes differ by.
  - **`bin/next-dl`** (the rc-1 arm) — `board unreachable — skipping board check` → `board <id> could not be read at all (no response, a non-2xx status, or a 2xx carrying no card array) — refusing to mint from the local scan alone (would drop this board's DL floor and could re-mint a used DL)`. Gated to exactly one rc, so it is the one arm that MAY name causes; it names all three the rc-1 contract lists, where it named the old two. The arm is **no longer fail-soft**: this card's own round left it saying `— skipping board check`, and **card#6631**, in this same unreleased section, turned it into the refusal above. The literal recorded here is the one that ships from this section; `skipping board check` survives in the tool only on `next-dl`'s three `resolve_board_cfg` refusals, which are a different arm.
  - **`bin/next-dl`** (the refusal arm) — `board read incomplete or over NEXT_DL_PAGE_CAP — refusing to mint from a partial scan (could re-mint a used DL)` → `board <id> did not return a complete card list (fetch rc=N) — refusing …`.
  - **`bin/board-snapshot`** — `• <label>: (board API unreachable)` → `• <label>: (board read failed — fetch rc=N)`. On **stdout**, not stderr: this line IS the board's snapshot row.
  - **`bin/dl-a0-backfill-triaged`** — `board <id> read was incomplete or unreachable — aborting (no partial backfill)` → `board <id> did not return a complete card list (fetch rc=N) — aborting (no partial backfill)`.

  **`bin/board-stats` is the only consumer whose wording never moved** — both its `case` arms already named the rc and no cause, and rewriting a correct message is a defect. `bin/kbcard`'s archive-twin site has no wording to move: it swallows every rc (`|| true`, `2>/dev/null`) and makes no operator-facing claim at all. Separately from the six messages, **eight caller-side COMMENT blocks** that state which rcs a caller sees, or which causes reach an arm, were rewritten — three in `kbcard`, three in `next-dl`, one in `dl-a0-backfill-triaged`, one in `board-card-start` — and the wording rule now binds such a comment explicitly, because the eighth was minted by the very commit that wrote the rule. ⚠ The round-by-round narrative two paragraphs above counts corrections **as they landed across intermediate commits**, and counts message and comment sites together; its figures are a different denominator from these six and are not interchangeable with them.

  **Docs converged rather than re-synced:** `docs/CONSOLIDATION-PLAN.md`'s "the two implementations disagree on the safety branch" note is **deleted** — the safety branch is closed in both — and replaced by a pointer plus the one deliberate difference and a warning not to converge the predicates; the `_kbc_list_project` sub-entry and `bin/kbcard`'s header comment, which both described the pre-fix `[]`-at-rc-0 behaviour, are brought up to date at the same time. The header comment is corrected by **deleting** its statement of what a `[]` from `list` means and pointing at README § Reliability posture, which that same block already names as its one owner: the statement is still true and still nuanced (a *filtered* `[]`, and a board the token cannot see, are both still `[]`), and a synchronized third copy of it is how it would go stale. README § Reliability posture states the new quantifier and both residuals.

  **Coverage:** new legs in `tests/kb-board-lib-selftest.sh` (`== fetch_board_cards: an unreadable 2xx is not an empty board (card#6594) ==`), each paired with a well-formed-body control on the same route, and each watched **red** against the pre-change lib restored from a byte snapshot and verified with `cmp`. Plus a `tests/board-snapshot-selftest.sh` leg pinning that the snapshot's failure line names the paginator **rc** and asserts no cause, over both rcs that arm catches (1 and 2) with a rc-0 render as the control — watched red against the old `(board API unreachable)` wording. Plus `tests/dl-a0-backfill-triaged-selftest.sh`, whose two fail-closed legs now pin the **rc** its abort carries — 1 on a page-1 HTTP failure, 4 on a short read — watched red against the old `read was incomplete or unreachable` wording, and which gains a third leg for the page-1 unreadable-body cause it never covered, watched red against the pre-change lib restored from a byte snapshot and verified with `cmp` (under it the tool exits **0** and announces a target count over a board it never read). The class round adds `tests/fetch-board-cards-caller-claims-selftest.sh`, which carries the rule's controls (the pre-fix `board-card-start` wording as the negative control, the prescribed shape as the positive, an outcome-word control so a conforming `INCOMPLETE` line is not demanded rewritten, a hardcoded-rc control, and a novel-cause control that must FAIL the outcome allow-list), the re-derived denominator, and the per-arm verdicts. **Both halves of that denominator are derived — and the second half only because review measured the first half's gaps.** The invocation predicate was `fetch_board_cards "`, which is a question about the QUOTING and not about the call: a real consumer written `fetch_board_cards $api $token $board` was shellcheck-clean and passed green. And the ARM table was hand-listed, so a new cause-naming arm inserted at an already-registered call moved no derived number and also passed — the original card#6594 defect wording, invisible to the guard built to see its class. The predicate is now a comment-stripped word boundary, and the arms are derived too: every emitting line on a call's own line or within ten code lines after it must resolve to a registered arm or to an explicitly ruled exemption, and every registered arm must still be found INSIDE that window (so the window cannot quietly stop covering one). Its bounds are stated in the file rather than implied — an arm beyond the window, an arm emitting through a helper outside the derived vocabulary, and COMMENTS, which the rule now binds and which no test covers, are each named as uncovered. **Thirteen mutations** were each watched **red** on the leg that owns them — each of the three reworded bins and the lib header reverted from a byte snapshot, a registry row regressed to the pre-fix wording, an extra invocation added inside an already-registered consumer, a wholly new consumer bin, a cause dropped from the rc-1 arm, and the five the review round added: a new cause-naming arm ahead of `board-snapshot`'s existing one, a new consumer hook whose call is unquoted (shellcheck-clean; red on four legs), a registered arm pushed past the window, a multi-rc arm reworded to an unruled outcome word, and a duplicated rc-contract line — every restore verified with `cmp`, none by `git checkout`. Registration in the `ci.yml` matrix was confirmed load-bearing by watching `ci-matrix-parity-selftest` name the file `unrun` with the entry removed. The reworded diagnostics have no cheap in-process seam and are measured rather than pinned there: **against the class round's own baseline (`0b4f17d`)**, all six bins were driven as real processes over all seven paginator outcomes, before and after, and the whole matrix diffed — the only differences are that round's three intended message lines. That matrix was **not** re-run against v0.28.0; what differs there is not measured in this paragraph but stated above — the six message sites, plus the acceptance change this entry leads with.

## [0.28.0] - 2026-08-14

**Minor — `install-board-hooks` measures whether the seat it is installing onto can actually keep a symlink and refuses rather than report a healthy install over hook copies; `kbcard` gains the card-comment verbs; `bin/board-stats` arrives; and the release-artifact gate stops reading the verdict surface out of a file the PR under judgment can edit.** 11 PRs since v0.27.0 (#234–#244): nine card-carrying, plus two docs-only solo-orientation syncs (coord v0.34.0 in #235, coord v0.35.0 in #243). **Minor** per `VERSIONING.md § Bump sizing` — a new `bin/` tool, two new `kbcard` verbs, and a new flag plus new refusal exits on `install-board-hooks`.

**⚠ UPGRADING — four actions, all in [`docs/UPGRADE.md`](UPGRADE.md) §6 `v0.28.0`.** (1) **Re-run the `PATH` symlink loop** (`VERSIONING.md` step 11): this release ADDS a bin, and a symlink tracks its target's *content*, not the *set* of tools, so `board-stats` never lands on `PATH` without it. (2) **Re-run `install-board-hooks <repo>` on every seat** — a seat that installed hooks under the old behaviour may already be running copies that no `git pull` has ever reached, and nothing in the old logs distinguishes that from a healthy install; the new run either succeeds unchanged or refuses and tells you which case you are in. Use `--allow-copies` only where it refuses, and know it exits **3**, never 0. (3) **`install-board-hooks --check` is no longer write-free** — it runs the probe, which creates and removes two `.ibhp*` entries in the hooks directory (or its nearest existing ancestor). (4) **[release-CI] re-pin the `release-artifacts/` composite action, and read the new behaviour before you do:** an *ordinary* PR that drops a declared `artifacts` entry now reds unless the removal is acknowledged in a `retired_artifacts` array — the gate no longer asserts only on release PRs.

- **card#6527** — **`install-board-hooks` MEASURES symlink capability before it installs anything, and refuses rather than pin a seat to hook copies that report as a healthy install.** The hooks are installed by symlink, and that symlink IS the upgrade contract: a `git pull` in the toolkit checkout reaches every installed hook only because each hook entry points at the source. On a symlink-incapable seat the POSIX-emulation layer does not fail `ln -s` — measured on a consumer's Windows seat (Developer Mode off, non-elevated): it returns **rc 0 having substituted a COPY**. The install then reported plain success while that seat ran hook copies no upgrade would ever reach again, and the two outcomes were **byte-identical in every log this tool writes** — an operator reading the printed remediation was being actively misled by a success report. The installer now runs `_ibh_symlink_probe` against the target directory (or, where the hooks directory does not exist yet, the nearest existing ancestor — the directory `mkdir -p` is about to create it under, so the same filesystem and the same process privilege) **before the first write**, and disposes on three verdicts: **CAPABLE** installs symlinks (unchanged: an existing caller on a capable seat sees byte-identical behaviour); **NOT_CAPABLE** refuses at **rc 1**, in the shape and register of this tool's existing refusals (the tracked in-tree `core.hooksPath` one), naming the opt-in; **INDETERMINATE** refuses at **rc 4**.

  **The dispatch on the probe's status fails closed by DEFAULT rather than by enumeration:** only `0` selects the install and only `1` selects the not-capable refusal, and every other status takes that same exit-4 refusal — it used to enumerate `2` and let anything else fall through to the capable install path, which would have installed symlinks on the strength of a status nobody mapped. Those statuses exist: the probe's own `INT`/`TERM` traps exit `130`/`143`, and a signal they do not catch reads back off the command substitution as `129`/`137`. No new message, no new verdict, no new exit status — the default simply moved to the arm that refuses. The probe tests the property the contract actually needs, not a proxy: the entry must be a symlink AND must still deliver the source's content after the source is **replaced** (a new inode — what `git pull` does to a hook file, not a truncate-and-rewrite), so a link to a snapshot and a hard link are both caught. **The same default governs the two-reader case as well:** an unmapped `git:shell` pair now lands on `INDETERMINATE reason=unmapped-reader-pair` instead of surviving on the `CAPABLE`/`ok` initialiser the function opens with. It is **unreachable as written** — both variables are only ever assigned `no` or `yes` — so it closes no live hole; it makes the enumeration's default match its intent, which is what any arm added there later inherits. **And a refusal on the fail-closed arm no longer quotes an empty verdict:** the arm prints the probe's one-line report, and a probe KILLED before it reports has none (its own `INT`/`TERM` traps exit `130`/`143` ahead of the report line — the very statuses the fail-closed default made reachable), so it rendered `could not DETERMINE … ()` and then named four local faults, not one of which can be the cause. The empty case now says the measurement was interrupted rather than completed and drops that list; the arm, the exit status and the rc vocabulary are unchanged.

  **And it reads the replaced content TWICE, with two different readers, because on the seat this exists for they disagree.** Reading the entry from the shell is precisely what the emulation layer defeats: under `MSYS=winsymlinks:lnk` it creates a Windows `.lnk` shortcut that its own `cat` and `test -L` both resolve, while `git.exe` — the binary that actually dispatches hooks — opens the file and reads shortcut bytes, so a shell-only probe answers **CAPABLE** there. That is the silent degrade this card exists to close, reproduced inside the detector. The second reader is therefore git's own binary (`git hash-object --no-filters`, adding no dependency — the tool already invokes `git` unconditionally to read `core.hooksPath`; `--no-filters` because the probe's two paths differ in suffix and an attribute-selected clean filter applied to one and not the other would report a capable seat as NOT_CAPABLE, measured on git 2.43.0), and neither answer is discarded: **`no`/`yes` is `readers-disagree-native-git-does-not-track`** (the `lnk` signature) and **`yes`/`no` is `readers-disagree-shell-does-not-track`** (refused too — hooks execute under that shell), both NOT_CAPABLE, while the both-agree tokens are unchanged. Dropping `cat` and keeping only git would have refused the `lnk` seat without ever saying why; the token records the DIRECTION so **one** run on a real seat identifies the mode. A git that fails to READ (absent from `PATH`, a dangling entry, an unreadable repository context) is **`git-read-failed`, INDETERMINATE** — a failed measurement is not a fact about the seat, and scoring it NOT_CAPABLE would route an operator to `--allow-copies` over a local git fault. **That native read is pinned to the probed entry's own directory (`git -C "$(dirname …)"`) — the directory whose capability is being measured, so the answer no longer depends on where the caller stands.** A bare `git hash-object` resolves a repository context from the CALLER's cwd, and a BROKEN one there — a `.git` gitfile naming a directory that does not exist — is fatal: rc 128, read as `git-read-failed`, so the same command refused the whole install at rc 4 from one cwd and installed both hooks from another, with the refusal naming four faults that were none of them the cause and no way for the operator to see why re-running changed nothing. Three cwds had been measured and answer identically (outside a repository, inside a work tree, and from within a `.git/hooks` directory); the broken context had not been, and it was the one that differed. **The pin MOVES that fatal context rather than removing one, and the residual is disclosed rather than left to be rediscovered:** a broken context at the caller's cwd is now harmless (measured: the probe answers CAPABLE and the install exits 0), while the probed entry's *own* directory carrying a broken `.git` gitfile is now the fatal case (measured: the native read fails → `git-read-failed` → INDETERMINATE → the install refuses at **rc 4**) — fail-closed, and the rc-4 text already names "a git that cannot read the probe's own entries" among the faults to fix. What the pin is *not* is the cwd git dispatches a hook from: measured on git 2.43.0, a hook runs with cwd = the **work-tree root** (`githooks(5)`: `$GIT_DIR` in a bare repository, the root of the working tree otherwise), which is a different directory from the hooks directory and, under a `core.hooksPath` pointing outside the repo, not even the same repository. Filters stay off, so the pinned directory's own attributes change no answer either. Its one-line `VERDICT=… DIR=… REASON=…` output and its exit status come from **one** mapping, so the line an operator reads and the branch the caller takes cannot disagree, and `ln`'s own words go to stderr verbatim rather than being folded into a token.

  **The opt-in is `--allow-copies`,** and it is a permit, not a force: on a CAPABLE seat it changes nothing and symlinks are installed, so an operator carrying it in a shared script cannot silently pin a capable seat. A run that really installs copies prints `COPY <dst> <= <src>` per hook, states the re-install-after-every-upgrade obligation with the `rm` recipe the clobber guard requires, and exits **3 — never 0**. What no longer exists is the silent third state: an outcome that produces copies and reports what producing symlinks reports.

  **Two rulings, recorded because the alternatives were live.** (1) *Degrade-and-declare as the DEFAULT was rejected* — installing copies with a warning keeps the seat working, but a warning on a hook install is read once and the pin it announces lasts until someone independently notices a card that stopped moving; the failure this closes is one of *reporting*, so the default has to be the one disposition that cannot be misread, and the operator who wants copies now says so and gets an exit status their script can see. (2) *INDETERMINATE fails closed, and `--allow-copies` does not unblock it* — "we could not determine" collapsed into "we know you cannot" would re-mint, one size smaller, the information loss this change exists to end; and the flag opts into a **known** degradation (recorded in the operator's own paper trail), never into an unknown state. Every INDETERMINATE reason is a local, actionable fault — an unwritable or unreadable directory, a probe-name collision, an unlink that did not take, a `git` that cannot read the probe's own entries — and the refusal names it.

  **`--check` performs the probe too, and its header now says so**: the dry run creates and removes the probe's two `.ibhp*` entries and installs nothing else, because a `--check` that skipped the measurement would answer "this would succeed" on exactly the seat where the real run refuses — and its consumer, `board-session-close`'s remedy line, would print that refusal as the fix. Its stdout contract is unchanged (the target directory, nothing else) and its **status** now carries the verdict, so a would-be copies install answers 3 there as well. Also fixed while wiring it: a copies install `rm`s the destination **before** `cp`, since `cp` over an existing symlink writes THROUGH it — re-installing over a previous symlink install would have rewritten this toolkit's own hook source — and the symlink path now asserts the entry it just created **is** a symlink, which is the reported defect measured on the entry git will really dispatch rather than on the probe's stand-in. The three usage strings collapse onto one `_IBH_USAGE` owner.

  **Coverage, and its limits, stated plainly: the real surface is a Windows seat and none of this was run on one.** `tests/install-board-hooks-capability-selftest.sh` (new, CI-registered) forces all three verdicts with stub commands on `PATH` — an `ln` that reports success and copies (the measured shape), one that fails, one that yields a real symlink to a snapshot, and an `rm` whose unlink no-ops — each stub positive-controlled before it is relied on, with the capable baseline asserted on the same tree so "refuses everything" cannot pass. A stub is evidence about the stub. So `tests/install-board-hooks-capability-windows-check.sh` ships as the thing a real Windows seat runs: **no stubs**, no network, no configuration, no path outside its own temp dir, a printed seat report, per-verdict assertions, and one invariant asserted on every seat — *no run that exits 0 leaves a hook that is not a tracking symlink*, proven by replacing the source and reading it back through the installed entry.

  **It also gained the leg that stops INFERRING dispatch from a read, and that leg is the arbiter:** it makes a hook entry with the seat's own `ln -s` (deliberately not via the installer, which on a refused seat would create nothing and leave the one question worth spending that seat's run on unasked), replaces the hook source the way an upgrade does, runs a real `git checkout`, and reports which content actually **executed** — `tracks` / `frozen` / `broken`, with its own control firing the hook *before* the replacement so an unfired hook can never read as a pass. **That replacement is an unlink-and-create, never a truncate-in-place, and the distinction is this leg's whole authority:** it is allowed to overrule the probe, so it has to test the property the probe tests. A hard-linked entry follows a truncate and never sees a replacement, so a truncating arbiter reports `tracks` on a seat the probe correctly REFUSES — raising its “the refusal is WRONG” banner over a correct refusal, i.e. spending the one Windows dispatch there is to spend on a wrong instruction. One `_replace_file` owner performs the replacement for both this leg and the read-based tracking test, which already did it correctly. **And `broken` makes no pairing claim at all:** it means the control fired and then NOTHING executed, so no dispatched content was observed — folded in with `frozen` as “does not track” it asserted an equivalence it had not measured and printed “a real `git checkout` dispatched the OLD content”, which had not happened. It now lands on the same “no pairing assertion was made” arm as any other unknown. The seat report now also prints `command -v git` (the native reader's claim is about *which binary* answers, so it is audited rather than assumed) and `core.symlinks` as `git init` itself wrote it. The pair is restated on one `SEAT DISPATCH:` line, and the disagreement that matters is spelled out on the seat rather than left as a bare FAIL: a seat the probe **refuses** whose dispatch nonetheless delivers the replacement means **the refusal is wrong there** — the hypothesis being that Git-for-Windows dispatches hooks through a bundled `sh` that resolves what raw `git.exe` does not — and that outcome is worth more than a green run. Its NOT_CAPABLE arm and **all three** of the arbiter's MEASURING classifications are executed from the capability selftest **before** the real seat is spent, so a scripting error cannot consume the single Windows dispatch: `frozen` under a forced-incapable `PATH`; `frozen` again under an `ln -s` that yields a HARD LINK — the one seat on which truncating a source and replacing it give different answers, and therefore the only thing that reds a truncating arbiter; `broken` under a `git` whose second `checkout` silently no-ops; and — the branch the single Windows run exists to obtain, and the one that had never been executed at all — `probe=no` with `dispatch=yes`, under a `git` whose `hash-object` never answers twice the same, where the check REDS by design and the banner, not the exit status, is the deliverable. Its two NON-measurement states are pre-flighted too, so **every** classification the arbiter can reach has been executed before the seat is spent: `no-entry` (the seat's `ln -s` refused outright — rc 0, and the arm's own "then the probe must have refused too" assertion passes) and `control-failed` (the hook did not fire even before the replacement — which **reds by design**, exactly one FAIL, because a leg that measured nothing must not read as a pass, and it makes no pairing claim). That last leg is also what binds the two files that spell `readers-disagree-native-git-does-not-track`: the bin sets the token, the windows check keys its mechanism paragraph on it, and nothing else asserted the pair — so a rename would have kept every existing check green while silently dropping that paragraph from the one Windows run. It is deliberately not a `*-selftest.sh` (that glob is the CI matrix-parity registry's population, and a matrix entry with no matching file reads as dangling), so the capability selftest **invokes** it as its own anti-rot leg. Against the pre-change installer on a forced incapable seat it reds exactly where it should: `rc=0` with hooks that are neither symlinks nor tracking.

  **A refusal is now all-or-nothing.** The two per-entry refusals — an existing non-symlink hook, a symlink to a directory — are decided over the **whole** hook set in a pre-pass before the first write. They used to run inside the install loop, so a set whose *second* hook was one the operator wrote exited **1 with the first hook already installed**: on the copies branch that is a copy on disk under a refusal status, with the exit-3 summary that carries the `rm` recipe and the re-run obligation never printed, so a script gating on the status read a clean refusal. `--check` now returns from one place immediately before that first write rather than skipping each write in turn.

  **The probe cleans up after a signal too:** its two `.ibhp*` entries were removed on every path the code takes but not on the paths a *signal* takes, while its header claimed "every path" — and `--check` performs that same write into a directory the operator never asked to have written. A `trap … EXIT INT TERM` closes it, armed **after** the probe-name-collision check, because that check deliberately declines to touch entries it did not create and an earlier trap would delete them.

  **Three behaviours, three checks watched to fail** — including the per-entry post-`ln` assertion, which nothing could red before: reproducing it needs an `ln` that is capable for the probe's `.ibhp<n>.d` destination and copies only for one named `post-checkout`, since by construction that branch runs only after a CAPABLE verdict. Every stub is now positive-controlled, the two later ones included.

  **Named residuals — two, each stated rather than left to be rediscovered.** (1) **It needs its own card:** `board-session-close`'s `_bsc_manual_wiring` still emits a bare `ln -s <toolkit>/hooks/<h> <dir>/<h>` as its hand-wiring remedy, which on the seat class this whole change exists for **silently yields a copy** — the same permanent pin the installer now refuses, one surface over. `_bsc_fix_line` reaches it from **two** branches, neither decided by capability: the topology one (an installer that would target nothing — a linked worktree), and the one taken when the installer itself is not executable, whose own comment already names the Windows/MSYS copy topology as where that happens. Untouched here (out of this change's scope); the fix is to consult the capability verdict, or to say plainly that the `ln -s` must be verified to have produced a symlink. (2) **The failed-READ rule is applied to reader 2 and not to reader 1 — filed as ONE class item on card#6572 (board 12, Backlog): the rule across BOTH readers, not as two site fixes.** The doctrine this card establishes is that a read which FAILS is the measurement not happening: INDETERMINATE, never a measured absence. `_ibh_native_hash` implements it, and reader 1 does not — `cat -- "$dst" 2>/dev/null || :` swallows `cat`'s status, so an entry `cat` cannot read is scored as content that did not match, the pair reads `yes:no`, and the verdict is NOT_CAPABLE (`readers-disagree-shell-does-not-track`) — routing the operator to `--allow-copies`, a degradation, over a local fault, which is precisely what the doctrine forbids. It is **pre-existing** (present before this card's first commit) and low-reachability; what this change adds is a wrong diagnosis attached to it, not the hole. Deliberately not fixed in this round: the fix is the rule at both readers, and a one-site patch would leave the class open under a green suite.

- **card#6055** — **`release-artifacts-check` read the set it judges a PR by out of a file that same PR can edit, so a PR could weaken its own verdict; the declared set and the classification keys now come from the FORK POINT, and the one narrowing channel is a declared, logged `retired_artifacts` key.** Four shapes rode on the head-only read, and none of them left a trace: **(E1)** delete an `artifacts` entry while still updating the file it named — every remaining leg passes, rc 0, and from the next release the fork point no longer carries the entry either, so the narrowing is permanent and silent; **(E2)** retarget `version_file`/`version_regex` at a constant present at both ends and *every* release PR classifies as "version unchanged" — a silent non-run of the whole gate; **(E4)** rename the config and retarget the workflow input together; **(E5)** poison the version file's *contents* so the value reads unchanged. The fix is a read-surface change, not a new policy: **`version_file`/`version_regex` are read from the config at `merge-base(base, head)` and head's copies are IGNORED rather than refused** (so this leg has no false-positive surface at all — across 151 merged release PRs in the two consumer repos those keys were never once edited), and **`artifacts` is read at both ends with head allowed only to WIDEN**. A member declared at the fork point and absent from head is refused **in its own right** — independent of whether that member's own checks would have passed (E1's whole trick is that they do) and **independent of the PR's class**, release or not, because a classification-gated severity is exactly what E5 buys back for the price of one prepended line. **Comparison is on expanded members with `{{version}}` held as a placeholder**, never on entry strings and never on a concrete version: adding a brace alternative is a widening (rc 0), reordering one is a no-op, and dropping one names that alternative — under a string-identity unit every one of those reds, and under a concrete-version substitution every templated member reads as removed at every release. **The narrowing channel is `retired_artifacts`** — an optional array beside `artifacts`, same entry format, read at **head** because it is the PR's own declaration; the run prints a pasteable `RETIRED:` line instead of refusing, and a member listed in **both** arrays is rc 2, so a tombstone cannot be pre-planted against a member that is still declared. Measured cost of the whole predicate: **1 firing in 151** merged release PRs (a genuine retirement of a `public/build/` entry), against **7 in 151** for the obvious alternative ("a release PR that touches the config is refused"). **A fork point with no usable config is a `::warning::` and a fallback to head's keys with an empty baseline — never a refusal**: those keys can only reach the fork point through a merged release, so rc 2 there is an rc no PR can fix, and all three real adoption PRs created the config *inside* a release PR. **Also closed, same read surface:** a declared member's TYPE at head is now one `git ls-tree` mode read rather than an existence probe, because `git show <ref>:<path>` answers for three non-file types with something that can carry the version string — a symlink's target path, a directory's tree listing, and a gitlink's commit log — each of which passed the content assertion while asserting nothing about a release artifact. **⚠ FIVE CHANGES TO WHAT THE TOOL ACCEPTS OR REJECTS, all fail-closed but one:** (1) a PR that drops a declared artifact without acknowledging it now reds (release **or** non-release); (2) the config must be **committed at head** — it is read from the commit, never the working tree, since on a `pull_request` event the checkout is the merge ref, so a config that exists only in the checkout is rc 2 where it used to be read and used; (3) an **empty-string** `artifacts` entry is rc 2 where it was silently dropped — the whitespace-only spelling of the same malformed entry already died rc 2, and one class gets one disposition, because the silent half is this tool's own defect (a declared member no assertion reads and no count mentions); (4) a `--config` that resolves inside a **different repository** is rc 2 where it was read and used, since every read is now a blob read against *this* repository's refs; (5) the one *permissive* change — a config committed at head but absent from the checkout now proceeds, where it died rc 2 on the false diagnosis "no config in this repo". **Deliberately NOT closed, and stated in `INSTALL.md` §6c** so an adopter infers no stronger guarantee: a prose edit that downgrades a member to the weak arm still passes (the closing mechanism exists and is declined — attacker-weakening and a legitimate changelog-format change are the same observable, and since the fork point is the previous release the corrected prose could never land; the OK line names the arm, so the downgrade is legible), the paired config-rename + workflow-retarget, and E5's non-run of the member legs — a property of classification-by-value that predates this gate, tracked as its own defect rather than folded in here. **Review-round corrections, folded in before merge**, each with its own selftest case watched red under a mutation of the mechanism it guards: a `--config` resolving inside a *different* repository was accepted and silently re-rooted, so the run answered — at rc 0, on the wrong file — about **this** repository's own root config; the config's toplevel is now compared against the invocation repository's and a mismatch is refused by name (capturing the `rev-parse` rc answers "is this a repository at all", which is the wrong question one step out, and it is also why the rc is now captured on `--show-toplevel` rather than `--show-prefix`: a BARE repo answers the latter at rc 0). A config blob that **parses and then errors** (`{…} trailing junk`) passed an output-only parse guard — jq prints the leading value *and* fails — and died on the first real key read at rc **5** with a raw jq parse error as the whole diagnostic, on both ends of the range; the parse and type guards now test jq's exit **status** as well as its output. And the entry-level boundary refusals (no leading path token, an empty brace set, a literal comparison sentinel) fired rc 2 on the **fork-point** set, so the PR that repairs such an entry — which can only be repaired by *deleting* it — was redded by the very entry it removes: they are now dispositioned **by source**, rc 2 for a head-declared entry and a per-entry `::warning::` plus exclusion from the baseline for a fork-point one, which is the warn-never-refuse rule the unusable-*config* path already followed, applied one level down. `README.md`'s tool row and `VERSIONING.md`'s release-flow paragraph drop the last copies of "a non-release PR costs nothing", which the classification-independent narrowing check made false. **Coverage:** the selftest grows to 31 named cases, seven of which pin behaviour that is rc 0 today, and every new mechanism was watched red under a mutation of the code it guards (19 mutations in the first pass, 4 more in the first review round and 1 in the second — the member mode read's rc capture, whose loss turns a pathspec git refuses into a wrong-but-specific `missing at head` — each red exactly the checks it should and nothing else). **Consumer impact:** `.release-pr.json` gains one optional key; a repo that never removes an artifact entry sees no change beyond the commit-vs-tree rule. `bin/release-artifacts-check` and `release-artifacts/action.yml`'s `description:` both change; `promote/action.yml` and every board-writing CLI are untouched.

- **card#5281** — **`board-session-close`'s "do these two path spellings name the same directory?" test degraded to a raw string compare on any host whose `readlink` has no `-m`.** `-m` is a GNU coreutils extension, and the comparison carried a `|| <the raw path>` fallback for hosts without it — so on macOS/BSD/busybox it answered by comparing the two spellings as text, which is precisely the defect the function exists to prevent: a symlinked repo path, a trailing slash, or a sub-directory spelling then fell into the "your `.git` is a file" arm, asserting a falsehood about an ordinary checkout **and** withholding the remediation command that works. The comparison is now made without `readlink` at all. Where both paths **exist** it is the kernel's own identity primitive — bash's `-ef` (same device + inode), which follows symlinks, is immune to trailing and repeated slashes, needs no canonicalisation and no external tool, and is a stronger answer than string-comparing two canonicalised paths. Only where a compared path does **not** exist (a `core.hooksPath` naming a not-yet-created directory, reachable where the remediation line is built) is anything canonicalised, by a new pure-bash `_bsc_canon` that interleaves physical and lexical resolution rather than sequencing them: each segment is re-attempted against the filesystem after a `..` pop or a symlink splice, so a path like `<base>/missing/../L` resolves `L` physically instead of collapsing to the link's own name, and a component is taken lexically only where lexical **is** correct (it does not exist, or it exists and is not a symlink). Both compared spellings always take the same branch. There is no environment-conditional branch anywhere in the compare path, so there is nothing left to degrade silently, and `readlink -m` survives only as a **test oracle**. Two departures from that oracle are pinned in the test with their reasons (leading `//` normalises to one slash; symlink resolution is bounded at 40 hops, past which the remainder is appended lexically) — neither can produce a wrong verdict, because both spellings run the same code. **Disclosed, not claimed:** every measurement behind this change was taken on GNU coreutils/glibc; the fix is *unexercised on real macOS/BSD/busybox hardware*, and what is exercised everywhere is the single code path plus a PATH-front `readlink` stub that errors on `-m` exactly as those hosts do. New CI-registered `tests/bsc-same-dir-selftest.sh`: a verdict property corpus (symlink, trailing/repeated slash, `..`-through-symlink, dangling symlink, symlink to an unreadable directory, a newline inside a component name, missing tails), a branch-consistency check binding `_bsc_canon` equality to `-ef`, the `readlink -m` differential with its pinned departures and a deliberately-lexical control that is watched to red it, and the whole corpus again under the stub — plus a source assertion that fails on any non-comment `readlink -m` reintroduced into the bin.

- **card#6051** — **`kbcard comment` / `kbcard comments`: posting a card comment — the routine audit trail — had no verb, so every caller hand-rolled the API call.** `comment --task <id-or-ext> --content "text" | --content-file PATH` posts one and prints the **created comment id**; `comments --task <id-or-ext>` reads them back, one block per comment (`comment <id> · user <user_id> · <created_at>`, then the content with **every** line indented, so a multi-line comment cannot read as two, and every C0 control character except the newline and the **tab** rendered as `?`, because comment content is untrusted text going to a terminal and an embedded `ESC` is a terminal command that can erase its own attribution line — the tab is exempt because it cannot move the cursor *backward*, so it cannot reach that line, while replacing it damages every pasted diff, log line or indented block a comment carries — and the **header line's own fields, which come from the same untrusted body, are sanitized too, more strictly**: every C0 control there including the newline and the tab, because a newline in `created_at` forges a whole second attribution block where one in the content cannot). Both take the same `ID-OR-EXT` ref every other verb takes, through the same resolver. **The API semantics were measured before any of it was written** (sandbox instance, board 1162), and each measurement is load-bearing rather than trivia: **(1)** the write body is **flat** `{"content": …}` — the wrapped `{"comment":{…}}` form is refused (`422`), so the flat shape is a measurement and not a guess; **(2)** adding a comment does **not** bump the card's `updated_at`, which makes the echoed id the *only* cheap verification that the write landed — so a success carrying no id is reported as an **unverified write** (rc 1) instead of printing a plausible-looking `null`; **(3)** there is **no comment read route** (`GET` on the comments path is `405`, POST-only), so the read verb projects the `comments` array the task-detail `GET` already carries and adds no route; **(4)** the author is a bare `user_id`, the only author field a comment row has — a name would be a fabrication; **(5)** a **soft-deleted** comment (its `deleted_at` stamped, the row still live) is **excluded** from that array, so `deleted_at` is deliberately not rendered; **(6)** that array is **complete and ordered** — 60 comments posted to one scratch card came back as **60** rows, `created_at` ascending, first and last matching the first and last posted, and the returned content set equal to the posted set (no gap, no duplicate), with no page argument anywhere on the read — which is what makes projecting it the *audit trail* rather than a first page, and is corroborated in the server's own source (`Task::comments()` is a `hasMany` ordered by `created_at` with **no limit**, eager-loaded whole by the task-detail resource; the probe bounds the observation at 60, the source is the argument that there is no cap at any *N*). **The trap this had to avoid, and the reason the tests are shaped as they are:** the un-suffixed `/tasks/<id>/comments` path answers `404` with a **well-formed JSON body** carrying no `.data`, so any "did the body parse?" success test reads it as a success and degrades to a *silent* empty answer rather than a crash — for the read verb that is the worst spelling of it, `no comments` at rc 0 for a request that never reached a card. Success is therefore decided by the shared `kb_api` on the **HTTP status class** alone, never on the body's shape, and both failure spellings are asserted against that exact 404 body. **One layer past that decision sits its twin:** a `2xx` whose body is not JSON *at all* (a proxy's HTML error page, a truncated read) reaches every projection as a success, where an unguarded `jq` dies under `set -e` at the **script's rc 5** with jq's raw parse error as the caller's whole diagnostic. That is now one `_kbc_parse_resp` helper adopted at the three sites these verbs touch (the created comment id, the comment array, and `show`'s card), each refusing through its own empty-value arm, in kbcard's words at kbcard's rc; the file's remaining projection sites are tracked on card#6426. Everything resolvable offline is resolved **before** any request, so `--content` + `--content-file` together, neither of them, an empty value, a missing/unreadable file, or content that is empty or **whitespace-only** from either source is `rc 2` with **no traffic at all** (only the CHECK is trimmed — what is sent keeps its leading whitespace and its interior newlines, loses its **trailing** newlines, and has **CRLF normalized to LF** at the one write site both sources pass through, because Windows/Git-Bash is a documented install target and an unnormalized `\r` reaches the reader as a C0 control, i.e. a `?` at the end of every line; a *lone* `\r` is not a line ending and is deliberately left to that sanitizer) (each paired with a positive control that the same probe does record a POST on the happy path). **Coverage:** `tests/kbcard-selftest.sh` gains the block, driven against a faked kanban through the existing `_kb-api-stub` `curl` stand-in — the bin runs as a real **process** so the real `kb_api`, arg parse and exit statuses are what the assertions see (a stubbed `kb_api` would replace the very code that decides status-vs-shape); each new guard was watched red under a mutation of the code it guards. **Consumer impact:** additive — two new verbs; no existing verb's body or output changes, and the **one** exit-code change is `show` on a `2xx` this tool cannot read a card out of — a body that is not JSON, which was the script's rc **5** (jq's parse error), and an **empty** `2xx` body, which was a silent rc **0** (no output, no diagnostic, a success) — both now rc **1** with kbcard's own diagnostic, matching what every other read in this tool already does with an unusable success. A well-formed body is byte-identical, status and all. `_kb-board-lib.sh`, `promote/action.yml` and `release-artifacts/action.yml` are untouched, so a vendored copy needs only `bin/kbcard` re-vendored to gain them.

- **card#5764** — **`docs/UPGRADE.md` §6 stopped at v0.14.0 while the toolkit shipped 16 more releases, and nothing made the entry a release-time obligation.** §6 instructs an upgrader to *"find your installed version and walk forward from the next entry"*, so a missing entry is indistinguishable from a release that required nothing — and that reading was false at least twice in the gap: **v0.23.1** narrows the CLIs to reject `--flag ""` where they previously accepted it silently, and **v0.20.0**'s flat-body v3 cutover is unconditional with no shim, so the tools must run against a kanban carrying DL-219 (a deploy-ordering constraint, recorded only in this file and the release table). All 16 releases **v0.15.0 → v0.27.0** now have an entry, in the existing per-version style with §6's audience tags; the ten that ask something of an upgrader say what, and the six that ask nothing carry an explicit `**No upgrade action.**` line, so a gap in the walk can only mean a doc defect. The population was derived twice and cross-checked — `git tag -l 'v*'` above v0.14.0 and the `## [X.Y.Z]` headings in this file agree on the same 16 members, newest equal to `VERSION` — and each release's vendorable surface was read from `git diff --name-only <prev> <tag> -- bin/ promote/ release-artifacts/ hooks/ examples/` rather than from prose, which is what distinguishes v0.20.1 (**no** file under any of those paths changed, so there is nothing to re-vendor) from the releases whose summaries merely say "vendor surface unchanged". **The root cause is the second half:** this is the same inversion #104 repaired once for v0.9.0–v0.13.0, and it re-accreted because the release checklist never named §6. `VERSIONING.md` gains **§ The §6 upgrade-action rule** — every release adds an entry or the no-action line, with what counts as an action and why omission may never be the way a release says "nothing to do" — release-flow step 4 points at it from the place the release's upgrade note is authored, and `docs/UPGRADE.md` joins **`.release-pr.json`'s `artifacts` array**, so `release-artifacts-check` reds a release PR that moves `VERSION` without moving §6. The gate's strength is disclosed where the rule is stated: the new member takes the **content-mention** arm, asserting that the file moved and carries the version being shipped, not that the entry is well-formed or true — it closes "forgot entirely", which is the failure that actually happened. Deliberately **not** a new numbered step in § Release flow: GitHub renumbers ordered lists by sequence, so inserting one shifts steps 6–11 and breaks the `step 8` / `step 11` citations in `README.md`, `CLAUDE.md` and two shipped entries of this file. **Docs and config only** — no `bin/` script, no workflow, no action input; both composite actions are byte-identical.

- **card#6160** — **which refs `release-artifacts-check` reads was stated in four places and derived from in three more; `docs/INSTALL.md` §6c is now the single owner and the rest point at it.** Found by sibling audit of card#6054, which had just corrected two of those copies for saying artifact contents are read at *both* ends when they are read at head only — the correction landed, and four other surfaces went on carrying their own wording of the same inventory. **Every copy was accurate at this change**, so nothing here corrects a claim: it removes the surfaces that can drift, which is why the next correction lands once instead of six times. §6c gains a **What the check reads** paragraph (merge base resolved, version file at both ends, each declared member's contents at head, all `git show`/`git cat-file` against the local clone — hence `fetch-depth: 0`) and owns the fail-closed rule it already stated. `release-artifacts-gate.yml`'s two comments — the `paths:`-filter note and the `fetch-depth` justification — are **deleted and replaced by pointers**, the §6c recipe's own inline `fetch-depth` comment becomes a pointer so a consumer copying it copies no claim, and `README.md`'s tool row and `VERSIONING.md`'s release-flow paragraph keep their audience's answer ("safe to run on every PR") while dropping their restatement of *why*. **[`release-artifacts/action.yml`](../release-artifacts/action.yml) keeps its copies and is untouched** — a SHA-pinned consumer reads the action's own description and cannot follow a pointer into these docs — so §6c carries a **lockstep note naming it**, which is what a future editor of the inventory needs in front of them; whether a CI leg should assert the two agree is a separate, undecided call and is deliberately not built here. **Docs and comments only:** no `bin/` script, no workflow logic, no action input or output changes, and both composite actions are byte-identical.

- **card#6442** — **`dependabot-deploy-reconcile` binds its read to the RUNNING channel-server process, and a tree the process never loaded gets its own disposition word.** The tool answered about the tree on disk; the question is about the code a live process loaded, and the two part company the moment the tree is written after the process started — `npm ci` into the install of a server nobody restarted is the ordinary way in, and it is silent: the tree measures clean while the process keeps serving what it loaded. Every run now compares the **oldest** matching process's start time against the **newest write anywhere under `node_modules`** (directories included — a removed package leaves no entry behind, only a bumped parent) and **states the binding on every run that reports at all** — the measurement either way, or an explicit `⚠ running-process binding — NOT MEASURED` line where the run died before the tree scan (a host that is *not configured* still prints its one line and no report at all, which is the one bound on that claim) — because a report that only speaks up on trouble is indistinguishable from one that never looked. A tree written after the process started is **`PROCESS_PREDATES_TREE`** — a fifth disposition beside `FIXED_CONFIRMED` / `STILL_EXPOSED` / `UNDECIDABLE` / `UNVERIFIABLE`, deliberately **not** folded into `UNVERIFIABLE`, since it is the one state that turns every other row into a confident answer about an artifact nothing is executing — and it reaches the coverage statement as well as the rows, so the two halves of one claim cannot disagree. **A diverged row still carries its on-disk read.** The word states what is unknowable — which code the process loaded — and does not also erase what IS known: `installed_version` stays populated, and where that read succeeded the row carries an explicitly on-disk verdict in the disposition detail (`…; on-disk read: installed X vs patched Y → STILL_EXPOSED on disk`) and in a new row field `on_disk_disposition` (`FIXED_ON_DISK` / `STILL_EXPOSED_ON_DISK` — spelled so nothing can join them to the live vocabulary, and never counted in `by_disposition`, which stays keyed on the live word alone). A still-exposed on-disk read also renders `✗` rather than `⚠`, since the loaded tree is older than an already-exposed one. Where the read FAILED — a package missing while `npm ci` repopulates the tree, an unreadable subtree — there is deliberately **no** annotation: a transient absence must not mint a decidable fact about a deployment being rewritten under the scan. **Anything parsing the disposition vocabulary gains a value** (rows and `scope.denominator.by_disposition` alike); rc is unchanged — this is a finding, so it rides the report at **rc 0**. The per-package `package.json`-mtime check it replaces was strictly weaker and is gone rather than kept beside it: the tree-level maximum is `>=` any single file's, so nothing it caught can escape, while a tree rewritten *around* a package (which node may still load lazily) was invisible to it. The binding's **limitations ride the report**, not a design document — mtime is the only evidence, so a timestamp-preserving replacement (`rsync -a`, `cp -p`) reads as unchanged; `ps lstart` has one-second resolution, and the comparison is strict, which errs toward reporting divergence; the binding is a property of the TREE, so one write unbinds every row including packages whose own files did not move. An **incomplete tree scan makes the newest write a FLOOR**, which is asymmetric on purpose: a floor already past the process start still proves divergence, while a floor below it proves nothing and reports `NOT ESTABLISHED` rather than `BOUND`. Every **other channel-server install** detected on this host now prints its own stated `UNVERIFIABLE` line on stdout instead of only a `covered:false` entry a reader must find inside the JSON dump. One traversal still does both jobs (`nested_copies` became `scan_tree`, answering nested copies *and* the newest write in the pass it already made — 0.14s over a 4k-entry live tree). Read-only, no new bin, no new `PATH` symlink, and the `board-session-close` advisory leg is unchanged apart from naming the new word in its non-zero warning.

- **card#5981** — **`bin/board-stats`: a per-board stock + flow report whose flow half is read from the changelog event log, because the field everyone reaches for cannot answer a rate question.** The tool prints two sections per board, from two different surfaces **on purpose**. **Stock** — per-column card counts plus the oldest card's age in the *pullable* columns — is a **state** question, so it is read from the card snapshot through the shared `fetch_board_cards`. **Flow** over a `--since` window — cards created, every `from -> to` transition counted and **split human/service**, resolutions listed **per destination stage**, and every terminal→non-terminal move as its own **wash** row — is a **rate** question, and the only surface that answers it is the board changelog (`GET /boards/{id}/changelog.json`). It is deliberately **not** derived from a card's `last_stage_move`, and that is the defect this tool exists to correct: that field is a snapshot of the card's **last hop**, so a census over it is wrong in three directions at once — a card that moved twice in the window contributes one transition and loses the intermediate stage, a card that shipped and then bounced back reads only as the bounce (the resolution it also had that day is deleted, not netted), and a release batch that re-moves already-shipped cards reads as N fresh resolutions. Resolutions are never collapsed into one number, because "resolved" reads identically whether work shipped or was declined. **The window read is a cursor walk, not a filter:** the endpoint has no date parameter, so `board-stats` pages backwards (`order=desc`, `limit=200`, `before=<row id>`) and stops when a page's **oldest** row already precedes the cutoff — every later page is older still — or when the log itself runs short. **Two of the three stops can truncate a window, and both say so:** the page cap is one, and the other is the log itself running out — a short page is a complete window only when the log reaches back PAST the cutoff, so when its oldest row is still inside the window (retention, or a board younger than the window) the read stopped because the DATA ran out, not the window. That case records `data_available_from` (the oldest timestamp the log holds), flags the window truncated and names it on the board's own line, rather than rendering a 90-day report over a 44-day log as complete. An empty log asserts nothing about retention and is not flagged. **`--since` arithmetic is deliberately not delegated to `date(1)`:** GNU date reads `24h` as *now + 24 hours* and answers an **empty** spec with today's midnight at rc 0, so the relative forms are computed arithmetically and an empty/whitespace spec is refused before date is reached; date is consulted only for an ISO-8601 instant, which keeps every relative window working on a box whose `date` cannot parse one. The **shape** is validated before date is consulted at all, because everything else date parses it answers at rc 0 with a window nobody asked for — `--since 7` becomes 07:00 *today* rather than seven days, `--since tomorrow` a future window whose flow is all zeros — so only `<N>h`, `<N>d` and an ISO-8601 instant **in the past** are accepted; anything else, including a cutoff at or after now, is rc 2 naming the accepted forms. **A swimlane-only move is not a transition:** the board emits `task.moved` when the stage **or** the swimlane changes, so a swimlaned board's log carries rows whose `from_stage_id` equals their `to_stage_id` — one of those inside a terminal stage mints a phantom resolution out of a card that never changed column. They are excluded from the transition grouping and counted on their own `same-stage moves` line (text and JSON), which is what keeps `moved` = Σ transitions + same-stage. **Relationship to the server's own surface:** kanban v0.38.0 (DL-227) ships `GET /api/v3/boards/{board}/stats.json`, which owns these same answers server-side; this changelog walk is the pre-v0.38.0 implementation and stays the fallback for a host that does not serve that endpoint, with probe-then-consume adoption tracked separately and deliberately not built here. **Board discovery, classification and fail-soft behaviour are the sibling's**, not new inventions: the roster is `~/.kanban-snapshot-boards` (`--all`, the default) or one `--board <name>`; terminal and pullable stages are **derived from the board's own `lane_type`** — a `done`-typed lane is terminal, a `backlog_inventory`-typed one is pullable, the classification `_kbc-archive-eligible.py` already makes off the same stage shape — with the board env's `KB_STAGE_*` ids as a **per-key override** on top (a key that is set adds its stage id to that class; a key that is absent now costs nothing, where an env-only classification silently emptied a whole class on a board whose env omits `KB_STAGE_WONT_DO`, as the shipped `examples/kanban-board.env.example` does), and a class that is still empty after both is reported on the board's own line instead of rendering as zeros; no numeric stage id is baked in either way, and column names come from the same `preload.json`, degrading to the name the changelog event recorded and then to `stage <id>`; and one unreachable board never kills the report — its failure is named on its own line, its sections read `UNAVAILABLE`, and the run ends with a `⚠ PARTIAL REPORT` line. A board is never silently omitted, including when the aggregation itself fails on its data. Read-only (every call is a `GET`); rc 0 = the report ran, including a partial one, rc 1 = no board could be read at all so there is nothing to believe, rc 2 = usage. `--format json` carries exactly what the text prints because both are rendered from **one** derivation. **Added to the shared lib:** `kb_board_roster` — the roster-file parser hoisted for its second caller, with discovery of every `~/.kanban-*-board.env` as its fallback. `board-snapshot` deliberately still carries the inline original: its fallback is a different one (two hardcoded board names, kept for back-compat), so adopting the primitive there changes what a roster-less box renders at SessionStart and is a decision rather than a cleanup — recorded at the function, not left implicit. **Coverage:** new `tests/board-stats-selftest.sh` (network-free — the argument surface including every spec `date(1)` would have answered at rc 0, the pagination cutoff against a fixture whose third page holds rows a correct run must never reach, the log-exhausted-inside-the-window case with the control that flips it back, the `lane_type` derivation with the control that strips the lane types and watches the class empty, the swimlane-only move that must not become a transition, the human/service split in both directions plus the API's third `actor_type` value, and the text renderer's wash/partial/truncation rows), each leg watched red under a mutation of the code it guards — the assertion count is the suite's own output (`bash tests/board-stats-selftest.sh | grep -c '^  ok'`), re-derivable rather than recorded here; `tests/kb-board-lib-selftest.sh` gains the `kb_board_roster` cases. **One existing check changed:** `help-output-selftest.sh`'s "does not leak code past the header" leg matched the literal `set -euo pipefail`, which is not the first post-header line in a fail-soft bin — it now **derives** that canary from the file under test and witnesses it present in the file first, so the absence assertion cannot pass vacuously. **Consumer impact:** additive. `promote/action.yml` and `release-artifacts/action.yml` are untouched, no existing CLI's behaviour or exit codes change, and `_kb-board-lib.sh` gains one new function and no altered one — but the lib IS co-vendored, and `board-stats` is a **lib-sourcing** bin, so a vendor-by-copy consumer that takes it must copy `_kb-board-lib.sh` beside it (ADOPTION.md and `docs/INSTALL.md` §6b list it accordingly). A `PATH` install must re-run the symlink loop for the new bin, which `agent-board-toolkit-runtime-check` now names in its `TOOLS` set.

- **card#5779** — **`kbcard list`'s projected field set is the only surface a consumer can filter on, and it dropped the two fields cards are actually hunted by.** The projection emitted `id`, `name`, `stage`, `type`, `swimlane_id`, `swimlane`, `dl`, `pr` — so a downstream `grep`/`jq select` over `tags` or `external_id` matched nothing on every card, and an empty result is indistinguishable from an empty board. A live consumer read it as **"745 cards, 0 dependabot cards"** while the `id:dep:…` provenance tag was on the card the whole time and `kbcard show` returned it. `tags` and `external_id` now ride every row (absent tags project as `[]` — the same normalization the `--type` tag filter already applies, so the filter and the emitted value cannot disagree; absent `external_id` projects `null`, like `dl`/`pr`). `description` stays deliberately **out**: it is unbounded free text on every row of a whole-board read, and `show` is where a card is read whole. **Additive — no key changed or was removed**, so a `jq`/key-access consumer is unaffected. **The tool's own flag surface was already honest** and is unchanged: `list` takes only `--column`/`--type`/`--swimlane`, all three filter on projected fields, and any other argument already exited 2 — the silent-0 lived entirely in downstream filtering, which is why the fix widens the projection rather than adding a refusal for a state that cannot occur. **Also: a filtered read now names its denominator on stderr** — one line, `kbcard: list <flags as typed>: <M> of <N> board cards matched`, so a suspicious `0`-of-`N` is legible at the call site. **stdout is byte-identical** (still just the array, trailing newline included — asserted by `cmp`), so nothing parsing it is affected; a consumer that folds stderr into stdout (`2>&1`) will see the line. An **unfiltered** read prints nothing: the array's own length already IS the denominator. `--tag` was considered and **not** added — the query that motivated this is a *prefix* over the tag namespace (`id:dep:`), which an exact-match `--tag` cannot answer, so it would ship a flag reading as "the tag filter" that returns 0 for the very case at hand; with `tags` now projected, a `jq select`/`grep` answers both prefix and exact.

## [0.27.0] - 2026-08-11

**Minor — a new host-local instrument that asks whether the fix a CLOSED Dependabot alert claims is actually present in what is deployed and running, wired as a fourth advisory leg of `board-session-close`.** 2 PRs since v0.26.0 (#230–#231): one feature, plus a docs-only solo-orientation sync (coord v0.33.0, #230). **Minor** per `VERSIONING.md § Bump sizing` — a new `bin/` tool plus new user-visible output on an existing one.

**⚠ UPGRADING — re-run the `PATH` symlink loop (`VERSIONING.md` step 11).** This release ADDS a bin, and a symlink tracks its target's *content*, not the *set* of tools: without the loop `dependabot-deploy-reconcile` never lands on `PATH`, and `agent-board-toolkit-runtime-check` — which gains it in its `TOOLS` set — prints a new warn line naming exactly that. The warn line is the check doing its job; the fix is the re-symlink it already prescribes. **Two new preflight entries in `docs/INSTALL.md` §0, neither fatal:** `ps` (absent ⇒ the new tool reports an instrument failure rather than a wrong answer) and coreutils `timeout` (absent — the default on macOS, and possible under the MSYS install — ⇒ `board-session-close`'s advisory legs still run, each printing a one-line warning that it is running **unbounded**).

**No narrowings this release, and no interface change for action-pinning consumers** — `promote/action.yml` and `release-artifacts/action.yml` are untouched, as are the board-writing CLIs (`bin/kbcard`, `bin/board-card-start`, `bin/next-dl`, `bin/promote-released-cards`, `bin/release-pr-body`). Two existing bins do change what a consumer sees: **`board-session-close`** grows a `── Dependabot fixed-alert vs deployed-tree reconciliation ──` section with its own warnings, runs **both** advisory legs under a 60s wall-clock bound (a leg killed at the budget prints a ⚠ and still never blocks the close), and reworded the archive leg's missing-sibling warning slightly — which matters only to anything matching on its exact text; **`agent-board-toolkit-runtime-check`** gains one `TOOLS` entry. New files: `bin/dependabot-deploy-reconcile` + `bin/_dependabot-reconcile.py` (host-local by design — fixed scope, not a vendorable per-repo tool).

### Added

- **card#6277** — **`bin/dependabot-deploy-reconcile`: does the fix a CLOSED Dependabot alert claims actually exist in what is deployed and RUNNING?** An alert closes when its fix reaches the repository's default branch. Production serves something else — a tag, a release clone, an installed dependency tree — cut by a separate process on its own cadence, and nothing in the toolkit compared the two. A repository whose alert list reads 0-open can therefore be serving the vulnerable version indefinitely, with no symptom anywhere and no artifact recording that nobody checked. The new tool implements **one leg**: an installed npm dependency tree, whose evidence class is `installed-file` — the identity IS the version of the file the runtime loads, so no git tag is read and none is claimed. It resolves the deployed directory from the MCP config that **launches** the server (never a hardcoded path — that resolution is what ties "the tree I read" and "the tree it is running" to the same claim), reads each alerted package's installed `package.json` version, and compares it against the alert's own `first_patched_version` under **SemVer 2.0 precedence**. **The comparator is an explicit conservative proxy — `installed >= first_patched_version`, and `vulnerable_version_range` is never parsed:** each ecosystem's range grammar differs with no single published spec, and a range-parsing bug is a silent FALSE NEGATIVE (a genuinely vulnerable version cleared), where the proxy can only ever over-report. `>=` and not `>`, so a version EQUAL to the first patched version is FIXED — a boundary the suite exercises through the shipped argument path in both directions (equal ⇒ `FIXED_CONFIRMED`, one patch below ⇒ `STILL_EXPOSED`), because a comparator accidentally spelled `>` passes every healthy-tree case. **Four dispositions, and only two of them are answers:** `FIXED_CONFIRMED` / `STILL_EXPOSED` / `UNDECIDABLE` (the read is fine, the comparison cannot be made — no `first_patched_version`, a non-semver version, the package is not in the deployment) / `UNVERIFIABLE` (the read itself cannot be trusted — no running process for this install, a `package.json` newer than the running process, a nested `node_modules` copy making the loaded copy ambiguous, an alert outside the leg's declared ecosystem/manifest set). Nothing is ever silently skipped: an alert this leg does not cover gets a row naming why. **The tree's own readability is gated before any per-package read, and that gate is the difference between a finding and a fiction:** with `node_modules` absent or unreadable, every alerted package's `package.json` is missing, and answered one package at a time that renders as `UNDECIDABLE: package not in deployment` — a decidable FACT about the deployment minted out of a read that never happened, at rc 0, beside a coverage entry still claiming the install is covered. The window is the ordinary one (`npm ci` REMOVES `node_modules` before repopulating it, and this runs at session close), so every alert now resolves `UNVERIFIABLE: node_modules not readable at <path>` and the coverage entry flips to `{covered: false}` carrying the **same reason string** — rows and coverage statement are one claim about one install, and an instrument reporting both ways leaves a reader no way to tell which half to believe. Absent and unreadable are named apart because the operator remedies differ (wait out the reinstall vs. fix the permissions). **That predicate is now one primitive applied at every directory read rather than a check written per site** — a sibling audit found the same shape open at two more of them, and both mattered: the nested-copy scan (`os.walk` swallows every listing error by default, and a subtree it cannot enter reads as "no nested copy here", which is precisely what LETS the top-level read stand — so an unreadable subtree did not degrade the verdict, it UPGRADED it to `FIXED_CONFIRMED`), and the per-package read (`os.path.exists` answers False for a package that is missing AND for one whose directory cannot be traversed, so the code contradicted its own docstring's claim to separate the two). The readability test is read+execute, not read: a directory with read but no execute lists its children and then reports every one of them as a non-directory, which is the quietest form of the same lie. The nested-copy scan now classifies **every** directory entry positively — a plain directory, a plain file, or a symlink resolving to a regular file are the only outcomes confirmed harmless, and everything else is recorded — because enumerating the unsafe shapes kept missing one: a symlinked subtree escaped the walk entirely, and then a symlink whose *target* could not be stat'd escaped the symlink check too (`os.walk` swallows the error and files it as a regular file). A symlinked subtree is recorded rather than followed — under the default `followlinks=False` a symlinked directory is listed but never walked, so neither the error handler nor the per-directory check ever sees it, and an unwalked subtree reads as "no nested copy here" (file-symlinks, as in `node_modules/.bin`, correctly do not fire it). **No count of these routes is claimed in prose any more** — that claim has been falsified twice — so the code points at the fixture set that asserts them instead. **Every alert query is gated, because this API fails in ways a status code does not show** — measured, not assumed: an **unknown** state value answers **200 with `[]`** (`?state=all` and `?state=bogus` both do), so a typo'd filter yields an empty population at a success status, indistinguishable from a real "no alerts in this state". A **mis-cased known** value is not that case — the filter is applied case-insensitively (`?state=Fixed` returns the same set as `?state=fixed`) — so it is the enum guard, not casing luck, that keeps a typo out; the per-response `.state` re-assert is a **boundary check on a remote answer**, kept because no gate should rest on the absence of a currently-known bad shape.
