Where a write-absence assertion could not be made to fail by any single regression it was **deleted, not kept** — dl-a0's page-1-failure leg emits nothing at all, so zero writes there follows from having no targets rather than from the refusal (canon #9); that property is asserted on the short-read leg, where it is live. **All 189 assertions were each observed RED** under 59 targeted mutations, with the union of red labels diffed against the full assertion list and every mutation checked for having changed bytes at all. Three methodology corrections came out of that pass: a mutation must be verified to have *changed* something (a pattern that no longer matches leaves the suite green and reads as proof); **duplicate assertion labels silently collapse a mutation-union proof** — three labels were reused across scenarios, hiding three assertions from the analysis until they were disambiguated; and a card-specific request count (`PATCH /tasks/777.json`) could not see a teardown fired against an **empty** task id, so the assertion that no teardown follows a failed create had to count any `/tasks/` write. New `tests/_kb-api-stub.sh` + `tests/_kb-api-stub-curl.sh` own the shared harness — the scratch board config, the request log, and a `curl` stand-in that honors the caller's own `-w` format (so the `__HTTP__` marker is never hardcoded twice), drains the auth herestring rather than reading the token from argv, emulates `-f` so reintroducing it reds the error-body assertions, and **dies on an option it has not been taught** rather than misparsing it silently. The stub is a real `tests/*.sh` file rather than a heredoc so `shellcheck` sees it, and routing is an **exported bash function** owned by each selftest, so the route tables are shellchecked too. Registration in the `ci.yml` matrix was confirmed load-bearing by watching card#5355's `ci-matrix-parity` job name both files as `unrun` with the entries removed. `bin/` and `promote/action.yml` are untouched, so no vendored surface moves. `tests/help-output-selftest.sh`'s `CLIS` registry still excludes both bins — deliberately, per the v0.23.1 card#5334 rationale (they echo a one-line `$USAGE`, not a header block, so a line-count-equality assertion does not apply); their `--help` surface is now covered behaviourally here instead.
- **card#5356** — **`agent-board-toolkit-drift-check` had one CI exercise, and two of its three legs could not fail in it.** The `drift-check-selftest` job runs `./bin/agent-board-toolkit-drift-check . .` — TOOLKIT and REPO the **same directory** — so **content-drift** `diff -q`s every file against **itself** and can never report drift, and **version-skew** is gated on a `.agent-board-toolkit-version` that the toolkit root does not carry, so the block is skipped entirely. Only **MISSING-LIB** is live there. The green tick on that job therefore certified exactly one thing: that `bin/_kb-board-lib.sh` still ships beside its seven sourcers. Two of three legs were decoration (canon #9) — and the consolidation plan that scheduled this work had named only the drift axis, so the skew leg was found by re-reading the tool rather than by executing the plan. New `tests/drift-check-fixture-selftest.sh` drives the bin **as a process** (it is not main-guarded) against real temp-dir fixtures: a diverged vendored tool, a diverged vendored **lib**, a lib-sourcing bin vendored without the lib, a recorded version disagreeing with `VERSION`, a clean co-vendored copy, an empty repo, a repo-local bin with no toolkit counterpart, and the four bad-invocation forms. **It ADDS a job, it does not replace one:** the self-run's MISSING-LIB leg is the only assertion anywhere about the **shipped** tree, which a temp-dir fixture cannot make — an earlier plan revision said "replace" and review disproved it. **Every rc-0 / "not named" assertion is an assertion of ABSENCE, and drift-check answers OK vacuously for a repo that vendors nothing** — so each is paired with a mutation of the **same directory** whose message is observed present (diverge the tool, then restore it and diverge the lib; add a sourcing bin to the lib-less standalone fixture; write an agreeing version, then remove the file). The skew assertion builds its expected message from `VERSION` read at test time rather than a literal, so it is tied to the authority instead of pinned to a past value, and a hardcoded version in the tool goes red. **All 36 assertions were each observed RED** under 14 targeted mutations — the three legs removed in turn, the drift condition inverted, MISSING-LIB made unconditional and separately made predicate-free, the recorded-version file made mandatory, the version hardcoded, `die()` re-exiting 1, the failure exit collapsed to 0, the walk taught to skip `_`-prefixed files, the not-a-toolkit-tool skip removed, and both fixture **premises** falsified in `bin/kbcard` / `bin/promote-released-cards` — with the union of red labels diffed against the full assertion list to prove none was left uncovered. Two mutation-quality corrections came out of that pass: hardcoding the version to `0.23.1` **is not a mutation** (it equals the current `VERSION`, so nothing changed and the suite stayed green — the value must differ for the assertion to be exercised), and the "a recorded-version file becomes mandatory" mutation is what proves the deliberately-asserted `no recorded version → rc 0` case is a real check rather than a restatement of today's behavior. **The job keeps its name:** `drift-check-selftest` is a **required status check on `main`**, so renaming it would leave that context permanently unreported and every release PR sitting on a check that can never be satisfied — the card offered renaming either side, and the **file** took the distinguishing name (`drift-check-FIXTURE-selftest`) instead. **What a green run proves — the weakest property:** that on temp-dir fixtures each leg reports its own defect by name and exits 1, and a clean fixture exits 0 silently. It says nothing about any real consumer repo, nor about whether a consumer's CI invokes the tool at all. Registration is guarded on arrival by card#5355's `ci-matrix-parity` job — confirmed by watching it name this file as unrun **before** the matrix entry was added. `bin/` and `promote/action.yml` are untouched, so no vendored surface moves.
- **card#5355** — **CI now fails when a `tests/*-selftest.sh` exists that nothing runs.** The `selftest` job's `strategy.matrix.test` list and the contents of `tests/` were aligned **by hand**, behind a `# KEEP THIS LIST IN SYNC` comment — and a comment is not a gate. Adding a selftest and forgetting the matrix line meant the test never ran in CI: permanently, silently, with every PR green and the author seeing it pass locally. Same defect *shape* as card#5339's hand-maintained `CLIS` registry, one layer up — the registry deciding which tests run **at all**. New `tests/ci-matrix-parity-selftest.sh` enforces **one rule with no exception list**: every `tests/*-selftest.sh` must be run by `ci.yml`, either as a matrix entry **or** named in some job's `run:` block. It parses the YAML rather than grepping it (the matrix is structured data; `- foo` matches under any key), and gets its **own top-level job** instead of a matrix entry — a matrix entry would be self-referential, so deleting that one line would disable the guard silently, the exact hole it closes; being named in that job's `run:` is also how it satisfies its own rule. Honest scope: the `unrun` direction is the load-bearing one (nothing else in the repo reports it); the `dangling` direction (a name in `ci.yml` with no file) already fails CI loudly at `bash tests/x.sh` → 127, and is asserted only because it is the other half of the same set comparison. Verified against **three mutations** — a dropped matrix entry, an emptied `jobs` map, and the guard's own `run:` line removed — each reddening the specific assertion that claims to catch it; the emptied-`jobs` case is what proves the **positive control** (both streams non-empty, each carrying a named known member) is not decoration, since every live assertion here is an assertion of *absence* and an extraction that silently yielded nothing would otherwise read as a clean pass. The guard also went red on its first real run, before its CI job existed, correctly naming **itself** as the unregistered test. All three collation points (`sort`, Python's `sorted`, **and `comm` itself**) are pinned to `LC_ALL=C`: `comm` validates input order in the *ambient* locale, and en_US.UTF-8's punctuation-ignoring primary pass orders this suite differently from codepoint order (`kbc-archive-eligible` and `kbcard-field` invert), which produced an unreliable diff until pinned.
- **card#5200** — **`board-session-close` reports checkouts whose `post-checkout` no longer reaches `board-card-start`.** A board-mapped repo can lose its git-hook dispatch while every routine check stays green: `board-card-start` is **fail-soft by contract**, so a missing hook and a working hook are indistinguishable from the operator's seat; the inverse-drift leg sees only the *symptom* (a card that never left its backlog column) and corrects it as ordinary drift, **masking the cause**; and `agent-board-toolkit-drift-check` compares vendored repo *content*, not host hook *wiring*. A new report-only `── Git hook dispatch ──` leg resolves, per local checkout, the directory git **actually** dispatches from and reports a hook that is missing, dangling, non-executable, not reaching the tool, or symlinked into a **different toolkit checkout** (⚠ wiring drift — it still fires — never labelled dead); per repo it reports `core.hooksPath` states that switch dispatch off wholesale, and per host it reports `board-card-start` being off `PATH`. The summary states **inspected vs skipped** counts and never prints an all-clear over zero inspected checkouts. Findings name `install-board-hooks <repo-dir>`; nothing is installed, repaired or mutated, and the ritual's **exit code is unchanged** (still owned by the inverse-drift leg). Placement was chosen against `agent-board-toolkit-drift-check`, which runs in CI where no checkout can ever satisfy a host-wiring assertion. Real `git init` fixture repos cover every state incl. linked worktrees; the two `core.hooksPath` cases assert against the naive `.git/hooks` answer **in both directions**, so simplifying the resolver back to `.git/hooks` goes red.

### Changed
- **card#5776** (same change as above, refactor half) — **`create-card` and `patch` now assemble their top-level task fields through one owner, `_kbc_task_fields`.** This is why five fields could drift onto one writer: each command carried its own field-assembly `jq`, so a field added to `create-card` had no reason to appear in `patch`, and a sixth would have drifted identically. Completes the consolidation `_kbc_build_payload` began for the payload keys (card#4511): payload keys share one assembler, top-level task columns now share this one. Guarded the way the *duplication* has to be guarded rather than the values — the selftest stubs the shared owner with a sentinel key and asserts **both** writers' bodies carry it, so re-inlining the `jq` in either command reds a test that no per-field value assertion would. `--type` alias validation and the `--external-id` numeric guard are likewise hoisted to one owner each. **One observable change for existing callers:** `patch --triaged` no longer re-**sorts** the card's tags. It applied `unique` — which sorts — so an unrelated `--triaged` patch reordered every tag the card carried; dedupe now preserves first-seen order, which is what `create-card` writes, so the two writers agree.
- **card#5740** (consolidation program, test-harness axis) — **`has()` was hand-copied into ten selftests and one copy took its arguments in the opposite order, a divergence that cannot go red.** Reversing the arguments of a literal-substring helper is neither a syntax nor a type error: `has <haystack> <needle>` under a needle-first definition simply tests two unrelated strings and answers `false`, so **every assertion expecting `false` keeps passing** while testing nothing. `tests/kbc-archive-eligible-selftest.sh` had carried the haystack-first spelling since it was written, consistently with its own eight call sites, so neither the file nor the shared prelude could reveal it in isolation. All ten local definitions are deleted in favour of the prelude's single one (hoisted under card#5566, which is why this change adds nothing and only removes); the inverted file's **call sites were audited and swapped to needle-first** rather than its definition flipped, since flipping the definition alone would have silently converted each of its calls into the false-true this card exists to remove. **Test-only — no `bin/` or `hooks/` change, so no vendored surface moves and `promote/action.yml` is untouched.** Measured rather than assumed, in the two halves the card required to stay separate: after the nine conforming copies were removed, **inverting the prelude's argument order took exactly those nine red and left the tenth green** — the tenth still shadowing the prelude with its own definition, which is the evidence that the nine now resolve to the shared one and that the inverted copy was genuinely independent of it. After the tenth was migrated, its three `false`-expecting assertions — the ones that pass vacuously under a wrong argument order, and therefore the only ones a green run cannot vouch for — were each **fed a needle known to be present** and **all three went red**, establishing that they can still fail. Full suite 25/25 green, `shellcheck -S error` clean; the prelude's comment is rewritten, since it described ten copies that no longer exist.
- **card#5566** (consolidation program **Stage C**, flag axis) — **one invariant — "a flag was given without a value" — answered in four spellings, and three of the bins told the operator only that *something* was wrong.** `next-dl`, `dl-a0-backfill-triaged` and `dl-a1-register-field` refused with a bare `$USAGE` line naming none of their flags, while `kbcard`/`adopt-to-dl` had named the offending flag through `kb_require_value` since v0.23.1. Those three now dispatch through the same primitive at all **6** caller-visible call sites — `dl-a0-backfill-triaged --board`, `dl-a1-register-field --board`/`--stage`/`--swimlane`/`--sentinel`, `next-dl --board` — in both the **missing** and the explicitly-**empty** form, which are separate inputs and are asserted separately. **Every one is an rc-2-stays-rc-2 message-text upgrade: no exit code changes on any healthy install.** **⚠ The stage's own headline — "one invariant, TWO exit codes → one" — was UNREACHABLE and is NOT claimed:** the only rc 1 in the corpus is `agent-board-toolkit-runtime-check`'s `"${2:?…}"`, which is excluded by design (it validates `_kb-board-lib.sh`, so it must not source it), so the exit-code divergence **survives, deliberately**; what this retires is the message-quality divergence on the adoptable set. **The scope came from a derived delta table, not the plan's four-row summary**, and the difference was load-bearing: the four rows are **57** individual call sites across 8 files, of which exactly 6 were caller-visibly adoptable (the rest already at target state, already at message parity in the two standalone mirrors, or out of scope), and one of them was **compound**. `next-dl`'s `[[ -n "${1:-}" && -z "$project" ]]` did the value-presence test **and** the "project named twice" mutual exclusion — a `Preserve:` item — in a single condition, so a drop-in `kb_require_value` swap would have **deleted the mutual exclusion and passed every test in the repo**, because `next-dl` had no argument-surface coverage at all. It is **split** into two sequential checks, presence first (the order the `&&` already short-circuited in, now a choice and therefore asserted): the primitive answers presence and names the flag, and the exclusion keeps its own guard and its own generic usage line, since a mutual-exclusion violation is not a value-guard failure. **⚠ One caller-visible change beyond the message text, on a BROKEN install only:** `dl-a0-backfill-triaged` and `dl-a1-register-field` parsed their arguments *before* sourcing the lib, so the source block moved above the arg loop (the ordering `next-dl`/`kbcard`/`adopt-to-dl`/`board-card-start` already had) — a copy vendored **without** `_kb-board-lib.sh` beside it now answers every invocation with the missing-lib refusal at rc 1, `--help` included, where `--help` previously answered rc 0 before the lib was consulted. **`--help` is not the only rc that moves**, and naming it alone understates the flip: with the check below the loop, the missing lib was reported only for an invocation the loop let through, so the loop's own refusals — `--bogus`, a trailing value-taking flag, a stray positional — went **2 → 1** as well (measured lib-less on both binaries, pre and post). The two alternatives were worse: an inline mirror of the primitive is the defect this program exists to remove, and a conditional `[ -r "$KB_LIB" ] && source "$KB_LIB"` evades `agent-board-toolkit-drift-check`'s anchored `^[[:space:]]*source "\$KB_LIB"` probe — deleting a live guard to preserve a refusal path. Both bins' selftests now pin the lib-less behaviour. **New coverage where the hole was:** `tests/next-dl-selftest.sh` gains an argument surface (it previously only lifted `max_int`/`max_dl`), driving the bin as a process against the shared `curl` stub — the value guard in both forms, and the mutual exclusion in all four spellings of the collision, each pinned to rc 2, to the **generic usage line**, to minting nothing and to issuing no request, with every `--board` naming a **resolvable** board on purpose (against an unknown board name the run dies at config resolution anyway and those zeros pass on a deleted exclusion — measured: they did). **Canon #5, in the file this coverage lands in:** that selftest's `has()` — the literal-substring helper — would have been the **11th** hand-rolled copy under `tests/`, in a repo whose consolidation program exists to stop exactly that, so it is deleted and the helper **hoisted into `tests/_selftest-prelude.sh`** (needle-first, the order 10 of the 11 used); this change therefore adds **zero** copies. The 10 standing local definitions shadow the shared one and keep working unchanged as of this change — migrating them is **card#5740**, which lands **in this same release** (entry below) and had to audit the one **argument-inverted** copy (`tests/kbc-archive-eligible-selftest.sh` took haystack first) at its call sites rather than flip it blind, since a blind flip produces a false green. **All 43 new or changed assertions have been observed RED** under **20** targeted mutations, the union of red labels diffed against the full new-label set rather than eyeballed: the value guard reverted to usage-only (per flag), made unfailable, deleted outright, made to name a hardcoded flag, and made to exit 5 instead of 2; the `next-dl` split collapsed back to a bare swap — **8 assertions red, the exact regression this stage was warned about** — plus its value half deleted, its two halves reordered, its value routed to another board, its claim reading the wrong JSON key, and **both** mutual-exclusion arms in turn deleted and then made to answer with the value-guard message instead of the usage line; the missing-lib refusal neutered and made to exit 0; and `bin/kbcard`'s `cmd_field` sub-verb guard deleted, the 20th, which is the mutation that caught the false Preserve claim recorded below. **An earlier revision of this entry excepted one assertion as never-red *by construction*, and the exception does not survive measurement.** It named `a trailing --board does not leak an unbound-variable error` and described it as *"a pre-existing check this change only re-worded"* — two different assertions: that label is `next-dl`'s **new** one (this bin had no argument surface at all before this change, as this entry says above), while the re-worded one is `dl-a0-backfill-triaged`'s `the trailing-flag refusal does not leak an unbound-variable error`. **Both** were re-measured red under mutations already named in the list above: deleting `next-dl`'s value half leaves `project="$1"` reading an exhausted stack, so `next-dl --board` dies with `line 55: $1: unbound variable` (**5** labels red in that file, this one among them), and deleting `dl-a0`'s guard outright does the same at that bin's own `line 41` (**6** labels red, its own included). `${2:-}` holds only while the guard that reads it is *present*, which is exactly what a mutation removes — so no assertion in this change is excepted. **Three zero-request assertions were drafted and dropped rather than shipped**, each with the reason left at the call site: a *trailing* flag reaches no request even with the guard deleted outright (the arg loop's closing `shift` fails on an exhausted stack and `set -e` ends the run), and an empty `next-dl --board` is caught by the `[[ -n "$project" ]]` refusal after the loop — all three are held by something other than the guard under test, so they are decoration (canon #9), while the empty-value zeros beside them are live and are kept. The mutation pass also caught the first draft's **witness lying**: `--board dev claimed against board 42` counted an *unqualified* `/dl-sequence/claim.json`, so it stayed green while the value was routed to a different board — it is board-qualified now, and the fixture gives `alt` and `bridge` **resolvable** board configs precisely so the exclusion cases fail loudly rather than dying at config resolution. All 25 selftests green (`shellcheck -S error` clean, no new findings at default severity either). **⚠ Vendored surface IS affected** — `bin/next-dl`, `bin/dl-a0-backfill-triaged`, `bin/dl-a1-register-field`; `bin/_kb-board-lib.sh` is unchanged and `promote/action.yml` is untouched, so action-pinning consumers see no interface change. **Doc-sync, including two stale claims found in passing and verified before being called stale:** `docs/CONSOLIDATION-PLAN.md` § Status and § Stage C record the landed reality (6 sites not 4 rows, the unreachable exit-code half, the split, and the Preserve items now each named with the test that pins them **and with what that test actually covers** — review measured the first spelling of that claim false, since `tests/kbcard-field-selftest.sh` pinned `kbcard field`'s missing-sub-verb refusal by **rc only**: deleting the guard in `bin/kbcard` left the full 25-file suite green, because an absent sub-verb falls through to the `*)` catch-all which answers rc 2 as well, while `kbcard field` driven as a **process** went from a named rc 2 to a **silent rc 1**. A message assertion now separates the two arms, driven red by that deletion — it is this change's 43rd new assertion and its 20th mutation), the 2026-07-29 deferral ruling is kept and marked superseded with its reason 1 measured smaller than it assumed; `ADOPTION.md` and `docs/INSTALL.md` both said omitting the lib yields *"an inscrutable runtime `source: … No such file`"*, which has been false since v0.11.2 for all seven lib-sourcing bins (six refuse by name at rc 1, `board-card-start` by name at rc 0 by its fail-soft hook contract) — `docs/UPGRADE.md` already recorded the fix, so the two were contradicting a sibling doc, not just the code; `ADOPTION.md` — the front door, and the one place omitting the `--help`/rc facet — additionally dated *"refuse at **startup**"* to v0.11.2, which measurement at that tag refutes for two of the seven: `dl-a0-backfill-triaged`/`dl-a1-register-field` carried the named refusal but placed it **below** the arg loop, so a lib-less `--help` answered **rc 0** there and "at startup" became true for them only at this commit — it now mirrors `docs/INSTALL.md`'s clause, anchoring `v0.11.2` to the message quality and stating the every-invocation fact with its mechanism (the lib is loaded before any argument is read); and the *"all 36 `kbcard` arms"* count is corrected to **37** in all **four** places it was restated — the v0.23.1 and card#5200 entries above, its copy in the root `CLAUDE.md` release table, and a **source comment** in `bin/adopt-to-dl` that the first sweep missed because that sweep was scoped to `*.md` (a clean grep over the wrong file set is not a clean audit). Re-counted at the commit that wrote it, where it was already 37: the 37th is `main()`'s global `--board` arm, outside the per-subcommand loops the figure evidently counted.
- **card#5227** (consolidation program **Stage E**) — **`board-session-close` held a THIRD hardcoded repo list, and three copies of "which repos do I care about" is what let one of them silently drift and lose a repo.** card#5200 hoisted the branch-reality and hook-dispatch legs onto a single `BSC_WORKTREES` — and found, in doing so, that one of those two copies had been silently missing `agent-board-toolkit` entirely. The Open-PRs leg's own list was deliberately left out of that hoist, and the reason is worth keeping: it is a **different semantic** — unique GitHub remotes, not local checkouts, and two worktrees of one repo are two entries in `BSC_WORKTREES` but must be **one** entry in a PR query. So the fix is not "reuse the array"; the query set is now **derived** from `BSC_WORKTREES` by de-duplicating on `git remote get-url origin`, keeping the first **present** checkout of each distinct remote in list order. On the reference host that reproduces the retired literal list exactly — asserted by diffing the live `── Open PRs ──` section of the old and new bins, **byte-identical**, which is the only check in this change that touches real `git remote` output (every other assertion runs over stubbed strings). **The load-bearing decision inverts the naive derivation: an entry whose remote cannot be read keeps its OWN slot and is NAMED — it is never deduped away.** The query set is the *required* side, so dropping an unreadable entry would fail **open** — a clean-looking section that silently covered fewer repos, with no diff anywhere to record it, which is strictly worse than the literal list it replaces (that at least needed a human edit to lose a repo). An extra query is the harmless direction. The warning rides the leg's **own stdout section rather than stderr**, deliberately: the failure direction is safe by construction, so this is not the canon-#9 *"degraded coverage must not read as a clean pass"* class that this file reserves stderr + ⚠ for, and stderr is shared by every leg — a per-leg diagnostic there would couple every other block's stderr assertions to this leg's stub fidelity. **The dedup key is the RAW URL with no normalizer**, and the weakest property is stated at the call site: the dedupe holds for **byte-identical** URLs only (an ssh-vs-https re-clone under-dedupes and lists one repo twice under two labels — visible, labelled, harmless), and it assumes each checkout's `gh` target **is** its `origin`, which a fork remote or `gh repo set-default` could disagree with — both reasons the key is never allowed to DROP a repo, only to merge two that agree exactly. A normalizer was rejected as a new primitive with its own defect surface bought for zero measured benefit. **⚠ One real behavior change, not glossed:** `git remote get-url` needs a checkout, so the `-e .git` filter necessarily runs **before** the remote read — the rule is first *present* checkout. If a repo's primary checkout lost its `.git` while a `-prod` clone kept one, the repo is now queried via the clone where it previously went **unqueried**; better coverage, but a change. **This was only safe to touch because card#5358 (Stage B) gave the leg assertions first**, and that coverage immediately caught two traps: the fixture's `git` stub was a silent `exit 0` that does not answer `remote get-url`, which would hand every entry an EMPTY key and collapse all five checkouts into one group (empty is not "no duplicate" — it is the leg's *unreadable* signal), so the stub now answers per-directory with the two `-prod` clones returning the **same** URL as their siblings, that byte-for-byte identity being the entire input the dedupe acts on; and the fail-closed path needed its own case, **directly paired** with the assertion that the same checkout is absent when its remote IS readable, so it cannot pass on a leg that simply queries everything. Five new assertions; the pre-existing `-prod` absence+witness pairs now test the **dedupe** rather than a list that omitted them, and were driven **red** by mutating the dedupe to a no-op (6 assertions red, rc 1) before being trusted. All 24 selftests green. `bin/board-session-close` is host-local and not vendored, so `promote/action.yml` and the vendored `bin/` surface are untouched. **Out of scope, unchanged:** the card's second smell — operator-specific absolute `$HOME/<name>` paths — which stays blocked on roundtable **#187** (the coord-config `worktrees[]` schema FR).
- **card#5311** — **`--separate-git-dir` checkouts and submodules now INSTALL instead of being refused; the linked-worktree refusal is unchanged.** card#5226 made `install-board-hooks` refuse all three separated topologies with an accurate per-topology message. Two of those three were refused on a rationale that does not apply to them: the decision was *surprising blast radius* — installing silently rewires checkouts the operator never named — which is true of a **linked worktree**, whose hooks dir is shared with the main checkout and every sibling worktree, and false of a `--separate-git-dir` checkout and a submodule, which have exactly **one** work tree each. Those two now install into the directory git actually dispatches from (`<sepgit>/hooks`, `<super>/.git/modules/<name>/hooks`) and report it on **stderr** — never stdout, because `--check`'s only stdout is the target dir and `board-session-close` consumes it. **The disposition moved into one primitive**, `_ibh_install_dir_source <repo> <root> <common-dir>`, rather than staying spelled out at the call site: `bin/board-session-close` **modelled** that call site (`cdir="$root/.git"` under the comment *"it targets `<root>/.git/hooks` and nothing else"`*) to decide whether to offer the installer as a remedy, so this change would have made it tell operators to hand-wire the very repos the installer now fixes. It asks the primitive instead (canon #5) — the second copy being the exact drift class that whole dispatch check exists to catch. **Two pre-existing false claims fixed in passing**, both introduced by card#5226 and both verified against the pre-change binaries before and after: for a linked worktree `_bsc_fix_line` printed *"install-board-hooks would target `<wt>/.git/hooks`"* when the installer had refused outright since #5226 — it targets **nothing** — and the installer view now yields empty stdout + a new **rc 9** so the main-checkout branch (the correct remedy) is reached, with the installer's own refusal quoted where it is not. **One defect found and fixed during the build:** the installer consults the topology disposition *only while `core.hooksPath` is unset* (a set hooksPath wins on every topology), and asking it before reading hooksPath made the installer view report "refused" for a linked worktree that configures one — a checkout the installer services happily; the view now gates on hooksPath exactly as `_ibh_main` does, pinned in both directions. **Deliberately NOT fixed here, filed as card#5434:** `git clone --separate-git-dir=<x>/.git` (basename literally `.git`) is misclassified as a linked worktree by `_ibh_main_checkout` and refused, pointing the operator at a path that is not a checkout — pre-existing since #5226, but this card raises its blast radius from wrong-wording to wrong-**disposition**, and the corrected discriminator is an accept/reject decision of its own. **Tests** extend the card#5226 fixture block with **real** `git init` topologies: a **dispatch proof** that runs a real `git checkout -b` against a marker hook placed in the computed dir before anything is installed (an `-L` presence check alone never establishes that git reads that dir), `--check` stdout captured **without** stderr so the new topology note cannot pollute an equality assert, sub-directory arguments pinning the measurement that git answers the common dir **absolute** for both installing topologies (so no normalization and no `--path-format` version floor is needed), and assertions that nothing lands in the work tree's own `.git/hooks`. **Two test-integrity corrections worth recording:** the `--separate-git-dir` fixture had no commit, and `git checkout -b` on an **unborn HEAD** does not dispatch `post-checkout`, so the dispatch proof was a check that could not pass until a commit was added; and the prove-it-can-fail control initially passed while **never running** — the mutant copy resolved its hook sources at `<dirname $0>/../hooks` and exited before reading any argument, so it "failed" for the wrong reason and an inequality assertion accepted it. The control now reproduces a real toolkit layout and requires a **witness** (the mutant's own diagnostic naming the mutated target) alongside the red assertion. The linked-worktree refusal assertions and the set-`core.hooksPath` positive control survive **verbatim**.
- **card#5343** — **one positional-argument rule was hand-rolled in three bins, and the three copies had already drifted textually.** The rule — *refuse an EMPTY positional by name, refuse a SECOND one* — lived independently in `bin/install-board-hooks`, `bin/adopt-to-dl` and (as of card#5333) `bin/board-card-start`. They agreed behaviourally and disagreed everywhere else: `unknown option:` vs `unknown flag`, `: $arg` vs `'$1'`, `for arg in "$@"` vs `while/shift`. Canon #7 files the duplication itself as the defect at the third copy — the next copy is free to pick any of the three answers. **Hoisted to `kb_require_positional <slot> <arg> <name> [suffix]`** in `bin/_kb-board-lib.sh`, with a documented inline mirror `_ibh_require_positional` in `install-board-hooks`, which is vendored standalone and must not source the lib (the idiom `promote-released-cards` already carries for `host_ok`/`require_value`). **The primitive answers the QUESTION; the caller owns the POLICY** — its own usage line and its own exit status: `board-card-start` refuses at **rc 0** because it runs from a git hook that must never block a checkout (`docs/HOOKS.md`), the two ordinary CLIs at **rc 2**. That rc divergence is justified, not drift, and an owner that also owned the exit would have had to break one of them. `<slot>` is the **destination variable's current value**, not a new seen-flag: refusing an empty positional rather than storing it is exactly what makes `-n "$slot"` a sound "have I seen one?", and `install-board-hooks` had already tried a seen-flag and removed it as unreachable. **Empty is tested FIRST**, as all three copies did, so the corner case *slot already full AND arg empty* still reports the empty — a reorder there would have been a silent user-visible change on a case nobody would have thought to test. **It is deliberately NOT merged into `kb_require_value`** despite the twin naming: inside a function `$#` is the *function's* arity, and `kb_require_value`'s 1-arg shape is contracted with the OPPOSITE answer (`kb_require_value --dl` → rc 1, asserted in `tests/kb-board-lib-selftest.sh`); the flag-value axis and the positional axis need opposite verdicts on the same input shape, and the new function says so at its definition. **⚠ ONE user-visible delta, and only one:** `adopt-to-dl: unexpected extra argument '4242'` becomes `adopt-to-dl: unexpected extra argument: 4242` — the primitive owns the wording, and `: $arg` is what 2 of the 3 already printed, so exactly one message moves. Letting each caller keep its own wording would have deduplicated two comparisons while preserving every drift this card measured. **`--` handling is deliberately UNCHANGED at all three sites** (`install-board-hooks` hard rc 2, `adopt-to-dl` accepts-then-ignores, `board-card-start` a real terminator): unifying it changes what two of them ACCEPT, and bundling an accept/reject change with a hoist puts a behaviour change and a refactor in one reviewable unit. Verified by diffing **18 refusal invocations** across all three bins, old binaries vs new, with each bin's own lib copy beside it — byte-identical on 17, the intended `adopt-to-dl` message on the 18th. **New `tests/kb-positional-guard-selftest.sh`** (network-free, CI-registered) runs ONE assertion matrix against BOTH copies — extract-and-exercise, never a text diff, the shape `tests/kb-host-guard-selftest.sh` uses — and reports *"the two copies DISAGREE"* as its own failure class. Its **prefix equalization** is what makes that a comparison of the **diagnostic** rather than only the rc: the mirror hardcodes `install-board-hooks: ` while the lib copy renders `$(_kb_prog)`, so the test sets `KB_PROG` to that name, the direct analogue of host-guard's `KANBAN_EXPECTED_HOST`/`EXPECT_HOST` pairing. Driven **red** under three mutations against the live tree — the mirror's extra-arg wording drifted (the DISAGREE arm fired), the lib copy's two tests reordered (the empty-wins case fired), and one call site left hand-rolled. **What a green run proves — the weakest property:** that the two implementations agree on the inputs the matrix feeds, and nothing about an input class absent from it. It is also **blind to a call site left hand-rolled** — a bin that calls neither copy passes it, which the third mutation demonstrates — so the file's second half **runs all three bins** on both refusal shapes and asserts rc *and* the first stderr line; those pins are the only thing covering `install-board-hooks`' arm, which had no selftest of its own before this change. `bin/_kb-board-lib.sh` gains a function and no existing signature moves; `promote/action.yml` is untouched, so no vendored interface changes. **Filed, NOT fixed here:** `install-board-hooks -- <dir>` returning a hard rc 2 for a universal shell idiom, which got sharper with card#5333 because `board-card-start`'s refusal now actively teaches operators to reach for `--` (card#5427); `adopt-to-dl`'s decorative `--) ;;` arm is already owned by card#5351; and — from the canon-#7 sibling audit this change ran, which found a **fourth** site the card never named — `agent-board-toolkit-drift-check` **silently discards a third positional** (`drift-check . <repo> IGNORED` → rc 0 `OK`, reproduced live) and names neither of its two positionals when one is empty (card#5429). That is the precise hazard `install-board-hooks`' own arg-loop comment already gives as its reason for refusing an extra positional — written down in one bin, never applied to the sibling. It is deliberately **not** a fourth copy of the fix: `kb_require_positional` is single-slot by construction (`<slot>` IS the destination variable), `drift-check` has two ordered positionals, and refusing an argument it accepts today is an accept/reject change that must not ride a refactor.
- **card#5200** — **`board-session-close`'s existing "Local branch reality" leg now lists 5 checkouts instead of 3.** It and the new dispatch leg read **one** list (`BSC_WORKTREES`) rather than two hand-maintained literals, so the branch/dirty-state lines gained this repo's own checkout and the deploy worktree that owns the on-PATH tools. Additive and read-only — no line was removed — but it is a visible change to a pre-existing section's output.

### Fixed
- **card#5276** — **`kbcard "" <verb>` printed the usage block and exited 0, so a caller checking `rc` read a no-op as a completed write.** `kbcard "$VERB" move --task N --column in_progress` with `VERB` unset moved nothing and reported success. One `[[ -z "$cmd" ]]` answered two different questions — *no arguments*, which is a help request, and *an empty first argument*, which is a failed expansion — and gave both the help answer. They are now discriminated on `$#`: no arguments still prints usage on stdout at rc 0, an empty verb is refused on stderr at rc 2 through the same `kb_require_positional` owner the three sites in card#5200 use, so kbcard's refusal cannot drift from theirs. **This is the fourth position of the empty-vs-absent axis** (v0.23.1 flag values → `core.hooksPath` → the `install-board-hooks`/`adopt-to-dl` positionals → this), and the first one in the tool that already applied the flag-value guard correctly at ~40 arms: the axis is the *shape*, never the file or the argument kind it was last found in. The positional axis was re-derived across every shell bin as part of this fix and every remaining bin was then MEASURED refusing an empty positional at rc 2, not read as doing so; the two bins that consume no arguments at all (`board-snapshot`, `board-session-close`) silently ignore anything passed, which is the extra-positional axis and is recorded on card#5429, not fixed here. The sweep's own method had to be rewritten mid-audit: its first form treated a one-line `die() { …; }` as an unclosed function scope and therefore never printed `agent-board-toolkit-drift-check`'s top-level positional read — a miss in the *scan*, not the tree, and the reason the count it produced was retired rather than corrected. **⚠ `kbcard` now rejects an invocation it previously accepted** — `kbcard "" list` is rc 2 rather than usage-at-rc-0 — the same narrowing, and the same reason, as the v0.23.1 flag-value change. A bare `kbcard` is unaffected.
- **card#5409** — **every guard spelled as a bash bracket RANGE meant something wider than its own error message under a UTF-8 shell.** A bracket range in a bash pattern — `[[ $x =~ ^[0-9]+$ ]]` and glob `case "$x" in *[!0-9]*)` alike — is a **COLLATION** range, not an ASCII range. Measured under `LC_ALL=en_US.UTF-8`: `^[0-9]+$` matches U+0663 ARABIC-INDIC DIGIT THREE and `^[A-Za-z0-9_-]+$` matches U+00E9 / U+2163 / U+FB01; all four are rejected under `LC_ALL=C`. GNU `grep -E` does not widen `[0-9]` in either locale, which is what identifies **bash's engine** rather than the pattern as the cause. **Bounded honestly — this was not a corruption or injection vector.** `kbcard --type`'s stated job is to reject the commas and spaces that would corrupt the tag CSV, and a comma and a space were measured **still rejected** under the wide locale. What was wrong is narrower and real: the guard **admitted input its own error message declares invalid** (`--type 'Ⅳ'` accepted against "must be alphanumeric / '-' / '_'"), and one PARSE site captured a non-ASCII "digit" as a card id — `BOARD-CARD: toolkit#٣` parsed, and `kbcard move --task ٣` was invoked. Three further sites degraded their own diagnostics rather than their verdict: `kb_dl_num` and `native_type_id` got past the pattern and died in bash arithmetic (`[[: ٣: syntax error`), and `kbcard field --field` reached `jq --argjson` and printed a jq parse error in front of the real "not defined on board" message. **The fix is one mechanism, applied at every site:** `kb_ere_match` / `kb_is_uint` in `_kb-board-lib.sh` match under a **function-scoped** `LC_ALL=C` (`local`, so it is restored on return, and `BASH_REMATCH` is deliberately not localized so captures survive) — mechanism in the primitive, refusal **policy** unchanged at each caller, per `CONSOLIDATION-PLAN.md` ground rule 5. `[[:digit:]]`/`[[:alnum:]]` were **not** used: a POSIX class is locale-defined by construction, so it re-poses the same question. Adopted at 17 sites: `kbcard` (`--type`, `--external-id`, `resolve_task`, `swimlane_id`, `native_type_id`, `field --field`), `_kb-board-lib.sh` (`kb_dl_num`, and `fetch_board_cards`' `last_page` + short-read census), `adopt-to-dl` (`<card-id>`, `--issue`), `next-dl` (claim value, claimed DL, board DL max), `dl-a1-register-field` (`--sentinel`, throwaway id). The two standalones that must not source the lib carry **inline mirrors** in the established pattern (as `require_value`/`host_ok` already do): `promote-released-cards` gains `uint_ok`/`uint_csv_ok`, which also collapse four hand-copied numeric globs onto one definition each, and `hooks/agent-dispatch-card-start` gains `_kbads_marker`. **The corpus was swept by SHEBANG and by embedded-shell-in-YAML, never by extension** — 46 files in this tree carry a shell shebang or a `.sh` name where only 30 are named `*.sh`, and the 16-file remainder an extension filter cannot see is where `hooks/agent-dispatch-card-start` and every `bin/` CLI live (51 files swept, counting the 5 YAML); the YAML pass is what found `.github/workflows/auto-tag-version.yml`'s merge-time VERSION gate, which a `*.sh` sweep cannot see (its exposure is **latent, not asserted live**: the hosted runner's locale was not read, and C.UTF-8 measures ASCII-only). **New `tests/locale-range-guard-selftest.sh`** asserts every fixed site under **both** `LC_ALL=C` and `LC_ALL=en_US.UTF-8`, each paired with an ASCII **positive control** that must still be accepted, and positive-controls the **locale itself** first — if the runner's `en_US.UTF-8` does not actually widen `[0-9]`, the UTF-8 half says so loudly instead of passing vacuously. A static backstop covers the adopters no behaviour case can reach (next-dl's three need a live board) by flagging any `[[ =~ ]]` bracket range with no `LC_ALL=C` on that line or the two above it. All 17 sites were **observed RED** under a one-line revert of exactly that site, each restore verified byte-identical. **⚠ This narrows what these tools accept** on non-ASCII input, where the accept-set now matches the wording that was always shown; ASCII behaviour is unchanged at every site (`promote/action.yml` is untouched, but `bin/` and `hooks/` **are** vendored surfaces — re-vendor `_kb-board-lib.sh` with anything that sources it, and re-vendor `promote-released-cards`). **Other engines — re-measured, and NO site in this repo is affected:** GNU **sed** widens `[a-z]` under a UTF-8 locale but does **not** widen `[0-9]`, and the only sed range site (`release-pr-body`'s PR-suffix strip, `s/ *\(#[0-9]+\)$//`) uses `[0-9]`, so it is immune. GNU **grep** widens **neither** class, measured in positive (`^[a-z]+$`) and negated (`[^a-z0-9/#-]`) form against U+00E9 / U+FB01 / U+2163 with a live ASCII control in every cell — so `board-card-start`'s fail-soft branch-name hint is immune too. An earlier revision of this entry stated both engines' behaviour wrongly and inverted between them; the sites were never at risk. **jq**'s Oniguruma and **tr** were measured locale-invariant, and `next-dl`'s `2[0-9][0-9])` HTTP-status arm reads curl's own `%{http_code}`, ASCII by construction.
- **card#5337** — **`adopt-to-dl --help > f` produced an EMPTY `f` at rc 0.** `usage()` baked `>&2` into itself, so the one arm whose entire job is to *emit* help wrote to the diagnostic stream: a caller redirecting stdout got a successful exit and no bytes, which reads as "this tool has no help" rather than as an error. The redirect moved out of `usage()` to the **four error arms** that still want it, because the two uses genuinely differ — an explicitly-requested `--help` is the requested OUTPUT (pipeable, pageable) while usage-after-a-bad-argument is diagnostic — so the channel is the CALLER's to choose and `usage()` is no longer entitled to choose it. **The error paths are byte-identical**: PRE-vs-POST stderr matched byte-for-byte with rc unchanged across five argument shapes (unknown flag, no arguments, two positionals, a non-numeric id, a trailing valueless `--dl`), and stdout was empty on every one of them; `--help` went from 0B stdout / 89B stderr to **89B / 0B**, rc 0 either way. **Why nothing caught it:** `tests/adopt-to-dl-selftest.sh` captures with `2>&1`, merging the streams — it asserts the *text*, and is deliberately channel-agnostic, so it stayed green through the defect and stays green through the fix. `tests/help-output-selftest.sh` said so explicitly and did not rule on channel. It does now, and over the **whole registry** rather than this one bin: every `CLIS` ∪ `EXCLUDED` member is asserted to exit 0 with `--help` on stdout and stderr silent — the six bins were measured green before the assertion was written, so the leg is a record, not an aspiration — driven off `_registry()` so a name added to either list is channel-asserted with no second edit. A second leg is **adopt-to-dl-only, a population of one on purpose**: it guards the regression this change *creates*, since the redirect now lives at four call sites and a later tidy could drop one, putting a diagnostic on stdout with nothing to notice. Both predicates report rc **and both streams as one record**, so every "this stream is silent" assertion carries a "that stream has data" witness measured on the same run — a silent stream is thereby distinguished from a command that produced nothing at all or never executed. **Prove-it-can-fail** uses two fixtures carrying one defect each, each the other's presence witness (a fixture failing both predicates would be indistinguishable from one that does not run): the pre-fix shape fails the help predicate while still satisfying the error one, and a dropped error-arm redirect fails the error predicate while still satisfying the help one. They are synthetic rather than `git show <rev>:bin/adopt-to-dl` — the real pre-fix binary IS a faithful negative control and was run as one by hand, but naming a revision pins the check to a history position a squash-merge invalidates, and the extracted file needs `_kb-board-lib.sh` copied alongside or it exits 1 before reaching any argument. All 11 new assertions were each observed **RED** under a targeted mutation whose byte change was confirmed, including one per registry member so the loop is proven to cover the five bins that are not adopt-to-dl. **Doc-sync:** two present-tense claims that the channel was an open question — in this file's card#5339 and card#5334 entries, both **unreleased**, so they would have shipped alongside their own refutation — were corrected in the same change, as was `help-output-selftest.sh`'s own weakest-property block. **⚠ This changes user-facing output routing:** anything capturing `adopt-to-dl --help` off **stderr** now sees an empty stream (the help is on stdout); error output is unchanged on every path. `bin/` is untouched for every other tool and `promote/action.yml` is untouched, so no other vendored surface moves.
- **card#5359** — **`ADOPTION.md`'s "What you get (`bin/`)" list named a tool retired in v0.9.0, and omitted six that exist.** The line enumerated `board-transition-sync` — retired by #55 (card#3649, superseded by the bridge writeback) and absent from `bin/` since — while closing with *"See `README.md` for the one-line role of each"*, and README's table does **not** list it: an adopter following the pointer finds nothing and cannot tell which of the two docs is wrong. The card scoped the fix to deleting that one name, which would have left the actual defect standing. The line is a **second copy** of README's table, and comparing it name-by-name against `bin/` — the check a stale-name grep can never be, because a grep finds a phantom and never an omission — shows it was also missing **six** live tools (`adopt-to-dl`, `board-card-start`, `install-board-hooks`, `dl-a0-backfill-triaged`, `dl-a1-register-field`, `agent-board-toolkit-runtime-check`). So the enumeration is **removed rather than corrected**: the section now names the tool categories and points at README's table as the single list. `docs/INSTALL.md`'s PATH-install loop was checked as a possible third copy and is already drift-proof — it globs `bin/*` rather than enumerating. **Also fixed in README:** its table was itself missing `agent-board-toolkit-runtime-check` (shipped v0.15.0, documented in `INSTALL.md` §2 and `VERSIONING.md`, never given a row) — a gap that becomes load-bearing the moment that table is the only list. The table and `bin/` now agree 13-for-13, verified in both directions, each direction positive-controlled by injecting the defect it is meant to catch. Docs only; no tool changed. **Now gated:** card#5374 shipped [`tests/readme-bin-coverage-selftest.sh`](../tests/readme-bin-coverage-selftest.sh) in this same release — the selftest that catches both directions.
- **card#5353** — **`docs/HOOKS.md` asserted that the pre-push lint and the auto-move "can never disagree" two lines above the paragraph that refutes it.** card#5333 appended a paragraph narrowing that property to the **grammar** — the argument surface being the one axis the shared matchers do not cover — but left the unqualified summary sentence above it standing, and the same pair sat in `hooks/pre-push`. The un-narrowed form was the one that propagated: `bin/board-card-start` cited it verbatim, so a reader who stopped at the summary got an absolute the subsystem does not provide. All three now say **about the grammar**. A fourth copy in the shipped v0.17.0 entry below is deliberately left alone — this log is the immutable canonical per-version record, and editing a released entry rewrites history. No behaviour change. **Also:** `tests/board-card-start-selftest.sh`'s `KBCARD_API= KBCARD_TOKEN_FILE=` env prefixes introduced **SC1007**, a warning class this repo did not previously carry anywhere. CI gates at `-S error` so nothing was failing, but `ci.yml` states the intent to tighten to `-S warning`; quoting them takes the repo-wide `-S warning` set back to `{SC1090, SC2034}` (measured 5 → 0). Both were raised by the card#5333 adversarial review, which returned after that PR had merged.
- **card#5333** — **`board-card-start <branch> --lint` silently dropped the flag and moved a card for real, and `board-card-start ""` moved the card for whatever branch `HEAD` was on.** Two defects on one unguarded argument surface of the tool that **writes to a live board**. `--lint` was honoured only in position 1 (`[ "${1:-}" = "--lint" ]`), so its most natural mis-spelling — the flag *after* the branch — fell through to the mover: a flag whose entire contract is "no board work" performing the write, **byte-for-byte the defect `install-board-hooks` documents fixing for `--check`**, in a parser that was never revisited when that one was. And `branch="${1:-$(git rev-parse …)}"` fires on **empty** as well as unset, so `board-card-start "$BRANCH"` with `BRANCH` unexpanded did not no-op — it retargeted the move to the current branch, i.e. **a write against a card the caller never named**. That is the empty-value class again — not a new *kind* of source (positionals were swept in this same release), but a **parser that sweep did not reach**: it covered `install-board-hooks` and `adopt-to-dl` and stopped there, leaving the instance sitting in the one tool of the three that **writes to the board**. Arguments are now parsed in a **loop** — `--lint` anywhere, an empty positional / an unknown option / a second positional refused **by name** — with "have I seen a positional?" still answered by the value, which is sound only because an empty one is now rejected outright — the same mechanism as the other two parsers. **The refusals keep the fail-soft contract:** every path still `exit 0`s, so a refusal is a *no-move*, never a non-zero exit — which is why `bcs_skip`/`kb_bcs_log` are deliberately **not** used for them (their wording asserts the branch carries a DL/card token, which an argument refusal has not established), and therefore why an argument refusal is **stderr-only** and never reaches the durable log. The mechanism that binds rc 0 here is **not** `hooks/post-checkout`, which passes **zero** arguments — no refusal arm is reachable from it — through a `>/dev/null 2>&1 || true` wrapper that would swallow a non-zero exit anyway. It is the **operator-authored chained** hook, an explicitly supported topology (HOOKS.md tells operators to chain the toolkit hook into a committed hook by hand; `board-session-close` models its `CHAIN-BROKEN` state and fixtures the unguarded `command -v board-card-start && board-card-start` shape), which carries no `|| true` — and per `githooks(5)`, `post-checkout`'s exit status **becomes** `git switch`/`git checkout`'s (measured on git 2.43: the checkout is not undone, but the command reports failure). **Zero arguments is untouched** and still resolves `HEAD`'s branch — `hooks/post-checkout` passes none, so discriminating `$# -eq 0` from `$1 == ""` was the whole point. **The `-*` arm needed a real end-of-options terminator, and its absence was a live false refusal.** The first cut of that arm was justified by "git rejects branch names starting with a dash", which is **false** and was refuted live on git 2.43: `git check-ref-format refs/heads/-foo` is rc 0 and `git update-ref refs/heads/-foo HEAD` creates the branch — only the `git branch` **porcelain** refuses the name — and a push of it fires `pre-push` with the bare name `-foo`. So `board-card-start --lint -foo` printed `unknown option: -foo — no card moved` on every push of such a branch, while the **mover still moved its card** (`post-checkout` passes no arguments, so it reaches the `git rev-parse HEAD` fallback and never enters the parser) — falsifying HOOKS.md's "the lint and the mover can never disagree". `--` is now a genuine terminator (it sets a flag the loop honors — **not** the decorative `--) ;;` that is accepted and then ignored), everything after it is a positional through the **one** positional owner both sides of the terminator reach, and `hooks/pre-push` passes `--lint -- <branch>`. A fixture-repo selftest covers all nine argument forms **plus `hooks/pre-push` itself** — the call site is exercised with real stdin in git's pushed-ref shape and a real `board-card-start` on `PATH`, because the false refusal was reachable only from there and a parser-only test would leave the hook free to drop the `--` again. The dash-leading branches are **created for real** with `git update-ref` rather than passed as strings, and git's acceptance of them — the premise the whole terminator serves — is itself an assertion, so a future git that rejected the name could not leave every other check passing over a premise that had silently gone false. Its 29 assertions were each watched **red** under a targeted mutation: the flag honoured in position 1 only, the flag accepted then dropped, an empty positional accepted, each refusal arm removed in turn, the `HEAD` fallback removed, the refusals given `install-board-hooks`' rc 2, the fail-soft skip and the lint path made non-zero, lint dispatch disabled, the terminator made **decorative** (`--) ;;` — accepted and ignored), the post-`--` argument re-exposed to the `-*` arm, the hook's `--` dropped (which reds the two call-site assertions and *only* those), the hook made unparseable, and a ref name git genuinely rejects fed to the premise check. The zero-args case doubles as the positive control that a run which *does* reach board work is detectable at all. **Two assertion-quality corrections came out of that pass and are worth recording:** a "no move attempted" check on the *warn-worthy* dash-name could not fail under any mutation (the lint warns only where the grammar does **not** recognize the branch, so such a name can never reach board work) and was moved onto the **correlating** dash-name; and the hook's rc-0 assertion pins that the hook **parses and runs**, not that `board-card-start` returned 0 — the `|| true` and the trailing `exit 0` each independently force rc 0 — which is now stated at the assertion so it is not read as a guarantee it does not give. **Audit:** the remaining positional CLIs are clean for this shape — `agent-board-toolkit-drift-check` requires both positionals non-empty, `runtime-check`'s `--reference` uses `${2:?…}`, and `board-snapshot`'s only positionals are function locals. **⚠ This narrows what the tool accepts:** `board-card-start ""` is a stderr refusal rather than a move against the current branch, `board-card-start <branch> --lint` lints instead of moving, and an unknown flag or a second positional is refused — all still at rc 0, so no caller's exit-status handling changes. A branch name that starts with `-` must now be passed after `--` (the refusal names that fix). **`hooks/pre-push` changed with it**, and the two must move together: on the normal symlink install the hook content tracks the toolkit automatically, but on the Windows/MSYS **copy** topology (INSTALL.md §2) the stale copy still calls the bin without `--`, so the false refusal survives there until `install-board-hooks` is re-run per HOOKS.md's copy procedure. `bin/` is unchanged for every other tool and `promote/action.yml` is untouched, so no other vendored surface moves.
- **card#5334** — **the third `--help` fixed-line-range instance was still live, and it failed in the LEAK direction.** `agent-board-toolkit-runtime-check` printed `sed -n '2,30p' "$0"` — the defect v0.23.1 fixed for both release movers (card#5145), in the one file that card's sibling audit never reached. Its header is **28** lines while the range said 30, so `--help` printed 29 lines and the last one was `set -euo pipefail`: the *inverse* symptom of the movers' truncation, from the same root cause, which is why the suite's central assertion was written as a line-count **equality** rather than a minimum. It now prints the contiguous leading comment block via the movers' `awk` form, and it **joins `tests/help-output-selftest.sh`'s `CLIS`** — so the leak direction, previously only a stated hazard in that file's rationale, now has a real in-repo witness. Adding it also reddened the suite's `Usage:` assertion, which turned out to be reporting a **second** defect: the header explained `--quiet` and `--reference` in prose but carried **no usage synopsis at all**, so `--help` never showed how to invoke the tool; a `Usage:` line was added. The other three `--help` arms were audited and deliberately **not** added to `CLIS`: `dl-a0-backfill-triaged` and `dl-a1-register-field` echo a one-line `$USAGE`, and `adopt-to-dl` echoed a one-line `usage()` — to **stderr** at the time, corrected by card#5337 below in this same release — so none prints a header block and a header-equality assertion does not apply to them; converting them would be an unrequested change to user-facing output.
- **card#5226** — **`install-board-hooks` answered three separated git topologies with one message that was wrong for all of them.** `install-board-hooks <linked-worktree>` (and the same for a `--separate-git-dir` checkout or a submodule) reported `cannot create the hooks directory <root>/.git/hooks` and exited 1 — naming a path git never reads, asserting a cause that is not the problem, and offering no way forward. It is a **reachable** path: this project's own reference host keeps a linked worktree. The installer now resolves the repo's real git **common dir** and refuses when it is not `<root>/.git`, with the message the topology actually needs: a **linked worktree** is told to run `install-board-hooks <main-checkout>` (which wires it too, since the hooks dir is shared by the main checkout and every worktree — installing from the worktree would have changed hook behavior for all of them, the surprising blast radius that decided this disposition); a **`--separate-git-dir`** checkout is told there is no second checkout to run it against and given the dir to symlink into; a **submodule** is told that running it against the superproject would wire a *different repository*, and given `<super>/.git/modules/<name>/hooks`. Only the first has a command to print, which is why one generic message was not an option. **The discriminator named in the report was wrong and is corrected here:** `--git-common-dir` ≠ `--git-dir` is true **only** for the linked worktree — measured on git 2.43, the other two report them *equal* — so a guard built on it would have shipped covering one topology of three; the selftest carries that exact mutation and it reds. Detection is by common dir, never by `.git`-is-a-file (all three share that shape). The refusal is **conditional on `core.hooksPath` being unset**, not on topology alone: a set `hooksPath` wins everywhere, so a worktree that configures one is installable and refusing it would be a false refusal — a second mutation pins that. `board-session-close`'s own topology predicate is retired onto the installer's `_ibh_main_checkout`/`_ibh_common_dir`, so the check and the installer cannot drift apart on which checkout owns a shared hook dir.
- **card#5200** — **a `core.hooksPath` value git cannot expand is reported by a different arm on a different git build, and the disclosure said one of them was unreachable.** On git 2.43.0 such a value is expanded during the *general* config read, so every command in the repo fatals — `rev-parse` included — and the check reports git's own words. On git 2.54.0 `rev-parse` succeeds and only the explicit `--path` read fatals, so the second arm reports it. Both are live. The arm was kept deliberately (folding an unknown read status into "unset" is the guess this check exists to prevent) but was annotated *"the other arm always wins — verified"*, and the test **pinned that arm's exit code**: it passed four adversarial review rounds on one host and failed on CI's first run in a second environment. The disclosure now names the mechanism and both builds and claims no ordering; the test asserts the **guarantee both arms owe** — refused, never folded into "unset", with a cause specific enough to act on — and accepts either route; a `git` shim fixture forces the other arm so **both are exercised wherever the suite runs**, not only on the builds that happen to route to them. Both refusal arms now carry a cause on stdout, so the caller's contract no longer depends on which one fired. The transferable lesson is in the code: a measurement taken in one environment is scoped to it, and "verified" without naming where is how a local artifact gets read as a general property.
- **card#5200** — **`adopt-to-dl`'s four value-taking flags read an empty value as an absent flag, and `--dl ""` minted a SECOND DL.** `kb_require_value` was already sourced and in scope, and `kbcard` rejected the identical input at all 37 of its arms; these four arms were never converted. `adopt-to-dl "$CARD" --dl "$DL"` with `DL` unexpanded therefore took the **mint** path — but `--dl N` exists *only* for the crash-retry-after-mint case, so a retry that meant "re-stamp DL-N" minted a new one instead, **orphaning DL-N and stranding any branch or PR already named for it**: exactly what `_ata_adopt_decision`'s refuse-adopted arm exists to prevent. `--issue ""` silently dropped the co-stamp while still printing "adopted … verified via by-ref". All four now use the shared guard, which also converts a trailing flag with no argument from a bare `shift` failure (rc 1, **no output at all**) into a named diagnostic. **This is the empty-value class in its third position on one axis** — config reads, then positionals, now flag values — and the finding that matters is procedural: the sweep that declared the class closed was run against every bin *except the one being edited*, where the instance sat four lines above the arm it had just hardened. A sibling audit is scoped by the **shape**, and it must include the file the change is in.
- **card#5200** — **an explicitly-empty POSITIONAL argument was invisible, so the next one silently took its place.** `install-board-hooks "" <repo>` installed into `<repo>` at rc 0 — defeating the "an extra positional is rc 2" guarantee in the very parser that had just added it — and `adopt-to-dl "" 4242` adopted card 4242 the same way. Both tracked "have I seen a positional?" by testing the value for emptiness, and an unexpanded shell variable is the way in. **This is the v0.23.1 empty-value class for the third time in this branch**, and it says the earlier sibling audit was scoped to the wrong axis: that sweep covered *sources that can be set-empty* (config reads, env knobs) and correctly found `core.hooksPath`, but v0.23.1's own original instance was **argument parsing**, which the sweep never covered — so the shape survived into a parser written afterwards. The axis for a sibling audit is the **shape** (a value that can be present-but-empty where *presence* is the real question), never the category of source it was last found in. Both parsers now reject an empty positional **by name**, which is what makes their existing value-emptiness test a sound "have I seen one?" — a separate seen-flag was written and removed, because with empties rejected nothing could reach it and a mutation of it could not be made to fail. (That sweep still missed a third position on the same axis — `adopt-to-dl`'s own flag values, in the file it was editing; see the entry above.) **⚠ Both tools now reject invocations they previously accepted** — `adopt-to-dl "" <id>` is rc 2 rather than adopting `<id>` — the same narrowing, and the same reason, as the v0.23.1 flag-value change.
- **card#5200** — **"can git run this hook?" was computed in three places, and each review round found a new fact that had to be added to all of them.** The executable bit was added to the chain path after the direct path already had it; exec-ability was then added to both; and three more one-predicate gaps followed in the same family — a **tab-separated shebang** (`#!<TAB>/bin/sh` and `#!/bin/sh<TAB>` both *run*, and were being reported as a dead hook: a false RED on a healthy repo), an interpreter that is a **directory** (`-x` is true for one), and a first line with **no trailing newline** (`read` returns non-zero while still assigning, so the verdict was discarded). Applying each new fact to both paths, correctly, still left the next one to be applied to both paths. The split was the defect, so the property is now **one predicate — `_bsc_runnable`** — that owns the bit, the file type, the shebang read, the kernel's separator set (space and tab — **only** those two; a POSIX `[[:space:]]` class is a superset that also matches CR/VT/NL/FF, and reading it as "the kernel's whitespace rule" trimmed a broken interpreter name down to a runnable one, so `#!/bin/sh<CR> -e` and `#!<VT>/bin/sh` reported healthy while git failed `cannot exec`), the interpreter's existence/type/bit, and CR/CRLF; both the direct path and the chain path call it and decide nothing themselves. The extraction immediately closed a fourth gap nobody had reported: a **directory** where the hook should be. A hook git cannot run is reported as one `NOT-RUNNABLE` state whose reason text comes from the predicate, so the operator sees which of those facts failed without the report carrying a state per fact. (The intermediate per-fact states this work passed through never shipped.)
- **card#5200** — **a fixture asserted a DEAD hook was "genuinely runnable", and it blocked the correct fix.** The disclosed shebang bound had been stated as a truncation case with a "safe half": that a long shebang line naming a short interpreter "runs normally". Run for real, `#!/bin/sh <500 a's>` dies with `/bin/sh: cannot open aaaa…` — the auto-move is dead while the check says ✓, and the test asserted that as safe. The real bound is broader and was undisclosed: **the shebang argument is never judged**, and twelve bytes are enough (`#!/bin/sh zzz` → check OK, hook dead; `#!/bin/sh -e` → correctly fires). Worst of all, implementing over-long detection reddened *two* assertions, one of which was that false fixture — **a mutation count vouching for a defect**. The false assertion is deleted, the argument bound is disclosed with a control that pins the accepted-argument case, and the same mutation now reds exactly the one true assertion.
- **card#5200** — **a hook that is executable but cannot be EXEC'd reported healthy.** The executable bit is not exec-ability: a hook with **CRLF line endings** or a **missing shebang interpreter** is `-x`, looks perfect, and makes git fail with `fatal: cannot exec '<hook>': No such file or directory` (verified against git 2.43) — so the card auto-move is dead while the check says ✓. CRLF is the native hazard of the Windows/MSYS **copy** install topology this toolkit explicitly supports, making it a supported-platform failure rather than a hypothesis. Detecting it is a **pure read of line 1**, so it is *fixed*, not disclosed — the "closing this would require executing an arbitrary hook" rationale that makes the other two false-OK bounds acceptable does not apply, and disclosing it under that rationale would have stated something untrue. Applied on the direct hook **and** the chained target, because the `-x` check itself had just been fixed in one of those two places only. (Both report through the consolidated `NOT-RUNNABLE` / `CHAIN-BROKEN` states described above; the intermediate token this fix first introduced never shipped.)
- **card#5200** — **a non-canonical path spelling was told its `.git` is a file.** The fix-line compared the installer's target against the dispatch dir as **strings**, but `--git-common-dir` answers relative to the path the caller typed while the classification uses the always-canonical `--show-toplevel`. A **symlinked repo path**, a **trailing slash**, or a **sub-directory of the work tree** therefore fell through to the "linked worktree / --separate-git-dir / submodule" arm, which asserted a falsehood about an ordinary checkout **and** withheld the `install-board-hooks` invocation that in fact succeeds (the installer canonicalizes, so all three are rc 0). Both sides are now canonicalized before comparison, and the fall-through arm reports the two concrete directories instead of asserting a cause.
- **card#5200** — **the fix-line named a command that exits 1, in its MOST COMMON case, because it re-derived `install-board-hooks`' preconditions instead of asking.** The partial copy proved 2 of the installer's 9 preconditions, so every finding whose hook is a regular file — the normal shape of `NO-REACH`, `NOT-RUNNABLE` and `CHAIN-BROKEN`, and a repo carrying its own committed `post-checkout` is the likeliest real cause of a dead auto-move — was told to run a command that hits the installer's clobber refusal. An unwritable hooks directory was a second instance. **The copy was the defect**, and it produced a wrong answer in two consecutive reviews, so the fix is structural rather than two more predicates: `install-board-hooks` gains a **`--check` dry run** that evaluates every precondition in the same function and the same order as a real run, stops before the first write, prints the directory it *would* target on success and its own diagnostic on refusal — and `board-session-close` now **gates on that verdict**. What stays caller-side is the question `--check` cannot answer: whether that target is the directory git *dispatches* from (the installer can succeed while installing where git never looks). A refusal is now reported in the installer's own words with no remedy invented — notably **no `ln -s` suggestion**, which for the clobber case would have told an operator to destroy their own hook.
- **card#5200** — **a hook chaining to a NON-EXECUTABLE toolkit hook reported healthy.** The direct-hook path checked the executable bit from the start (git ignores a non-executable hook), but the one-level chain path did not, so a committed hook that `exec`s a toolkit hook whose bit had been dropped read as `OK` while git's exec failed and the card auto-move was dead — a false green of exactly the class this check exists to eliminate. The chained target is now checked too, and reported as its own `CHAIN-BROKEN` state naming the file it cannot exec.
- **card#5200** — **the remediation line named a command that exits 1 on three real topologies.** A `.git` *file* — linked worktree, `--separate-git-dir` clone, submodule — makes `install-board-hooks <repo>` target an un-creatable `<repo>/.git/hooks`. The line is now derived by comparing the directory the installer would target against the one git dispatches from (the same resolver, one substituted input), so it names the repo only when that provably works, names a sibling checkout that owns the hook dir when one does, and otherwise states plainly that the installer cannot service the checkout and gives the manual `ln -s`. The earlier `*/.git/hooks` path heuristic covered only `git worktree` and silently missed the other two.
- **card#5200** — **a `..`-relative `core.hooksPath` that escapes the work tree was classified as inside it.** The prefix test compared the un-normalized string, so `../shared-hooks` still began with the repo root and drew the in-tree refusal and its hand-chain guidance. A pure lexical normalizer (no filesystem access, so the resolver stays testable) now runs before the classification only — the echoed path is left as git would open it, symlinked components included.
- **card#5200** — **`core.hooksPath` set to an EMPTY value was read as "not configured", by both the new check and `install-board-hooks`.** Verified against git 2.43: an empty value does **not** fall back to `.git/hooks` — git dispatches **no hooks at all** — and `git config --get` separates it from *unset* by **exit status only** (unset → rc 1, empty → rc 0 with empty output). Reading the value's emptiness therefore certified a repo whose card auto-move is dead, and made the installer `ln -sf` into a directory git never reads while reporting success: the exact **#4281 silent no-op** the installer exists to prevent. A single `_ibh_read_hooks_path` reader now returns **presence as a status**, `_ibh_hooks_dir` takes that presence explicitly and returns a distinct "hooks disabled" state, the check reports it with its own remediation (`--unset`, not a re-install), and the installer **refuses** rather than plant a no-op. This is a **sibling of the v0.23.1 empty-flag class** re-minted in a config read; the generalisation — *any source that can express set-empty must have its presence read as a status, never inferred from the value* — is recorded on the reader.
- **card#5200** — **a `~`-prefixed `core.hooksPath` was resolved as a relative path.** `core.hooksPath` is a path-type variable and git expands `~`/`~user` (verified: a hook in `~/<dir>` fires). Reading the raw value produced **both** a false red (a working repo reported dead, and mis-classified as inside the tracked work tree, so the operator was told to hand-chain a hook it did not need) **and** a false green (planting at the literal `<root>/~/<dir>` while the real directory stayed empty). Both callers now read via `git config --path`, which also leaves a relative value unchanged.
- **card#5200** — **a repo git refuses is no longer given a guessed cause.** `install-board-hooks` asserted *"not a git repo"* and the new check *"not a git work tree"* whenever `git rev-parse` failed. Several distinct states land there — a `core.hooksPath` git cannot expand (which fatals **every** command in the repo, the probe included), `dubious ownership` (`safe.directory`), and a genuine non-repo — and each needs a different fix. Both now print **git's own diagnostic**; a wrong-but-specific cause is worse than an honest one.

## [0.23.1] - 2026-07-27

**Patch — an explicitly-empty flag value no longer reads as an absent flag anywhere in the toolkit, `--help` stops truncating, and an unguarded promote says so.** 6 PRs since v0.23.0 (#169–#174).
