Per `VERSIONING.md § Bump sizing` this is a **patch**: every change is a fix, a doc correction, or an internal test addition — no new `bin/` tool, no new flag, no new capability. **This release DOES touch the vendored surface** (`bin/kbcard`, `bin/_kb-board-lib.sh`, `bin/promote-released-cards`, `bin/release-pr-body`) — consumers who vendor those scripts get the stricter flag handling and the new stderr line; `promote/action.yml` is unchanged, so consumers who only pin the action see no interface change.

**⚠ UPGRADING — the CLIs now reject `--flag ""` where they previously accepted it silently.** This narrows what the tools accept, and it is deliberate: an explicitly-empty value was indistinguishable from the flag being absent, so it selected a **default path** and exited 0. A caller passing an unexpanded shell variable (`--board "$KEY"` with `KEY` unset) was the common way in. Any script relying on `--flag ""` as a way to say "use the default" must now omit the flag instead. Whitespace-only values already died before this release and are unchanged.

### Fixed
- **#169 (card#5144)** — **`promote-released-cards --shipped-stages ""` ran UNGUARDED, with a summary identical to a guarded run.** Every consumer of the variable gated on non-emptiness, so an empty value skipped the validation block (`:100`), the anti-resurrection **source-stage guard** (`:304`), and the `stage-guarded` field in the summary (`:322`) — byte-identical to passing no flag at all. An unexpanded shell variable, an empty CI input, or `--shipped-stages "$STAGES"` with `STAGES` unset therefore produced exactly the failure the guard exists to prevent: a stale or recycled DL/PR stamp resurrecting a declined or backlog card into Released, reported clean. **Reproduced empirically against the selftest fixture before fixing** — flag absent ⇒ rc 0, `wont_do` card promoted (the documented opt-in default); `--shipped-stages 51` ⇒ rc 0, not promoted (guard works); `--shipped-stages ""` ⇒ rc 0, **promoted, unguarded**; `--shipped-stages " "` ⇒ rc 2 (already died). **A correction to the report rides with the fix:** the reporter's stated tell was that the `''` arm at `:103` is *unreachable* and that the code therefore already intended to reject this. The arm is **reachable** — a whitespace-only value normalizes into it and dies today — so the hole was only the *pre-normalization* empty string; the intent reading still holds, but the arm is live code and is now pinned by a test so it is not later deleted as dead. The fix is a `require_value <flag> <value>` primitive that **dispatches on the flag being seen rather than on its value being non-empty**, applied to **all five** value-taking flags (`--dls`, `--base`, `--head`, `--shipped-stages`, `--config`), not just the reported one — the same shape silently switched `--dls ""` from explicit-DL mode to git-range derivation and `--base ""` to local-tag derivation. It also converts a trailing flag with no argument from a bare `set -u` unbound-variable error into a named diagnostic.
- **#171 (card#5146)** — **the same empty-value shape, in every other CLI — found by sibling audit, not by a report.** Filed unprompted from card#5144's audit (canon #7) and approved before implementing. Any flag that consumes `"$2"` and is then dispatched with `[[ -n "$var" ]]` reads an explicitly-empty value as an absent flag, silently selecting the default path and exiting 0. **The instance that matters most is the correlation stamp:** `kbcard patch --task 5137 --dl "$DL"` with `DL` unset sent payload `{}`, PATCHed `{}`, printed a normal success echo, and exited 0 — leaving the card without the ref it was told to carry, and **a card with no `dl_number` / `pr_number` never promotes at release.** The same held for `--pr`, `--pr-url`, `--issue`, `--issue-url`, `--version`, `--column`, `--swimlane`, and `--description`. **The worst is `--board ""`**, which fell through to the *default* board — so an unexpanded `--board "$KEY"` read and wrote **a different board than the caller named**, a wrong-board answer rather than a no-op, demonstrated live against the real tooling before fixing. `kb_require_value` in `bin/_kb-board-lib.sh` is the single owner, adopted by `kbcard` at all **37** value-taking arms; the two self-contained standalones that must not source the lib carry inline mirrors, the same convention already used for `kb_require_https_host`.
- **#170 (card#5145)** — **`--help` printed a fixed line range, so it truncated the header it exists to show.** Both release movers printed their header with `sed -n '2,20p' "$0"` — silently wrong in **both** directions, truncating once the header outgrows the range and leaking code once it shrinks. `promote-released-cards`'s header had grown to **43 lines**, so `--help` stopped at line 20 and never reached `Usage:` (line 35) or the `--shipped-stages` explanation (lines 37–44) — the single block a `--help` user most needs was unreachable. `release-pr-body`'s header is exactly 19 lines, so `2,20p` happened to print all of it: correct today, and it would have begun truncating silently at the first line added. Widening the range was rejected as the fix — it leaks `set -euo pipefail` and the helper functions into user-facing output. Both now print the contiguous leading comment block via `awk 'NR>1 { if (substr($0,1,1) == "#") print; else exit }'`, so output tracks the header at whatever length it reaches. New `tests/help-output-selftest.sh` covers both CLIs from one owner, and its central assertion is a **line-count equality** against the file's own contiguous-comment count, re-derived from the file on every run — an "at least N lines" or "contains `Usage:`" assertion passes while truncating everything after the match, which is precisely how this survived; equality catches truncation **and** over-printing.
- **#173 (card#5152)** — **an unguarded promote now states that it was unguarded, on stderr.** card#5144 made an explicitly-empty `--shipped-stages` die, but the **absent** case still printed a summary indistinguishable from a guarded run, so an unguarded promote read as clean — the same *"looks enabled, isn't"* shape the guard exists to prevent, one layer out, and it also covers the #172 case where an unset repo variable yields no guard at all. **Fixing the summary line was the obvious change and the wrong one:** the summary is byte-identical when the guard is absent by an explicit v0.21.0 fleet-adoption decision (*"absent = byte-identical prior behavior — consumers opt in"*), anything parsing it would break, and a selftest asserts it — overturning that needed its own DL and an argument that could not be made honestly, because the compat property is doing real work. A **separate stderr line** gets the visibility without touching it: the summary stays a stable parse target, and stderr is already where this tool puts its operator-facing findings. The OFF line names **what actually happened** and the remedy, not just a state. Verification asserts both directions plus **that the OFF line is not on stdout**, which would silently break the parse target the change exists to preserve. Split out of card#5148 rather than bundled, because it collided with that compat property and needed its own decision.

### Docs
- **#172 (card#5148)** — **`docs/INSTALL.md` §6a recommended a wiring that silently disables the guard it was enabling.** The snippet wired `shipped-stage-ids: ${{ vars.KANBAN_SHIPPED_STAGE_IDS }}`; an unset variable renders as a blank input, `promote/action.yml` correctly omits the flag, and the anti-resurrection guard is **off** — no error, no warning, and a promote summary identical to a run that never asked for it, while the workflow file still *reads* as guard-enabled to the next person auditing it. The same **"looks enabled, isn't"** shape as card#5144, one layer up, and it was the **recommended** wiring, so the docs walked consumers into it. The stage ids are per-board constants that change only when a board's columns are reconfigured, so the variable indirection bought nothing and cost silent failure: the snippet now carries a **literal** (explicitly marked an example id, not a prescription), and the variable form is demoted to an aside naming the hazard with a preflight mirroring the two already documented directly above it for `KANBAN_API_BASE` / `KANBAN_EXPECTED_HOST`, so an unset variable fails the job instead of quietly downgrading it. **The card's original direction also asked for an action-side `::warning::` on blank; it was dropped after working the mechanism** — `promote/action.yml` cannot distinguish "the operator omitted the input" from "the operator wired a variable that is unset", since both arrive as a blank string, so an unconditional warning would fire at every consumer who has deliberately not adopted an **opt-in** guard, and a check that cries wolf is one the first person it annoys switches off. Only the consumer knows its own intent, which is why the preflight belongs consumer-side; that reasoning is recorded in the doc so the next reader does not re-propose it.
- **#174** — the managed `coord:solo-orientation` block re-synced to coord plugin **v0.24.0** (from v0.23.0) via `orientation-sync.py --apply --role solo --integration dev`. Block-only change; this repo's own addendum outside the markers is untouched. The content delta adds `MIRROR-BEGIN/END` markers around the context-budget chat-gate prose, which are load-bearing: the framework's `chat-gate-mirror-drift.selftest.py` extracts exactly what they enclose and reds if any of the five lockstep copies diverges.

## [0.23.0] - 2026-07-24

**Minor — `board-session-close` gains a read-only "archive-eligible done cards" surfacing leg, and the archive-gate plumbing is factored into a shared lib.** 1 feature PR (#166, card#5094) since v0.22.0, plus a solo-orientation sync to coord v0.23.0 (#165, chore). The new leg reports each board's done-card backlog and gates a bounded sample through the shipped `may_archive` gate so a session-close can surface concrete archive candidates; the shared `kanban_common` locator + GitHub-state resolver are extracted so `_kbc-may-archive.py` and the new leg share one copy (canon #5, `_kbc-may-archive.py` byte-identical). No breaking CLI change; `promote/action.yml` vendor surface unchanged.

### Added
- **#166 (card#5094)** — **`board-session-close` surfaces archive-eligible done cards (read-only).** A new sibling leg — `bin/_kbc-archive-eligible.py`, surfaced under a `── Archive-eligible done cards (consider: kbcard archive #N) ──` section after the inverse-drift check — reports, per configured board (`kanban.boards[]`), **how many terminal (done-lane) cards are not yet archived** and runs the framework's shipped `may_archive` gate over a **bounded sample** of them to show a concrete "safe to archive now" starting point, so the agent can decide to run `kbcard archive #N`. It fetches the one board GET, collects the non-archived cards, filters to those whose stage `lane_type == "done"`, and gates the first `_MAX_DETAIL` with `may_archive(card, resolve, surviving_cards=<all non-archived>)` (a live source with a surviving non-archived twin is correctly reported eligible). The gate makes one `gh` call per backing source and the authoritative per-card gate already runs in `kbcard archive` at archive time, so the sample is bounded to keep the session-close ritual fast — the exact done-count is the actionable signal. It is **READ-ONLY** — it only surfaces; the archive is the agent's call. It fails **loud**: a board with an unset/`REPLACE_ME` id is reported skipped, and an unresolvable `kanban_common` or a board-fetch failure prints a ⚠ to stderr and exits non-zero. In `board-session-close` a **missing sibling is a warning, not a hard failure** (the drift check already ran; advisory). The shared archive-gate plumbing — the version-agnostic `kanban_common` locator and the tri-state GitHub-state `resolve(src)` reader — is **extracted into a new `bin/_kbc-archive-lib.py`** that BOTH the new leg and the refactored `_kbc-may-archive.py` path-load (canon #5: the second consumer extends the primitive instead of siblinging a divergent copy); `_kbc-may-archive.py`'s behavior is byte-identical (its selftest passes unchanged). New network-free `tests/kbc-archive-eligible-selftest.sh` (fake `kanban_common` wrapping the real `may_archive` + a fake board + a per-PR-number fake `gh`) pins the eligible-when-all-terminal, blocked-when-live-and-untwinned, done-lane-only-candidate, and archived-skipped cases, with a prove-it-can-fail that inverting the done-lane filter makes the in-progress case appear. `promote/action.yml` vendor surface unchanged.

## [0.22.0] - 2026-07-23

**Minor — a dispatch-time card-start hook closes the dispatch→branch-creation latency window, plus the promote source-stage guard adopted in the toolkit's own release-promote workflow.** 2 PRs since v0.21.0 (#159, #162), cards #4945/#4962. The headline is a new Claude Code `PreToolUse` hook that moves a card to In Progress the moment a subagent build is dispatched — rather than at the later branch creation. No `bin/` CLI behavior change; `promote/action.yml` vendor surface unchanged.

### Added
- **#159 (card#4945)** — **`hooks/agent-dispatch-card-start` — a dispatch-time "In Progress" card move.** A Claude Code `PreToolUse` hook for the `Agent` (subagent-dispatch) tool that moves a card to In Progress the moment a build is dispatched, closing the latency window that `hooks/post-checkout` (→ `board-card-start`) only closes at branch creation. It is a **peer** of that hook, not a replacement — either can fire first, and the move is idempotent in either order. It acts **only** on an explicit `BOARD-CARD: <board-key>#<card-id>` marker line anchored at line start in the dispatch prompt (`.tool_input.prompt` from the stdin event JSON); a bare card-number scan is deliberately **not** used, because review/report dispatches routinely mention many card ids in prose. Multiple marker lines are each acted on (exact duplicates deduped) and routed per marker to their board key. Transport is the existing `kbcard --board <key> move --task <id> --column in_progress` primitive — kbcard owns board-env/token resolution, so the hook hand-rolls no `curl` and the board-key charset forbids traversal into the `~/.kanban-<key>-board.env` resolve. It is **fail-soft on every path** (exit 0 always — a nonzero `PreToolUse` would block the dispatch): unparseable stdin, no marker, unknown board key, `kbcard` missing, or an API error all diagnose to stderr and continue, and each move is bounded by `timeout` (`KBADS_TIMEOUT`, default 10s). Registration is a **manual operator step** — a `PreToolUse` matcher `Agent` entry in Claude Code `settings.json` (snippet in `docs/HOOKS.md`); it is intentionally **not** installed by `bin/install-board-hooks`, which symlinks git hooks, not Claude Code settings. New `tests/agent-dispatch-card-start-selftest.sh` (PATH-shim `kbcard`, scratch HOME) covers anchor / no-marker / malformed-JSON / unknown-key / multi-marker / dedupe / kbcard-failure cases and joins the CI selftest matrix + a dedicated `ci.yml` job.

### Changed
- **#162 (card#4962)** — **the toolkit's own `release-promote-cards.yml` adopts the v0.21.0 `shipped-stage-ids` source-stage guard.** The workflow consumes the **local** `./promote` composite action (no external pin to bump) and now opts in to the `shipped-stage-ids` input introduced in v0.21.0, passing the tracking board's Shipped-to-Dev stage id. At release-promote time a DL/PR-matched card whose **current** stage is not Shipped-to-Dev is skipped (with a log line) instead of moved — a stale or recycled correlation stamp can no longer resurrect a declined or backlog card into Released. The guard is strictly **opt-in per consumer**: a blank input is byte-identical prior behavior. This applies the guard to the repo's own promotion, mirroring the adoption sibling consumers make via their SHA-pinned action reference.

## [0.21.0] - 2026-07-23

**Minor — the `kbcard field` verb family (custom-field schema read + enum converge-to-set reconcile) + the promote source-stage guard and decline stamp-clear, plus two solo-orientation syncs.** 4 PRs since v0.20.1 (#157, #155, #156, #154), cards #4939/#4940 (roundtable PupFuzz/agent-roundtable#132). `promote/action.yml` vendor surface: **one new optional input `shipped-stage-ids`** — absent = byte-identical prior behavior (proven empirically), so consumers adopt on their own windows. No other CLI behavior change absent the new flags.

### Added
- **#157 (card#4939, roundtable PupFuzz/agent-roundtable#132)** — **`kbcard field` verb family: the allow-listed custom-field/board-schema edit surface replacing raw PATCHes.** `kbcard --board <key> field list` reads the board's custom fields via `GET /boards/<id>/custom_fields.json` (empirically the only field-read surface — the per-id GET is 405), projecting id/key/label/type plus the full `{value,label}` option set for enum/multi_select fields. `kbcard --board <key> field set-options --field <name|id> --options a,b,c` is an **idempotent converge-to-set reconcile** (NOT an append): it PATCHes the whole options array only on drift and no-ops on an identical set — a provisioning preflight can call it with its full derived set and heal drift in one call. Refuses non-enum fields, empty/duplicate values; an unresolved `--field` enumerates the board's defined fields (mirroring `--swimlane`); API error bodies are preserved on failure. **Empirically pinned option-removal semantics** (sandbox-only experiment, stated in `--help` + README): removal is a **definition-only** change — the PATCH returns 200 even with the value in use, and a card holding a removed value keeps it verbatim (orphaned; no clear, no cascade). `add-option`/`rename-option` deliberately deferred to a v2. New network-free `tests/kbcard-field-selftest.sh` in the CI matrix (drift-write and identical-set-no-op mutually pinned; error-body preservation guarded).
- **#155 leg 1 (card#4940)** — **`promote-released-cards` source-stage guard (opt-in)**: `--shipped-stages <ids>` / action input `shipped-stage-ids`. A DL/PR-matched card whose current stage is not in the given set is **skipped + logged** — promotion means Shipped→Released and must never resurrect a declined/backlog card that retained a stale correlation stamp. Input absent = byte-identical prior behavior (proven empirically: identical stdout/stderr/rc/PATCH targets vs the pristine script); a malformed set fails loud (rc 2) before any PATCH. The 0-promoted die-branch deliberately keeps its PR-marker-completeness meaning (a source-stage-skipped DL match cannot disprove it; new red-when-reverted die-branch test).

### Changed
- **#155 leg 2 (card#4940)** — **`kbcard` decline stamp-clear**: both decline call-sites (`move --column wont_do` and `patch --column wont_do`) now clear `payload.dl_number`/`pr_number`/`pr_url` via explicit per-key JSON nulls in the same PATCH, through one shared owner (`_kbc_decline_nulls`) — closing the other half of the stale-stamp promote class at the write-site. `--keep-refs` opts out; an explicit ref flag passed in the declining call wins over the null; non-decline moves are byte-unchanged. (Also fixed en route: a `${1:-{}}` bash brace-matching bug in the shared helper.)

### Docs
- **solo orientation synced to coord v0.20.0** (#156; subagent dispatch REQUIRED + STOP-and-retry on API/limit termination; context-backstop CLEAR auto-fire nudge; Compliance section compressed to owner-pointer) and **v0.17.0** (#154; human-prompt bookends; repo-agnostic subagent dispatch).

## [0.20.1] - 2026-07-21

**Patch — a CI/test-only static regression guard against re-introducing the DL-219 flat-body wrapper, plus a docs sync.** 2 PRs since v0.20.0 (#148, #151). The substantive change is a network-free static test that fails if any `bin/` script re-introduces a `{task: {…}}` v3 write-body wrapper — covering even the standalone-vendored `promote-released-cards`, which can't source the shared lib — so the flat-body cutover can't silently regress. Plus a docs-only solo-orientation stamp sync (coord v0.15.1). No runtime CLI behavior change; `bin/` and `promote/action.yml` vendor surfaces unchanged.

### Added
- **#148** — **`tests/no-task-wrapper-selftest.sh` — a static regression guard against re-introducing the DL-219 `{task: {…}}` write-body wrapper** (card#4811). Follow-on hardening to card#4772: that cutover removed 8 wrapped sends, but two hid for two review rounds because they were hand-built escaped-quote string literals (`"{\"task\":{…}}"`) a plain `"task":` grep never saw. The new network-free selftest statically scans every regular file under `bin/` and fails if any **non-comment** line carries a `task`-keyed body wrapper in any of the three re-introduction shapes — unescaped JSON (`{"task":`), escaped literal (`{\"task\":`), or jq construction (`{task: {…}}` / `{task: $var}`) — via the matcher `("task"\s*:)|(\"task\"\s*:)|((^|[ ({,])task\s*:)`. The jq arm matches the wrapper key alone (not requiring a following `{`), so the brace-less argjson form is caught too; lines whose first non-whitespace char is `#` are excluded (the tree's one legitimate mention is the `# … {task: …}` doc note in `bin/kbcard`). A static test rather than a code helper because `bin/promote-released-cards` is standalone-vendored and cannot source the shared lib — and post-DL-219 there is no shared wrapper primitive left to centralize on — so only a source scan covers every bin uniformly. The test carries its own positive control (three temp fixtures, one per shape, asserted flagged — proving the matcher can fail) and a benign fixture (`task_id`/`to_task_id`/`from_task_id`/`task_links`/`.task-list`/`_action` + a `#`-comment mention, asserted **not** flagged). Added to the CI selftest matrix. No `bin/` or vendor-surface change.

### Docs
- **#151** — **solo-orientation stamp synced to coord v0.15.1** (`CLAUDE_AGENTBOARD.md`, v0.10.0 → v0.15.1). Stamp-only; the solo-orientation text itself is unchanged. No `bin/`/CI/vendor-surface change.

## [0.20.0] - 2026-07-21

**Minor — the v3 task-write API cutover drops the `{task: {…}}` body wrapper (in lockstep with the kanban DL-219 prod deploy), plus a live-source archive gate and two `release-pr-body` syncs.** 5 PRs since v0.19.0 (#143–#147), cards #4772/#4747/#4751/#4761/#4762 (roundtable PupFuzz/agent-roundtable#39/#109/#111). The headline is the unconditional flat-body cutover (#147) — no shim, no version probe, so the tools move in lockstep with the kanban prod deploy carrying DL-219 — riding with `kbcard archive`'s new `may_archive` live-and-untwinned-source gate and the owner-side `release-pr-body` `tag_format` sync. Plus a docs-only solo-orientation sync (coord v0.10.0). `promote/action.yml` vendor surface unchanged.

### Added
- **#146** — **`kbcard archive` gates on the shipped `may_archive` invariant, with an audited `--force` override** (card#4747, roundtable PupFuzz/agent-roundtable#39). Before the archive `PATCH`, `kbcard archive` now consults the framework's shipped `kanban_common.may_archive` primitive (path-loaded version-agnostically, the same ladder `board-session-close` uses for the reconcile hook) via a thin new `bin/_kbc-may-archive.py` shim: it builds a tri-state per-source resolver over the card's backing coordination sources (`pr_number`/`issue_number` by-ref → `gh pr|issue view --json state`, mapping `OPEN`→live, `CLOSED`/`MERGED`→terminal, a read that 404s/errors→unresolvable; an `id:<sid>` provenance tag → unresolvable, since kbcard has no GitHub handle for a coordination stable-id) and passes the board's live cards as `surviving_cards` for twin-awareness. If a backing source is still **live** (or unresolvable — fail-closed) and no surviving twin card keeps it discoverable, the archive is **refused** (rc 1, no `PATCH`) with a drift-vocabulary line (`⚠ archive withheld: card #<id> — <reason>`) — archiving the only card of a live source would hide it from the reconcile/detect backstop. `--force` performs the archive anyway and records an **audited** override (`⚠ archive FORCED past live-source gate: …` to stderr + the kbcard failure log), never a silent bypass. If the `may_archive` primitive cannot be located the gate **fails loud** (refuses, names `--force`) rather than archiving unchecked. A card fetch that returns HTTP 2xx with `.data` **null/absent** (a trashed / permission-limited / edge card) is treated as fail-closed at **both** choke points — the bash `_kbc_archive_decision` short-circuits to `noprimitive` before the board fetch, and the shim rejects an explicit-null `card` — so a null card can never collapse to a source-less `{}` that would false-`ok` and archive a live-and-untwinned card (the card-side sibling of the board input's safe `[]` default). And a **gate-infra failure never blocks `--force`**: if the decision can't be computed (e.g. `python3` absent), `--force` still archives AND still writes the audited override line (`… infra could not run — forced`), so the escape hatch isn't held hostage by the gate it exists to bypass — while the non-force path stays fail-closed (a gate-infra failure refuses). The by-ref GitHub-state read reuses the framework's own `gh_json`/`gh` credential path; the board→repo fallback reads `kanban.boards[]` from the coord config, and a card's own `pr_url`/`issue_url` source is derived via the framework normalizer when a descriptor carries no repo. `--dry-run` is unchanged (offline, no gate evaluation, no mutation). New `tests/kbc-may-archive-selftest.sh` exercises the shim's resolver tri-state over the **real** primitive with a fake `gh` (skipping the primitive-backed cases only where the plugin's `kanban_common` is absent), and `tests/kbcard-selftest.sh` gains the wiring cases (refuse / forced-audit / ok / fail-loud), red-when-reverted (removing the gate lets a blocked card archive). `promote/action.yml` vendor surface unchanged.

### Changed
- **#147** — **Task write bodies are sent FLAT (top-level), dropping the `{task: {…}}` wrapper** (card#4772). The kanban v3 write API unified to take task fields at the top level of the request body and no longer accepts the `{task: {…}}` envelope (kanban DL-219); every task `POST`/`PATCH` these tools issue now sends the task object flat. This is an **unconditional** cutover — no version probe, no wrapped fallback — so the tools must run against a kanban deploy that carries DL-219 (there is no shim; they move in lockstep with the kanban prod deploy). Migrated (8 task-write sites across 5 files, found by enumerating every `/tasks` write rather than grepping for `task:` — two were hand-built escaped-quote literals a name grep slid past): `kbcard` `create-card`/`move`/`patch`; the DL-stamp write **and** the move-to-In-Progress write in `board-card-start`; the tag backfill in `dl-a0-backfill-triaged`; the throwaway-card create + dl-clear in `dl-a1-register-field`; and the stage-move in `promote-released-cards`. Unaffected: `task_links` bodies (already flat, different endpoint) and `_action` bodies (never wrapped). `tests/kbcard-selftest.sh` now asserts the flat body shape (create-card tags/type, the shared create/patch payload, the swimlane setter). `promote/action.yml` vendor surface unchanged.
- **#145** — **`board-session-close` delegates its inverse-drift leg to the shipped `kanban-reconcile.py --detect`** (card#4751, roundtable PupFuzz/agent-roundtable#109 + #111). The bespoke bash inverse-drift check (In-Review cards whose recorded `pr_number` matches no open PR) is retired in favor of the framework-maintained detect hook — the sanctioned conforming implementation of the inverse-drift CONTRACT (the framework's `BOARD-SYNC.md` § "The detect backstop contract"), which previously named this bash as an accepted-divergence conforming implementation "until the solo hook's adoption completes." Both implementations were proven equivalent before the swap: on a synthetic positive control they flag the same two drift sub-cases (stale ref + missing correlation) and suppress the same clean card; on the live boards both report no inverse drift. The delegated hook is a net gain — it adds a **forward** leg (an open PR of a board's repo with no live card carrying its ref) the bash never had, and its inverse leg is repo-qualified `(source, ref)` tuples rather than a flat ref-number set. The hook path is resolved **version-agnostically** (explicit `$KANBAN_RECONCILE_HOOK` override → the coord plugin dir on `$PATH` → the marketplace clone → the newest cached version by `sort -V`) so a plugin update never breaks it, and an unresolvable hook **fails loud** (rc 1, "DID NOT RUN") rather than silently skipping the drift check; a non-zero hook exit propagates so a config/API failure isn't read as a reconciled board. New `tests/board-session-close-selftest.sh` (network-free) covers the resolver ladder and the surfacing / fail-loud / rc-propagation paths (verified red-when-reverted: 3 mutations each red their guarding check). Because a board without `inverse_check_columns` runs the forward leg only and reports `inverse check OFF` at rc 0 — a silent reduction of coverage where `board-session-close` previously keyed success purely on the hook's exit code — the ritual now scans the hook output and **re-emits the OFF state as a loud `⚠` warning on stderr**, naming the affected board key, outside the per-board `(no drift detected)` framing, so the degraded (inverse-off) coverage is never read as a clean pass. A board wholly `skipped` by the hook (absent from `kanban.boards[]`, or lacking a single-repo `repo`) is surfaced the same way. OFF is **not** a hard error (a board may intentionally not arm inverse) — the exit code and forward-leg output are unchanged; only the warning is added. **Adoption note:** the inverse leg is armed by an `inverse_check_columns` entry per board in `coordination.config.json` (`kanban.boards[]`); without it the hook runs the forward leg only and `board-session-close` warns that the inverse-drift check is off for that board.
- **#143** — **`release-pr-body` `tag_format` config knob + a second version-extraction stage (see the correction below — it did not do what this line said)** (card#4761, roundtable PupFuzz/agent-roundtable#39). Adopted from the agent-board-framework's `templates/release/release-pr-body`, which had evolved ahead of the toolkit copy: a new optional `.tag_format` key in `.release-pr.json` (default `v{{version}}`, preserving the historical behavior byte-for-byte) maps a version to its git tag name for non-`v`-prefixed schemes (`{{version}}`, `release-{{version}}`, …) — and the own-tag exclude in the baseline resolution now derives from it instead of a hardcoded `v` prefix, so a re-run after tagging under a non-`v` scheme no longer reports the current release as its own previous tag. Version-file extraction gained a second `[0-9]+(\.[0-9]+){1,3}` numeric stage, described here at the time as accepting 2–4 segments so a .NET `Major.Minor.Build.Revision` version (`1.22.1.0`) would no longer be silently truncated to `1.22.1`. **⛔ CORRECTED (card#7208, [Unreleased]): that stage ran AFTER the project's own `version_regex` and could only narrow what that regex had already matched — under a 3-segment `version_regex`, `1.22.1.0` still truncated to `1.22.1`. The claim was false when written; the stage has been removed and `version_regex` is now the only declaration of the shape.** Both behaviors gain red-when-reverted selftest coverage (verified: 5 checks fail on the pre-sync bin). **Ownership declared** (README): the toolkit owns both release bins (`release-pr-body` + `promote-released-cards`) — tests and release discipline live here; the framework's `templates/release/` copies are mirrors synced at toolkit tags, closing the fork this change reconciles.

### Fixed
- **#144** — **`release-pr-body` display header renders the real tag via `tag_format`** (card#4762). Owner-side follow-on to the card#4761 sync: the PR-body header hardcoded `v${VERSION}` independent of `tag_format`, so a non-`v` scheme showed a `v`-prefixed header while the actual tag differed. The header now renders the tag derived from `tag_format`, and the tag computation is hoisted ahead of the baseline block so it is also live on the explicit `--base` path; under the default `v{{version}}` the output is byte-identical. Red-when-reverted selftest coverage (verified: 3 checks fail on the pre-change bin, including the `--base` path).

## [0.19.0] - 2026-07-19

**Minor — the swimlane write-side lands: `kbcard patch --swimlane` plus a defined-swimlane enumeration on every unresolved name.** 1 PR since v0.18.0 (#140, bundling cards #4714 + #4713, roundtable PupFuzz/agent-roundtable#101 + #100), plus a docs-only solo-orientation sync. Both features activate only on a board env that declares `KB_SWIMLANE_<id>=<name>` entries; `promote/action.yml` vendor surface unchanged.

### Added
- **#140** — **`kbcard patch --swimlane <name|id>` — write-side swimlane setter** (card#4714, roundtable PupFuzz/agent-roundtable#101). Closes the read/write asymmetry left by `list --swimlane` (the read filter): a card could be listed by swimlane but only assigned to one via a raw `swimlane_id` card `PATCH`. The new flag writes the card's top-level `swimlane_id` column (rides the inner task object like `--column`'s `workflow_stage_id`, not the payload merge), resolving a name through the **same** `swimlane_id()` helper `list` already uses — no second resolver. `--swimlane none` (or `--swimlane 0`) is the explicit un-assign form, writing `swimlane_id` null; every other ref resolves name→id (numeric ids pass through) and fails loud (rc 2) on a typo. The write echo surfaces the resulting `swimlane_id` only when `--swimlane` was set (parity with it always showing `workflow_stage_id`), so other patches keep their echo shape. Additive and `patch`-only: `move` stays column-only.

### Fixed
- **#140** — **An unresolved `--swimlane <name>` now enumerates the board's defined swimlanes** (card#4713, roundtable PupFuzz/agent-roundtable#100). `swimlane_id()` printed `swimlane 'X' is not defined on this board` and stopped — with nothing listed, a reader reasonably infers the board has *no* such swimlane (e.g. missing a required lane prefix), and could report that false board fact. The error now appends the id→name map `_kbc_swimlane_map()` already builds (`defined swimlanes: 1=…, 2=…`), mirroring the board-not-found error's "boards found on this box: …" style; a board with no swimlanes declared gets a distinct honest line rather than an empty list. Error text only — the rc-2 loud-fail contract is unchanged, so `list --swimlane` still exits 2 on a typo.

## [0.18.0] - 2026-07-19

**Minor — one new `kbcard` correlation surface plus a fail-loud config error, riding on three same-behavior consolidations.** 5 PRs since v0.17.0 (#133–#137). The feature is issue↔card correlation stamping (`--issue`/`--issue-url`); the fix makes an unconfigured-board error name its own remedy; the remaining three are duplication-removal refactors with byte-identical runtime behavior. No config migration; `promote/action.yml` vendor surface unchanged.

### Added
- **#136** — **`kbcard --issue`/`--issue-url` for issue↔card correlation.** New optional flags on `create-card` and `patch` stamp `payload.issue_number` (a JSON number, matching `pr_number`'s numeric canonicalization) and `payload.issue_url` (which drives the by-ref `source` owner/repo for multi-repo boards, exactly as `pr_url` does), so a card can be correlated to a tracked issue without dropping to a raw per-key payload `PATCH`. The two keys are independent and co-stampable — a card can carry both `issue_*` and `pr_*` with no exclusivity check, the way `dl`/`pr` already coexist. `adopt-to-dl` gains a matching optional `--issue <N>` co-adoption path that co-stamps `issue_number` + a source-qualified `issue_url` in the same atomic write and fail-loud-verifies the `github_issue` by-ref parallel to the DL one. Additive only: omitted flags leave the keys absent (not null), nothing previously accepted is now rejected, and adoption without `--issue` is byte-identical to before. The shared `_kbc_build_payload` helper is extended to carry the new keys, so `issue_number` inherits the same numeric coercion `pr_number` already had rather than a duplicated merge.

### Fixed
- **#133** — **the unconfigured-board error now names its fix, and default-board resolution is documented.** On a box whose primary board env file isn't named `dev`, a bare invocation failed with `board env file not readable: …` and no path forward, reading as broken when it was merely unconfigured for a default. The `rc=2` board-env arm — the one sibling in `kb_load_config` that printed no remedy — now names `--board <name>` / `KBCARD_BOARD_ENV` (context-aware for the bare-default case vs a named board whose file is absent) and lists the `~/.kanban-*-board.env` files that do exist on the box. `INSTALL.md` documents `KBCARD_BOARD_ENV` as the flag-free option and adds the error to troubleshooting. Docs + error text only — the rc, the trigger, and default-resolution behavior are unchanged. Covers every `kb_load_config` consumer (`kbcard`, `adopt-to-dl`, `dl-a0-backfill-triaged`, `dl-a1-register-field`).

### Changed
- **#134** — **`_kbc_build_payload` shared by `create-card` + `patch`.** Four verbatim-duplicated payload-assembly blocks in `bin/kbcard` (the args→JSON merge `jq`, the `version_target` board-capability guard, the DL-canon append, and the `pr_number`/`pr_url` appends) are hoisted into one helper both commands call. Pure same-behavior refactor: the assembled `task.payload` is byte-identical to what each command produced before — numeric-string→JSON-number coercion, the version guard and its warning, and DL canonicalization all preserved; no command's accepted/rejected inputs or emitted payload change.
- **#135** — **selftest harnesses consolidated onto a shared `tests/_selftest-prelude.sh`.** The harness every `tests/*-selftest.sh` duplicated verbatim (`ok`/`bad`/`fails`, `eq`, `expect_rc`/`expect_out`, the required-bin guard, the scratch-dir setup, and the trailing PASS/FAIL summary) moves to one sourced prelude, removing nine copies and normalizing summary wording that had drifted across files. Harness-only: no test case or assertion changed, and all selftests pass identically before and after.
- **#137** — **assorted intra-file + CI deduplication (dedup-audit finding D6), same-behavior throughout.** Single-sources several rules that were each written twice, executable behavior byte-for-byte preserved: a `max_int` "highest int in a stream" helper shared by `next-dl`'s two scans (with new `tests/next-dl-selftest.sh` coverage it previously lacked); the throwaway-teardown request bodies in `dl-a1-register-field`; a shared jq leading-zero normalization across `promote-released-cards`' `dl_number`/`pr_number` fields; and a doubled token-file ladder in `board-snapshot`. On the CI side, `auto-tag-version.yml` now reads the version shape from `.release-pr.json`'s `version_regex` instead of a divergently-anchored hardcoded copy, and the byte-identical per-selftest jobs collapse to a `strategy.matrix` (no required check renamed; `shellcheck`, `drift-check-selftest`, and the composite-action smoke test stay separate). `promote/action.yml` / `_kb-board-lib.sh` unchanged — vendored consumers see only an internal refactor.

## [0.17.0] - 2026-07-18

**Minor — an 8-PR quality release: two roundtable-driven `kbcard`/hook features, a pagination-completeness fix ported into both board movers, a DL-primitive consolidation, and an error-body-preserving `board-card-start` refactor.** 8 PRs since v0.16.0 (#123–#130). Cards #4510/#4513/#4514/#4413/#4617/#4621/#4623/#4637. Two new feature surfaces (`create-card --triaged`, `kbcard list --swimlane`); no config migration; `promote/action.yml` vendor surface unchanged.

### Added
- **#124** — **`kbcard create-card --triaged` flag** (card #4617, roundtable FR PupFuzz/agent-roundtable#86). Mirrors the existing `patch --triaged` onto `create-card` so a card can be born triaged — a plain PM-minted product card that forgot `--tags triaged` otherwise re-surfaced as a false-positive untriaged card every session (the untriaged check keys on a missing `triaged` tag). On a fresh card the tags CSV is built from scratch, so a plain append suffices (no read-merge-write like the `patch` path). Scope is the generic `--triaged` verb only; any PM policy tag stays caller-passed. Works on native-type, tag-typing, and mixed boards.
- **#125** — **`board-card-start` accepts the glued `card<N>` branch spelling + a fail-soft pre-push branch-name lint** (card #4621). The post-checkout auto-move required a separator after `card`, so the natural glued spelling `fix/card4524-slug` matched no grammar — the hook fired, found no token, and the card silently never moved to In Progress. The separator is now optional (`card[-/#]?`), so `card4524`/`card-4524`/`card/4524`/`card#4524`/`#4524` all correlate (still requires the literal `card`/`#` at a token boundary + ≥2 digits, so DL tokens and version numbers stay safe). A new fail-soft `pre-push` lint warns (never blocks; always exit 0) only on a card-ish token the grammar still won't accept (e.g. `card_4524`), reusing the exact matcher the mover uses so the two can't disagree. The widened grammar affects the local hook only; the bridge writeback still requires `card-<id>`/`card#<id>`, which stays the canonical full-lifecycle convention.
- **#130** — **`kbcard list` surfaces `swimlane_id` + a resolved name, and a `--swimlane <name|id>` filter** (card #4637, roundtable FR PupFuzz/agent-roundtable#88). Per-swimlane triage previously dropped to raw-API glue because `list` omitted swimlane. Names resolve from the board env, id-keyed `KB_SWIMLANE_<id>=<name>` (the inverse of `KB_STAGE_<name>=<id>`, since swimlane names are freeform and can't be env suffixes) — zero extra API call. `--swimlane` composes with `--column`/`--type`; a typo'd name errors loud (rc 2), a numeric id passes through. The filter keys on the stringified id because `swimlane_id`'s API type is unverifiable on any reachable board (no board has swimlanes), where a numeric `==` would silently drop a string-typed id.

### Fixed
- **#127** — **`promote-released-cards` now censuses the whole-board read against `meta.total` so a short read can't silently promote a partial board** (card #4513). The mover's inline pagination had drifted from the lib's `fetch_board_cards` — it broke solely on a <200-row page and never consulted `meta.total`, so a server short read terminated the scan early and promoted from an INCOMPLETE board (a card its scan misses is a card it leaves un-promoted). Extracted into a self-contained `fetch_whole_board()` (the standalone must not source the lib) that captures `meta.total`, tracks a pre-dedup running sum, and after dedup distinguishes a genuine undercount (sum < total → DIE, matching the tool's page-cap refuse policy) from a page-boundary dedup artifact (sum ≥ total → warn only).
- **#129** — **`fetch_board_cards` defaults `meta.last_page` to UNKNOWN, not `1`** (card #4623). The lib defaulted `last_page` to `1` and broke unconditionally on `page >= last_page`; a server returning a full 200-row page 1 with neither `meta.last_page` nor `meta.total` present would skip the census and break at page 1 — a silently truncated read. Unreachable against the real Laravel `LengthAwarePaginator` (always emits both), hence latent, but it is the exact miss #4513 already guarded in the co-vendored standalone. Ported for parity: `last_page` is usable only as a positive integer (absent/non-numeric/<1 → unknown), and the secondary break fires only when the server positively declares it — byte-identical behavior on the real API path; a meta-absent read only becomes MORE complete.

### Changed
- **#123** — **`board-card-start` routes its card read + two writes through `kb_api`/`kb_api_status`** (card #4510). The read used a local `get()` and the dl-stamp + move-to-In-Progress writes used `curl -fsS`, which discards the HTTP status and the 4xx/5xx body — exactly the 403-scope-vs-422 indistinguishability the shared lib was fixed to remove (#4337), a fix that never reached these local calls. The "kept local so it sends THIS board's token" rationale was void (`kb_api` reads `KB_TOKEN`, which this hook already set). The two writes now go through a shared `_bcs_patch` helper that captures `KB_HTTP` and durably records a failure via `kb_bcs_log` — the previously-silent failed stamp/move now leaves a trace. Success-path behavior unchanged.
- **#126** — **DL int/format/by-ref-hit consolidated into shared `_kb-board-lib.sh` primitives** (card #4514, dedup-audit finding D5). Three DL-family duplications hoisted: `kb_dl_int_lenient` (the lenient client mirror of the server's `canonicalize('dl')` key, migrated from `adopt-to-dl`, kept a deliberate sibling of the strict `kb_dl_num`), `kb_by_ref_hit` (the "contains card N" predicate centralized from two forks; the lib keeps the object-or-array-tolerant form, so `adopt-to-dl`'s verify safely widens), and one canonical `DL-NNNN` formatter (`kb_dl_canon`) replacing six inline `DL-%04d` literals — `next-dl` gains a fail-loud guard on a computed DL outside the canonical range. `promote/action.yml` consumers unaffected.

### Docs
- **#128** — **`ADOPTION.md`'s "Is this for you?" gate keys on board API access, not instance ownership** (card #4413). The gate read adoption onto instance ownership ("your own kanban board (your own instance)") when the toolkit's real precondition is board API access (an api base + token + board id; it never reads who operates the host) — the parenthetical read successful tenant adopters out of the audience in the doc's first decision paragraph. Reworded to board access, naming the tenant case explicitly; consumer-agnostic.

## [0.16.0] - 2026-07-17

**Minor — three roundtable-driven board-tooling deliverables: `kbcard` card-removal verbs + two `board-snapshot` fixes.** 3 PRs since v0.15.1 (#118–#120). Cards #4479/#4448/#4447. One new command surface (`kbcard archive`/`delete`); the two `board-snapshot` changes are a correctness fix + a perf fix with no config migration.

### Added
- **#118** — **`kbcard archive` + `kbcard delete [--hard]` card-removal verbs** (card #4479, roundtable FR #74 sola-pm). The board API has no HTTP `DELETE` — removal is a non-discoverable top-level PATCH `_action` plus a two-step force-delete — so agents were hand-rolling `curl` to remove a test card. `archive` (`_action:archive`) takes a card off-board as a recoverable live row (keeps its id + `dl_number`); `delete` (`_action:delete`) trashes it recoverably within the retention window (**retains** `dl_number`); `delete --hard` soft-deletes then `POST force-delete.json` for a permanent cascade (**releases** `dl_number`). Thin orchestration over the existing `resolve_task` + `kb_api` primitives (mirrors `cmd_move`/`cmd_patch` — no write-path reimplementation); `--dry-run` + a `--yes` confirmation gate on the destructive paths.

### Fixed
- **#119** — **`board-snapshot`'s `untriaged()` inherited a sibling board's stage ids** (card #4448). `board-snapshot` reads each board's config by **sourcing** its env file (which `export`s `KB_BOARD_ID` + the `KB_STAGE_*` ids); `snap()` scrubbed a hardcoded subset before the next board but `untriaged()` unset only `KBCARD_TOKEN_FILE`, so in an operator shell that had sourced board A, a board B env **omitting** any terminal-stage id (`KB_STAGE_SHIPPED_TO_DEV`/`RELEASED_TO_MAIN`/`WONT_DO`) or `KB_BOARD_ID` silently inherited A's value — B's terminal set went wrong and cards were mis-suppressed or wrongly flagged UNTRIAGED, a **quiet** failure of the "triage is never silently missed" contract. `untriaged()` now scrubs the same full set `snap()` does and gains the `KB_BOARD_ID` guard.

### Changed
- **#120** — **`board-snapshot` fetches each board once, not twice** (card #4447). `snap()` and `untriaged()` were independent subshells that each sourced the env and called `fetch_board_cards` for the **same** board, so every SessionStart pulled each board's ~215KB payload twice (~half the API time; each `curl` is a fresh TLS handshake, so the cost is per-request). At the 10s SessionStart hook timeout a kill leaves the agent with **no** snapshot, and the waste scales with board count. Root cause was the two functions being copies of one setup+fetch (canon #5) — merged into one `board_report()` that fetches once and renders the in-flight snapshot to stdout + the untriaged list to fd 3 (7.24s → 4.85s on the reference multi-board host).

## [0.15.1] - 2026-07-16

**Patch — the v0.15.0 staleness guard now reaches the reader, plus a board-snapshot stage-name fix.** 2 PRs since v0.15.0 (#114, #115). Cards #4393/#4444. Both are fixes; no new surface, no config migration.

### Fixed
- **#114** — **the runtime staleness guard warned into the void** (card #4393). `board-snapshot` ran `agent-board-toolkit-runtime-check --quiet` but its warnings go to **stderr**, while `board-snapshot`'s consumer is a SessionStart hook that surfaces only **stdout** — so a correct, installed guard on a stale pin would have warned where nothing reads (the exact channel trap `board-card-start` already ate). The warning is now folded to **stdout**, where the snapshot itself lands, so the `v0.15.0`-shipped guard actually surfaces a stale pin at SessionStart. Pinned by a test that drives a real stale pin and asserts the warning survives a stderr-dropping consumer, with a positive control proving the same warning vanishes without the fold (dropping the `2>&1` reds 2 checks).
- **#115** — **`board-snapshot` printed a stale hardcoded stage name** (card #4444). The label map hardcoded `In Progress`/`In Review`/`Held` — a private second copy of the board's column names — so a board-side column rename left the snapshot asserting a name the board no longer used (it printed `[Held]` for a column renamed `Blocked`/`Gated`). Names are now sourced from the board via `preload.json` (~2.5KB — `boards/{id}.json` inlines every task at ~220KB, and there is no GET index for `workflow_stages`); selection still keys on the env stage ids (unchanged contract). An unreachable preload or an env id the board does not define degrades to `stage <id>` (the shape the untriaged block already prints) rather than a stale literal — an unnamed stage is honest, a guessed one is how the bug read as working. `kb_api` now honors `KB_CURL_MAX_TIME` (the cap `fetch_board_cards` already honored), so the new preload call cannot reintroduce the SessionStart-stall the cap prevents; unset = no cap, every existing caller unaffected.

### Docs
- **#114** — **`VERSIONING.md` gains the missing DEPLOY step** (release flow step 11). The flow previously ended at the tag — a tag is not a deploy, so `v0.15.0` shipped while the on-PATH runtime silently stayed at `v0.14.0`. Step 11 now advances the pinned runtime, **re-runs the symlink loop** (a symlink tracks its target's content, not the *set* of tools — a release that ADDS a bin never lands on PATH otherwise), runs the guard, and exercises one real command; it also documents the bootstrap rule (a guard shipped *in* the artifact it guards cannot force its own first run).


## [0.15.0] - 2026-07-15

**Minor — a quiet-wrong sweep: three read/diagnostic surfaces that answered plausibly-wrong instead of failing loud, one new guard that judges what EXECUTES, and the UPGRADE walk restored.** 10 PRs since v0.14.0 (#101–#110). **No breaking change; one new tool.** Consumers who vendor: re-vendor `promote-released-cards` (#110, diagnostic-only) and `_kb-board-lib.sh` (#103/#106).

### Added
- **#108** — **`bin/agent-board-toolkit-runtime-check`: verification that judges what EXECUTES, not the checkout** (cards #4361 + #4351). "Patch the checkout, then grep the checkout" certifies the wrong artifact on two live-observed topologies: real-file **copies** (MSYS/Windows `ln`, manual `cp`) and a **pinned worktree** (PATH detached at a tag that never advances — a maintainer box false-greened under the toolkit's own advisory). The check resolves each on-PATH tool through its symlink chain and **fails loud** on: a pinned runtime behind the newest tag (fetching tags first — a never-fetching pin is stale-blind), stale copies vs a reference checkout (`cmp`), and **mixed runtimes** (a partial upgrade where tools disagree about the lib they source). Honest UNKNOWN ≠ ok: an unverifiable copies topology **warns**, never passes silently. A tool the runtime ships but PATH lacks warns (found a real gap on the first run). `board-snapshot` runs it `--quiet` at SessionStart, closing #4351's silent-pin exposure (the tools keep working, just older — no symptom). 7-topology decision-matrix selftest + CI job.

### Fixed
- **#107** — **`kbcard show` emitted `stage: null` for a card that IS in a stage** while `list` mapped the real id (reported by an integrator; reproduced same-second). `null` is an assertion, not an absence — it read as "the card never moved" and caused a false "automation is broken" escalation downstream. `show` now maps `stage` from `workflow_stage_id` and resolves `column` by reverse `KB_STAGE_*` lookup, **omitting** any key it cannot populate. Shape-independent (correct whether the serializer omits the keys or emits nulls). New `kbcard-selftest` + CI job.
- **#109** — the same shape on the **write** echoes (card #4390): `create-card`/`patch` fabricated `payload: null` on boards whose serializer omits the key. One `_kbc_write_echo` primitive replaces two divergent inline filters; a server-**sent** null still passes through.
- **#103** — **`fetch_board_cards` lost the API error body** in the v0.8.2 lib extraction (`curl -fsS`): a 403 token-scope failure and a 422 validation failure were indistinguishable in the failure log (`curl-rc=22`). Restored `HTTP-<status> <body>` logging + loud-mode stderr; the selftest stub emulates `-f` so a regression reds.
- **#106** — **the short-read backstop no caller could reach** (card #4338): `fetch_board_cards` detected `total > read_n`, warned INCOMPLETE, then returned **0** — so `next-dl` minted a DL from a read the lib had just declared incomplete (its whole exit-code contract exists to prevent DL re-mints, DL-154). Now a distinct **rc 4** (partial data still emitted), with the pre-dedup page sum distinguishing a real undercount from a **page-boundary dedup artifact**. `next-dl`/`dl-a0` refuse it via their existing branches; `kbcard list`'s documented never-print-truncated contract now actually holds; `board-snapshot` still displays partials.
- **#105** — **`release-pr-body` computed the release baseline from the LOCAL `main` ref** (card #4348), which the documented release flow never updates — so it drifted a full release per cycle and the "generated — do not hand-edit" body authoritatively listed already-shipped PRs as new. Now fetches `origin/<main>` (`--tags` is load-bearing: release tags point at main-only merge commits a dev-side fetch never auto-follows); an unfetchable origin **fails loud** (exit 2) naming `--base <tag>` as the offline override — never a silent stale fallback. New fixture-repo selftest + CI job.
- **#110** — `promote-released-cards`' local dry-run derives its baseline from local tags and can over-report the shipped range; a stderr NOTE names it (suppressed under `GITHUB_ACTIONS`, where the CI invocation is remote-truth by construction). Diagnostic-only (card #4389).

### Docs
- **#104** — **`docs/UPGRADE.md` §6 walk-forward contract restored** (card #4345): the required `KANBAN_EXPECTED_HOST` action sat under a stale "Unreleased (after v0.8.2)" heading (it shipped in **v0.9.0** — pinned by tag inspection, not inferred) and **v0.9.0–v0.13.0 had no entries at all**, so an upgrader on v0.8.2..v0.12.x walked forward and found nothing. Reconstructed per-version with citations; no-action releases get explicit one-liners so a gap is never ambiguous.
- **#102** — Windows/MSYS **copy-not-symlink** note (reported by an integrator's Windows adopter): `ln -s` produces copies on native mingw64, silently changing the upgrade contract; INSTALL.md §2 now states it, plus the pinned-topology sibling and the verify-what-`~/.local/bin`-resolves-to rule.
- **#101** — HOOKS.md's bridge correlation-grammar row tracks the agent-webhook-bridge **v0.57.0** correlation grammar — the `card-<id>` dash alias + DL-shaped boundary (`\bcard[-#](\d+)`) — version-scoped, with the `<type>/card-<id>-<slug>` branch convention it enables.


_(empty after each tagged release; accumulates as feature PRs land on dev)_

## [0.14.0] - 2026-07-15

**Minor — a security fix in the `api_base` host guard, plus the restoration of the board-env token precedence that v0.8.2 silently broke.** 2 PRs since v0.13.0 (#98, #97). Cards #4346/#4325.

**Read before upgrading — two consumer-visible changes.** (1) **[vendor]** a repo that vendored `promote-released-cards` **must re-vendor** to get the security fix; the vendored copy carries its own guard and does not track the toolkit. **[release-CI]** SHA-pinned action consumers get it with the pin bump (`promote/action.yml` is unchanged). (2) **[host]** if you set `KBCARD_TOKEN_FILE` in **both** a board env and `~/.kanban-host.env`, the **board's** token now wins where the host's silently did since v0.8.2 — a live change in *which credential is sent*. Full actions: `docs/UPGRADE.md` § v0.14.0.

### Security
- **SECURITY — the `api_base` host guard accepted a URL that sends the bearer token to an attacker-controlled host.** `kb_require_https_host` (`_kb-board-lib.sh`) and its standalone mirror `host_ok` (`promote-released-cards`) terminated the URL authority at **`/` alone**. RFC 3986 ends it at the first of **`/`, `?`, or `#`** — so with a delimiter placed before an `@`, the userinfo strip (`${h##*@}`) reached past it and lifted a fake host out of the query/fragment:

  ```
  api_base: https://evil.example#@kanban.your-host.tld
  guard parses host -> kanban.your-host.tld  -> ACCEPTED
  curl connects to  -> evil.example          -> bearer token sent to the attacker
  ```

  Because `api_base` is read from the **committed, PR-editable** `.release-pr.json`, a pull request alone was enough: for `board-card-start` a maintainer merely checking out the branch leaked their kanban token, and for `promote-released-cards` a repo running promote on untrusted config would leak the `KANBAN_WRITEBACK_TOKEN` CI secret. Neither required any access to `$HOME` or `git config`. Both copies now terminate the authority at `[/?#]`. Verified end-to-end against a listener standing in for the attacker: the pre-fix hook delivered the bearer token, the fixed hook sends nothing and names the real host in its refusal. Legitimate values — subdomains, `:port`, real `user:pw@` userinfo, queries — are unaffected.

  It survived because the guard had **no decision coverage**: it was wired and ran on every checkout, but nothing had ever asked it to judge a hostile URL. New `tests/kb-host-guard-selftest.sh` (+ CI job) pins the matrix and additionally asserts the two copies agree — they are sync-paired by comment only, and both carried the identical defect.

  **[vendor] Action required:** `promote-released-cards` changed, so a repo that **vendored** it must re-vendor (UPGRADE §3) to get the fix. **[release-CI]** consumers of the SHA-pinned composite action get it with the pin bump — `promote/action.yml` itself is unchanged.
### Fixed
- **A board env's `KBCARD_TOKEN_FILE` is honored again — the documented precedence is restored.** `kbcard` honored it from v0.4.2 through v0.8.1 (`v0.8.1:bin/kbcard:440`, *"after host + board env are sourced, so a config-file `KBCARD_TOKEN_FILE` is honored"*). The **v0.8.2 shared-library extraction** consolidated six divergent copies of the config resolution onto the two that had the source order backwards (`next-dl`, `dl-a1-register-field`) and **silently regressed** it: from v0.8.2 through v0.13.0 a board-env `KBCARD_TOKEN_FILE` was ignored and the host/default token was sent to every board. The docs were right; the code drifted. One ladder now serves every tool — **board > host > ambient > `~/.kanban-dev-token`** — as a property of source order (host sourced first, board second) rather than six hand-rolled precedence tests. **Operator-visible where a board env *and* the host env both set `KBCARD_TOKEN_FILE`:** the board's token now wins, changing which credential is sent — see the `docs/UPGRADE.md` v0.14.0 entry.
- **A board env that sets the board-independent `KBCARD_API` is refused, loudly.** Same inverted order, same silence: v0.4.2–v0.8.1 ignored one (as INSTALL.md documented, because `API` was frozen before the board env was sourced), while v0.8.2–v0.13.0 sourced the board env *first* and so silently **honored** it — quietly re-pointing the tool at another host. `kb_resolve_env` now returns rc 4 and names the offending file; `next-dl` no longer swallows that message (it muted stderr, leaving a bare "config incomplete" while losing both the atomic DL claim and the board's `dl_number` seed — the DL-154 re-mint collision class).
- **`board-snapshot` no longer goes dark on a host with no `~/.kanban-dev-token`.** It read one token up-front and `exit 0`'d when that file was missing, so a host using only per-board tokens rendered **nothing**. It now reads each board's token as it renders that board, honoring that board's `KBCARD_TOKEN_FILE`; a board whose token is unreadable reports itself instead of blanking the entire snapshot.
- **`board-card-start` attributes a failed card read to its actual cause.** `get()` used `curl -f`, so an unreachable API, a rejected token (401/403), and a server error were all an empty body — every one reported as **"card … does not exist"**, sending an agent to hunt a missing card instead of a broken token. Each cause is now distinct in the durable diagnostic log. (The status is returned on stdout: `$(get …)` is a subshell, so a global set inside it cannot reach the caller.)

### Changed
- **`board-card-start`: per-board tokens are a host-local opt-in, and `git config kanban.board-id` outranks `.release-pr.json`.** The hook consults a board env's `KBCARD_TOKEN_FILE` **only** when the repo's board id came from the repo-local `git config`; a board id from the committed, PR-editable `.release-pr.json` keeps the host/default token (identical to prior behavior). Reordering the two sources alone would not have closed this — they are documented mutually-exclusive populations, so the inversion only helps the population that never had the exposure; gating the *capability* on the trusted source is the actual fix. The committed value is now digits-validated (`^[0-9]+$`) before it keys a board-env lookup.
- **Reading a board env is now clean-slate** (`kb_board_env_get`): every key is unset before the file is sourced, so a key the file does not set reads as **empty** instead of inheriting whatever is in the environment. Board envs `export` their keys, so in an operator shell that sourced one board's env, that board's values are live for every later tool. Scope, precisely: stage ids are unique to one board, so an inherited id never matches another board's cards — the reachable cases are `board-snapshot` (a board env with no `KB_BOARD_ID` would render a *different* board's cards under this board's label, or abort the subshell under `set -u`) and a board env missing a required stage id (the guard would pass on an inherited value and PATCH a stage that isn't on that board). `board-snapshot` now also says so (`• <label>: (env … sets no KB_BOARD_ID)`) rather than silently rendering nothing.
- **`board-card-start` resolves its board env once**, using it for both the stage ids and the token — the two lookups were separate matchers, free to disagree about which file wins when two board envs claim the same `KB_BOARD_ID`. A duplicate `KB_BOARD_ID` now warns (which file wins is arbitrary either way; being silent about it was the defect), and the stage ids are read newline-delimited so an unset optional `KB_STAGE_HELD` cannot shift another id into its place.
- **`kb_load_host_token`'s `gated`/`unconditional` mode flag is gone**, replaced by `kb_load_host_env`. The two modes differed only because the gate conflated "source the host env" with "let the host's `KBCARD_API` win"; separating those makes one behavior serve both callers. It also fixes the gated bug where a stray ambient `KBCARD_API` skipped the host env entirely, dropping the host's `KBCARD_TOKEN_FILE` — `board-snapshot` was the only `gated` caller, so it was the only tool affected.

### Added
- **`tests/kb-board-lib-selftest.sh` + a CI job** — network-free unit checks for the shared config resolution under a scratch `HOME`: the full token ladder in all four cases, the `KBCARD_API` refusal and its message, the failure return codes, the board-env matcher (no-match, a leaked `KB_BOARD_ID` false-match, duplicates, an unparsable env), and token paths containing spaces. The ladder is a property of source *order*, which is precisely how it regressed unnoticed for five minor versions — nothing exercised it.

## [0.13.0] - 2026-07-14

**Minor — `board-card-start` adoption hardening + Windows/Git-Bash curl portability.** 1 PR since v0.12.2 (#94). No config migration; back-compat (absent-config behavior is identical). Consumer-visible: the token auth mechanism, a new board-id resolution fallback, `core.hooksPath` support, and a durable diagnostic log. Cards #4281/#4289/#4290; correlation roundtable #30/#32/#34.

### Fixed
- **#94** — **Windows/Git-Bash portability: every kanban API call now works on native mingw64 curl.** The bearer token was fed to `curl` via `-H @<(…)` process substitution, which needs a `/dev/fd` named pipe that native mingw64/Git-Bash curl cannot open (rc=26) — so every API call fail-softed. All **8 call-sites** — the shared `_kb-board-lib.sh` (`kb_api`/`kb_api_status`/`fetch_board_cards`), `board-card-start`, `next-dl`, and the standalone `promote-released-cards` — now feed the token via a stdin herestring (`-H @- <<<`), which redirects a regular temp file onto fd 0. This keeps the token out of `argv` (unchanged security property) **and** off persistent disk (bash writes the herestring temp `0600` and unlinks it before exec), and additionally fixes a latent `promote-released-cards --retry` bug (the old process-sub pipe was non-seekable, so a retried request could not re-read the header). Vendored surface: `promote-released-cards` changes an internal auth-feed line, but `promote/action.yml` is unchanged — a SHA-pinned action consumer sees only the internal fix.
- **#94** — **`install-board-hooks` honors `core.hooksPath`.** It hardcoded `<repo>/.git/hooks/post-checkout`; when a repo sets `core.hooksPath` (gitleaks, the pre-commit framework, Husky, many Windows setups) git dispatches hooks only from there, so the install was a silent no-op (symlink created, install reports success, hook never fires). It now installs into `<core.hooksPath>/post-checkout`, and refuses — with operator guidance — a `core.hooksPath` that resolves inside the tracked work tree, where a machine-specific absolute symlink would show as a work-tree change and break on other clones.

### Added
- **#94** — **`board-card-start` resolves its board id without a `.release-pr.json`.** A repo that does not cut releases can bind itself to a board with a repo-local, uncommitted `git config kanban.board-id <id>` — so it adopts the hook without adding a committed, token-adjacent `api_base` surface (the board id is not a secret; `api_base` still resolves from `~/.kanban-host.env`). `.release-pr.json` `promote.board_id` still wins when present (back-compat).
- **#94** — **`board-card-start` durable diagnostic log.** When a branch carries a DL/card token but the move does not happen for an infrastructure reason (no resolvable board id, an unloadable token/host, an untrusted `api_base`, unresolved stage ids, an unreachable board, a named `card#N` that does not exist, or a pinned card), the hook prints the reason and appends it to `~/.cache/agent-board-toolkit/board-card-start.log` (`KB_BCS_LOG` overrides the path) — the installed hook wrapper discards stderr, so the log is the durable record. Still always `exit 0`. Genuine no-ops (no token), already-advanced cards, and the card-id board-scope guard stay silent (no cry-wolf).

### Changed
- **#94** — the host-scrub placeholder detector recognizes the RFC-2606/6761 reserved forms (`.invalid`, `.test`, `.localhost`, the bare `.example` TLD) in addition to `example.{com,net,org}`, each anchored to a host-label boundary so a real host that merely *contains* the substring (e.g. `kanban.latest-corp.com`) is not misread as a placeholder.

## [0.12.2] - 2026-07-14

**Patch — `docs/HOOKS.md` clarifies that un-parking a pinned card is intentionally NOT mirrored by the local hook (docs only; no bin/CI/vendored-surface change).** 1 PR since v0.12.1 (#91).

### Changed
- **#91** — `docs/HOOKS.md`: document that un-parking a pinned card is **owned by the real-time push-path mover, not the local `board-card-start` post-checkout hook**, by design. A push-path mover can override a deliberate pin and promote a pinned card from an opt-in unpark stage set on branch-cut *because it has a durable compensating override-alert surface*; a post-checkout hook's only surface is stderr — effectively silent when an agent drives git, never persisted — so it lacks the durable alert that would make reversing a deliberate pin safe. The local hook therefore keeps refusing pinned cards. Documentation only: `bin/*`, `promote/action.yml`, `examples/*`, and CI are byte-identical to v0.12.1, so a consumer that pins the action or vendors the scripts sees no functional change.

## [0.12.1] - 2026-07-13

**Patch — new agent-facing DL-counter recovery runbook (docs; one comment-only `bin/next-dl` change).** 1 PR since v0.12.0 (#88).

### Added
- **#88** — `docs/DL-COUNTER-RECOVERY.md`: an agent-facing, **API-driven** runbook for recovering a stranded per-board DL counter — the scenario where `next-dl` mints numbers in a far-too-high range because the board's monotonic DL high-water mark was pushed above the real sequence (e.g. by a hard-deleted or trashed high-DL card). Written for an operator/agent who drives a board **over its API token with no server shell**: diagnose (inspect the sequence + list refs), exhaustively sweep every trashed/junk pinner (not just the top one), force-delete each, then reset the counter — mirroring the board's own console recovery path via the board API's DL-sequence inspect/reset endpoints. `README.md` gains an index pointer to the runbook.

### Changed
- **#88** — `bin/next-dl` gains a **comment-only** pointer to the recovery runbook for the "minting far-too-high numbers" symptom. No behavior change — the executable path is byte-identical. `promote/action.yml`, all other `bin/*`, and `examples/*` are byte-identical to v0.12.0, so a consumer that pins the action or vendors the scripts sees no functional change.

## [0.12.0] - 2026-07-12

**Minor — new `bin/adopt-to-dl`: the pull-into-build adoption seam for card-first boards.** 1 PR since v0.11.4 (#85).

### Added
- **#85** — `bin/adopt-to-dl <card-id> --repo <owner/name> --board <name> [--dl N]`: stamps an existing *plain* board card with `payload.dl_number` + a source-qualified placeholder `pr_url` in **one atomic write**, then **fail-loud-verifies** the card resolves via the kanban `by-ref?system=dl&ref=N&source=<repo>` correlation — so a card crosses from manual ownership into the bridge writeback's PR-lifecycle authority with its `source` resolving **at adoption time**, not silently at the next `bridge:check`. Thin orchestration over existing primitives (no write-path reimplementation): `next-dl` mints the DL (atomic server-side claim, echoed to the caller **before** the write so a crash mid-seam is retried with `--dl N`), and `kbcard patch --dl … --pr-url …` performs the single-request `dl_number`+`pr_url` PATCH (kanban per-key payload merge). An **already-adopted guard** refuses to re-mint over a card that already carries a `dl_number` (which would orphan the old DL and strand any branch/PR named for it); `--dl N` re-stamps idempotently for a crash-retry. The by-ref verify **lowercases the source** (the kanban server stores and looks up `source` lowercased), so a mixed-case `--repo` verifies correctly. Sibling bins (`next-dl`, `kbcard`) resolve from the script's own directory, not `$PATH`. Ships with a network-free pure-logic selftest (`tests/adopt-to-dl-selftest.sh`) wired into CI as the `adopt-to-dl-selftest` job; `shellcheck -S error` now also covers `tests/`. `adopt-to-dl` is the write-site adoption seam **for CLI-adopted (card-first) boards** — a board whose `dl_number` is server-derived by a bridge writeback does not need it. It `source`s `bin/_kb-board-lib.sh` (a co-vendored dependency for vendor-by-copy consumers). All other `bin/*`, `promote/action.yml`, and `examples/*` are byte-identical to v0.11.4, so a consumer that pins the action or vendors existing scripts sees only the new bin. Card #4020.
